# The disease was recognised. The syndrome was divided. {#the-disease-was-recognised.-the-syndrome-was-divided. .case-thesis}

The GP referred one progressive, multidimensional clinical trajectory. Neurology correctly recognised severe young-onset Parkinsonism and documented a dystonic-looking lower limb. Yet the painful and structural foot territory was allowed to become a separate clinical object before the record shows an equivalent evidential bridge for that separation. The separation then became the architecture of care.

::: {.case-proof aria-label="The case in five propositions"}
<div>

01 · Referral\
**One trajectory**\

Feet, gait, tone, toe posture, movement, dexterity and functional decline arrived together under an open neurological question.

</div>

<div>

02 · Recognition\
**Neurological disease seen**\

Parkinsonism was identified at high resolution; the lower-limb gait was itself described as dystonic-looking.

</div>

<div>

03 · Division\
**Feet became separate**\

The synthesis allowed corns, callouses, burning and pain to become "completely separate" / peripheral while invoking the patient's "markedly divorced" distinction.

</div>

<div>

04 · Inheritance\
**The split generated care**\

Referrals, labels, treatment choices, service destinations and later records inherited the specialist representation.

</div>

<div>

05 · Return\
**Evidence crossed the split**\

Treatment-state effects, toe curling, OFF phenomena, dyskinesia, pain response and finally PRKN dystonia repeatedly reconnected the lower-limb territory to neurological disease.

</div>
:::

The case is not that Parkinsonism was missed. **It is that the dominant neurological syndrome was correctly recognised, while part of the same presenting lower-limb problem was separated from it without an equally visible evidential bridge --- and that separation became durable enough to organise the years that followed.**

::: {.cover-actions .landing-actions}
[Read the case](#section-what-happened){.primary-action}[See the proof](#section-governing-spine)[Open the full record](#section-main-analysis)
:::
:::

::: {#reader-map .reader-map .landing-boundary}
::: map-title
The evidential boundary
:::

::: boundary-grid
::: boundary-card
**What the record can carry**\

A high-dimensional neurological trajectory entered hospital; a dystonic-looking lower-limb sign was documented; the painful/structural foot territory became a separate explanatory track; that track generated downstream care; later treatment-state and dystonic evidence repeatedly crossed it; 2026 established that Parkinsonism and dystonia were manifestations of PRKN disease.
:::

::: boundary-card
**What remains genuinely open**\

The mechanism of every historical foot-pain episode; the proportion attributable to dystonia, structural pathology, neuropathic mechanisms or interaction; the clinical standard of the 2017 work-up and treatment; causal injury; and any claim about private motive or conscious knowledge of error.
:::
:::

## MUST READ --- Patient-authored statement of the case {#must-read-patient-authored-statement-of-the-case .source-h1 .anchored-heading}

**Status:** Primary patient-authored formulation. Read this in full before the analytical sections below. It states what the patient says went wrong and must not be silently rewritten into expert, legal, or institutional findings. Where its propositions exceed the documentary record, the analytical sections below retain the controlling distinction between record fact, documentary inference, clinical expert question, governance question, and legal conclusion.

## Statement of What I Say Went Wrong {#statement-of-what-i-say-went-wrong .source-h1 .anchored-heading}

My case is not that doctors in 2017 should somehow have possessed knowledge that only became available years later. I do not need hindsight to make this case.

My case is that they had enough information in front of them, at the time, to know that the relationship between my longstanding painful feet and my neurological disease had not been resolved. What I find extraordinarily difficult to understand is how an unresolved question appears to have been converted into an explanation, how that explanation then entered the medical record, and how the existence of that record seems subsequently to have given the explanation increasing authority.

What began as uncertainty appears to have become history.

What became history then appears to have influenced treatment.

And when later evidence repeatedly crossed the boundary that had originally been drawn, the boundary survived.

That is the central problem.

The safeguarding issue is ultimately even more serious. Whatever argument remains about diagnosis, mechanism or causation, the later medical record itself documents the severity of the physical and psychological state I had reached. By that stage, nobody needed to agree with my theory of the feet in order to recognise that something had gone profoundly wrong for the person living through it.

## The original presentation was one problem {#the-original-presentation-was-one-problem .anchored-heading}

The medicine is important, but the starting point is not complicated.

Idiopathic Parkinson's disease and PRKN-related Parkinson disease are both Parkinsonian disorders. PRKN disease is particularly associated with very young onset and lower-limb dystonia, and recognised descriptions include dystonia of the legs preceding the fuller Parkinsonian syndrome, sometimes for years.

That matters because the history recorded before Neurology constructed its explanation has a strikingly similar shape.

My painful or "sore" feet dated back to approximately 2004--2005.

When my GP first encountered me in April 2017, I was not presenting with two established diseases called Parkinson's disease and peripheral neuropathy. I was presenting with bilateral foot pain and an abnormal gait.

Over the following weeks, the neurological nature of the presentation became increasingly obvious. Difficulty initiating movement became apparent. Stiffness and increased tone developed. Reflexes were brisk. Dexterity deteriorated. Work became difficult. I described being unable to relax my muscles and clawing my toes in bed. Eventually my GP raised the possibility of Parkinsonism or another underlying neurological disorder and arranged urgent hospital assessment.

That is the actual starting point.

One patient.

One evolving presentation.

Feet, gait, tone, movement, toe posture, dexterity and function occurring in the same clinical history.

The later division between "Parkinson's" and "the feet" had not yet been made.

## Neurology itself saw dystonia in the lower limb {#neurology-itself-saw-dystonia-in-the-lower-limb .anchored-heading}

Neurology correctly recognised severe young-onset Parkinsonism. I was 39.

The syndrome was relatively symmetrical. There was marked rigidity and bradykinesia, brisk reflexes, little tremor and a profoundly abnormal gait.

But one observation is particularly difficult to reconcile with what subsequently happened.

The neurologist himself described the lower-limb appearance as:

**"somewhat suggestive of a dystonic posture --- NOT FOOT DROP."**

That observation did not come from me.

It was not generated retrospectively.

It was not produced by the later genetic result.

It was the treating neurologist's own examination finding in 2017.

The hospital was therefore not looking at an ordinary middle-aged case of Parkinson's disease accompanied coincidentally by sore feet.

It was looking at very-young-onset, atypical Parkinsonism, a dystonic-looking lower limb and a history in which painful feet had been present from the beginning.

The DaTscan referral itself described the unusual dystonic-looking walk and contemplated an autosomal-recessive Parkinsonian disorder.

PRKN/PARK2 was already within the clinical landscape.

That is why what happened next is so difficult for me to understand.

## The dystonic lower limb appears to disappear from the problem that mattered {#the-dystonic-lower-limb-appears-to-disappear-from-the-problem-that-mattered .anchored-heading}

I cannot identify in the record a developed neurological investigation of the relationship between:

the longstanding painful feet,

the abnormal gait,

the toe posture,

the dystonic-looking lower limb,

the rigidity,

the abnormal loading of the feet,

and the visible structural lesions.

Instead, the dystonic observation seems simply to reach a dead end.

A different explanation then appears in the synthesis.

The foot or leg symptoms might be "completely separate."

Or perhaps there was a secondary peripheral neuropathy.

Past alcohol consumption was suggested as a possible contributor.

I have never objected to peripheral neuropathy being considered.

What I object to is something much more fundamental.

**A possibility appears to have been allowed to occupy the place where an unresolved relationship required investigation.**

That is not the same thing.

A differential diagnosis is legitimate.

A hypothesis is legitimate.

What is difficult to defend is allowing the hypothesis to become the working destination of symptoms whose relationship to a dystonic neurological syndrome had not been worked through.

## I noticed the gap while the process was still open {#i-noticed-the-gap-while-the-process-was-still-open .anchored-heading}

I noticed the underlying problem myself before the admission process had completely finished.

I did not tell the doctors that I had dystonia.

I did not tell them that peripheral neuropathy was wrong.

I did not possess the specialist knowledge to make either claim.

I was a patient who had lived with painful feet from the beginning of the illness, and I noticed something much simpler:

**the feet had barely been investigated.**

I was drawing attention to an absence.

That should matter.

There was still time to respond.

The documentary process remained open after I physically left hospital. The DaTscan was subsequently performed and incorporated before final consultant sign-off. The record was therefore capable of receiving new information and modifying the final account.

Yet I cannot identify an equivalent attempt to reopen the unresolved relationship between the feet and the neurological syndrome.

Instead, the plan proceeded:

**"As per above."**

Those three words are not misconduct.

But their functional importance is enormous.

The material "above" became the information state from which care proceeded.

An unresolved explanation had become operational.

## And almost immediately, the feet began behaving like part of the neurological treatment problem {#and-almost-immediately-the-feet-began-behaving-like-part-of-the-neurological-treatment-problem .anchored-heading}

The information state created in 2017 did not remain unchallenged for years.

It was challenged almost immediately.

After neurological treatment with procyclidine and rasagiline, my physical improvement was dramatic.

My gait improved.

My writing improved.

I could stand straight.

My legs became loose and limber.

Normal physical function returned to an extent I described contemporaneously as almost miraculous.

Then procyclidine was withdrawn.

During that withdrawal, the outside-left-foot problem returned rapidly. I became more "shifty" and "unrelaxed." My feet became restless. My walk began falling apart.

When treatment was restored, the physical state improved again.

I do not claim that procyclidine removed a corn.

I do not claim that one medication response proves the mechanism of every painful sensation I had ever experienced.

I do not need to make either claim.

The important fact is much more difficult to dismiss:

**the disputed foot territory was behaving according to neurological medication state.**

Once that had happened, how could the neurological significance of the feet simply remain where it had been placed before the treatment experiment occurred?

That is the question I cannot answer from the record.

## The structural lesions did not resolve the neurological question {#the-structural-lesions-did-not-resolve-the-neurological-question .anchored-heading}

There was unquestionably structural disease in my feet.

There were corns and calluses.

There was a particularly persistent painful lesion around the lateral left forefoot, including the fifth metatarsophalangeal region.

But the existence of a structural lesion does not answer what produced the mechanical loading that repeatedly generated or aggravated it.

A callus is produced by pressure.

If dystonia, rigidity, abnormal toe posture or another neurological motor abnormality repeatedly changed how the foot contacted the ground, then Podiatry and Neurology were never rival explanations.

Podiatry could treat the visible lesion.

Neurology could still be responsible for the abnormal biomechanics producing or aggravating it.

That possibility was sitting directly between two services.

I cannot identify where it was investigated.

That absence is astonishing to me because the neurological examination had already described the lower limb as dystonic-looking.

## Then an independent neurologist said I probably had PARK2 {#then-an-independent-neurologist-said-i-probably-had-park2 .anchored-heading}

The next development should, in my view, have made the original separation harder to maintain.

Professor Daniel Healy did not simply see me and endorse ordinary idiopathic Parkinson's disease.

He wrote that I **"probably has Park II."**

He recommended testing PARK2 first and moving to a broader panel if it was negative.

By April 2018 my own treating neurologist was recording that PARK2/PRKN was what **"we/Prof Healy suspect is the cause."**

That date matters profoundly.

By then the treating service knew:

I had very-young-onset Parkinsonism;

the presentation was atypical and relatively symmetrical;

the lower limb had looked dystonic;

an autosomal-recessive Parkinsonian disorder had already been considered;

an independent neurologist thought I probably had PARK2;

PARK2 testing remained available;

my feet continued to cramp;

and the disputed foot territory had already changed according to neurological medication state.

How much more was required before the original separation of the feet from the neurological disease at least had to be reopened?

That question does not depend on my eventual genetic result.

They did not need a result from 2026.

They had the problem in front of them in 2017 and 2018.

## The hypothesis then became a mechanism {#the-hypothesis-then-became-a-mechanism .anchored-heading}

This is why I use the term **path dependence**.

Once peripheral neuropathy entered the record, it no longer had to win the diagnostic argument every year.

It was already there.

The feet had already been allocated.

Later evidence therefore arrived in a system that already had somewhere to put it.

That matters because over the following years the supposed boundary was crossed again and again.

Foot symptoms changed with neurological medication state.

Foot movement was later described as Parkinsonian.

OFF dystonia became an explicit possibility.

Toe curling persisted.

The record itself acknowledged that the earliest symptoms had involved the feet.

Dopaminergic medication repeatedly changed my experience of those feet.

And yet the peripheral-neuropathy formulation survived.

At some point, the question becomes unavoidable:

**What exactly would have been sufficient to dislodge it?**

If dystonic phenomenology did not do it;

if anticholinergic treatment response did not do it;

if medication withdrawal affecting the feet did not do it;

if probable PARK2 did not do it;

if continuing cramping did not do it;

if later OFF dystonia did not do it;

if toe curling did not do it;

and if dopaminergic responsiveness of the painful territory did not do it;

then what evidence was the original hypothesis actually vulnerable to?

A clinical hypothesis that apparently survives every contradiction without requiring reconstruction begins to look less like a hypothesis and more like an organising assumption.

That is what I say happened here.

## The language itself acquired authority {#the-language-itself-acquired-authority .anchored-heading}

There is another problem.

Once "peripheral neuropathy" and "neuropathic pain" became the medical vocabulary of the case, those terms naturally travelled.

Doctors used them.

Nurses used them.

I used them.

I was trying to communicate with the people responsible for treating me in language they recognised.

The later appearance of the same terminology from multiple mouths cannot automatically be treated as multiple independent confirmations of the original proposition.

If the service first supplied the language, the service cannot later point to the circulation of that language as though the diagnosis had independently reproduced itself throughout the record.

That would confuse repetition with validation.

The origin of a proposition matters.

The evidential bridge matters.

And I cannot identify the point at which the tentative 2017 possibility became independently established strongly enough to justify the increasingly settled language later used.

## The 2021 treatment transition makes the later history even harder to comprehend {#the-2021-treatment-transition-makes-the-later-history-even-harder-to-comprehend .anchored-heading}

The pharmacological history adds another layer.

Procyclidine had produced dramatic symptomatic benefit.

It was later removed because of ocular concerns.

It was replaced with Sinemet.

But Sinemet was not a neutral substitute.

The dopaminergic treatment rapidly became constrained by dyskinesia and chorea.

That creates an obvious therapeutic problem.

If one treatment modality that had provided major control was withdrawn, and its replacement could not simply be increased because excessive dopaminergic exposure produced involuntary movement, then the patient may occupy a narrow therapeutic window.

Too much treatment causes one problem.

Too little may permit another.

That is precisely the sort of situation in which unexplained lower-limb symptoms should become more, not less, interesting.

Yet the feet remained within the inherited neuropathic framework.

I find that extraordinarily difficult to reconcile with the clinical history.

## The subsequent record repeatedly supplied reasons to reconstruct the model {#the-subsequent-record-repeatedly-supplied-reasons-to-reconstruct-the-model .anchored-heading}

The years that followed did not preserve a clean separation between Parkinsonism and the feet.

The opposite happened.

The boundary was repeatedly crossed.

Foot symptoms varied according to medication state.

Dystonia became an explicit consideration.

Toe curling remained.

The feet continued to interfere with walking.

Neurological medication continued to affect them.

The record itself documented that the early history involved the feet.

None of this proves that every painful sensation had one neurological mechanism.

It does something more basic.

It repeatedly undermines confidence in any model that treats "the feet" as a stable, separate object whose neurological relationship was already settled in 2017.

And yet that is approximately how the category seems to have functioned.

## By 2023 the human consequences were becoming unmistakable {#by-2023-the-human-consequences-were-becoming-unmistakable .anchored-heading}

Meanwhile, the problem was no longer merely diagnostic.

The suffering was becoming severe.

By 2023 the record described the foot problem as a **"heavy psychological burden."**

I described walking as something that **"psychologically consumes"** me.

Another walk could appear as a **"psychological mountain."**

The same clinical record still contained bilateral foot problems, cramping and consideration of Parkinsonian OFF dystonia.

Whatever anybody believed about the precise cause, something had become deeply wrong.

This was not an abstract debate over nomenclature.

A patient was becoming psychologically consumed by a physical problem that remained unresolved after years of specialist care.

## By 2024 my world was contracting {#by-2024-my-world-was-contracting .anchored-heading}

By 2024 the record described my life as **"a lot smaller."**

Activities had contracted.

Foot pain and toe curling were interfering with ordinary function.

Again, nobody had to accept my causal interpretation to understand what this meant.

This was prolonged suffering affecting daily life.

The unresolved clinical problem had become a lived restriction on existence.

And still the historical explanatory structure remained.

## By 2025 the language was extreme {#by-2025-the-language-was-extreme .anchored-heading}

By 2025 my feet could be too painful to tie my runners.

Mornings were described as **"murder in the feet."**

The first 150 footsteps were **"torture."**

Those are not retrospective descriptions invented for a complaint.

They are the language contained within the clinical history itself.

The same record also says that when Parkinson's medication reached my system, the feet felt better.

And yet the old formulation was still present:

**"if there is a degree of peripheral neuropathy here."**

I find the coexistence of those facts very difficult to comprehend.

The patient has an early dystonic-looking lower limb.

The feet respond to neurological medication state.

PARK2 is suspected.

Later OFF dystonia is considered.

Toe curling persists.

Dopaminergic treatment affects the feet.

By 2025 pain is described as torture.

And the old peripheral formulation is still there.

At some point it becomes reasonable to ask whether the persistence of the explanation reflects the strength of its evidence, or simply the strength of its position in the record.

## Then Sinemet CR supplied another major reason to reopen everything {#then-sinemet-cr-supplied-another-major-reason-to-reopen-everything .anchored-heading}

I subsequently experienced profound relief from a longstanding component of the foot pain after using Sinemet CR at night.

Again, I do not need that to prove that every historical pain sensation was dystonia.

I do not claim that treatment response alone proves mechanism.

That is not the point.

The point is that after everything that had already happened, this was yet another substantial challenge to the original partition.

At minimum, it demanded explanation.

At minimum, it made the relationship between dopaminergic state and the painful lower limb impossible to regard as trivial.

At minimum, it should have reopened the question.

What happened instead is what I find most disturbing.

## When I complained, the historical structure was defended {#when-i-complained-the-historical-structure-was-defended .anchored-heading}

The response to my complaint continued to refer to **"peripheral neuropathic pain symptomatologies."**

The striking Sinemet response was accommodated by saying that Parkinson's medication could alter the experience of pain **"of whatever cause."**

That formulation preserves the original structure perfectly.

The drug response can be accepted.

The pain relief can be accepted.

The historical classification need not change.

Everything new can be absorbed without forcing reconstruction of the original model.

That is precisely the problem.

The response also relied on the fact that I had been given an early second opinion from Professor Healy.

But Healy's actual documented opinion was that I probably had PARK2.

His opinion therefore cannot simply function as retrospective confirmation of an uncomplicated idiopathic-Parkinson's framework.

That distinction matters.

## The documentary history appears to have become its own defence {#the-documentary-history-appears-to-have-become-its-own-defence .anchored-heading}

This is perhaps what I find hardest to believe.

An uncertain explanation entered the record in 2017.

Subsequent care proceeded from that information state.

The terminology propagated through the record.

Later symptoms were interpreted within it.

The patient himself eventually used its vocabulary when asking for help.

Years of letters therefore accumulated in which the same broad framework appeared again and again.

Then, when the framework was challenged, the existence of those letters could itself be cited as evidence that the issue had always been adequately considered.

That risks becoming circular.

The record proves the continuity of the record.

It does not necessarily prove repeated independent validation of the proposition inside it.

The question is not how many times the words appeared.

The question is what evidence established them.

## I do not need to allege malice {#i-do-not-need-to-allege-malice .anchored-heading}

I cannot know another person\'s motive.

I do not need to allege that anybody deliberately harmed me.

I do not need to allege falsification.

The sequence is serious enough without doing so.

What I say is that an early explanatory decision became increasingly difficult to dislodge; that contradictory or qualifying information was repeatedly accommodated without reconstructing the whole problem; and that by the time I challenged the history formally, the accumulated documentation generated by that pathway was capable of being used to defend the pathway itself.

Whether that arose from anchoring, compartmentalisation, professional defensiveness, institutional self-protection, ordinary cognitive bias or some combination of these is a separate question.

The documentary problem exists regardless of motive.

## And then there is safeguarding {#and-then-there-is-safeguarding .anchored-heading}

Everything above could still be debated.

A clinician can be wrong without wrongdoing.

A peripheral neuropathy hypothesis can be reasonable.

A mixed structural and neurological foot problem can be genuinely difficult.

A genetic diagnosis can remain uncertain.

A medication response can have more than one interpretation.

I accept all of that.

But safeguarding is different.

By 2023 the medical record itself was describing my condition as a heavy psychological burden.

Walking psychologically consumed me.

Another walk could feel like a psychological mountain.

By 2024 my life was a lot smaller.

By 2025 my mornings were "murder in the feet."

The first 150 footsteps were "torture."

Those words describe a human being reaching the limits of what he could tolerate.

At that point, the correctness of the original 2017 differential diagnosis was no longer the only question.

There was another one:

**What was happening to the person living inside this unresolved problem?**

And:

**What was being done to keep him safe?**

## That question did not require diagnostic agreement {#that-question-did-not-require-diagnostic-agreement .anchored-heading}

Safeguarding did not require anybody to admit that the neuropathy hypothesis was wrong.

It did not require acceptance of my theory.

It did not require PRKN confirmation.

It did not require somebody to concede that the 2017 discharge synthesis had failed.

It did not even require immediate resolution of the pain.

It required recognition of what prolonged unresolved suffering was doing to the patient.

That is why I find this part particularly difficult to understand.

The record available to me shows increasingly serious descriptions of psychological deterioration.

I cannot identify a corresponding documented safeguarding response proportionate to what was being recorded.

I am careful about that formulation.

Absence from the surviving record does not prove absolutely nothing happened.

But where the record itself contains escalating descriptions of suffering, the absence of a visible corresponding protective response is not a trivial documentary gap.

It is a serious question in its own right.

## Two connected failures {#two-connected-failures .anchored-heading}

I therefore allege two connected failures.

The first is **clinical and informational**.

An integrated medication-naïve feet--gait--tone--movement presentation was decomposed. The neurological component was investigated and treated at high resolution. The unresolved painful-foot component was given a possible peripheral explanation before its relationship to the dystonic lower limb and neurological disease had been equivalently established. That explanation entered the record and acquired longitudinal authority. Repeated later reasons to reconstruct the relationship did not visibly produce a full reconstruction.

The second is **human and protective**.

While that unresolved pathway continued, the patient\'s physical suffering became severe and psychologically consuming. His activities contracted. Walking became a psychological burden. The language used to describe mornings became "murder" and "torture."

Yet the record available to me does not demonstrate a safeguarding response proportionate to that deterioration.

## The question I am left with {#the-question-i-am-left-with .anchored-heading}

I survived this history.

That sentence is more important to me than the diagnostic terminology.

My central question is no longer merely why a tentative 2017 explanation survived so much evidence that should at least have forced it back into question.

It is why the system appears to have been able to document, year after year, the consequences of the unresolved problem without allowing those consequences to break the explanatory structure that had been created at the beginning.

And ultimately it is this:

**How could a record document the dystonic-looking lower limb, dramatic neurological treatment response, probable PARK2, continuing cramping, medication-dependent foot symptoms, OFF dystonia, toe curling, severe pain, psychological consumption, a shrinking life, "murder in the feet" and "torture" --- and still arrive at a position in which the historical pathway required defence more urgently than the pathway required reconstruction?**

That is what I cannot understand.

That is what I say requires independent explanation.

And that is what I say went wrong.

------------------------------------------------------------------------
:::

::: {#section-what-happened .section .reader-section .kind-current .narrative-spine section="What happened — plain-language narrative spine"}
::: section-kicker
The strongest supportable narrative · read this first
:::

## The case {#what-happened .source-h1 .anchored-heading}

The record is most powerful when read as a sequence of transformations rather than as a dispute over labels. **One evolving clinical trajectory went in. A divided clinical architecture came out.**

## 1. The referral described a syndrome becoming visible over time {#what-happened-trajectory .anchored-heading}

The GP did not refer a settled diagnosis. The referral described an **evolving trajectory**: unusual festinant and stiff gait, bilateral foot problems, difficulty initiating movement, marked stiffness and increased tone in all four limbs, brisk reflexes, inability to relax, toe clawing in bed, worsening dexterity, deteriorating work function and progressive loss of capacity. The question remained open: **"?parkinsonian or other underlying diagnosis."**

That matters because the clinical object was relational. Feet, gait, tone, movement initiation, toe posture, dexterity and function were not presented as independent diagnoses. They were dimensions of the same worsening history requiring explanation.

## 2. Neurology recognised the neurological disease --- and also saw the lower-limb dystonia signal {#what-happened-recognition .anchored-heading}

The central neurological syndrome was not missed. Neurology documented marked symmetrical tone, brisk reflexes, lead-pipe and axial rigidity, gross bradykinesia and a strikingly abnormal gait. The lower-limb pattern was described as **"somewhat suggestive of a dystonic posture --- NOT FOOT DROP."** The case then entered sophisticated young-onset Parkinsonian and recessive-genetic reasoning.

This is the point that makes the later history difficult. The record did not begin with an ordinary Parkinsonian syndrome on one side and an unrelated foot complaint on the other. **The neurological examination itself placed dystonia in the lower-limb territory that was already clinically entangled with the feet.**

## 3. The syndrome was then divided {#what-happened-division .anchored-heading}

In the specialist synthesis, the Parkinsonian dimensions were developed at high resolution while the painful and structural foot territory became separable. The leg symptoms involving corns, callouses and burning were proposed as possibly **"completely separate"** or perhaps secondary peripheral neuropathy. The same passage recorded that the patient had **"markedly divorced"** the feelings of leg tension from the corns, callouses and foot pain.

The patient's discernment was phenomenological: different experiences felt different and had behaved differently over time. That was clinically useful information. But a distinction in experience is not, by itself, a demonstration of biological independence. **The consequential move was that the distinction acquired enough classificatory force to support a separate explanatory and service track.**

> **The disease was recognised. The syndrome was divided.**

## 4. The division became the pathway {#what-happened-generativity .anchored-heading}

Once the specialist representation acquired authority, it stopped being merely descriptive. Parkinsonism received neurological investigation, imaging, treatment strategy, genetic reasoning and specialist correspondence. The feet received structural and peripheral explanations, Podiatry involvement and later neuropathic terminology. Structural foot pathology was real, and peripheral mechanisms remained possible. **But coexistence is not independence.**

The split then generated the record that followed: referrals, service destinations, vocabulary, treatment choices and inherited assumptions. Later clinicians did not repeatedly encounter the untreated 2017 phenotype; they encountered a patient whose case had already been organised for them.

> **The model did not merely describe the pathway. It helped produce the pathway that later appeared to corroborate the model.**

## 5. The later record repeatedly crossed the division {#what-happened-boundary .anchored-heading}

Almost immediately, neurological treatment altered the wider physical state. During the 2017 procyclidine period the patient described his walk returning, his legs becoming loose and limber, posture and dexterity improving, and ordinary movement becoming possible. During planned withdrawal, deterioration again included the feet and gait.

Across the later record came further lower-limb information that did not respect the original boundary: cramping, toe curling, OFF-type phenomena, medication-state changes, levodopa sensitivity and dyskinesia. In 2023 the GP explicitly recalled severe bilateral burning foot and lower-leg pain against an earlier episode that had settled with better Parkinson's control. By March 2025 the treating neurologist acknowledged that Sinemet helped morning pain and that Sinemet CR had produced **"profound relief."**

None of this proves that every historical corn, callus, structural deformity or burning pain was dystonia. It establishes the narrower and more important point that **the lower-limb/foot territory repeatedly behaved as neurologically relevant and repeatedly supplied reasons to reconstruct the original syndrome.**

## 6. The endpoint sharpened the original question; it did not create it {#what-happened-endpoint .anchored-heading}

PARK2 was already a live specialist hypothesis in 2017. Prof Daniel Healy later recorded that the patient probably had PARK2, noted the dramatic response to low-dose procyclidine and rasagiline, and cautioned about dyskinesia with dopaminergic treatment. In 2026, genetic testing established that the Parkinsonism and dystonia were manifestations of compound-heterozygous PRKN disease.

The 2026 result must not be projected backwards as if it proved the mechanism of every earlier symptom. Its legitimate force is different: **it confirms that the dystonic and Parkinsonian components belonged to the same neurological disease, while the historical record already shows that the lower-limb/foot territory had been part of the unresolved clinical problem from the beginning.**

## 7. What is missing is not documentation. It is independent reconstruction {#what-happened-independence .anchored-heading}

The later record contains abundant documentation, genuine treatment adjustment and sustained clinical attention. What is much harder to identify is a later event in which an appropriately qualified reviewer returned to the source phenotype, temporarily suspended the inherited categories, reconstructed feet--gait--tone--dystonia--pain from first principles, incorporated the treatment-state evidence, and independently re-derived the original separation.

That distinction matters because **continuity is not convergence**. Repetition of a classification across records can prove that the classification travelled. It does not automatically prove that each later source independently earned it.

::: case-verdict
**The strongest supportable case is therefore this:** a progressive multidimensional neurological syndrome entered specialist care; the dominant Parkinsonian disease was correctly recognised; the painful lower-limb/foot territory was nevertheless separated into another clinical object before the surviving record shows an equivalent evidential bridge for that separation; the separation then acquired enough authority to organise subsequent care; later evidence repeatedly crossed it; and the accumulated record shows far more clearly how the inherited model was managed than where it was independently reconstructed from first principles.
:::

## The question the record has to answer {#what-happened-bottom-line .anchored-heading}

> **What evidence justified making the painful lower-limb/foot territory durably separate from the neurological syndrome --- and where is the later independent reconstruction showing that this separation remained justified after the subsequent evidence accumulated?**
:::

::: {#section-governing-spine .section .reader-section .kind-current .thesis-band section="Governing spine — integrated v0.16"}
::: section-kicker
Governing spine · integrated v0.17
:::

## The case in its cleanest current form {#governing-spine .source-h1 .anchored-heading}

The case is best understood as **recognition followed by decomposition, then inheritance**. The hospital was not wrong to recognise severe young-onset Parkinsonism. The disputed event is narrower: part of the lower-limb syndrome became a separate explanatory object before the surviving record shows that the separation had earned the longitudinal weight later placed on it.

**Core proposition:** the founding separation became more durable than the evidence demonstrating its independence.

::: evidence-ledger
<div>

**Input**\

A progressive referral joined feet, gait, tone, movement, toe posture, dexterity and function under one open neurological question.

</div>

<div>

**Transformation**\

Neurology documented a dystonic-looking lower-limb sign, yet the painful/structural foot territory became separable as "completely separate" / possibly peripheral.

</div>

<div>

**Authority**\

The output carried specialist and institutional authority and generated referrals, terminology, treatment strategy and inherited information states.

</div>

<div>

**Challenge**\

Later treatment-state and dystonic evidence repeatedly crossed the split; the record shows local adaptation more clearly than first-principles reconstruction.

</div>
:::

**Endpoint discipline:** the 2026 PRKN diagnosis confirms that Parkinsonism and dystonia belonged to one genetic disease. It does not prove the mechanism of every historical foot-pain episode, and it is not needed to establish the earlier documentary transformation.

> **Documentary density is not evidential independence. Authority increases portability; it does not itself increase independent validation.**

## Keep the evidential levels separate {#governing-evidential-levels .anchored-heading}

::: evidence-levels
::: evidence-level
**Record fact**\
What the contemporaneous source actually records: observations, language, treatment, correspondence, meetings and later responses.
:::

::: evidence-level
**Documentary inference**\
How information was compressed, partitioned, inherited, reclassified or given increasing grammatical authority.
:::

::: evidence-level
**Clinical expert question**\
Whether the examination, classification, treatment strategy, DBS information, therapeutic-window handling or safeguarding response met appropriate clinical expectations.
:::

::: evidence-level
**Governance hypothesis**\
Whether complaint and institutional processes independently reconstructed the upstream model or mainly assessed the defensibility of decisions taken within it.
:::
:::

::: callout
**Protected restraint**\
This architecture supports path dependence, asymmetric evidential resolution, prospective defensibility and a testable hypothesis of retrospective constructive defence. It does **not** presently establish concealment, falsification, improper motive, conscious recognition of error, or that all foot pain was dystonic/PRKN-mediated.
:::
:::

::: {#section-24-hour-compression .section .reader-section .kind-current section="24-Hour Compression Point"}
::: section-kicker
Critical opening interval · 3--4 July 2017
:::

## The 24-Hour Compression Point {#24-hour-compression-point .source-h1 .anchored-heading}

**The longest-lasting feature of the eight-year pathway may have been created between Monday 3 July and the early Complex Case Meeting 2017.**

The significance of this interval is not that Parkinsonism was missed. It was recognised rapidly and investigated at high resolution. Nor is the point that specialist expertise was unavailable. The case was brought into collective Neurology reasoning almost immediately. The question is narrower: **what representation of the patient reached that expertise?**

## Established chronology {#24h-established-chronology .anchored-heading}

**Monday, 3 July 2017:** the patient entered hospital for neurological assessment. The pre-admission GP material described an integrated feet--gait--tone--movement problem, including abnormal gait, increased tone, difficulty initiating movement, inability to relax, toe clawing, deteriorating dexterity and functional decline under an open neurological question.

The early neurological examination then recovered much of the untreated phenotype and described a strikingly abnormal gait with an apparent foot-drop-like lower-limb posture that was **"somewhat suggestive of a dystonic posture --- NOT FOOT DROP."**

**Tuesday, 4 July 2017:** the case was considered at the Neurology **Complex Case Meeting**. The surviving specialist synthesis records: *"Seen at the Neurology Complex case meeting with my colleagues --- likely an IPD albeit with atypical features."* The date follows from the Monday admission chronology together with the department\'s Tuesday Complex Case Meeting schedule.

::: callout
**Temporal hinge**\
Monday: integrated medication-naïve phenotype and dystonic-looking lower-limb sign.\
Tuesday: collective specialist formulation.
:::

## Evidential levels {#24h-evidential-levels .anchored-heading}

**Documented fact:** the broad GP problem preceded admission; Neurology documented the dystonic-looking gait; the specialist synthesis records Complex Case Meeting review; subsequent contemporaneous correspondence shows collective consultation around treatment and genetics.

**Date reconstruction:** Monday 3 July is independently anchored by the admission chronology; the Complex Case Meeting was the Tuesday meeting, fixing the relevant meeting to the early Complex Case Meeting 2017. The record should preserve the specific source establishing the Tuesday schedule alongside this reconstruction.

**Not established by the surviving material:** the exact oral presentation made to the meeting; which source documents each participant personally reviewed; whether the GP\'s relational framing was explicitly reproduced; whether the painful feet and dystonic-looking gait were discussed as one problem; or whether the later partition was generated by the meeting or inherited by it.

## Two possible pathways {#24h-two-pathways .anchored-heading}

### Pathway A --- the integrated phenotype reached collective review

If the Tuesday meeting received the problem substantially as it existed on Monday --- longstanding painful feet, abnormal gait, toe posture, inability to relax, severe tone/rigidity, deteriorating dexterity, structural foot abnormalities and a medication-naïve dystonic-looking lower limb --- then the later separation of part of the foot syndrome into potentially structural or peripheral-neuropathic territory requires explanation at the level of **collective specialist reasoning**.

> **What evidence led the group to distinguish the painful-foot territory from the neurological lower-limb phenotype, and what findings were considered sufficient to support that separation?**

Multiple mechanisms could legitimately coexist. The missing evidential object would be the proposition-by-proposition bridge showing how the branches were distinguished, how interaction between them was considered, and what would have triggered reconsideration.

### Pathway B --- the problem had already been compressed before collective review

If the case reached the meeting already reformatted as **young-onset Parkinsonism requiring sophisticated neurological investigation** plus **a potentially separate foot/pain/peripheral problem**, the systems issue moves upstream.

The specialist group could have given entirely reasonable advice to the case as presented while never being asked to adjudicate the relationship that had been central to the original GP presentation. In that scenario, the decisive transformation occurred between Monday\'s integrated assessment and Tuesday\'s collective formulation.

**Fact recovery is not relationship preservation.**

## The decisive question {#24h-decisive-question .anchored-heading}

The crucial unknown is no longer primarily *when* the collective formulation occurred. It is:

> **What exactly did the the early Complex Case Meeting 2017 Complex Case Meeting understand the clinical problem to be?**

If the whole feet--gait--tone--posture--pain relationship was presented and expressly adjudicated, the record should be capable of showing how. If it was not presented as a relationship, then the case becomes one of **problem-representation failure occurring before collective expertise was applied**.

The strongest present formulation is:

> **The right expertise may have been available within twenty-four hours, but the clinically decisive relationship may no longer have been the question placed before it.**

This is a testable reconstruction, not a finding of clinical breach. Its strongest falsifier would be contemporaneous Complex Case Meeting material showing that the integrated lower-limb relationship was expressly presented, debated and given a reasoned disposition or reopening plan.

------------------------------------------------------------------------
:::

::: {#section-generativity .section .reader-section .kind-current section="Input, transformation and generative authority"}
::: section-kicker
Observable systems event · no mind-reading required
:::

## Input, transformation and generative authority {#input-transformation-generative-authority .source-h1 .anchored-heading}

**The 2017 representation can be audited as an observable input → transformation → authority-bearing output → downstream generativity sequence.**

## 1. What was demonstrably available {#generativity-input .anchored-heading}

The GP referral did not present a bare Parkinsonism query. It linked bilateral foot problems, an unusual festinant/stiff gait, increasing movement-initiation difficulty, marked upper- and lower-limb tone, brisk reflexes, inability to relax, toe clawing, deteriorating dexterity, occupational impairment and an expressly open *"?parkinsonian or other underlying diagnosis"* question.

Neurology\'s own medication-naïve examination then added information: markedly increased tone, brisk reflexes, lead-pipe/axial rigidity, gross bradykinesia, structural foot abnormalities and a striking pseudo-foot-drop gait described as somewhat suggestive of **dystonic posture** and explicitly **not true foot drop**.

::: callout
**Input adequacy**\
By the time the specialist model was being constructed, the available clinical object was richer---not poorer---than the original referral. The central question is therefore what happened to the *relationships* among those facts.
:::

## 2. The compression is documentarily visible {#generativity-transformation .anchored-heading}

The hospital\'s portable referral/problem representation foregrounded difficulty initiating movement, four-limb increased tone/stiffness and wrist cogwheeling. Compared with the GP input, that representation is narrower: the feet, gait, toe clawing, inability to relax, functional trajectory and explicit "parkinsonian or other" relationship lose equivalent prominence.

> **The compression does not need to be inferred from anyone\'s private mental state. It is visible by comparing what went into the hospital with the clinical object reproduced in the hospital\'s own portable representation.**

This does not prove the compression was clinically unreasonable. It identifies the transformation that a later expert or reviewer must evaluate.

## 3. What response did the relational input require? {#generativity-response .anchored-heading}

At documentary level, the referral placed the feet--gait--tone relationship inside the problem sent for neurological assessment. Once Neurology additionally documented a dystonic-looking lower-limb sign, any later separation of that territory became an explanatory act requiring an evidential basis.

That does **not** establish what specific 2017 examination, investigation or treatment the standard of care required. Those remain clinical expert questions. The narrower documentary proposition is that the relationship had entered the hospital\'s information state and therefore could not be treated as though it had never been part of the problem.

## 4. The output was authority-bearing {#generativity-authority .anchored-heading}

The resulting representation came from Consultant Neurology, was recorded as having undergone Complex Case consideration, and generated specialist-to-specialist correspondence, treatment planning and service destinations. That gave it legitimate institutional authority and made later reliance upon it rational.

> **Authority increases portability; it does not itself increase evidential independence.**

The more authoritative an early representation becomes, the more important it is to distinguish later *inheritance* of that representation from later *independent re-derivation* of its premises.

## 5. The representation was generative {#generativity-output .anchored-heading}

The 2017 model did not merely describe the pathway. It generated the pathway\'s future information state: treatment strategy, Podiatry and rehabilitation destinations, follow-up structure, genetic questions, symptom categories, correspondence, and terminology available to GPs, nurses, other services and eventually the patient himself.

> **The model did not merely describe the pathway. It helped produce the pathway that later appeared to corroborate the model.**

This is why documentary multiplicity cannot be counted naively. A later note, referral or patient communication may be a new document without constituting a new independent evidential event if its organising category descends from the original specialist representation.

## The three-part opening test {#generativity-three-part-test .anchored-heading}

::: independence-test
**Input adequacy:** What clinically material information was demonstrably available?\
\
**Representational adequacy:** What relationships did the specialist response preserve, adjudicate, compress or reassign?\
\
**Generative authority:** What later classifications, service destinations, management decisions and apparent corroboration were downstream products of that response rather than genuinely independent of it?
:::

This framework remains compatible with conscientious care and legitimate diagnostic uncertainty. It tests the information architecture without converting documentary transformation into a claim about motive.
:::

::: {#section-expertise .section .reader-section .kind-current section="Expertise at the point of compression"}
::: section-kicker
Expertise proximity · why the question matters
:::

## The relevant disease territory was not remote from the network surrounding the case {#expertise-at-compression .source-h1 .anchored-heading}

The strongest use of the specialist context is not to say that PRKN should have been diagnosed immediately or that every painful foot symptom should have been classified as dystonia. It is to ask whether the **right expertise was applied to the right clinical object**.

## What the record itself establishes {#expertise-record-facts .anchored-heading}

Contemporaneous clinician-to-clinician correspondence described a 39-year-old first presenting to Neurology after an apparent thirteen-year symptom history and sought Daniel Healy\'s advice on genetics and treatment. The same page states: **"Myself and Tim are keen to hold back on Levodopa insofar as maybe possible unless you\'d advise otherwise?"** The page itself does not identify "Tim"; later clinician material identifies him as Professor Timothy Counihan. The exact original date/header of that reproduced page remains a source gap. [CLIN-001720--001733; CLIN-003423--003437]{.record-ref}

After personally reviewing the patient in September 2017, Professor Healy wrote that he thought the patient **"probably has Park II"**, recommended testing PARK2 first, recorded a dramatic motor improvement with low-dose procyclidine and rasagiline, and stated that PARK II patients can manage for long periods on relatively low dopamine replacement while becoming markedly dyskinetic if over-used. [CLIN-001742--001760]{.record-ref}

The 2026 laboratory report later identified two pathogenic PRKN variants, including **c.1289G\>A; p.Gly430Asp (G430D)**. That result establishes the endpoint; it must not be used as proof of what clinicians were required to know in July 2017. [CLIN-006195--006203]{.record-ref}

## Contemporaneous scholarly context --- externally verified {#expertise-external-context .anchored-heading}

Healy and Counihan were co-authors of the 2013 *JAMA Neurology* paper *Parkin Disease: A Clinicopathologic Entity?*. The paper described genetically confirmed parkin disease with early onset and dystonia among presenting features, and its genetic series included the **c.1289G\>A, p.G430D** variant.

In August 2017, Counihan and colleagues in the University Hospital Galway Neurology Department published *Clinical Reasoning: A demure teenager and her dystonic foot*. That case involved abnormal gait/dystonic foot phenomenology, compound-heterozygous parkin mutations including **c.1289G\>A**, procyclidine treatment, and discussion of dyskinesia risk in recessive Parkinsonism.

::: source-note
**Temporal restraint:** the dystonic-foot paper was published online on 14 August 2017, after the July admission. It therefore cannot be treated as a paper Monaghan necessarily had in hand during the admission. Its legitimate use is narrower: it demonstrates that highly adjacent parkin/dystonia expertise was present in the Galway professional environment during the same period. It does not establish who discussed that case with whom or what Counihan personally reviewed in this patient\'s July presentation.
:::

External references: [Doherty et al., JAMA Neurology 2013 --- Parkin Disease: A Clinicopathologic Entity?](https://jamanetwork.com/journals/jamaneurology/fullarticle/1659731){rel="noopener" target="_blank"} · [Cullinane et al., Neurology 2017 --- A demure teenager and her dystonic foot](https://www.neurology.org/doi/10.1212/WNL.0000000000004231){rel="noopener" target="_blank"}.

::: independence-test
**The case may not show failure to recognise the disease territory. It may show failure to preserve the clinical object to which the available expertise needed to be applied.**
:::

The central conditional question is therefore whether the original feet--gait--tone--posture--pain relationship survived the Monday-to-Tuesday transformation with enough force to become the object of collective specialist reasoning. If it did, the evidential basis for the partition requires explanation. If it did not, the possible systems failure is upstream problem representation.
:::

::: {#section-independence .section .reader-section .kind-current section="Continuity is not convergence"}
::: section-kicker
Evidential independence · the closed-loop question
:::

## Continuity is not convergence {#continuity-not-convergence .source-h1 .anchored-heading}

The longitudinal record appears to contain very substantial continuity of neurological authorship and interpretation. That continuity is evidentially important: it can demonstrate what the treating specialist consistently thought, how carefully the model was documented, and how management evolved within it. It cannot, merely by accumulating, establish that the disputed originating partition was repeatedly and independently re-derived.

> **Documentary density is not evidential independence. Continuity of authorship is not independent confirmation.**

This matters because apparently different documentary sources may be downstream of the same originating representation. GP referrals, nursing communications, other service notes and even the patient\'s own later use of terms such as "neuropathy" may reproduce specialist vocabulary without independently establishing the proposition that vocabulary carries.

## Count independent evidential events, not mentions {#independence-counting-rule .anchored-heading}

The correct forensic question is not "How many documents mention neuropathy?" It is:

::: independence-test
**How many genuinely independent evidential events support the disputed classification, and precisely which proposition does each event establish?**
:::

Objective support for Parkinsonism, an external specialist opinion supporting PARK2, a new examination, a laboratory result, a fresh treatment-state observation, or a genuinely independent re-derivation of the feet--pain relationship are different evidential events. Repetition, copying, summarising or downstream adoption of an existing category is not the same thing.

::: inference-ladder
documentation existed*→*subject was mentioned*→*possibility was recognised*→*proposition was investigated*→*relationship was adjudicated*→*management was appropriate
:::

Those steps are not interchangeable. A later letter can truthfully show that something was discussed without proving that the underlying proposition was independently revalidated.

## The 2025 response: explanation versus independent revalidation {#independence-2025 .anchored-heading}

The difficult feature of the complaint stage is not that the originating clinician was forbidden from explaining his own care. His explanation is directly relevant to historical rationale. The evidential distinction is that **self-explanation and independent revalidation perform different functions**.

A response can show that treatment was cautious, second opinions were sought, genetics was discussed, dyskinesia risk was considered and medication was adjusted. All of that may be true while leaving the upstream question untouched: **was the original separation of painful feet, dystonic lower-limb phenomena and Parkinsonism adequately founded and later independently reconstructed?**

The parallel institutional question is therefore:

> **Did the hospital independently test the clinical model, or principally test the reasonableness of decisions made within that model?**

The latter can establish internal coherence and prospective defensibility. It does not automatically establish the validity of the originating representation.

## Self-sealing coherence --- structural hypothesis, not motive {#independence-self-sealing .anchored-heading}

A clinical model can become functionally self-sealing without anyone acting dishonestly. New observations may repeatedly be accommodated by auxiliary explanations---pain modulation, local OFF dystonia, structural lesions, pregabalin response, medication timing---without making the parent partition itself the proposition under test.

The strongest systems hypothesis is therefore not conspiracy or concealment. It is **recursive coherence**: the model generates categories; the categories organise care; care produces documentation; documentation demonstrates continuity; later observations are interpreted through the categories; and a complaint is then assessed against the accumulated record. If nobody deliberately interrupts that chain with a first-principles reconstruction, persistence can be mistaken for validation.

## Weight-bearing language {#independence-weight-bearing .anchored-heading}

**A statement\'s literal truth does not determine the inferential weight it can safely bear.** "Sinemet since 2021," "second opinion," "neuropathic pain," "idiopathic Parkinson\'s," "Lyrica removed the pain," and "as the letters show" may each be accurate in some limited sense while carrying broader implications that require separate proof.

> **Direction is not magnitude; magnitude is not mechanism; documentary continuity is not independent validation.**

## The decisive independence test {#independence-decisive-test .anchored-heading}

::: independence-test
**Did an appropriately qualified, genuinely independent reviewer ever return to the untreated/source material, temporarily suspend the inherited labels, reconstruct the feet--gait--tone--dystonia--pain syndrome from first principles, incorporate the procyclidine experiment, PARK2 evidence, later dyskinesia, OFF dystonia, toe curling and medication-state pain, and independently conclude that the original causal separation remained the best explanation?**
:::

If yes, the closed-loop hypothesis weakens substantially. If no such reconstruction can be identified, extraordinary longitudinal consistency should not automatically be treated as repeated independent validation. It may principally demonstrate continuity of one model.

::: callout
**Safeguarding remains separate.**\
Even if every diagnostic and therapeutic interpretation remained reasonable, the later record\'s severe language and contraction of life create an independent question about recognition and response to escalating human risk. That issue does not depend on proving the pain mechanism.
:::
:::

::: {#section-current-architecture .section .reader-section .kind-current .kind-reference section="Current case architecture"}
::: section-kicker
Full reference synthesis · preserved in full
:::

**Reference layer.** The integrated governing spine above controls where older formulations differ in emphasis. This section is preserved because its detailed reasoning, counter-case, falsifiers and expert questions remain useful; it should not be read as a separate competing theory.
:::

## 130. Integrated case architecture --- v0.17 {#130-tidied-case-architecture-v0-9 .source-h1 .anchored-heading}

## 130.1 What is now treated as established documentary fact {#130-1-what-is-now-treated-as-established-documentary-fact .anchored-heading}

1.  The GP\'s 2017 referral carried a broad deteriorating relational problem rather than a bare Parkinsonism query.
2.  The hospital\'s concise neurological task narrowed that object before detailed Neurology history-taking.
3.  Neurology later recovered many omitted facts, so this is not a simple information-loss case.
4.  The 2017 examination directly documented a striking pseudo-foot-drop gait somewhat suggestive of dystonic posture and explicitly "NOT FOOT DROP," alongside structural foot abnormalities.
5.  The specialist synthesis simultaneously created a tentative separate/peripheral-neuropathic branch for painful/burning/structural lower-limb material and marked it "Not clear."
6.  The Parkinsonian/genetic branch received much higher visible evidential resolution than the peripheral-neuropathic branch.
7.  "As per above" made the mixed information state operational without demonstrating that all relationships had been epistemically closed.
8.  Subsequent records show both documentary inheritance and genuine local reassessment.
9.  Primary care repeatedly preserved or regenerated cross-domain relationships while deferring specialist-level adjudication.
10. By March 2025 the complaint response reconstructed management history more completely than it reconstructed the provenance and revalidation history of the disputed categories.
11. The admission chronology places the untreated specialist assessment on Monday 3 July 2017 and the department\'s Tuesday Complex Case Meeting on 4 July, creating a narrow opening interval in which the governing representation may have consolidated.
12. Relevant PARK2/dystonia expertise was present in the professional network around the case; this strengthens the question of problem representation but does not prove that every relevant relationship was considered or that PRKN should have been diagnosed in July 2017.
13. Longitudinal documentary multiplicity must be distinguished from evidential independence: later repetition of inherited terminology does not by itself constitute fresh revalidation.
14. The 2025 complaint response is substantively informative about the originating clinician\'s rationale, but self-explanation is not equivalent to independent first-principles reconstruction of the framework under challenge.
15. The opening 2017 event is now best analysed as observable input → transformation → authority-bearing output → downstream generativity: the hospital had a rich relational input, produced a narrower portable task representation, and that authoritative representation then generated treatment, referrals, destinations and terminology that later records could inherit.
16. Because the representation itself generated downstream documentation, documentary multiplicity must be genealogically audited before it is treated as independent convergence.

## 130.2 Strongest documentary inference {#130-2-strongest-documentary-inference .anchored-heading}

> **A broad relational presentation appears to have been transformed very early into a narrower, authority-bearing specialist architecture. The Parkinsonian/PRKN problem then received high-resolution collective reasoning while the painful-foot relationship remained less fully adjudicated. Because that specialist output generated treatment, referrals, service destinations and later terminology, the model did not merely describe the pathway: it helped produce the documentary pathway that later appeared to corroborate it. Later boundary-crossing evidence was repeatedly assimilated locally, while the record more clearly demonstrates continuity and generativity of the originating model than genuinely independent reconstruction of the disputed partition.**

## 130.3 Strongest therapeutic expert question {#130-3-strongest-therapeutic-expert-question .anchored-heading}

> **Did the inherited separation of the painful-foot territory from the dystonic/Parkinsonian territory later constrain whether PRKN suspicion, anticholinergic response/withdrawal, levodopa sensitivity, dyskinesia, OFF dystonia, medication timing and pain were assembled and tested as a single dose-state lower-limb problem?**

This is an expert question, not a documentary conclusion.

## 130.4 Strongest information-quality question {#130-4-strongest-information-quality-question .anchored-heading}

> **Was the 2021 statement that DBS was "only ever for the tremor element" an adequately accurate explanation of the therapeutic scope of DBS in the context then documented?**

This is deliberately separated from any assertion that DBS should have been offered or performed.

## 130.5 Strongest governance question {#130-5-strongest-governance-question .anchored-heading}

> **When the upstream representation itself was challenged, did governance processes independently reconstruct that representation from source evidence, or principally assess whether the historical decisions were reasonable within the representation already embedded in the record?**

## 130.6 Strongest retrospective-defence question {#130-6-strongest-retrospective-defence-question .anchored-heading}

> **Does the March 2025 response establish independent revalidation of the disputed categories, or does it more strongly establish longstanding documentation and management within those categories?**

## 130.7 Claims retired or quarantined {#130-7-claims-retired-or-quarantined .anchored-heading}

The following should not appear as established propositions in future drafts:

-   "the feet were ignored";
-   "Neurology never reconsidered";
-   "the pain was dystonia";
-   "the neuropathy diagnosis was fabricated";
-   "PRKN should obviously have been diagnosed in 2017";
-   "levodopa response proves neurological causation";
-   "there was a therapeutic-window failure";
-   "DBS should have been performed";
-   "the hospital recognised its error and protected itself";
-   "the record was constructed to conceal a mistake."

Permitted substitutes are respectively:

-   relationships were unevenly resolved;
-   local reassessment occurred without clearly identified global reconstruction;
-   dystonia/pain relationship requires expert adjudication;
-   neuropathic classification provenance and revalidation require reconstruction;
-   PRKN was a specific contemporaneous unresolved hypothesis;
-   treatment response generated a testable hypothesis but was not diagnostic proof;
-   the record documents a balancing problem and raises a therapeutic-window question;
-   the DBS explanation raises an information-quality question;
-   protective consolidation is a source-dependent hypothesis only;
-   concealment/falsification are unsupported absent direct evidence.

## 130.8 The current apex statement {#130-8-the-current-apex-statement .anchored-heading}

> **The strongest current case is that an integrated medication-naïve lower-limb and motor presentation entered hospital with sufficient relational detail to require preservation or explicit adjudication of the feet--gait--tone relationship; that the hospital then produced a narrower, authority-bearing specialist representation before the relationship among painful feet, dystonic-looking posture, structural loading and Parkinsonism had been fully adjudicated; that this representation was generative, producing treatment strategy, service destinations and terminology later inherited across the record; that relevant expertise was available but may have been applied to the narrowed clinical object; that later treatment-state and PRKN-related evidence repeatedly crossed the resulting boundary without a clearly identifiable independent reconstruction of the parent model; and that by 2025 documentary continuity could function as apparent validation even though authority and continuity do not equal independent evidential convergence.**
:::

::: {#section-current-object .section .reader-section .kind-current .kind-reference section="Current strongest case object"}
::: section-kicker
Full reference synthesis · preserved in full
:::
