::: archive-note
**Deep evidence layer.** The sections above state the current integrated case. What follows preserves the detailed reasoning that built and stress-tested it, including contrary evidence and falsifiers. Repetition below is retained where it records how a proposition was tested, not because each repetition is a new evidential event.
:::

## 1. Evidential discipline {#1-evidential-discipline .anchored-heading}

Every proposition in this file should be tagged mentally as one of four things.

**Record fact** means the source directly records an observation, statement, prescription, referral, test, treatment response, or result. A record fact proves that the statement or event appears in the record. It does not automatically prove the clinical truth of every proposition embedded in the statement.

**Documentary or linguistic inference** concerns how information behaves in the record: what is foregrounded, compressed, inherited, repeated, rephrased, hardened, backgrounded, locally updated, or globally reconstructed. It is not a medical-standard conclusion.

**Clinical expert question** includes what examination was reasonably required, how particular signs should have been interpreted, whether a differential was adequate, whether genetic testing should have occurred, and when later evidence ought to have triggered broader reassessment.

**Legal conclusion** includes negligence, breach, causation, procedural impropriety, dishonesty, concealment, bad faith, or competence. None should be asserted from documentary architecture alone.

The case should remain disciplined in this hierarchy throughout.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 2. Hindsight control {#2-hindsight-control .anchored-heading}

The February 2026 PRKN result is highly important, but it must be used only after the historical record has been analysed on its own terms.

The later result may establish that pathogenic Parkin variants were eventually found and that the receiving neurologist attributed Parkinsonism and dystonia to compound-heterozygous Parkin mutations. It also shows that the eventual molecular answer concerned substantially the same PRKN/PARK2 question already raised in 2017 and still suspected in 2018.

It does **not** by itself establish what clinicians should have known in 2017, that every foot symptom was dystonia, that structural foot disease was irrelevant, that peripheral neuropathy was impossible, or that any earlier decision was negligent.

The correct use of 2026 is:

> **endpoint to an already-existing historical question**

not:

> **retrospective proof of what every earlier symptom meant.**

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 3. Treatment-response control {#3-treatment-response-control .anchored-heading}

Treatment response must never be treated as diagnostic proof. The record itself contains a useful same-author control in the 2019 shoulder episode: the treating neurologist expressly explains that gabapentin response does not identify cause because multiple competing processes could improve, and similarly that steroid response would not necessarily provide a definable answer.

That same evidential discipline must be applied to procyclidine, pregabalin/Lyrica, Stalevo, Sinemet, and Sinemet CR. Each response may be clinically important. None is, by itself, a mechanism detector.

This becomes especially important later when pain improves with both pregabalin and dopaminergic medication. The record may therefore contain meaningful evidence about modifiability without automatically resolving etiology.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 4. The entry object: an integrated trajectory of a constellation {#4-the-entry-object-an-integrated-trajectory-of-a-constellation .anchored-heading}

The GP referral at `CLIN-000851–000884` is best characterised as an **integrated trajectory of a constellation**, with trajectory predominating.

The sequence is temporally organised. The GP first notes an unusual "festinant and stiff gait" while the patient is attending with bilateral foot problems, including corns/callouses and a global description that the feet are "not in a good state." The referral then moves into an explicitly changing state: "over recent weeks" it had become "increasingly evident" that there was difficulty initiating movement, marked stiffness and increased tone in upper and lower limbs, difficulty relaxing, and toe clawing in bed.

The narrative then proceeds to worsening dexterity and function. Work that had been manageable becomes increasingly difficult; "of late" dexterity is worse; simple repetitive tasks are affected. The GP then synthesises the trajectory as increased tone with abnormal gait "now affecting" employment and daily activities, under the open question "?parkinsonian or other underlying diagnosis," and concludes that the patient "certainly seems to be deteriorating," with urgency because persistence threatens employment.

The referral does not prove that all manifestations share one cause. It does show that, at referral, these features are still travelling together as the clinical object requiring explanation.

**Working formulation:**

> **The GP referred not merely a symptom constellation, but a worsening relationship among feet, gait, toe posture, tone, movement initiation, dexterity and function under an open neurological question.**

Neurology later reconstructs substantial chronology independently. The defensible proposition is therefore not that trajectory disappeared, but that the **shape of what was understood to be evolving together changed**.

\[PLACEHOLDER: insert exact GP referral transcription and source provenance.\]

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 5. Hospital PRECIS: relational compression rather than permanent fact loss {#5-hospital-precis-relational-compression-rather-than-permanent-fact-loss .anchored-heading}

The hospital PRECIS or immediate task representation foregrounded a narrower motor-neurological subset: difficulty initiating movement, increased tone/stiffness in all four limbs, cogwheeling, and a request for neurological assessment.

That compression is not inherently improper. Referral and admission fields routinely simplify complex histories. The relevant question is what kind of information lost salience.

The material most clearly backgrounded included the painful/problematic feet, toe-clawing/postural phenomena, the feet--gait relationship, functional deterioration and employment impact, and the integrated question of how feet, gait, tone, movement and decline related.

Yet many omitted facts later returned. Neurology recovered foot history, structural abnormalities, abnormal gait, rigidity, bradykinesia, functional impairment and deterioration. The simple model "PRECIS omitted the feet, therefore Neurology never saw them" is not supported.

The stronger concept is **relational compression**:

> **The facts came back more successfully than the original relationships among them.**

The motor signs retained in the PRECIS subsequently received deep neurological development. The backgrounded material often returned as history, structural findings, podiatric material, or possible peripheral phenomena. The question is whether the original relationships regained equivalent organising prominence.

\[PLACEHOLDER: construct exact GP-vs-PRECIS comparison table: retained / omitted / transformed / relationally disconnected.\]

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 5A. Counterfactual question architecture: different investigative object, not necessarily different diagnosis {#5a-counterfactual-question-architecture-different-investigative-object-not-necessarily-different-diagnosis .anchored-heading}

The full GP referral and the hospital PRECIS should be treated as two materially different **problem representations**.

The GP referral did not merely contain more facts. It organised those facts as a deteriorating relationship under an open question. The feet, abnormal gait, increasingly evident movement-initiation difficulty, tone/stiffness, inability to relax, toe clawing, declining dexterity, loss of function and threatened employment were presented together and through time. The GP\'s formulation remained open: `?parkinsonian or other underlying diagnosis`.

The PRECIS, by contrast, presented a narrower but entirely legitimate neurological task: difficulty initiating movement, four-limb tone/stiffness, cogwheeling and a request for neurological opinion. That representation was already strongly shaped around a central motor syndrome.

The documentary counterfactual should therefore **not** be framed as:

> full GP referral → different diagnosis.

The safer formulation is:

> **full GP referral → different investigative object.**

Or, more precisely:

> **Had the integrated feet--gait--tone--toe--deterioration trajectory remained the neurologist\'s primary organising problem, the relationships within that phenotype would have remained more explicitly decision-live at the outset.**

This is still a counterfactual. The record cannot establish what the clinician definitely would have done. Its evidential basis comes from two internal controls.

### 5A.1 Same-author control: the 2019 shoulder problem {#5a-1-same-author-control-the-2019-shoulder-problem .anchored-heading}

When the same neurologist later encountered a comparatively fresh shoulder/arm problem, his documented reasoning was explicitly generative. He asked whether the problem was in the shoulder or radicular; used focal weakness and other examination findings to localise possibilities; generated radial-nerve, posterior-cord/brachial-plexus, root, structural, inflammatory and malignant alternatives; tied investigations to those propositions; stated limitations of negative tests; and explicitly refused to treat gabapentin or steroid response as diagnostic proof.

His language included the proposition that the actual cause was important and that he genuinely did not know the cause. Uncertainty therefore functioned as an engine of investigation:

> **problem → competing explanations → focused examination → discriminating observations → targeted investigation → limits of evidence → preserved uncertainty.**

This does not prove that a chronic mixed lower-limb problem should have been investigated identically. The shoulder was more acute, focal and anatomically localisable. Its value is narrower: it shows that explicit proposition-by-proposition causal discrimination was available within this clinician\'s own documented reasoning style.

### 5A.2 Same-anatomy control: later foot-throw discrimination {#5a-2-same-anatomy-control-later-foot-throw-discrimination .anchored-heading}

The later lower-limb record provides a second internal comparator. At `CLIN-002286`, an apparent footdrop-like foot throw was not merely labelled Parkinsonian. The neurologist recorded the discriminator: dorsiflexion, plantarflexion, inversion and eversion power were normal, and on that basis described the phenomenon as actually Parkinsonian.

That is important because it demonstrates that the clinician could apply:

> **question → specific distal examination → discriminating finding → classification**

within the same anatomical territory.

### 5A.3 What a fuller initial problem representation might have kept live {#5a-3-what-a-fuller-initial-problem-representation-might-have-kept-live .anchored-heading}

The documentary record therefore supports a limited inference that preserving the GP\'s full relational framing might have made questions such as the following more explicit at the outset:

-   Is the apparent footdrop true weakness, dystonic posture, painful adaptation, structural gait change, or a mixture?
-   Is toe clawing fixed deformity, intermittent/dynamic posture, pain behaviour, or something else?
-   Are distal power and sensation normal or abnormal?
-   Does foot pain track loading, gait, tone or movement state?
-   Does abnormal posture plausibly alter loading through structurally abnormal feet?
-   Which observations favour peripheral neuropathy, and which observations weaken it?
-   Are the structural and neurological findings independent, causally related, or mixed?

These are **illustrative discriminators**, not a claim that each test was clinically mandatory in 2017. Whether a particular examination or investigation was required is an expert question.

### 5A.4 Why this matters to the genetic construction {#5a-4-why-this-matters-to-the-genetic-construction .anchored-heading}

The eventual 2017 genetic reasoning was highly sophisticated. It selected very young onset, akinetic rigidity, marked symmetry, lack of dominant family history and preserved cognition as explicit PRKN/PARK2 indicators. The dystonic-looking lower limb remained visible in the broader atypical/autosomal-recessive picture but was not one of the enumerated PRKN indicators.

The counterfactual should again be modest. It is not:

> full GP referral → dystonia incorporated into PRKN → earlier molecular diagnosis.

It is:

> **if feet, toe posture and gait had remained part of the originating problem representation, the lower-limb phenomenology might have retained greater salience as the phenotype was partitioned and the genetic model was constructed.**

Whether that would have changed the differential, testing or treatment cannot be determined from the record alone.

### 5A.5 Most likely difference: partition quality rather than service destination {#5a-5-most-likely-difference-partition-quality-rather-than-service-destination .anchored-heading}

The likely management pathway might still have been very similar. Severe Parkinsonism would still have justified neurological investigation and treatment. Genuine structural foot disease would still have justified Podiatry. The counterfactual is principally about the **bridge between those destinations**.

Instead of the relationship becoming implicit within:

> Parkinsonism → Neurology\
> feet → Podiatry\
> burning/pain → possible peripheral process

a fuller preserved question architecture might have yielded an explicit statement closer to:

> Parkinsonism is strongly supported; structural foot pathology is genuine; however abnormal gait, toe posture, loading and pain may contain both neurological and structural contributions, and that relationship remains an active proposition requiring defined follow-up or discriminating evidence.

Again, this is not offered as text the clinician was required to write. It illustrates the kind of **relational uncertainty** that the GP referral made visible.

### 5A.6 Strongest counterfactual formulation {#5a-6-strongest-counterfactual-formulation .anchored-heading}

The formulation to carry forward is:

> **The evidence does not justify saying that the full GP referral would have produced a different diagnosis. It does support a documentary counterfactual that preserving the integrated feet--gait--tone--toe--deterioration trajectory as the neurologist\'s initial problem representation would have made the relationships within that phenotype more explicitly decision-live. The same clinician\'s later record demonstrates that, when relationships were presented as unresolved propositions, he was capable of highly granular discrimination among them.**

The cleanest shorthand is:

> **The counterfactual is not different diagnosis. It is different investigative object.**

### 5A.7 Counterfactual limits and falsifiers {#5a-7-counterfactual-limits-and-falsifiers .anchored-heading}

This inference weakens materially if the canonical record shows that, before or during the initial investigative design, the neurologist actually had the full GP referral in front of him, expressly reconstructed its integrated feet--gait--tone--toe trajectory, and used that relationship as the organising problem despite the PRECIS.

It would also weaken if a contemporaneous note shows the lower-limb relationships being examined proposition-by-proposition before the final partition, even if the later discharge summary compressed that work.

The shoulder comparator cannot establish causation by the PRECIS. The difference may also reflect problem structure: an acute focal shoulder syndrome has clearer anatomical discriminators than a longstanding heterogeneous lower-limb syndrome. The correct conclusion is therefore one of **plausible problem-representation effect**, not deterministic causation.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 6. Admission examination: what was actually observed {#6-admission-examination-what-was-actually-observed .anchored-heading}

The 2017 admission examination is clinically substantial for the Parkinsonian syndrome. It documents markedly increased and fairly symmetrical tone, brisk reflexes, lower-limb tremor, lead-pipe rigidity, axial rigidity, gross bradykinesia, absent or reduced arm swing, a strikingly abnormal gait, an apparent footdrop-like phenomenon described as "somewhat suggestive of a dystonic posture --- NOT FOOT DROP," a left-lower-limb kick/throw, pes planus, hammertoes, and mild oedema.

Those are important findings. They should not be minimised.

The analytical issue is that the surviving record is much richer for the general Parkinsonian motor syndrome than for the **distal relational map** among dynamic posture, toe configuration, fixed deformity, loading, pressure areas, pain distribution, pain provocation, sensation, distal power and gait mechanics.

The 2017 examination block does not visibly contain, at comparable granularity, directional ankle power, detailed distal sensory mapping, dynamic toe posture during gait/loading, pressure-site mapping, pain reproduction under load, fixed-versus-dynamic deformity analysis, or an explicit bridge linking the dystonic-looking gait to painful structural foot findings.

This must be stated narrowly:

> **not documented at comparable resolution**

rather than:

> **never examined.**

\[PLACEHOLDER: verify all examination CLIN ranges and identify any overlooked distal examination details.\]

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 7. The synthesis--examination differential {#7-the-synthesisexamination-differential .anchored-heading}

The later 2017 synthesis becomes far more analytically elaborate than the distal bedside map. The record moves from observed signs into young-onset Parkinsonism, possible young-onset Parkinson's disease, atypical/synucleinopathy possibilities, autosomal-recessive disease, PRKN/PARK2, PARK6, PARK7, PARK9, inheritance reasoning, genetic counselling, prognosis and treatment strategy.

For PRKN/PARK2, the record explicitly selects phenotype features such as very young onset, akinetic rigidity, lack of dominant family history, marked symmetry and excellent cognition. This is high-resolution phenotype-to-etiology reasoning.

The comparison is therefore not "many words versus few words." It is analytical:

> **high-resolution central/genetic phenotype construction versus lower-resolution documented reconstruction of the disputed distal structural--dynamic--pain relationship.**

The 2017 record is genuinely sophisticated. The strongest wording is therefore:

> **The problem is not lack of sophistication. It is where the sophistication was applied.**

This should remain a central formulation because it avoids the inaccurate suggestion that the final note was naive or unsophisticated.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 8. The dystonic-looking lower limb: partial incorporation, not disappearance {#8-the-dystonic-looking-lower-limb-partial-incorporation-not-disappearance .anchored-heading}

The dystonic-looking lower-limb observation was not simply forgotten. It appears in the examination and also in broader atypical/autosomal-recessive reasoning. A contemporaneous request describes the walk as "almost dystonically unusual" beside suspicion of an autosomal-recessive Parkinsonian condition.

However, when the record later gives an explicit PRKN/PARK2 indicator list, the stated features are age, akinetic rigidity, family pattern, symmetry and cognition. The dystonic-looking lower limb is not one of the enumerated PRKN indicators.

The safest formulation is:

> **The dystonic-looking gait remained visible and contributed to the broad atypical/recessive impression, but it was not visibly integrated into the explicit high-resolution PRKN rationale or into an equally high-resolution feet--pain phenotype.**

This is a documentary distinction. Whether the sign was too nonspecific to deserve greater etiological weight is a clinical expert question.

\[PLACEHOLDER: verify exact wording and original CLIN source of the "almost dystonically unusual" request.\]

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 9. The lower-limb branch before operational closure {#9-the-lower-limb-branch-before-operational-closure .anchored-heading}

The final 2017 reasoning does not make a confident disease-level peripheral neuropathy diagnosis. The language remains conditional: the leg/foot material "may have been completely separate," "perhaps a secondary peripheral neuropathy," possibly related to prior alcohol exposure, ending with "Not clear."

The patient's own distinction between leg tension and corns/callouses/foot pain is also documented. That is important counterevidence against a claim that separation was invented without basis. The patient experienced the phenomena as distinct.

But phenomenological distinction and biological independence are not identical. The record therefore contains, at once:

-   genuine structural pathology;
-   genuine patient-perceived difference;
-   a possible peripheral explanation;
-   a neurological gait/posture abnormality;
-   uncertainty about their relationship.

The core question is not whether uncertainty was legitimate. It is what happened to that unresolved relationship once workable management destinations became available.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 10. Podiatry: destination without necessarily resolving the relationship {#10-podiatry-destination-without-necessarily-resolving-the-relationship .anchored-heading}

Podiatry was a legitimate destination for structural disease. The record contains pes planus, hammertoes, corns/callouses, pressure lesions, recurrent focal pathology, debridement and offloading. This case should never be formulated as "all foot disease was neurological."

The narrower question is whether Podiatry answered **where** the structural component should be managed while leaving unresolved **how** structural pathology interacted with dynamic neurological posture, gait and pain.

This is the destination-versus-explanation distinction.

> **A destination can produce practical completion without explanatory completion.**

The related ownership question is:

> Who owned the relationship between services once the patient had a structural foot pathway and a neurological movement pathway?

\[PLACEHOLDER: map all Podiatry referrals, feedback loops, and whether any neurological re-integration trigger was specified.\]

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 11. 'As per above' as operational hinge {#11-as-per-above-as-operational-hinge .anchored-heading}

At `CLIN-001613–001614` the record states:

> **Plan/Recommendations: As per above.**

The material "above" included severe Parkinsonism, dystonic-looking gait, structural feet, possible separate/peripheral explanation, "Not clear," genetic uncertainty, specific PRKN reasoning, Podiatry, medication strategy, rehabilitation and follow-up.

Therefore "As per above" should not be read as proof that all those questions were solved.

Its more plausible immediate function is:

> **the mixed information state above is sufficient to operate as the plan.**

This is the distinction between **epistemic closure** and **operational closure**.

The key working phrase is:

> **unresolved synthesis → actionable management state**

The phrase did not erase uncertainty; it made an arrangement of uncertainty operationally durable.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 12. What travelled best, and what travelled least well {#12-what-travelled-best-and-what-travelled-least-well .anchored-heading}

Once the synthesis operated as the plan, several elements became highly portable:

-   Parkinsonism/Parkinson's/iPD as the main neurological frame;
-   medication strategy and later levodopa/dyskinesia logic;
-   Podiatry as structural-foot destination;
-   Parkinson nurse/Neurology follow-up;
-   later neuropathic pain terminology;
-   the previous letters and summaries themselves.

What appears less portable as an active question includes:

-   the full force of "perhaps" and "Not clear";
-   the original feet--gait--tone relationship;
-   the specific PRKN/PARK2 question after deferral;
-   the significance of the dystonic-looking lower limb within the whole lower-limb phenotype;
-   the question of who owned the cross-service relationship.

The uncertainty may remain findable in the archive. The issue is whether it remains operationally active.

> **Decisions, destinations and usable working categories travelled more efficiently than the relationships that originally qualified them.**

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 13. PRKN as the clearest internal example of management without resolution {#13-prkn-as-the-clearest-internal-example-of-management-without-resolution .anchored-heading}

Prof Daniel Healy's 2017 specialist correspondence independently strengthens the PRKN/PARK2 hypothesis and recommends testing it first, with a panel if negative. This is important because the later molecular diagnosis was not an unforeseen concept retrospectively imposed on the case.

In 2018 the treating neurologist still records PRKN/PARK2 as what he and Prof Healy suspect is the cause, but testing is deliberately deferred because it does not particularly change current management and there is "no great loss in 'not knowing' currently right now."

This is one of the clearest conceptual sources in the whole record because it explicitly separates:

> **epistemic resolution**

from

> **management sufficiency.**

The clinical reasonableness of that deferral is an expert question. The documentary significance is that unresolved causation could be consciously tolerated when treatment did not depend on resolving it.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 14. The reopening problem {#14-the-reopening-problem .anchored-heading}

Deferral is not, by itself, the objection. The longitudinal question is:

> **How does "we do not need to know right now" later become "now we should reopen the question"?**

A robust architecture for managed uncertainty might contain a named owner, review date, trigger, test threshold, change-in-phenotype trigger, treatment-state signal, or specialist referral criterion.

For PRKN, the question remained conceptually preserved, but the route back to testing appears only weakly scheduled in the surviving record. For the feet--gait--tone relationship, the record is even less clear about a named reopening mechanism.

This is the difference between uncertainty that is merely tolerated and uncertainty that is actively managed over time.

\[PLACEHOLDER: audit all explicit review triggers in 2017--2019, including DAT, pending investigations, genetics, Podiatry, and lower-limb reassessment.\]

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 15. Procyclidine withdrawal as boundary-crossing evidence {#15-procyclidine-withdrawal-as-boundary-crossing-evidence .anchored-heading}

Later treatment-state changes generate empirical information capable of testing the original partition. During procyclidine withdrawal, the patient reports tension building, feet becoming restless and gait falling apart. A later clinician-authored account records rapid return of the outside-left-foot phenomenon as one of the first changes after withdrawal.

This does not prove that dystonia caused the structural lesion or that all foot pain was neurological. It does show that multiple features across the documentary boundary co-varied with neurological treatment state.

> **Treatment perturbation generated information capable of testing the partition.**

The important question is what level of the model that information was allowed to revise.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 16. Structural truth and neurological truth can coexist {#16-structural-truth-and-neurological-truth-can-coexist .anchored-heading}

Podiatry generated genuine local mechanical information: recurrent pressure lesions, focal pathology, debridement, offloading, structural foot type and toe deformity. Neurology generated genuine dynamic information: movement state, treatment state, dystonic-looking gait, later Parkinsonian foot throw, OFF phenomena and dopaminergic effects.

Neither stream invalidates the other.

The possible missing operation is the **join**:

> focal mechanical pathology + dynamic neurological state → what is the relationship?

The case should therefore protect the formulation:

> **Structural truth and neurological contribution can both be genuine. The unresolved question is who owned the arrow between them.**

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 17. Documentary inheritance becomes explicit {#17-documentary-inheritance-becomes-explicit .anchored-heading}

By 2019 the treating neurologist expressly relies on prior documentation: "all previous details are as per older letters as previously supplied," with related statements that everything is as detailed in earlier summaries and will not be reiterated for brevity. The 2017 synthesis is itself reproduced in later longitudinal material.

This establishes documentary inheritance as a record fact.

It does not prove uncritical copying. It does show that a mature case representation existed and later encounters could begin from it rather than rebuilding the entire case.

> **The chronic case acquired an information state that later encounters could inherit.**

Repeated same-author terminology therefore strongly proves persistence. It is weaker evidence of independent revalidation unless the underlying proposition is actually retested.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 18. The 2019 shoulder episode as same-author internal control {#18-the-2019-shoulder-episode-as-same-author-internal-control .anchored-heading}

The 2019 shoulder episode is methodologically valuable because it offers a same-author control. In the same general documentary environment, a comparatively fresh focal problem is reasoned through in a very different form.

The shoulder sequence is highly explicit:

1.  a new worsening symptom is described;
2.  the clinician asks whether the source is shoulder or radicular/disc-related;
3.  examination findings are used to localise the problem;
4.  multiple anatomical alternatives are generated;
5.  observations are used to favour or weaken particular hypotheses;
6.  MRI and other tests are linked to specific propositions;
7.  limitations of negative tests are stated;
8.  gabapentin response is expressly declared non-diagnostic;
9.  possible steroid response is also treated as non-specific;
10. uncertainty is preserved in explicit language such as "I genuinely do not know what the cause is here."

This weakens explanations based simply on inability to reason analytically, inability to write detailed differentials, or habitual brevity.

The shoulder problem was different---acute, focal, anatomically localisable and amenable to structural tests---so the comparison is not perfect. But it is a powerful control for documentary capacity.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 19. Generative uncertainty versus contained uncertainty {#19-generative-uncertainty-versus-contained-uncertainty .anchored-heading}

The shoulder comparator reveals two different uses of uncertainty.

**Fresh shoulder:**

> uncertainty → question generation → discrimination → test → stated limitations → continued uncertainty if evidence remains non-discriminating

**Inherited PRKN/feet pathway:**

> uncertainty → workable management → local updates → uncertainty may remain technically open while categories remain operational

This should not be simplified to "shoulder = thinking, feet = no thinking." The chronic pathway contains substantial fresh reasoning.

The stronger distinction is:

> **fresh-model construction**

versus

> **inherited-model updating.**

The core formulation is:

> **When the model was fresh, uncertainty generated questions. Once the model was inherited, uncertainty was more often managed within it.**

This is a documentary description, not a claim about psychology or motive.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 20. 2020 foot throw: local discrimination inside the inherited pathway {#20-2020-foot-throw-local-discrimination-inside-the-inherited-pathway .anchored-heading}

The later foot-throw episode is important contrary evidence to any rigid-inheritance thesis. The clinician tests dorsiflexion, plantarflexion, inversion and eversion power and concludes that the apparent footdrop-like phenomenon is "actually parkinsonian" because power is normal.

This is a clean:

> **observation → discriminator → classification**

sequence.

It proves that the inherited lower-limb model was permeable and that targeted distal discrimination did occur later.

It also supplies an internal comparator for the 2017 record: this is what a more explicitly discriminating distal examination can look like in the same clinician's documentation.

The remaining question is whether this local reclassification ever propagated backwards into a reconstruction of the original feet--gait--pain relationship.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 21. 2023: the strongest counterexample to a static-framework theory {#21-2023-the-strongest-counterexample-to-a-static-framework-theory .anchored-heading}

The 2023 review contains substantial fresh reasoning and must be treated fairly. The neurologist considers ON versus OFF dystonia, uses medication timing and evening behaviour to reason toward OFF-state impairment, states that literal overnight onset is not typical of peripheral neuropathy, revisits the historical neuropathy question, considers Fabry disease as an overlap possibility, seeks further testing, and incorporates the patient's own belief that some of the foot problem may be separate.

This means the defensible contrast is not:

> shoulder = analytical reasoning / feet = no analytical reasoning.

It is:

> **shoulder = fresh causal model-building from the ground up**

versus

> **feet/Parkinsonism = substantial local re-analysis within a pre-existing longitudinal model.**

The inherited architecture was therefore **permeable, not frozen**.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 22. Local updating versus global reconstruction {#22-local-updating-versus-global-reconstruction .anchored-heading}

The record contains local answers to multiple questions:

-   apparent footdrop can be Parkinsonian where directional power is normal;
-   cramping may be OFF dystonia;
-   abrupt overnight onset argues against ordinary peripheral neuropathy;
-   evening difficulty may be OFF-related;
-   Fabry can be tested as an overlap possibility;
-   dopaminergic treatment can alter pain.

What is harder to identify is a later source saying, in substance:

> **Given these accumulated revisions, let us reconstruct from first principles the original relationship among structural feet, dystonic posture, pain, loading, gait, OFF state, neuropathic possibilities and the original presentation.**

That is the distinction between **local updating** and **global reconstruction**.

\[PLACEHOLDER: conduct dedicated canonical search for any post-2020 source performing such a global reconstruction.\]

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 23. Semantic migration in the neuropathic branch {#23-semantic-migration-in-the-neuropathic-branch .anchored-heading}

The 2017 peripheral/neuropathy wording is expressly tentative. Over time the record uses formulations such as "peripheral neuropathy question," possible peripheral neuropathic disorder, "? neuropathic pain," neuropathic pain, and eventually "peripheral neuropathic pain symptomatologies."

At the same time, disease-level neuropathy can remain conditional: "If there is a degree of peripheral neuropathy here\..."

These propositions must be separated:

1.  peripheral neuropathy as disease;
2.  neuropathic pain as symptom phenotype;
3.  cause of that pain;
4.  response to medication commonly used for neuropathic pain.

The 2025 phrase "Whatever the underlying cause for the peripheral neuropathic pain symptomatologies\..." places uncertainty grammatically on the **cause**, while the symptom category itself is expressed comparatively affirmatively.

The strongest formulation is:

> **classification hardening without equivalent disease-level diagnostic hardening**

or:

> **uncertainty migrated from classification toward etiology.**

\[PLACEHOLDER: build complete dated corpus of every "neuropathy/neuropathic/neuralgic/burning/sensitivity" use by source and speaker.\]

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 24. 2023 onward: uncertainty becomes operationally expensive {#24-2023-onward-uncertainty-becomes-operationally-expensive .anchored-heading}

By March 2023 severe bilateral burning foot/lower-leg pain is described with language including "death wish," GP concern, a request for review before "we get into trouble," debilitating pain and no quality of life despite high-dose pregabalin.

This should not be overread as proving suicidal intent or plan. Its documentary importance is that the unresolved pain problem becomes contemporaneously safety- and endurance-relevant.

The later record contains genuine response and reconsideration. The appropriate sequence is:

> **severe symptom failure → pressure for reconsideration → genuine local neurological/pain reasoning → local rescue**

not:

> **crisis → no thought → same plan.**

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 25. Recursive manageability {#25-recursive-manageability .anchored-heading}

A possible longitudinal mechanism is:

> unresolved mechanism → severe symptom failure → pressure for reconsideration → local intervention → manageability restored → pressure for global explanation falls → unresolved parent relationship survives → later recurrence

This is a documentary model, not established causation.

Its value is that it explains how active care and unresolved explanation can coexist for years.

> **Successful local rescue may restore operational sufficiency without producing explanatory closure.**

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 26. Pregabalin: treatment benefit versus mechanistic validation {#26-pregabalin-treatment-benefit-versus-mechanistic-validation .anchored-heading}

The record supports genuine pregabalin benefit at points. It also supports severe pain despite high-dose pregabalin, later recurrence, residual pain, persistent toe curling and substantial activity restriction.

Therefore "Lyrica removed the foot pain" is best read as a later favourable snapshot or compressed summary, not as proof of durable bilateral eradication or proof of neuropathic mechanism.

The appropriate distinction is between:

-   absolute symptom level;
-   counterfactual medication effect;
-   functional restoration;
-   adaptation/activity restriction.

A patient can be meaningfully better on a drug while still having major residual pain and a smaller life.

> **A favourable treatment snapshot can be accurate while still being an incomplete summary of longitudinal efficacy.**

\[PLACEHOLDER: build dated pain / dose / function / activity / dopaminergic-state table.\]

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 27. 2025 neurological reopening and Sinemet CR {#27-2025-neurological-reopening-and-sinemet-cr .anchored-heading}

By March 2025 the foot pain is again dominant, with severe morning pain, first steps described as torture, "I won't live with it," and relief after Parkinson medication begins working. Peripheral neuropathy remains conditional, painful dystonia is acknowledged, and controlled-release Sinemet is introduced.

This is meaningful neurological reopening. It is not merely passive symptom treatment.

At the same time, the note does not visibly reconstruct the entire historical genealogy of the feet classification.

The strongest phrase is:

> **therapeutic reopening without full classificatory reconstruction.**

The later report of "profound relief currently" after Sinemet CR is important treatment-state evidence but still not a mechanism detector. It raises the expert question: what should this observation do to the inherited differential?

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 28. The complaint changes the object of review {#28-the-complaint-changes-the-object-of-review .anchored-heading}

The March 2025 complaint is significant because it challenges the longitudinal reasoning itself, including classification persistence and adequacy of reassessment, and seeks independent review of the reasoning from 2017 to 2025.

The complaint is not proof that its allegations are correct.

Its documentary significance is that the **genealogy of the reasoning** becomes the object requiring explanation.

A routine clinic note may reasonably focus on current management. A complaint response to a historical reasoning challenge is being asked a different question: how did the framework arise, and how was it tested over time?

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 29. 'As the letters show': retrospective authority {#29-as-the-letters-show-retrospective-authority .anchored-heading}

The 2025 response explicitly relies on the accumulated letters. This is legitimate use of contemporaneous records. The letters are strong evidence that decisions occurred, treatment strategies existed, and certain understandings persisted.

The source-audit distinction is:

> **Does documentary continuity prove persistence of a reasoning pathway, or independent revalidation of the propositions that originally created it?**

A response can accurately show that levodopa was delayed for stated reasons, Lyrica was used, Sinemet later helped pain, and dyskinesia risks were considered. Those facts do not automatically establish that the original feet/pain classification was independently re-derived or that PRKN had been closed.

The strongest formulation is:

> **A long coherent pathway can retrospectively resemble validation of the pathway.**

That is a normal documentary phenomenon and should not be framed as dishonesty or retrospective falsification.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 30. Parkinson's diagnosis versus Parkinson's etiology {#30-parkinsons-diagnosis-versus-parkinsons-etiology .anchored-heading}

The record demonstrates continuity of the Parkinsonian diagnosis very strongly. It less clearly demonstrates resolution of Parkinson's etiology before 2026.

Early use of "idiopathic" appears elastic enough to coexist with autosomal-recessive suspicion, so the word alone cannot safely be treated as proof that monogenic disease was excluded.

By March 2025, however, the complaint response states the service's "belief and understanding" that the patient has idiopathic Parkinson's disease. The documentary question is therefore:

> **What happened to the previously specific PRKN/PARK2 hypothesis?**

The question is not: why did clinicians fail to know a 2026 result in 2017?

The strongest formulation is:

> **The record demonstrates continuity of the Parkinson's diagnosis far more clearly than it demonstrates resolution of Parkinson's etiology.**

\[PLACEHOLDER: complete idiopathic/iPD/PRKN/recessive terminology audit.\]

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 31. 2026 endpoint {#31-2026-endpoint .anchored-heading}

The 2026 result resolves the historical PRKN branch. It establishes a molecular endpoint and provides later specialist attribution of Parkinsonism and dystonia to Parkin mutations.

Its documentary significance is that the historical PRKN question was not empty speculation; it was specific, prospective, independently reinforced, deliberately left unresolved, and eventually answered.

The correct line is:

> **The endpoint tells us what eventually happened to a question the record itself had already asked.**

It must not be used to prove every historical foot symptom was dystonic or to determine historical standard of care without expert evidence.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 32. Full information-pathway mechanism {#32-full-information-pathway-mechanism .anchored-heading}

The present working mechanism can be staged as follows.

**Stage 1 --- Integrated referral:** feet, gait, tone, toe phenomena, movement difficulty, function and deterioration travel together under an open neurological question.

**Stage 2 --- Relational compression:** the hospital task representation foregrounds cardinal motor-neurological signs and reduces the prominence of the integrated lower-limb relationship.

**Stage 3 --- Selective high-resolution construction:** Parkinsonism and genetic etiology receive extensive examination and synthesis.

**Stage 4 --- Factual recovery without equivalent relational recovery:** omitted facts return, but their relationships remain disproportionately uncertain.

**Stage 5 --- Operational partition:** Neurology, Podiatry, rehabilitation, symptomatic treatment and deferred genetics provide workable destinations.

**Stage 6 --- "As per above":** the mixed state becomes the plan.

**Stage 7 --- Documentary inheritance:** older letters and summaries become reusable information objects.

**Stage 8 --- Boundary crossings:** treatment state, OFF phenomena, structural findings, pain recurrence and new examinations repeatedly cross the earlier compartments.

**Stage 9 --- Local updating:** new evidence produces genuine branch-level reassessment.

**Stage 10 --- Restored manageability:** local treatment can reduce immediate pressure for global explanation.

**Stage 11 --- Accumulated continuity:** years of care produce a coherent documentary pathway.

**Stage 12 --- Retrospective authority:** "As the letters show" uses that pathway itself as explanatory evidence.

**Stage 13 --- External endpoint:** 2026 molecularly resolves one of the oldest unresolved etiological questions.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 33. Strongest full formulation {#33-strongest-full-formulation .anchored-heading}

The strongest current formulation is:

> **A broad, deteriorating relational problem entered hospital from primary care: painful/problematic feet, abnormal gait, tone/stiffness, inability to relax, toe clawing/posture, movement-initiation difficulty, dexterity loss, functional decline and an open "?parkinsonian or other underlying diagnosis." The hospital\'s immediate neurological task contracted that object around initiation difficulty, four-limb tone/stiffness and cogwheeling. Neurology later recovered much of the omitted history and identified a severe very-young-onset Parkinsonian syndrome, including a lower-limb appearance described as somewhat dystonic and genuine structural foot disease. The critical documentary transformation therefore occurred not because the facts vanished, but between recovery and synthesis: the Parkinsonian/dystonic branch received high-resolution examination, differential reasoning, imaging, DaT investigation, complex-case discussion, genetic reasoning and external specialist input, while overlapping painful/structural/peripheral lower-limb phenomena were grouped more tentatively as potentially separate or neuropathic without an equally visible proposition-by-proposition discriminator connecting or separating those branches. "Plan/Recommendations: As per above" then made that mixed information state operational: treatment, referrals, rehabilitation, Podiatry, follow-up and deferred genetics could proceed while important explanatory relationships remained "perhaps" / "Not clear." The components acquired service destinations, but responsibility for the arrows between them was less explicit. Later documentation increasingly inherited previous summaries; tentative categories became reusable; new observations repeatedly crossed the original partition and produced genuine local reclassification---Parkinsonian foot throw, OFF dystonia, medication-state pain modulation, renewed neuropathic questions---without a clearly identifiable pre-2025 reconstruction of the original whole. Primary care did not simply lose the relationships: the GP repeatedly regenerated feet--gait--tone--pain links but deferred neurological adjudication to specialist services. By March 2025, the treating neurologist\'s retrospective response reconstructed management history and treatment rationale much more fully than it reconstructed the evidential genealogy by which the disputed pain/neuropathy and etiological categories acquired their later grammatical certainty. "As the letters show" therefore functions most securely as evidence of documentary and management continuity, and only proposition-specifically as evidence of independent revalidation. The external 2025 Movement Disorders/dystonia referral is the clearest later reopening of the parent model. The 2026 PRKN result then resolves one etiological question the historical record itself had generated and deliberately left open; it does not prove what clinicians should have known earlier or establish one mechanism for every historical foot symptom.**

### Strongest documentary object {#strongest-documentary-object .anchored-heading}

The case should now be described, at its narrowest and strongest, as:

> **problem-scope contraction followed by asymmetric epistemic resolution, operational closure, distributed residual ownership, and documentary/classification inheritance.**

The deepest issue is not loss of facts. It is:

> **the conversion of an incompletely discriminated specialist classification into a durable organising structure for later facts.**

The most important internal asymmetry is:

> **the Parkinsonian/dystonic branch is positively demonstrated at much higher evidential resolution than the peripheral-neuropathic branch, while the surviving documentation does not visibly supply an equivalent bridge explaining how those branches were discriminated within the same lower-limb territory.**

That is a documentary finding. Whether the partition was clinically correct is a clinical-expert question. Whether the resulting ownership/provenance structure met professional or governance standards is a separate expert/governance question.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

### Counterfactual refinement {#counterfactual-refinement .anchored-heading}

The full formulation should also preserve a narrower counterfactual distinction. The GP referral and the PRECIS were not merely longer and shorter versions of the same question. They were differently organised clinical objects. The GP asked, in effect, what explained a deteriorating relationship among feet, gait, tone, toe posture, movement and function; the PRECIS presented a concentrated motor-neurological task. Because the same neurologist later demonstrated explicit fresh hypothesis-generation in the shoulder problem and specific distal discrimination in a later foot-throw episode, there is a documentary basis for considering **question architecture** as one plausible contributor to the difference in investigative granularity.

The claim should remain:

> **different investigative object may plausibly have produced different investigative emphasis**

not:

> **different referral would have produced a different diagnosis.**

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 34. Strongest defence and counter-case {#34-strongest-defence-and-counter-case .anchored-heading}

A fair final document must preserve the strongest contrary evidence.

First, Parkinsonism was real and strongly supported. This is not a case in which a weak Parkinsonian hypothesis displaced everything else.

Second, structural foot disease was real and legitimately treated.

Third, the patient himself distinguished some foot pain from leg tension, giving a contemporaneous reason for separation.

Fourth, the dystonic-looking gait was recognised rather than missed.

Fifth, PRKN uncertainty was explicit rather than concealed.

Sixth, later care contains genuine fresh reasoning: foot throw, OFF dystonia, abrupt onset against neuropathy, Fabry testing, medication-state reasoning and pain analysis.

Seventh, chronic disease care legitimately relies on longitudinal summaries; re-deriving a mature case from zero at every appointment is neither realistic nor necessarily desirable.

Eighth, the shoulder problem was acute, focal, anatomically localisable and amenable to structural testing. Greater proposition-by-proposition detail may therefore be clinically appropriate without implying path-dependent constraint.

Ninth, absence of detailed documentation is not proof that an examination was not performed.

These considerations materially weaken any formulation based on "no thought," "no reassessment," "feet ignored," "structure unreal," or "framework frozen."

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 35. What would falsify or materially weaken the formulation {#35-what-would-falsify-or-materially-weaken-the-formulation .anchored-heading}

The analysis should specify its own falsifiers.

**Falsifier 1: contemporaneous 2017 distal reconstruction.** A note mapping dynamic toe/foot posture, fixed versus dynamic deformity, distal power, sensation, pain distribution, loading/pressure sites and gait relationship, followed by explicit reasoning on neurological versus structural versus peripheral mechanisms.

**Falsifier 2: explicit ownership/reopening plan.** A source stating that the feet--gait--tone relationship remained unresolved, Neurology retained ownership, Podiatry findings were to return, and specified triggers would reopen the model.

**Falsifier 3: formal PRKN closure before 2025.** A negative PRKN test, specialist downgrading, reasoned rejection, or explicit statement that iPD was only operational shorthand pending molecular clarification.

**Falsifier 4: later global reconstruction.** A later Neurology or Movement Disorders source gathering structural feet, pain, dystonia, gait, OFF state, neuropathy, treatment responses and genetics into a new parent model.

**Falsifier 5: shoulder provenance failure.** If the key shoulder reasoning was not authored by the treating neurologist or was copied from another source, its force as same-author control would diminish.

**Falsifier 6: full-referral framing already active.** If the canonical 2017 record shows that the treating neurologist reviewed the complete GP referral before designing the investigation, explicitly reconstructed the integrated feet--gait--tone--toe--deterioration trajectory, and treated that relationship as a primary decision-live problem, the claim that the PRECIS plausibly altered the initial question architecture would weaken substantially.

The final case should actively search for these sources rather than assume they do not exist.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 36. Expert questions --- Movement Disorders {#36-expert-questions-movement-disorders .anchored-heading}

A Movement Disorders expert should be asked focused questions rather than a global negligence question.

1.  What phenotype is documented in the contemporaneous 2017 record?
2.  What lower-limb relationships would ordinarily be clinically important in a very-young-onset Parkinsonian presentation with painful feet, toe phenomena, structural deformity and dystonic-looking gait?
3.  What does the documented examination establish and not establish?
4.  What examination would distinguish fixed deformity from dynamic dystonia or mixed loading?
5.  What significance, if any, should be attached to the dystonic-looking gait in 2017?
6.  What significance should be attached to foot/gait changes with procyclidine withdrawal?
7.  What significance should be attached to later normal directional foot power and Parkinsonian reclassification?
8.  Was PRKN a clinically meaningful hypothesis at the time?
9.  What would PRKN confirmation ordinarily change in diagnosis, prognosis, counselling, referral or treatment, if anything?
10. What later observations would ordinarily reduce confidence in the original structural/peripheral partition?
11. At what point, if any, would repeated OFF/dystonia/treatment-state observations justify global reassessment?
12. How should the 2026 endpoint be used without hindsight?

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 37. Expert questions --- pain/neuropathy {#37-expert-questions-pain-neuropathy .anchored-heading}

1.  What findings are ordinarily required to classify pain as neuropathic?
2.  How discriminating are burning and exquisite sensitivity?
3.  What weight can be given to pregabalin response?
4.  What significance does severe pain despite high-dose pregabalin have?
5.  What significance does literal overnight onset have?
6.  Can Parkinsonian OFF states magnify or generate pain without proving that the underlying pain is directly Parkinsonian?
7.  How should substantial Sinemet/Sinemet-CR response alter the differential?
8.  How should persistent toe curling despite analgesic benefit be interpreted?
9.  What objective findings would weaken or falsify a peripheral/neuropathic classification?
10. What evidence would justify describing a symptom phenotype as neuropathic while disease-level neuropathy remains uncertain?

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 38. Expert questions --- podiatry/biomechanics {#38-expert-questions-podiatry-biomechanics .anchored-heading}

1.  Could dystonic gait or abnormal lower-limb posture contribute to focal loading and recurrent callus formation?
2.  How can primary structural disease be distinguished from secondary loading effects?
3.  Can pes planus/hammertoes coexist with neurologically driven toe posture or dystonia?
4.  What does recurrent fifth-MTPJ pathology signify mechanically?
5.  What examination would help decide whether a structural and neurological mechanism are independent or mixed?
6.  What information would ideally travel back to Neurology where dynamic posture is also present?

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 39. Documentary/linguistic questions {#39-documentary-linguistic-questions .anchored-heading}

1.  How does incorporation by reference affect later semantic stability?
2.  Does repeated same-author wording constitute independent corroboration?
3.  What is the significance of grammatical movement from "perhaps" to affirmative noun phrases?
4.  How does the record distinguish category uncertainty from cause uncertainty?
5.  How do "As per above" and "As the letters show" function prospectively and retrospectively?
6.  Does the shoulder episode demonstrate a materially different discourse form?
7.  Can differences be described as information-state/path dependence without inferring motive?
8.  When does continuity become documentary recursion rather than new evidential support?

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 42. Proposition ledger --- strongly supported documentary propositions {#42-proposition-ledger-strongly-supported-documentary-propositions .anchored-heading}

**P1.** The GP referral described an integrated and worsening feet--gait--tone--movement--function presentation.

**P2.** The hospital PRECIS foregrounded the cardinal motor-neurological signs and did not preserve the full relational framing.

**P3.** Neurology later recovered many omitted component facts.

**P4.** The admission examination documented a dystonic-looking footdrop-like gait and structural foot abnormalities.

**P5.** The surviving documented distal structural--dynamic--pain examination is less granular than the final central/genetic synthesis.

**P6.** The 2017 synthesis generated a specific PRKN/PARK2 hypothesis.

**P7.** Prof Healy independently supported PARK2 and recommended testing.

**P8.** PRKN remained suspected in 2018 while testing was deliberately deferred because management could continue without knowing.

**P9.** "As per above" made an unresolved synthesis operational.

**P10.** Later notes explicitly relied on older letters/summaries.

**P11.** Later lower-limb evidence crossed the original documentary compartments and produced genuine local reassessment.

**P12.** The same neurologist demonstrated explicit fresh hypothesis-testing in the shoulder problem.

**P13.** Neuropathic pain language became more affirmative at symptom-classification level while disease-level neuropathy could remain conditional.

**P14.** The 2025 response relied explicitly on accumulated letters to explain historical management.

**P15.** The 2026 PRKN result resolved a question already present in 2017--2018.

**P16.** The GP referral and hospital PRECIS presented materially different problem representations: the former an integrated deteriorating relationship under an open diagnosis, the latter a narrower motor-neurological task.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 43. Proposition ledger --- documentary inferences requiring careful language {#43-proposition-ledger-documentary-inferences-requiring-careful-language .anchored-heading}

**I1.** The PRECIS may have contributed to relational rather than factual compression.

**I2.** The 2017 synthesis shows asymmetric allocation of analytical resolution.

**I3.** Operational destinations may have reduced immediate pressure for global explanatory resolution.

**I4.** The chronic record shows path dependence through inheritance of a mature information state.

**I5.** Later reasoning is more often local/accommodative than globally reconstructive.

**I6.** The shoulder episode supports a difference in information-state reasoning mode rather than lack of analytical capacity.

**I7.** Neuropathic terminology underwent semantic/classificatory hardening.

**I8.** By 2025 documentary continuity had acquired retrospective explanatory authority.

**I9.** Preserving the GP referral as the primary organising document would plausibly have kept the feet--gait--tone--toe relationship more explicitly decision-live; this is a documentary counterfactual about investigative emphasis, not a prediction of diagnostic outcome.

Each of these remains an inference about the organisation of the record, not a medical-standard conclusion.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 44. Proposition ledger --- expert questions {#44-proposition-ledger-expert-questions .anchored-heading}

**E1.** Whether the 2017 distal examination was clinically adequate.

**E2.** Whether Podiatry allocation was appropriate without more neurological integration.

**E3.** Whether PRKN testing should have occurred earlier.

**E4.** Whether earlier PRKN confirmation would have altered treatment, prognosis, counselling or referral.

**E5.** Whether particular foot pain episodes were dystonic, neuropathic, structural, mixed, or Parkinsonian-modulated.

**E6.** Whether 2023 or 2025 evidence required global reconstruction.

**E7.** Whether the difference between shoulder and feet reasoning was clinically appropriate.

**E8.** Whether the 2025 complaint response adequately addressed the provenance challenge rather than only management within the existing framework.

**E9.** Whether the fuller GP problem representation would reasonably have required different or more granular distal examination, differential testing, or cross-service integration.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 45. Propositions not to assert on the present documentary record {#45-propositions-not-to-assert-on-the-present-documentary-record .anchored-heading}

Do not assert deliberate concealment, dishonesty, bad faith, incompetence, negligence, malpractice, intentional anchoring, conscious refusal to reconsider, that every foot symptom was dystonia, that no peripheral neuropathy existed, that structural disease was irrelevant, or that 2026 proves what clinicians should have known in 2017.

Do not convert four GPT outputs into four independent expert opinions. Their value is convergence across separately produced documentary analyses, especially where they identify the same source anchors, chronology and counterevidence. Prompt-seeded evaluative convergence should be given less weight than source-level convergence.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 46. Suggested final chapter architecture {#46-suggested-final-chapter-architecture .anchored-heading}

**Chapter 1 --- What entered Neurology.** Build the GP trajectory without projecting later categories backwards.

**Chapter 2 --- What the hospital task representation selected.** Show PRECIS compression and distinguish fact loss from relationship compression.

**Chapter 2A --- The counterfactual investigative object.** Compare the GP and PRECIS as competing question architectures; use the shoulder and later foot-throw as same-author controls; keep the conclusion at investigative emphasis rather than predicted diagnosis.

**Chapter 3 --- What was actually examined.** Separate history, observation, immediate interpretation and later synthesis.

**Chapter 4 --- How the sophisticated 2017 model was built.** Explain Parkinsonism, atypicality, genetic thinking, PRKN, dystonic-looking gait, structural feet and peripheral possibility.

**Chapter 5 --- How uncertainty became a plan.** Focus on "Not clear," Podiatry, genetics and "As per above."

**Chapter 6 --- How the plan became an inherited information state.** Use older-letter and summary language.

**Chapter 7 --- How biology repeatedly crossed the partitions.** Use treatment-state changes, Podiatry truth, foot throw, OFF dystonia, neuropathy timing and pain modulation.

**Chapter 8 --- The author's own internal control.** Use the shoulder episode to show fresh-model reasoning.

**Chapter 9 --- Semantic migration and pain classification.** Trace neuropathic terminology.

**Chapter 10 --- When management becomes endurance/safety relevant.** Use 2023 onward.

**Chapter 11 --- The complaint forces a genealogy test.** Distinguish management explanation from classification provenance.

**Chapter 12 --- PRKN endpoint.** Introduce 2026 last.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 47. Working expert-instruction strategy {#47-working-expert-instruction-strategy .anchored-heading}

The expert should initially be asked to evaluate propositions rather than conclusions. The most useful instructions will likely begin with the contemporaneous phenotype, ask what examination and differential would ordinarily be expected, ask what significance should be assigned to dynamic lower-limb signs and treatment-state changes, and then ask what later information would reasonably require broader reassessment.

The expert should be given the strongest contrary evidence at the same time: real structural disease, patient-perceived distinction, detailed Parkinsonian examination, genuine later reassessments, chronic-care efficiency and the anatomical differences between the shoulder and feet problems.

A neutral expert package should therefore be adversarial to the working theory rather than merely confirmatory.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 48. Why the case is strongest as an information-pathway case {#48-why-the-case-is-strongest-as-an-information-pathway-case .anchored-heading}

The case is not presently strongest as a simple missed-diagnosis case, a simple failure-to-examine case, a simple "neuropathy was wrong" case, or a simple "PRKN should have been diagnosed in 2017" case. Those formulations are too crude and invite easy rebuttal.

The stronger account is that a broad, worsening relational clinical object entered; its immediate representation became narrower; the specialist process recovered facts but unevenly reconstructed relationships; the final synthesis achieved exceptional sophistication in one domain; unresolved relationships nonetheless became actionable; that action state became portable; new evidence repeatedly crossed the categories; local reasoning remained active and sometimes sophisticated; the original parent relationship is less visibly reconstructed than its branches are updated; a fresh same-author problem demonstrates what full proposition-by-proposition causal reconstruction looks like in this clinician's own writing; by 2025 accumulated documentary continuity was itself being used retrospectively as explanatory evidence; and by 2026 one of the oldest unresolved etiological questions was finally molecularly resolved.

That architecture is more precise, more falsifiable and less accusatory than a simple anchoring narrative.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 49. Formulations to protect {#49-formulations-to-protect .anchored-heading}

**Core:**

> **The problem is not lack of sophistication. It is where the sophistication was applied.**

**Information state:**

> **When the model was fresh, uncertainty generated questions. Once the model was inherited, uncertainty was more often managed within it.**

**PRECIS:**

> **The facts came back. The relationships did not come back with equal force.**

**Counterfactual / question architecture:**

> **The counterfactual is not different diagnosis. It is different investigative object.**

> **The full GP referral made the relationships themselves part of the presenting question; the PRECIS made the motor-neurological subset the immediate task.**

**As per above:**

> **"As per above" did not make the uncertainties disappear; it made an arrangement of those uncertainties operationally durable.**

**Longitudinal:**

> **The inherited architecture was permeable, not frozen: later evidence repeatedly crossed its boundaries and generated genuine local reassessment. The surviving record nevertheless shows local updating more clearly than a later global reconstruction of the founding feet--gait--tone--pain relationship.**

**Retrospective:**

> **Continuity of a pathway can retrospectively resemble validation of the pathway.**

**PRKN:**

> **The record demonstrates continuity of the Parkinson's diagnosis far more clearly than it demonstrates resolution of Parkinson's etiology.**

------------------------------------------------------------------------
