## Appendix A --- Why relationship preservation matters {#appendix-a-why-relationship-preservation-matters .anchored-heading}

A longitudinal record can be rich in observations and still lose the question that originally made those observations clinically meaningful together. That distinction is central here. The GP referral placed feet, gait, tone, toe posture, movement initiation, dexterity, deterioration and work impact within a single evolving communication. Later specialist care recovered much of this material, often in greater detail. But factual recovery does not necessarily equal relational recovery.

Consider the difference between recording pes planus and asking whether abnormal dynamic posture changes loading through a pes planus foot. Consider the difference between documenting hammertoes and asking whether reported toe clawing is fixed deformity, dynamic dystonia, or both. Consider the difference between recording burning pain and asking whether its timing, distribution, loading relationship and medication-state behaviour support a peripheral neuropathic mechanism, a central modulation mechanism, a dystonic mechanism, structural pain, or a mixture.

The documentary question is therefore not whether enough facts exist somewhere in the chart. It is whether the relationships among those facts remain visible, testable and owned over time.

This is what the phrase "who owned the arrows?" is intended to capture. Each service can own a node. Podiatry can own structural lesions. Neurology can own Parkinsonism. Primary care can own continuity. Nursing can own symptom escalation and medication communication. Yet the arrow between structural loading and neurological posture may remain difficult to assign. A fragmented system can still work well when the relationship itself has a clear owner or reopening mechanism. The source-audit question is whether that architecture is visible here.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## Appendix B --- Why operational closure is not a criticism by itself {#appendix-b-why-operational-closure-is-not-a-criticism-by-itself .anchored-heading}

Operational closure is normal medicine. A patient cannot remain indefinitely in diagnostic suspension while every causal question is solved. Treatment, rehabilitation, referral and follow-up often proceed under uncertainty. The 2017 record itself demonstrates this explicitly.

The analytical value of operational closure is that it explains how a record can be simultaneously sophisticated, uncertain and actionable. "As per above" can therefore be read as a management device rather than a declaration of epistemic certainty.

The vulnerability arises only if unresolved questions later become difficult to reactivate because the working arrangement functions adequately. The 2018 PRKN passage is especially useful because the clinician expressly states that current management does not depend on knowing the molecular cause. That may be entirely defensible. The longitudinal issue is what happens to the question after it stops obstructing immediate action.

This distinction should protect the case from the false premise that all uncertainty requires immediate closure. The more precise question is whether uncertainty was **managed**, including how it was carried, who owned it, and what would trigger its return to the foreground.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## Appendix C --- Why the shoulder comparator matters without proving causation {#appendix-c-why-the-shoulder-comparator-matters-without-proving-causation .anchored-heading}

The shoulder comparator is strongest when used narrowly. It does not prove that the feet should have been investigated with identical tests or that the same level of anatomical localisation was clinically required. Different problems warrant different methods.

Its value is to establish a documentary capability. The same neurologist could explicitly separate observation from inference, name multiple competing hypotheses, state what each finding would mean, articulate the purpose and limits of tests, and reject treatment response as a simple proof of mechanism.

That matters because it makes one class of explanation less persuasive: the idea that sparse relational reasoning elsewhere merely reflects this clinician's normal inability or unwillingness to document analytical uncertainty. The record shows that, under at least some conditions, he documented it in detail.

The inference that remains is not personal but structural: mature inherited problems may be processed differently from fresh ones. That is consistent with ordinary clinical efficiency, and it is also consistent with documentary path dependence. The reason for the difference cannot be settled by language alone. The comparison simply identifies the difference more securely.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## Appendix D --- Why 'anchoring' is not the preferred primary term {#appendix-d-why-anchoring-is-not-the-preferred-primary-term .anchored-heading}

"Anchoring" is a cognitive label. It suggests that a clinician fixed on an initial explanation and insufficiently adjusted thereafter. The documentary record cannot directly reveal cognitive state. It can show stable categories, repeated wording, inherited summaries, local reinterpretations, missing bridges and delayed reconstruction.

For that reason, the primary terminology should remain observational:

-   relational compression;
-   inherited information state;
-   operational closure;
-   documentary inheritance;
-   semantic hardening;
-   local updating;
-   global reconstruction;
-   generative versus accommodative reasoning;
-   path dependence.

An expert may later conclude that anchoring is an appropriate clinical description. Until then, the record-based language is both stronger and fairer because it describes what can actually be demonstrated.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## Appendix E --- Why the 2025 response is analytically special {#appendix-e-why-the-2025-response-is-analytically-special .anchored-heading}

The 2025 response is not merely another entry in the treatment history. It is a response to a challenge about the history of reasoning itself. That changes the level of analysis.

When the response says "As the letters show," it invokes documentary continuity as evidence. That is entirely legitimate for proving that treatments were offered, risks considered, and management choices made. But if the complaint concerns how a tentative 2017 classification became a durable later category, the response must be analysed for whether it reconstructs that classificatory genealogy.

This is where the phrase "Whatever the underlying cause for the peripheral neuropathic pain symptomatologies\..." matters. It preserves uncertainty about cause while grammatically treating the symptom category as comparatively settled. That may simply be ordinary shorthand. But if the complaint challenges the parent category itself, beginning inside the category answers a different level of question.

The response should therefore be read proposition by proposition: what does it explain well, what does it presuppose, what does it reopen, and what remains inherited.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## Appendix F --- Why the PRKN endpoint should be introduced last {#appendix-f-why-the-prkn-endpoint-should-be-introduced-last .anchored-heading}

The eventual PRKN result is unusually powerful because it resonates with early age, symmetry, rigidity, cognition and dystonic features. That very power creates risk. If introduced too early, it can cause the analyst to reinterpret every prior ambiguity in its light.

The safer method is chronological sequestration. First determine what the 2017 record actually observed and thought. Then determine what happened to the PRKN question in 2018 and thereafter. Only then introduce 2026.

Used this way, the endpoint does something precise: it demonstrates that a historical etiological branch, already generated from the contemporaneous phenotype and independently reinforced, was ultimately real and resolvable. It does not decide the standard of care or every historical symptom mechanism.

This disciplined sequencing strengthens rather than weakens the significance of the endpoint.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## Appendix G --- Draft one-page theory {#appendix-g-draft-one-page-theory .anchored-heading}

The patient entered Neurology in 2017 with a worsening, integrated lower-limb and movement presentation in which painful/problematic feet, abnormal gait, toe posture, increased tone, movement-initiation difficulty, dexterity loss and functional decline were still being presented together under an open diagnostic question. The hospital's short task representation foregrounded the most recognisable neurological signs, particularly initiation difficulty, tone/stiffness and cogwheeling. During the full admission Neurology recovered much of the omitted history and documented a severe, symmetrical Parkinsonian syndrome, a strikingly abnormal footdrop-like gait considered somewhat dystonic rather than true footdrop, and genuine structural foot abnormalities.

The final 2017 synthesis was sophisticated. It developed young-onset Parkinsonism, atypicality, autosomal-recessive reasoning and a specific PRKN/PARK2 hypothesis. At the same time, the painful/structural foot material was left at lower relational resolution: possibly separate, perhaps peripheral neuropathy, "Not clear." The practical plan could nevertheless proceed through Neurology, Podiatry, medication, rehabilitation and follow-up. "Plan/Recommendations: As per above" therefore appears to have made an unresolved but elaborate synthesis operational without demonstrating complete explanatory closure.

The later record makes inheritance of that information state explicit through reliance on older letters and summaries. PRKN itself provides a clear example of uncertainty becoming operationally tolerable: in 2018 it remained the suspected cause, yet testing was deferred because current management would not change and there was "no great loss in not knowing" at that time.

Subsequent biology repeatedly generated information crossing the operational compartments. Feet and gait changed with neurological treatment state. A later foot-throw was specifically examined and classified as Parkinsonian. OFF dystonia was considered. Abrupt onset was used against straightforward peripheral neuropathy. Fabry was investigated. Pregabalin and dopaminergic therapies both altered symptom states. Structural Podiatry findings remained real. These are not signs of a closed or thoughtless system. They show meaningful local updating.

The documentary question is whether those local revisions were ever assembled into a new account of the original whole. The surviving record more clearly shows individual branches being adjusted than the original feet--gait--tone--pain relationship being re-derived globally.

The 2019 shoulder episode provides a powerful internal comparator. The same neurologist, when faced with a fresh focal problem, explicitly constructs competing anatomical hypotheses, links examination findings to those hypotheses, orders tests for particular questions, specifies limitations of negative tests, and expressly refuses to treat medication response as proof of cause. This demonstrates analytical capacity and supports a narrower information-state interpretation: fresh problems are visibly modelled from the ground up, while chronic inherited problems are more often updated inside an existing architecture.

By 2025 the accumulated record had acquired retrospective explanatory force. The complaint asked for review of reasoning across the whole period, and the response relied on what "the letters show." That reliance may accurately prove continuity of treatment and decision-making while leaving a separate source-audit question about whether the founding classifications were independently revalidated.

The February 2026 PRKN result is important only after that historical analysis is complete. It resolved the same specific genetic question generated in 2017, independently reinforced, and deliberately left unresolved in 2018. It does not prove that every historical foot symptom was dystonia or that all earlier decisions were wrong. It confirms that at least one major unresolved etiological branch was real, specific and ultimately answerable.

The current strongest formulation is therefore not a claim of diagnostic incompetence. It is a claim about the architecture of information: **the problem is not lack of sophistication; it is where the sophistication was applied.** And the same-author comparator supports a second formulation: **when the model was fresh, uncertainty generated questions; once the model was inherited, uncertainty was more often managed within it.**
:::

## Part XXXII --- Canonical GP information-state trial: sequential findings after unblinding {#part-xxxii-canonical-gp-information-state-trial-sequential-findings-after-unblinding .source-h1 .anchored-heading}

## 114. Trial status and source discipline {#114-trial-status-and-source-discipline .anchored-heading}

This section records the results of the pre-registered GP information-state audit after review of the canonical record supplied after the hypotheses had been frozen.

The exercise is not literally blind in the experimental sense. Earlier controlled work had already exposed selected quotations and broad themes. The protection against drift is therefore procedural rather than absolute: the object, hypotheses, timepoints and falsifiers were frozen before the canonical GP/Podiatry sequence was examined as a whole.

The trial asks only what the GP\'s **documented and operational information state** shows at each timepoint. It does not infer private mental state. It also does not treat later PRKN results as information available to the GP before 2026.

The most important finding is that the GP pathway is not a simple story of specialist framing being passively copied into primary care. The GP repeatedly preserves or regenerates the relationships among feet, gait, tone, Parkinsonian state and pain, while relying on specialist Neurology to adjudicate questions beyond primary-care scope.

That distinction now becomes an important control for the wider case.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 115. State 1 --- Before hospital: integrated relational object {#115-state-1-before-hospital-integrated-relational-object .anchored-heading}

The pre-hospital GP state is strongly integrated.

### 115.1 April 2017 begins with feet and gait together {#115-1-april-2017-begins-with-feet-and-gait-together .anchored-heading}

The first GP presentation is coded as a FOOT/TOE complaint. The record contains bilateral foot pain and abnormal gait together, with marked mallet toes, onychogryphosis, callouses and pressure areas. Podiatry is planned, but the GP does not stop at a structural explanation.

### 115.2 June 2017 explicitly joins Neurology to gait, feet and muscle tension {#115-2-june-2017-explicitly-joins-neurology-to-gait-feet-and-muscle-tension .anchored-heading}

Before the urgent referral, the GP records movement-initiation difficulty and says he may seek a neurological opinion of the patient\'s:

> **gait, feet and \"muscle tension\" issues.**

This is direct evidence that the GP\'s neurological question included the feet rather than beginning from a pre-separated Parkinsonism object.

### 115.3 Occupational Health independently increases concern {#115-3-occupational-health-independently-increases-concern .anchored-heading}

A second doctor contacts the GP regarding the gait. The GP records that this colleague echoes his concerns and decides to refer to Neurology even before older records are obtained.

### 115.4 The urgent referral preserves the full trajectory {#115-4-the-urgent-referral-preserves-the-full-trajectory .anchored-heading}

The 29 June referral presents:

-   bilateral foot issues and poor foot condition;
-   unusual festinant and stiff gait;
-   increasingly evident movement-initiation difficulty;
-   marked upper- and lower-limb stiffness/tone;
-   inability to relax;
-   toe clawing in bed;
-   worsening dexterity;
-   work and daily-activity decline;
-   an open question of \"?parkinsonian or other underlying diagnosis\";
-   explicit deterioration and employment risk.

### Frozen State-1 conclusion {#frozen-state-1-conclusion .anchored-heading}

> **Before hospitalisation, the GP\'s clinical object is relational and prospective: feet, gait, tone, toe posture, movement initiation and function are travelling together under an open neurological question.**

This strongly confirms the pre-registered hypothesis that the GP began with an integrated problem representation.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 116. State 2 --- During admission and specialist return: diagnosis gains authority, but the feet are not simply detached {#116-state-2-during-admission-and-specialist-return-diagnosis-gains-authority-but-the-feet-are-not-simply-detached .anchored-heading}

The second state produces a more complex result than the initial hypothesis predicted.

### 116.1 During the admission the GP re-contacts Podiatry \"as Parkinson\'s\" {#116-1-during-the-admission-the-gp-re-contacts-podiatry-as-parkinson-s .anchored-heading}

On 6 July 2017, while the hospital admission is still ongoing, the GP phones Podiatry and records that the case will be marked as a priority:

> **\"as Parkinson\'s.\"**

This is important.

The GP does not merely continue a pre-existing structural-foot referral in a separate silo. He actively updates the Podiatry pathway with the newly emerging neurological diagnosis.

Documentarily, this is an early cross-service integration signal:

> **feet destination + Parkinsonian information travel together.**

It does not establish what detailed information Podiatry received or what causal relationship the GP believed existed.

### 116.2 The short discharge summary presented to the GP is Parkinsonism-dominant {#116-2-the-short-discharge-summary-presented-to-the-gp-is-parkinsonism-dominant .anchored-heading}

The electronic discharge summary identifies Parkinson\'s disease as the primary diagnosis and summarises the chief complaint as movement-initiation difficulty, bradykinesia, reduced dexterity, impaired balance and unsteady gait. It records clonazepam for leg tension and a Podiatry referral for \"issues with feet reported by the patient.\"

This short summary does not reproduce the integrated GP trajectory or the full later lower-limb uncertainty.

### 116.3 The GP subsequently records receipt of extensive Neurology correspondence {#116-3-the-gp-subsequently-records-receipt-of-extensive-neurology-correspondence .anchored-heading}

On 18 July the GP records:

> **\"Extensive correspondence Dr Thomas Monaghan - Diagnosis early Parkinsonism. Further testing awaited but positive DaTscan and clinical features - see scanning.\"**

The long Neurology correspondence is addressed to the GP and includes the full sophisticated synthesis, including the foot-history discussion, \"completely separate / perhaps secondary peripheral neuropathy / Not clear,\" the dystonic-looking gait and the genetic reasoning.

The GP therefore appears to have received far more than the short electronic discharge summary.

### 116.4 What changes in the GP record immediately afterwards {#116-4-what-changes-in-the-gp-record-immediately-afterwards .anchored-heading}

The GP codes Parkinson\'s disease/Parkinsonism and manages medication titration, certification and return-to-work issues within that diagnosis.

There is no contemporaneous GP note visible here explicitly reopening the neurological-foot relationship immediately after receipt of the long Neurology letter.

That does not establish that the GP concluded the question was fully resolved.

It does establish that the specialist diagnosis becomes the dominant operative category in primary care.

### Frozen State-2 conclusion {#frozen-state-2-conclusion .anchored-heading}

> **The Neurology diagnosis acquires strong operational authority in the GP record, but the GP does not simply route the feet away from Parkinsonism: even during the admission he updates Podiatry \"as Parkinson\'s.\" The evidence therefore supports specialist-authority effects without supporting a simple \"GP adopted feet as separate\" theory.**

This qualifies the pre-registered \"apparent specialist clearance\" hypothesis. Specialist authority is real; complete relational extinguishing is not demonstrated.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 117. State 3 --- Podiatry 2017--2019: structural truth accumulates, while the GP later explicitly reconnects the feet to Parkinsonian tone and gait {#117-state-3-podiatry-20172019-structural-truth-accumulates-while-the-gp-later-explicitly-reconnects-the-feet-to-parkinsonian-tone-and-gait .anchored-heading}

### 117.1 Podiatry establishes genuine local pathology {#117-1-podiatry-establishes-genuine-local-pathology .anchored-heading}

The Podiatry record documents real and recurrent mechanical/structural findings and treatment:

-   callus at the left fifth MTPJ;
-   nail pathology;
-   bilateral toe props;
-   tight gastroc/soleus;
-   recurrent callus debridement;
-   insoles/offloading;
-   clawed toes;
-   slight leg-length difference;
-   pronating foot type;
-   lateral-foot pain;
-   pressure/offloading modifications.

This strongly confirms that the structural branch was clinically real and locally productive.

### 117.2 Podiatry also records dynamic improvement in gait after Parkinson\'s diagnosis/treatment {#117-2-podiatry-also-records-dynamic-improvement-in-gait-after-parkinson-s-diagnosis-treatment .anchored-heading}

At the first Podiatry treatment in September 2017, the patient reports:

> **\"my gait was very bad but it\'s not any more\"**

while attending after the Parkinson\'s diagnosis.

This is patient-reported treatment-state/disease-state information within the structural service. It is not mechanistic proof.

### 117.3 Podiatry repeatedly documents clawed toes {#117-3-podiatry-repeatedly-documents-clawed-toes .anchored-heading}

By March and June 2018, clawed toes are objective findings in the Podiatry record.

The structural record therefore contains a feature that also belongs to the original GP relational presentation.

### 117.4 The key 2019 GP re-referral explicitly reconnects the branches {#117-4-the-key-2019-gp-re-referral-explicitly-reconnects-the-branches .anchored-heading}

The 2019 Podiatry re-referral is one of the strongest sources in the GP information-state trial.

The GP writes:

> **\"Seen by podiatry in the past due to feet issues in relating to increased tone and abnormal gait relating to Parkinson\'s disease.\"**

He then states:

> **\"Parkinsons at very young age has issues with \'clawing feet\' and pain.\"**

The referral also marks callus, rearfoot/arch pain and gait abnormalities.

This is not the language of a GP who has simply inherited a conclusion that the feet are clinically independent of the neurological syndrome.

It is a relational formulation:

> **feet issues ↔ increased tone ↔ abnormal gait ↔ Parkinson\'s disease ↔ clawing feet ↔ pain.**

### 117.5 The \"No neuropathy\" tick requires restraint {#117-5-the-no-neuropathy-tick-requires-restraint .anchored-heading}

The same 2019 form visibly ticks a Low-Risk item labelled \"No neuropathy.\"

This is documentary evidence that established neuropathy was not being carried as a risk-state proposition on that referral.

However the same form also ticks \"No deformity\" despite separate evidence of clawing/toe deformity. The risk checklist should therefore not be elevated into a specialist neurological exclusion test. It is evidence about the referral form\'s recorded risk state, not definitive proof against neuropathic mechanisms.

### Frozen State-3 conclusion {#frozen-state-3-conclusion .anchored-heading}

> **By 2019 the GP demonstrably retains or regenerates the original cross-domain relationship. Structural disease is real and continues to receive Podiatry care, but the GP\'s own referral explicitly links recurrent foot problems to increased tone, abnormal gait, very-young Parkinson\'s and clawing feet.**

This substantially weakens any theory that the GP\'s post-specialist information state became simply \"Parkinsonism over here / feet over there.\"

It instead supports:

> **relational persistence within primary care despite specialist partition.**

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 118. State 4 --- 2023 crisis: the GP actively reopens the pain--Parkinsonian relationship {#118-state-4-2023-crisis-the-gp-actively-reopens-the-painparkinsonian-relationship .anchored-heading}

The 26 March 2023 urgent Neurology referral is a particularly strong information-state source because it shows what the GP does when the pain branch becomes severe.

### 118.1 The GP does not merely transmit \"neuropathic pain\" {#118-1-the-gp-does-not-merely-transmit-neuropathic-pain .anchored-heading}

He reports severe bilateral burning of the feet and lower legs, escalating pregabalin and death-wish language.

But his reasoning does not stop at symptom classification or analgesia.

He recalls a prior episode in which pain had:

> **\"suddenly \... settled with better control of his parkinsons.\"**

He then states:

> **\"I am worried about him\"**

and asks Neurology to review:

> **\"before we get into trouble here.\"**

### 118.2 Why this matters to information flow {#118-2-why-this-matters-to-information-flow .anchored-heading}

The GP is carrying a cross-boundary historical observation into the specialist referral:

> **severe pain now + prior pain improvement with Parkinsonian control → Neurology should reassess.**

This is not equivalent to concluding that the pain is Parkinsonian.

It is exactly the preservation of an arrow:

> **pain state ↔ Parkinsonian control state.**

### Frozen State-4 conclusion {#frozen-state-4-conclusion .anchored-heading}

> **When the lower-limb pain becomes severe in 2023, the GP explicitly reintroduces Parkinsonian treatment-state history as relevant contrary information and sends the problem back to Neurology. The original relational question has therefore not disappeared from primary care.**

This is strong evidence that the GP functions as a longitudinal re-integration node rather than merely a passive carrier of the inherited neuropathic/structural compartments.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 119. State 5 --- 2025: specialist authority constraint becomes explicit {#119-state-5-2025-specialist-authority-constraint-becomes-explicit .anchored-heading}

The 2025 GP pathway is especially informative because it shows what happens when the patient challenges the inherited diagnosis itself.

### 119.1 The GP does not independently endorse the dystonia theory {#119-1-the-gp-does-not-independently-endorse-the-dystonia-theory .anchored-heading}

When the patient asks for Botox and frames the problem as dystonia versus dyskinesia, the GP does not adopt that mechanistic conclusion.

Instead, he states that Botox suitability is outside his scope and that he has already referred for a second neurological opinion regarding dystonia and the patient\'s concerns.

This is professional boundary language, not dismissal of the question.

### 119.2 The St Vincent\'s referral records an inherited diagnostic state plus an unresolved challenge {#119-2-the-st-vincent-s-referral-records-an-inherited-diagnostic-state-plus-an-unresolved-challenge .anchored-heading}

On 11 July 2025 the GP writes that the patient has carried a young-onset Parkinson\'s diagnosis for eight years but is now questioning the whole diagnosis and believes he has a complex dystonia instead.

The GP explicitly says that the patient\'s question about the positive predictive value of the DAT scan is:

> **\"way above my knowledge level!\"**

He therefore seeks an urgent second opinion from a neurologist specifically because of expertise in:

> **movement disorders but also dystonia.**

### 119.3 The GP attempts to transfer the inherited specialist record {#119-3-the-gp-attempts-to-transfer-the-inherited-specialist-record .anchored-heading}

The referral says there is extensive correspondence that cannot be attached to the form and that the GP intends to Healthmail it to the receiving Parkinson\'s nurse specialist or consultant.

This shows an important dual information state:

1.  the inherited eight-year specialist model remains highly authoritative and portable;
2.  the GP accepts that a specialist-level challenge to that model is now live and requires external adjudication.

### 119.4 Later 2025 correspondence reinforces authority constraint {#119-4-later-2025-correspondence-reinforces-authority-constraint .anchored-heading}

When medication adjustment is requested, the GP states that he cannot reasonably adjust levodopa without Neurology specialist guidance because doing so would be beyond ordinary GP expertise.

This makes the specialist hierarchy explicit.

### Frozen State-5 conclusion {#frozen-state-5-conclusion .anchored-heading}

> **By 2025 the GP\'s record does not show independent confidence that the inherited Neurology model is correct or incorrect. It shows a primary-care clinician recognising the limits of his own authority, preserving the patient\'s challenge as a specialist question, and deliberately seeking a movement-disorders/dystonia opinion.**

This strongly supports **authority constraint** as part of the information-flow architecture.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 120. Cross-time result --- what happened to the GP\'s clinical object? {#120-cross-time-result-what-happened-to-the-gp-s-clinical-object .anchored-heading}

The sequential GP pathway can now be summarised without hindsight:

### 2017 before specialist assessment {#2017-before-specialist-assessment .anchored-heading}

> **feet + gait + muscle tension + toe clawing + movement-initiation difficulty + functional decline → open neurological question**

### During/after admission {#during-after-admission .anchored-heading}

> **young-onset Parkinsonism becomes the authoritative specialist diagnosis; Podiatry remains active, but the GP updates it \"as Parkinson\'s\"**

### 2019 {#2019 .anchored-heading}

> **recurrent foot problem → GP explicitly reconnects feet to increased tone + abnormal gait + very-young Parkinson\'s + clawing feet + pain**

### 2023 {#2023 .anchored-heading}

> **severe burning pain → GP recalls prior pain settling with better Parkinson\'s control → urgent neurological reopening**

### 2025 {#2025 .anchored-heading}

> **diagnostic challenge/dystonia theory → GP states knowledge/scope limits → seeks specialist Movement Disorders/dystonia second opinion**

### Overall finding {#overall-finding .anchored-heading}

The best current description is not:

> the GP originally integrated the case, then specialist correspondence caused him to forget the relationship.

It is:

> **the GP repeatedly preserved or regenerated the relationship, but specialist Neurology retained authority to adjudicate it.**

That gives the case a more precise architecture:

> **primary care repeatedly sees/carries the arrows → specialist services own the high-authority explanatory model → local services manage components → GP re-escalates when the arrows become clinically salient → specialist hierarchy determines whether the parent model is reconstructed.**

This is stronger and fairer than a simple fragmentation account.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 121. The apparent specialist-clearance hypothesis: qualified, not rejected {#121-the-apparent-specialist-clearance-hypothesis-qualified-not-rejected .anchored-heading}

The trial does not support a strong proposition that the GP received the Neurology letter and therefore considered the feet neurologically \"cleared.\"

The 2019 and 2023 sources are inconsistent with such a complete inference.

However a narrower authority proposition survives:

> **Once a specialist Neurology service had constructed a sophisticated Parkinsonian diagnosis and had itself discussed the feet as possibly separate/peripheral, the GP could reasonably continue to operate within that specialist architecture while still noticing and re-referring relationship-crossing information.**

The important distinction is:

> **reliance on specialist adjudicative authority ≠ loss of relational awareness.**

The GP record supports the former and contradicts a strong version of the latter.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 122. Does the GP expect that an ordinary specialist neurological/movement examination has already occurred? {#122-does-the-gp-expect-that-an-ordinary-specialist-neurological-movement-examination-has-already-occurred .anchored-heading}

The record does not directly state:

> \"I assume Neurology examined the feet for dystonia.\"

That private expectation should not be attributed to the GP.

But the record does show professional reliance patterns:

-   he sends an integrated problem to Neurology;
-   he receives extensive specialist correspondence and adopts the Parkinsonism diagnosis operationally;
-   he continues to send cross-domain observations back to Neurology when they become salient;
-   he says specialist questions such as DAT interpretation, Botox suitability and levodopa adjustment exceed his knowledge/scope;
-   he seeks a Movement Disorders/dystonia specialist when the inherited model itself becomes contested.

The strongest documentary inference is therefore:

> **The GP treated specialist neurological adjudication as authoritative for questions beyond primary-care scope, while continuing to supply relational observations that could test that adjudication.**

Whether a reasonable GP would specifically assume that a particular movement-disorders examination of the feet had occurred is a professional-standard/expert question.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 123. Natural control: the GP versus the specialist pathway {#123-natural-control-the-gp-versus-the-specialist-pathway .anchored-heading}

The GP pathway now functions as an important internal control.

### Specialist pathway {#specialist-pathway .anchored-heading}

The specialist record achieves exceptionally high resolution in:

-   Parkinsonian syndrome construction;
-   genetics;
-   medication strategy;
-   later local questions.

The unresolved structural--dynamic lower-limb relationship becomes distributed across Podiatry, pain/neuropathy, Parkinsonian state and later dystonia/OFF reasoning.

### GP pathway {#gp-pathway .anchored-heading}

The GP repeatedly writes relationship-bearing formulations:

-   \"gait, feet and muscle tension\";
-   Podiatry prioritised \"as Parkinson\'s\";
-   foot issues related to increased tone and abnormal gait relating to Parkinson\'s;
-   very-young Parkinson\'s with clawing feet and pain;
-   prior pain settling with better Parkinson\'s control;
-   later need for a Movement Disorders/dystonia opinion.

### New working formulation {#new-working-formulation .anchored-heading}

> **The GP pathway preserves the arrows more consistently than it resolves them. The specialist pathway resolves individual nodes more authoritatively than it reconstructs all of the arrows.**

This may be one of the strongest information-flow formulations in the case.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 124. Implications for \"Who Owned the Arrows?\" {#124-implications-for-who-owned-the-arrows .anchored-heading}

The GP trial significantly refines the ownership question.

It is no longer enough to ask:

> Who noticed the relationships?

The GP plainly did, repeatedly.

The stronger question is:

> **Who had the authority and responsibility to adjudicate those relationships once primary care repeatedly returned them to specialist services?**

The longitudinal division begins to look like:

-   **GP:** identifies/re-identifies relationships, carries cross-time information, escalates;
-   **Podiatry:** establishes and treats local mechanical truth;
-   **Neurology:** holds authoritative Parkinsonian diagnosis and movement-state interpretation;
-   **Movement Disorders nursing:** manages specialist treatment state and escalation;
-   **later external Movement Disorders specialist:** receives the reopened top-level diagnostic question.

The possible vulnerability is therefore not that nobody saw the arrows.

It is that:

> **the service with the broadest authority to adjudicate them may have processed later crossings mainly as local questions without an equivalently visible global reconstruction.**

That remains an expert/documentary question rather than a concluded failure.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 125. Revised implications for the 50-question audit {#125-revised-implications-for-the-50-question-audit .anchored-heading}

The first canonical GP pass does **not** require replacing the 50-question protocol. It requires changing the level at which several questions operate.

### 125.1 Questions materially answered or retired {#125-1-questions-materially-answered-or-retired .anchored-heading}

The record materially answers the underlying question **whether the GP lost the integrated feet--gait--tone relationship after specialist involvement**: the strong version should be retired. The 2019 re-referral explicitly reconnects feet with increased tone, abnormal gait and Parkinson\'s, and the 2023 urgent referral reconnects severe pain with prior improvement under better Parkinsonian control.

Accordingly, any question framed as "did anyone downstream still see the relationship?" is now too weak.

### 125.2 Questions reframed upward {#125-2-questions-reframed-upward .anchored-heading}

The ownership questions must now operate at the level of **adjudicative authority**:

> **The GP kept seeing and transmitting arrows. Who had authority to decide what they meant, and what did specialist care do when those arrows were returned?**

This affects Q22--Q25, Q32--Q35 and Q46--Q50 most strongly.

### 125.3 Questions strengthened {#125-3-questions-strengthened .anchored-heading}

The GP trial strengthens the need to answer the specialist evidential-bridge questions. Because primary care later continued to link feet with Parkinsonian tone/gait and pain with Parkinsonian control, the record must more clearly explain what evidence allowed specialist separation/peripheral categories to remain operationally durable.

This strengthens Q11--Q15 and Q36--Q40.

### 125.4 Questions unchanged {#125-4-questions-unchanged .anchored-heading}

The following remain essentially unchanged:

-   the 2017 distal examination questions (Q6--Q10);
-   dystonic-sign provenance and PRKN incorporation (Q16--Q20);
-   PRKN deferral/closure genealogy (Q26--Q30);
-   same-author shoulder control (Q41--Q45).

The GP corpus does not answer those specialist-level questions.

### 125.5 New controlling distinction {#125-5-new-controlling-distinction .anchored-heading}

The trial establishes a distinction the original 50 questions did not state sharply enough:

> **recognition of a relationship is not the same as authority to adjudicate it.**

Primary care could recognise, preserve, and repeatedly refer a relationship while still depending on specialist Neurology or Movement Disorders to determine its clinical meaning.

### 125.6 Revised Q50 direction {#125-6-revised-q50-direction .anchored-heading}

The strongest pre-2026 theory is no longer "the integrated relationship was lost downstream." It is:

> **The integrated relationship remained visible in primary care and repeatedly re-entered specialist pathways. The unresolved question is how the specialist system with adjudicative authority processed those returns, and why repeated local revisions are easier to identify than a global specialist reconstruction of the original whole.**

## 126. Strongest counterarguments exposed by the GP trial {#126-strongest-counterarguments-exposed-by-the-gp-trial .anchored-heading}

The trial also strengthens the defence in several ways.

### 126.1 The pathway was not completely fragmented {#126-1-the-pathway-was-not-completely-fragmented .anchored-heading}

The GP repeatedly transmitted Parkinsonian context into Podiatry and later returned pain information to Neurology.

Any claim of absolute siloing should be retired.

### 126.2 Podiatry had genuine pathology to treat {#126-2-podiatry-had-genuine-pathology-to-treat .anchored-heading}

The GP\'s relational framing does not invalidate the structural branch.

### 126.3 The GP himself worked within the Parkinson\'s diagnosis for years {#126-3-the-gp-himself-worked-within-the-parkinson-s-diagnosis-for-years .anchored-heading}

This shows the specialist diagnosis was clinically persuasive and operationally useful, not merely imposed and resisted from the outset.

### 126.4 The 2025 dystonia theory initially came from the patient, not the GP {#126-4-the-2025-dystonia-theory-initially-came-from-the-patient-not-the-gp .anchored-heading}

The GP appropriately did not present himself as able to adjudicate a specialist movement-disorders dispute and sought expert review.

### 126.5 The GP record does not prove what Neurology was required to do {#126-5-the-gp-record-does-not-prove-what-neurology-was-required-to-do .anchored-heading}

It shows what information was generated and returned. Clinical significance, standard of care and required response remain expert questions.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 127. Falsifiers and source gaps after the GP trial {#127-falsifiers-and-source-gaps-after-the-gp-trial .anchored-heading}

The following could materially change the current formulation:

1.  **Full-referral proof:** evidence that the treating neurologist demonstrably read the entire GP referral before structuring the first assessment and explicitly treated the integrated relationship as the primary investigative object.
2.  **Specialist reconstruction:** a later source showing that recurrent GP relationship signals were globally synthesised and the parent model re-derived.
3.  **Podiatry feedback:** evidence of detailed Podiatry-to-GP or Podiatry-to-Neurology communications not presently visible in the SAR that supplied an explicit structural/dynamic synthesis.
4.  **GP contrary state:** contemporaneous GP documentation stating that the foot problem was neurologically settled and wholly independent after specialist review.
5.  **2019 referral authorship/provenance issue:** evidence that the relational wording on the 2019 referral was not authored or adopted by the GP.

Until such evidence appears, the GP pathway should be treated as evidence of **relational persistence plus specialist authority constraint**.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 128. Protected formulation added by the GP trial {#128-protected-formulation-added-by-the-gp-trial .anchored-heading}

The following formulation should now be protected from drift unless contrary primary evidence requires revision:

> **The GP did not simply lose the original feet--gait--tone relationship after Neurology became involved. He repeatedly carried or regenerated it: first by seeking Neurology for gait, feet and muscle tension; then by prioritising Podiatry \"as Parkinson\'s\"; later by re-referring foot problems as related to increased tone and abnormal gait in very-young Parkinson\'s with clawing feet and pain; and in 2023 by explicitly reminding Neurology that prior pain had settled with better Parkinsonian control. By 2025, when the inherited diagnosis itself was contested, he did not claim authority to resolve the dispute but sought a Movement Disorders/dystonia specialist. The resulting information-flow problem is therefore not simply loss of relationship in primary care. It is a question of how relationship-bearing information repeatedly generated in primary care was adjudicated within a specialist architecture whose existing model remained authoritative.**

The shortest form is:

> **The GP kept seeing the arrows; specialist care owned the authority to decide what they meant.**

This sentence is a documentary synthesis, not a legal or clinical conclusion.

------------------------------------------------------------------------
:::

::: {#section-questions .section .reader-section .kind-audit section="50-question anti-drift inquiry"}
::: section-kicker
50-question anti-drift inquiry
:::

::: archive-note
**Audit archive.** This material is intentionally exhaustive. It is retained for reproducibility, falsification and source-checking; it is not the recommended linear reading path.
:::

## Part XXXI --- Anti-drift inquiry protocol: the 50 questions the record must answer {#part-xxxi-anti-drift-inquiry-protocol-the-50-questions-the-record-must-answer .source-h1 .anchored-heading}

## 101. The impulse behind these questions {#101-the-impulse-behind-these-questions .anchored-heading}

These questions are not a new case theory. They are a **stress-testing instrument for the existing theory**.

The strongest current formulation already has a defined centre of gravity, now refined by the GP information-state trial:

> **integrated GP trajectory → specialist problem compression / selective reconstruction → high-resolution Parkinsonian-genetic construction → operational partition of unresolved lower-limb material → documentary inheritance → repeated GP re-integration of feet/gait/tone/pain relationships → specialist local updating → no presently identified equivalent global specialist reconstruction → accumulated continuity → retrospective authority**

The GP trial changes one important assumption: **primary care did not simply lose the arrows after specialist involvement.** The record now shows the GP repeatedly preserving or regenerating relationships among feet, gait, tone, pain and Parkinsonian control and returning those relationships to specialist services. The revised questions therefore ask increasingly about **adjudicative authority, specialist response, feedback and reconstruction**, rather than merely whether the relationship survived downstream.

The danger at this stage is not lack of material. It is **drift**: allowing each new document, striking phrase, later diagnosis, or treatment response to pull the case toward a different and weaker explanation.

The questions below therefore arise from ten specific reasoning impulses.

### Impulse 1 --- Protect the original clinical object {#impulse-1-protect-the-original-clinical-object .anchored-heading}

Before asking whether later decisions were right or wrong, establish exactly what clinical problem was presented at the threshold. The current theory depends on a difference between the **GP\'s integrated trajectory** and the **PRECIS-shaped neurological task**. The first questions therefore test what information actually reached the neurologist and what representation governed the encounter.

### Impulse 2 --- Find the bridges, not merely the endpoints {#impulse-2-find-the-bridges-not-merely-the-endpoints .anchored-heading}

The record contains many endpoints: Parkinsonism, Podiatry, possible neuropathy, PRKN suspicion, iPD language, OFF dystonia, neuropathic pain terminology. The weakness or strength of the case lies in the **evidential bridges** between them. Every major transition must therefore be asked: what evidence earned this move?

### Impulse 3 --- Separate examination from synthesis {#impulse-3-separate-examination-from-synthesis .anchored-heading}

A sophisticated discharge synthesis cannot be allowed to backfill the bedside record. The questions must preserve the distinction between what was **observed**, what was **reported**, what was **interpreted**, and what was later **constructed**.

### Impulse 4 --- Track ownership of unresolved relationships {#impulse-4-track-ownership-of-unresolved-relationships .anchored-heading}

A problem can be divided among appropriate services without the relationship among those services being owned. The questions therefore ask not only "who treated what?" but "who remained responsible for the arrow between them?"

### Impulse 5 --- Identify reopening architecture {#impulse-5-identify-reopening-architecture .anchored-heading}

Uncertainty is not itself a defect. The key question is whether unresolved material had a route back into active inquiry. The record must be searched for **review dates, triggers, thresholds, feedback loops, and explicit return conditions**.

### Impulse 6 --- Distinguish local updating from global reconstruction {#impulse-6-distinguish-local-updating-from-global-reconstruction .anchored-heading}

The chronic pathway contains real reasoning. That must be preserved. The question is whether each local correction remained local or whether accumulated corrections ever caused the parent model to be rebuilt.

### Impulse 7 --- Use the clinician as his own control {#impulse-7-use-the-clinician-as-his-own-control .anchored-heading}

The 2019 shoulder episode and the 2020 foot-throw show that the same clinician could reason explicitly through competing propositions and discriminators. The questions use those episodes as controls for **capacity and reasoning form**, not as claims that every clinical problem should look identical.

### Impulse 8 --- Track semantic migration {#impulse-8-track-semantic-migration .anchored-heading}

Words such as "perhaps," "neuropathy," "neuropathic," "idiopathic," "belief and understanding," "removed," and "profound relief" carry different epistemic force. The record must be asked where uncertainty moved, where categories hardened, and whether stronger language was earned by stronger evidence.

### Impulse 9 --- Preserve contrary evidence and falsifiers {#impulse-9-preserve-contrary-evidence-and-falsifiers .anchored-heading}

The theory should become stronger by surviving hostile testing, not by excluding inconvenient sources. Several questions below are designed specifically to **weaken or defeat** the current formulation if the record contains a stronger integrative source than has yet been identified.

### Impulse 10 --- Keep 2026 last {#impulse-10-keep-2026-last .anchored-heading}

The PRKN result is an endpoint to a historical question, not a master key. Questions about 2017--2025 should first be answered using the information available at those times. Only then should 2026 be used to show what ultimately happened to the question the record had already generated.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 102. Questions 1--5 --- Which problem representation actually governed the specialist encounter? {#102-questions-15-which-problem-representation-actually-governed-the-specialist-encounter .anchored-heading}

**GP-trial effect:** The GP side is no longer uncertain: before admission he explicitly linked gait, feet and "muscle tension," and the urgent referral preserved feet → gait → tone → toe-clawing → dexterity/function → deterioration under an open diagnostic question. What remains unresolved is what **specialist problem representation** governed the first neurological investigation.

### Q1. What exact referral material was physically or electronically available to the treating neurologist before his first substantive assessment? {#q1-what-exact-referral-material-was-physically-or-electronically-available-to-the-treating-neurologist-before-his-first-substantive-assessment .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** The counterfactual still depends on whether the full GP referral was absent, inaccessible, merely not foregrounded, or actually available to the neurologist.

**Current status:** **Open / partially bounded.** The canonical record proves GP direct referral and reproduces the hospital PRECIS in the Neurology letter, but does not yet prove whether the full GP referral was read by the treating neurologist before assessment.

**What could change:** Strengthen, qualify, or falsify the "different investigative object" counterfactual.

### Q2. Of the materials available, which representation demonstrably governed the neurologist\'s initial task: the full GP trajectory, the hospital PRECIS, or an independently reconstructed problem? {#q2-of-the-materials-available-which-representation-demonstrably-governed-the-neurologist-s-initial-task-the-full-gp-trajectory-the-hospital-precis-or-an-independently-reconstructed-problem .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** Availability is not use. The GP trial now makes the distinction sharper because the GP\'s own problem representation is established and materially broader than the PRECIS.

**Current status:** **Open.** The specialist letter explicitly reproduces the PRECIS-shaped referral task, while the subsequent history independently recovers much of the fuller story.

**What could change:** Determine whether the key mechanism is **entry compression**, **later specialist repartition**, or both.

### Q3. Which elements of the GP\'s integrated object did Neurology independently recover during history and examination, and which relationships among those elements were not reconstructed with equivalent force? {#q3-which-elements-of-the-gp-s-integrated-object-did-neurology-independently-recover-during-history-and-examination-and-which-relationships-among-those-elements-were-not-reconstructed-with-equivalent-force .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** The record already disproves a simple "facts omitted and never seen" theory. The question is now explicitly **facts versus relationships**.

**Current status:** **Partly answered.** Feet, structural abnormalities, gait, deterioration and dystonic-looking lower-limb posture substantially re-entered; the unresolved issue is the degree of relational reconstruction.

**What could change:** Refine the mechanism from "loss of information" to "uneven reconstruction of relationships."

### Q4. At what exact point did the specialist record change the object from "what explains this integrated deteriorating presentation?" to more compartmental questions such as Parkinsonism, structural feet, and possible peripheral symptoms? {#q4-at-what-exact-point-did-the-specialist-record-change-the-object-from-what-explains-this-integrated-deteriorating-presentation-to-more-compartmental-questions-such-as-parkinsonism-structural-feet-and-possible-peripheral-symptoms .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** This locates the **partition event** rather than assuming it occurred at referral.

**Current status:** **Open.** The evidence suggests a progressive transformation rather than a single sentence doing all the work.

**What could change:** Identify whether the decisive transition occurred at PRECIS, examination, impression, discharge synthesis, or only when the plan became operational.

### Q5. What information did the GP receive back from Neurology, and what did that return communication leave him entitled or expected to regard as neurologically adjudicated versus still unresolved? {#q5-what-information-did-the-gp-receive-back-from-neurology-and-what-did-that-return-communication-leave-him-entitled-or-expected-to-regard-as-neurologically-adjudicated-versus-still-unresolved .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** The GP trial has shown that he continued to preserve the relationship later. The question is no longer simply whether he "lost" the integrated object, but what the specialist return reasonably communicated about the status of that object.

**Current status:** **Partly answered.** The GP recorded extensive Neurology correspondence and adopted Parkinsonism as the operative diagnosis, yet his later referrals continued to reconnect feet with tone/gait/Parkinsonian state.

**What could change:** Test the narrower **specialist-authority / apparent-resolution** hypothesis without asserting that the GP believed the feet had been cleared.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 103. Questions 6--10 --- What did the 2017 examination actually discriminate? {#103-questions-610-what-did-the-2017-examination-actually-discriminate .anchored-heading}

### Q6. What lower-limb findings in 2017 were direct observations, what were patient-reported history, and what were immediate interpretations? {#q6-what-lower-limb-findings-in-2017-were-direct-observations-what-were-patient-reported-history-and-what-were-immediate-interpretations .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** "Dystonic posture," "footdrop appearance," structural deformity, burning pain and toe clawing do not all have the same evidential status.

**What could change:** Prevent retrospective blending of history, examination and synthesis.

### Q7. Was distal power specifically tested in 2017, including dorsiflexion, plantarflexion, inversion and eversion, and if so where is it documented? {#q7-was-distal-power-specifically-tested-in-2017-including-dorsiflexion-plantarflexion-inversion-and-eversion-and-if-so-where-is-it-documented .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** The 2020 foot-throw episode shows the same clinician later used exactly these discriminators. Their presence or absence in 2017 is therefore an unusually strong internal comparator.

**What could change:** Directly affect the synthesis--examination differential.

### Q8. Was there a mapped sensory examination of the painful feet in 2017, including modality and distribution? {#q8-was-there-a-mapped-sensory-examination-of-the-painful-feet-in-2017-including-modality-and-distribution .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** A peripheral/neuropathic explanation has a different evidential bridge if supported by documented sensory findings than if based mainly on burning quality, history and treatment response.

**What could change:** Strengthen or weaken the proposition that the peripheral classification outran the documented distal examination.

### Q9. Was dynamic toe posture examined at rest, in bed, on standing, during gait, under loading, or after repeated movement? {#q9-was-dynamic-toe-posture-examined-at-rest-in-bed-on-standing-during-gait-under-loading-or-after-repeated-movement .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** The GP\'s "claws his toes" observation and Neurology\'s dystonic-looking gait raise a dynamic-versus-fixed question. Hammertoes alone do not answer it.

**What could change:** Clarify whether the structural and dystonic propositions were actually placed against one another.

### Q10. Was pain examined relationally---location, provocation, pressure sites, footwear, loading, gait phase, or reproduction during examination? {#q10-was-pain-examined-relationallylocation-provocation-pressure-sites-footwear-loading-gait-phase-or-reproduction-during-examination .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** The case concerns the relationship between structural disease, dynamic posture and pain, not merely the existence of pain.

**What could change:** A detailed relational pain examination would materially weaken the current claim of lower distal relational resolution.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 104. Questions 11--15 --- What earned the first partition? {#104-questions-1115-what-earned-the-first-partition .anchored-heading}

### Q11. What exact positive evidence supported the statement that the foot/leg material "may have been completely separate"? {#q11-what-exact-positive-evidence-supported-the-statement-that-the-foot-leg-material-may-have-been-completely-separate .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** "Separate" is not merely uncertainty; it is one candidate relationship. The record should be asked what observations specifically favoured it.

**What could change:** Reveal whether separation was grounded in patient phenomenology, examination, chronology, structural findings, or another discriminator.

### Q12. What exact evidence supported "perhaps a secondary peripheral neuropathy"? {#q12-what-exact-evidence-supported-perhaps-a-secondary-peripheral-neuropathy .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** Burning symptoms and historical alcohol exposure may be compatible with neuropathy, but compatibility is not the same as discrimination.

**What could change:** Establish whether the neuropathy branch had a clear contemporaneous evidential bridge.

### Q13. What evidence at the same time pointed against separation or against peripheral neuropathy? {#q13-what-evidence-at-the-same-time-pointed-against-separation-or-against-peripheral-neuropathy .anchored-heading}

Examples might include the dystonic-looking gait, toe phenomena, treatment-state behaviour, symmetry, abruptness, or absence of sensory signs.

**Why this is asked:** A fair analysis needs the full evidential balance, not only evidence supporting the branch that survived.

**What could change:** Show whether the 2017 formulation was genuinely balanced or whether one branch acquired operational priority without comparable discrimination.

### Q14. Did the clinician state what would have made him abandon or downgrade the "separate/peripheral" hypothesis? {#q14-did-the-clinician-state-what-would-have-made-him-abandon-or-downgrade-the-separate-peripheral-hypothesis .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** This is the earliest falsifiability question. A hypothesis can be appropriately provisional yet later become durable if no revision threshold is specified.

**What could change:** Identify whether the original uncertainty had an internal stopping rule.

### Q15. How much causal weight did the specialist place on the patient\'s statement that leg tension felt distinct from corns/callouses/foot pain, especially against the GP\'s continuing integrated framing and Neurology\'s own dystonic-looking gait observation? {#q15-how-much-causal-weight-did-the-specialist-place-on-the-patient-s-statement-that-leg-tension-felt-distinct-from-corns-callouses-foot-pain-especially-against-the-gp-s-continuing-integrated-framing-and-neurology-s-own-dystonic-looking-gait-observation .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** The patient\'s distinction remains important contrary evidence, but the GP trial now supplies an independent longitudinal comparator: primary care later continued to link feet with Parkinsonian tone/gait. The question is therefore not whether the patient\'s phenomenology existed, but how far it legitimately carried the specialist inference toward separation.

**What could change:** Prevent overstatement in either direction: neither dismissing patient phenomenology nor allowing it to function silently as biological proof of independence.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 105. Questions 16--20 --- What happened to the dystonic-looking lower limb? {#105-questions-1620-what-happened-to-the-dystonic-looking-lower-limb .anchored-heading}

### Q16. Where is the phrase "somewhat suggestive of a dystonic posture --- NOT FOOT DROP" next referred to, if anywhere? {#q16-where-is-the-phrase-somewhat-suggestive-of-a-dystonic-posture-not-foot-drop-next-referred-to-if-anywhere .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** This traces whether a potentially important observation remained active or became archival.

**What could change:** Establish the persistence or disappearance of the sign as a live proposition.

### Q17. How does the "almost dystonically unusual" walk in the DAT request relate textually and temporally to the autosomal-recessive suspicion? {#q17-how-does-the-almost-dystonically-unusual-walk-in-the-dat-request-relate-textually-and-temporally-to-the-autosomal-recessive-suspicion .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** The sign did not simply vanish. It appears to contribute at least to the broader atypical/recessive impression. Exact chronology matters.

**What could change:** Prevent the case from overstating exclusion of dystonia from genetic reasoning.

### Q18. Is there any note explaining why dystonia was not included in the explicit enumerated PRKN/PARK2 feature list? {#q18-is-there-any-note-explaining-why-dystonia-was-not-included-in-the-explicit-enumerated-prkn-park2-feature-list .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** Omission may be entirely reasonable if the sign was judged nonspecific. The record should be asked whether that judgment was made explicitly.

**What could change:** A reasoned explanation would weaken the documentary-asymmetry inference.

### Q19. Was the lower-limb dystonic appearance ever linked to the patient\'s toe posture, painful feet, loading or structural deformity? {#q19-was-the-lower-limb-dystonic-appearance-ever-linked-to-the-patient-s-toe-posture-painful-feet-loading-or-structural-deformity .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** Recognition of dystonia is not the same as relational integration.

**What could change:** Directly test whether the missing "join" actually exists elsewhere in the record.

### Q20. Did any later clinician---especially a Movement Disorders specialist---reconstruct the 2017 dystonic-looking lower limb in light of subsequent phenotype evolution? {#q20-did-any-later-clinicianespecially-a-movement-disorders-specialistreconstruct-the-2017-dystonic-looking-lower-limb-in-light-of-subsequent-phenotype-evolution .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** A later specialist reconstruction could perform the global operation currently thought absent.

**What could change:** Strong potential falsifier of the "no clearly identifiable global reconstruction" proposition.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 106. Questions 21--25 --- What exactly did "As per above" make operational? {#106-questions-2125-what-exactly-did-as-per-above-make-operational .anchored-heading}

### Q21. What propositions sat immediately above `CLIN-001613–001614`, and which were settled, probable, possible, or expressly unresolved? {#q21-what-propositions-sat-immediately-above-clin-001613001614-and-which-were-settled-probable-possible-or-expressly-unresolved .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** "As per above" can only be interpreted by identifying the epistemic status of what it incorporates.

**What could change:** Clarify whether the plan inherited uncertainty explicitly or merely inherited decisions built around it.

### Q22. Which service destinations were assigned, and what unresolved relational question was communicated to each destination and back to the GP? {#q22-which-service-destinations-were-assigned-and-what-unresolved-relational-question-was-communicated-to-each-destination-and-back-to-the-gp .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** Destination and handover content are different. The GP trial shows that primary care later continued to carry the neurological relationship into Podiatry referrals, so the issue is now whether specialist handoff explicitly preserved that relationship or whether the GP regenerated it independently.

**What could change:** Distinguish simple service allocation from an integrated handover architecture.

### Q23. After discharge, which clinician or service possessed the **adjudicative authority** to decide what the feet--gait--tone--pain relationship meant when the GP continued to notice and transmit those relationships? {#q23-after-discharge-which-clinician-or-service-possessed-the-adjudicative-authority-to-decide-what-the-feetgaittonepain-relationship-meant-when-the-gp-continued-to-notice-and-transmit-those-relationships .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** The GP trial materially changes the old "who owned the arrows?" question. The GP demonstrably kept seeing arrows. The sharper question is who had the specialist authority and responsibility to adjudicate them.

**Current status:** **Reframed, not answered.** Primary care repeatedly transmitted relationship-bearing information; the specialist owner of global integration is still not clearly identified in the surviving record.

**What could change:** A clearly named integrative specialist owner would weaken the current authority/ownership formulation.

### Q24. What formal or informal feedback loop existed for GP and Podiatry observations to return to Neurology as evidence capable of changing the specialist model? {#q24-what-formal-or-informal-feedback-loop-existed-for-gp-and-podiatry-observations-to-return-to-neurology-as-evidence-capable-of-changing-the-specialist-model .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** The 2019 GP Podiatry re-referral explicitly reconnects feet with increased tone, abnormal gait and Parkinson\'s. That proves relationship information survived in primary care. The unanswered question is how such information was supposed to reach and alter specialist reasoning.

**What could change:** Strongly test whether the apparent partition had a genuine cross-service re-entry mechanism.

### Q25. What recurrent observation from the GP, Podiatry, patient, or treatment state would have been sufficient to trigger **global specialist reconstruction**, rather than another local branch response? {#q25-what-recurrent-observation-from-the-gp-podiatry-patient-or-treatment-state-would-have-been-sufficient-to-trigger-global-specialist-reconstruction-rather-than-another-local-branch-response .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** The record now contains several relationship-bearing returns from primary care. The key issue is no longer whether uncertainty could re-enter the system at all, but what threshold would make the inherited specialist model itself decision-live again.

**What could change:** A defined specialist trigger would materially strengthen the defence that uncertainty was actively governed rather than merely accommodated.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 107. Questions 26--30 --- How was PRKN uncertainty managed after 2017? {#107-questions-2630-how-was-prkn-uncertainty-managed-after-2017 .anchored-heading}

### Q26. What exactly did the 2018 phrase "no great loss in 'not knowing' currently right now" apply to? {#q26-what-exactly-did-the-2018-phrase-no-great-loss-in-not-knowing-currently-right-now-apply-to .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** It may refer narrowly to immediate medication management or more broadly to the value of molecular diagnosis. The scope matters.

**What could change:** Define the true reasoning behind deferral without retrospectively expanding it.

### Q27. Was any future date, clinical trigger, family event, treatment development, or technology milestone specified for reconsidering PRKN testing? {#q27-was-any-future-date-clinical-trigger-family-event-treatment-development-or-technology-milestone-specified-for-reconsidering-prkn-testing .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** Deferral with a reopening plan is different from open-ended deferral.

**What could change:** Directly test the "uncertainty without reopening architecture" proposition.

### Q28. After 2018, where does PRKN/PARK2 remain visible in the treating neurologist\'s active reasoning rather than merely in copied history? {#q28-after-2018-where-does-prkn-park2-remain-visible-in-the-treating-neurologist-s-active-reasoning-rather-than-merely-in-copied-history .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** Persistence in the archive is not the same as persistence as an operative hypothesis.

**What could change:** Establish when, if ever, the specific PRKN question lost operational salience.

### Q29. Is there any pre-2025 source explicitly downgrading, rejecting, or closing PRKN/PARK2? {#q29-is-there-any-pre-2025-source-explicitly-downgrading-rejecting-or-closing-prkn-park2 .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** This is the single strongest potential falsifier of the "missing etiological bridge" formulation.

**What could change:** A reasoned closure source would substantially alter the genetics narrative.

### Q30. When "iPD" or "idiopathic Parkinson\'s disease" is used, is it clinical shorthand, a contrastive etiological category, or an explicit statement that monogenic disease has become unlikely? {#q30-when-ipd-or-idiopathic-parkinson-s-disease-is-used-is-it-clinical-shorthand-a-contrastive-etiological-category-or-an-explicit-statement-that-monogenic-disease-has-become-unlikely .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** The author\'s use of "idiopathic" appears elastic. Each occurrence needs provenance and function, not dictionary assumptions.

**What could change:** Prevent overstatement that later iPD wording necessarily equals formal rejection of PRKN.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 108. Questions 31--35 --- What did later boundary-crossing evidence do to the model? {#108-questions-3135-what-did-later-boundary-crossing-evidence-do-to-the-model .anchored-heading}

### Q31. When procyclidine withdrawal produced renewed foot/tension/gait phenomena, did the clinician treat that as evidence about the original partition or only as a medication-management observation? {#q31-when-procyclidine-withdrawal-produced-renewed-foot-tension-gait-phenomena-did-the-clinician-treat-that-as-evidence-about-the-original-partition-or-only-as-a-medication-management-observation .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** This is an early natural perturbation capable of testing whether separated phenomena move together.

**What could change:** Clarify whether treatment-state information propagated to the parent model.

### Q32. When the GP re-referred to Podiatry in 2019 stating that the feet issues related to increased tone and abnormal gait from Parkinson\'s, and describing very-young-onset Parkinson\'s with "clawing feet" and pain, what did specialist services do with that re-integrated formulation? {#q32-when-the-gp-re-referred-to-podiatry-in-2019-stating-that-the-feet-issues-related-to-increased-tone-and-abnormal-gait-from-parkinson-s-and-describing-very-young-onset-parkinson-s-with-clawing-feet-and-pain-what-did-specialist-services-do-with-that-re-integrated-formulation .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** This is a major result of the GP trial. The GP did not merely maintain a structural-foot destination; he explicitly reconnected the feet to the neurological syndrome.

**Current status:** **GP side answered; specialist-response side open.**

**What could change:** Determine whether the relationship was merely visible downstream or actually fed back into neurological adjudication.

### Q33. When the 2020 foot-throw was declared "actually parkinsonian" because distal power was normal, did that reclassification prompt reconsideration of the earlier dystonic-looking footdrop-like gait or the GP\'s recurring feet--gait relationship? {#q33-when-the-2020-foot-throw-was-declared-actually-parkinsonian-because-distal-power-was-normal-did-that-reclassification-prompt-reconsideration-of-the-earlier-dystonic-looking-footdrop-like-gait-or-the-gp-s-recurring-feetgait-relationship .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** This is a near-anatomical repeat and a strong example of specialist proposition-testing. It could have propagated backwards into the historical model.

**What could change:** Strong test of local update versus backward reconstruction.

### Q34. When the GP\'s urgent 2023 referral reported severe burning feet/lower-leg pain, recalled that earlier pain had settled with better Parkinson\'s control, stated "I am worried about him," and sought urgent specialist review, how did Neurology treat that relationship-bearing referral? {#q34-when-the-gp-s-urgent-2023-referral-reported-severe-burning-feet-lower-leg-pain-recalled-that-earlier-pain-had-settled-with-better-parkinson-s-control-stated-i-am-worried-about-him-and-sought-urgent-specialist-review-how-did-neurology-treat-that-relationship-bearing-referral .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** The GP trial shows that the 2023 referral itself is a top-level re-integration signal, not merely a request to treat established neuropathic pain.

**Current status:** **Partly answered.** Neurology later performed substantial local reconsideration---OFF phenomena, dystonia, neuropathy timing, Fabry, levodopa state---but the scope of reconstruction remains the question.

**What could change:** Clarify whether specialist care answered the relationship the GP returned or mainly decomposed it again into local mechanisms.

### Q35. When OFF dystonia, OFF-related walking, abrupt onset against neuropathy, Fabry, levodopa response and the GP\'s Parkinsonian-pain linkage were all simultaneously available in 2023, did any specialist note explicitly rebuild the original parent model? {#q35-when-off-dystonia-off-related-walking-abrupt-onset-against-neuropathy-fabry-levodopa-response-and-the-gp-s-parkinsonian-pain-linkage-were-all-simultaneously-available-in-2023-did-any-specialist-note-explicitly-rebuild-the-original-parent-model .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** 2023 is now not merely a counterexample to rigid inheritance; it is a convergence point where **primary-care re-integration and specialist local re-analysis coincide**.

**What could change:** If a genuine global reconstruction exists here, the current central formulation must be narrowed materially.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 109. Questions 36--40 --- How did neuropathic language acquire stability? {#109-questions-3640-how-did-neuropathic-language-acquire-stability .anchored-heading}

### Q36. What is the complete chronological and **source-specific** sequence of the words "peripheral neuropathy," "neuropathic disorder," "neuropathic symptoms," "? neuropathic pain," "neuropathic pain," and "peripheral neuropathic pain symptomatologies"? {#q36-what-is-the-complete-chronological-and-source-specific-sequence-of-the-words-peripheral-neuropathy-neuropathic-disorder-neuropathic-symptoms-neuropathic-pain-neuropathic-pain-and-peripheral-neuropathic-pain-symptomatologies .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** These are not synonyms, and the GP trial now shows that different services did not necessarily carry the same classification. The genealogy must therefore identify **who is speaking**, not merely when the term appears.

**What could change:** Establish whether classificatory hardening is real, gradual, discontinuous, or only apparent.

### Q37. At each strengthening of neuropathic language, what new discriminating evidence had appeared, and did that evidence come from Neurology, GP, Podiatry, patient report, treatment response, or simple inheritance of terminology? {#q37-at-each-strengthening-of-neuropathic-language-what-new-discriminating-evidence-had-appeared-and-did-that-evidence-come-from-neurology-gp-podiatry-patient-report-treatment-response-or-simple-inheritance-of-terminology .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** The issue is not whether the category was plausible but whether stronger grammatical certainty was earned by stronger evidence **within the same evidential chain**, rather than by cross-service repetition.

**What could change:** Directly test the "evidential bridge" behind semantic migration.

### Q38.
