Did pregabalin response ever function in the record as proof of neuropathic mechanism, or only as evidence of therapeutic usefulness? {#q38-did-pregabalin-response-ever-function-in-the-record-as-proof-of-neuropathic-mechanism-or-only-as-evidence-of-therapeutic-usefulness .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** The same neurologist explicitly treated gabapentin response as non-diagnostic in the shoulder case.

**What could change:** A careful non-specific interpretation would strengthen the defence against claims of circularity.

### Q39. What did the principal contrary evidence do to confidence in the neuropathic classification: severe recurrent pain despite 600 mg/day pregabalin, the 2019 GP/Podiatry form\'s "No neuropathy" tick (with its documentary limitations), abrupt onset argued against neuropathy in 2023, and repeated Parkinsonian treatment-state effects? {#q39-what-did-the-principal-contrary-evidence-do-to-confidence-in-the-neuropathic-classification-severe-recurrent-pain-despite-600-mg-day-pregabalin-the-2019-gp-podiatry-form-s-no-neuropathy-tick-with-its-documentary-limitations-abrupt-onset-argued-against-neuropathy-in-2023-and-repeated-parkinsonian-treatment-state-effects .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** No single item disproves neuropathic pain. The question is whether the category demonstrates a visible response to cumulative contrary evidence across services.

**What could change:** Test whether the category had a meaningful falsification threshold.

### Q40. By 2025, when disease-level neuropathy remains conditional but "peripheral neuropathic pain symptomatologies" is affirmative, is the clinician distinguishing phenotype from disease, or has uncertainty simply migrated downstream? {#q40-by-2025-when-disease-level-neuropathy-remains-conditional-but-peripheral-neuropathic-pain-symptomatologies-is-affirmative-is-the-clinician-distinguishing-phenotype-from-disease-or-has-uncertainty-simply-migrated-downstream .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** This is the core linguistic question in `CLIN-003240`.

**What could change:** Refine whether the best formulation is benign phenotype/etiology separation, classificatory hardening, or both.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 110. Questions 41--45 --- What does the same-author comparator really establish? {#110-questions-4145-what-does-the-same-author-comparator-really-establish .anchored-heading}

### Q41. Are all key shoulder passages unquestionably authored by the treating neurologist, contemporaneous, and not copied from another clinician? {#q41-are-all-key-shoulder-passages-unquestionably-authored-by-the-treating-neurologist-contemporaneous-and-not-copied-from-another-clinician .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** The comparator only works if authorship and provenance are secure.

**What could change:** This is a direct falsifier of the same-author control if provenance fails.

### Q42. Does the shoulder record contain a complete test → result → revision loop, or only question → discrimination → planned testing? {#q42-does-the-shoulder-record-contain-a-complete-test-result-revision-loop-or-only-question-discrimination-planned-testing .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** Controlled GPT outputs have differed on whether final imaging-mediated closure is present.

**What could change:** Determine how strongly the comparator can be stated without overclaiming.

### Q43. Which exact reasoning moves in the shoulder episode have a true analogue in the feet pathway, and which do not? {#q43-which-exact-reasoning-moves-in-the-shoulder-episode-have-a-true-analogue-in-the-feet-pathway-and-which-do-not .anchored-heading}

Examples: explicit alternatives, expected findings, non-discriminating treatment response, negative-test limitations, revision thresholds.

**Why this is asked:** The comparator should identify a difference in reasoning form, not merely note length.

**What could change:** Make the comparison more precise and less vulnerable to "apples versus oranges" criticism.

### Q44. Is the shoulder difference better explained by information state, or by the shoulder being acute, focal and anatomically testable? {#q44-is-the-shoulder-difference-better-explained-by-information-state-or-by-the-shoulder-being-acute-focal-and-anatomically-testable .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** This is the strongest benign alternative explanation.

**What could change:** The answer may reduce the strength of the path-dependence inference without eliminating it.

### Q45. Does the 2020 foot-throw or 2023 review show that the clinician could deploy shoulder-like proposition-testing within the inherited pathway when a sufficiently specific question became live? {#q45-does-the-2020-foot-throw-or-2023-review-show-that-the-clinician-could-deploy-shoulder-like-proposition-testing-within-the-inherited-pathway-when-a-sufficiently-specific-question-became-live .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** The answer is probably yes, and that matters. It shifts the theory from "inheritance suppressed reasoning" to "inheritance shaped the scope at which reasoning occurred."

**What could change:** Protect the strongest formulation from drifting into an unsustainable incapacity narrative.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 111. Questions 46--50 --- Did local revisions ever become global reconstruction? {#111-questions-4650-did-local-revisions-ever-become-global-reconstruction .anchored-heading}

### Q46. Is there anywhere from 2018 to 2025 a **specialist** note that gathers structural feet, dystonic posture, pain, gait, OFF state, possible neuropathy, treatment response and genetics into one renewed synthesis? {#q46-is-there-anywhere-from-2018-to-2025-a-specialist-note-that-gathers-structural-feet-dystonic-posture-pain-gait-off-state-possible-neuropathy-treatment-response-and-genetics-into-one-renewed-synthesis .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** The GP trial now establishes that primary care itself repeatedly re-integrated portions of the problem. The missing operation, if missing, is therefore specifically **specialist adjudicative reconstruction**, not generic recognition.

**What could change:** A genuine specialist integrative note would materially weaken the current central proposition.

### Q47. How did specialist services respond to the GP\'s repeated relationship-bearing returns in 2019, 2023 and 2025? {#q47-how-did-specialist-services-respond-to-the-gp-s-repeated-relationship-bearing-returns-in-2019-2023-and-2025 .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** These referrals form a longitudinal natural experiment. The GP repeatedly sends back links among feet, gait/tone, pain, Parkinsonian control and later dystonia. The record must show whether each return altered the specialist parent model, generated only a local response, or was not visibly integrated.

**What could change:** This is now one of the strongest tests of the **authority-to-adjudicate-the-arrows** formulation.

### Q48. Does the 2025 GP move toward external Movement Disorders/dystonia review represent the first clearly documented transfer of the top-level relationship question to a new specialist authority? {#q48-does-the-2025-gp-move-toward-external-movement-disorders-dystonia-review-represent-the-first-clearly-documented-transfer-of-the-top-level-relationship-question-to-a-new-specialist-authority .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** By 2025 the GP expressly recognises limits of his own scope and seeks specialist expertise rather than independently adjudicating dystonia/Botox or DAT-scan meaning. This may represent a genuine **global re-routing** of the unresolved problem.

**What could change:** Clarify whether 2025 is merely another referral or the first explicit change in who is being asked to own the whole explanatory problem.

### Q49. Does the 2025 complaint response reconstruct the genealogy of the challenged categories and answer the recurrent GP-generated relationship information, or primarily explain why management was reasonable from inside the inherited specialist categories? {#q49-does-the-2025-complaint-response-reconstruct-the-genealogy-of-the-challenged-categories-and-answer-the-recurrent-gp-generated-relationship-information-or-primarily-explain-why-management-was-reasonable-from-inside-the-inherited-specialist-categories .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** The complaint makes provenance the object of review, while the GP history supplies an independent longitudinal source showing that the relationships remained live outside the treating specialist\'s documentation.

**What could change:** Determine whether 2025 contains the global reconstruction otherwise missing from the clinical notes.

### Q50. Before introducing the 2026 result, what is the strongest formulation that survives the GP information-state trial, the specialist record, the Podiatry record, the shoulder control, every contrary source, and every identified falsifier? {#q50-before-introducing-the-2026-result-what-is-the-strongest-formulation-that-survives-the-gp-information-state-trial-the-specialist-record-the-podiatry-record-the-shoulder-control-every-contrary-source-and-every-identified-falsifier .anchored-heading}

**Why this is asked:** This remains the anti-drift capstone, but its expected answer has changed because the GP trial falsified a strong "downstream loss of the arrows" version of the theory.

**Current expected answer, subject to the preceding 49 questions:**

> **The record shows an integrated deteriorating presentation generated in primary care; a narrower specialist task representation followed by substantial factual recovery and high-resolution Parkinsonian/genetic construction; comparatively lower documented adjudication of the distal feet--gait--posture--pain relationship; and an operational partition that became portable. Primary care did not simply adopt complete separation: the GP repeatedly preserved or regenerated relationships among feet, gait, tone, pain and Parkinsonian control and returned those relationships to specialist services. Specialist care repeatedly performed genuine local reassessment, but the surviving record still more clearly documents proposition-specific updates than a single specialist reconstruction of the founding relationship. The key longitudinal question is therefore not whether the arrows were seen, but how the service with authority to adjudicate them processed recurrent relationship-bearing information and what threshold would have caused the inherited specialist model itself to be rebuilt.**

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## 112. Priority order after the GP information-state trial {#112-priority-order-after-the-gp-information-state-trial .anchored-heading}

The GP trial changes the order of attack. The highest-value questions are now those that test **specialist adjudication of repeatedly returned relationship information**.

### Priority A --- What governed the first specialist encounter? {#priority-a-what-governed-the-first-specialist-encounter .anchored-heading}

-   **Q1:** What did Neurology actually receive?
-   **Q2:** Which representation actually governed the initial task?
-   **Q4:** Where did the integrated object become partitioned?
-   **Q5:** What did the specialist return communicate to the GP as resolved versus unresolved?

These determine whether the initial mechanism is best described as entry compression, specialist repartition, or both.

### Priority B --- What earned the first specialist partition? {#priority-b-what-earned-the-first-specialist-partition .anchored-heading}

-   **Q7--Q10:** What distal discriminating examination was actually documented?
-   **Q11--Q15:** What positive, negative and patient-derived evidence earned separation/peripheral reasoning?

These remain central because the GP trial does not answer the specialist evidential bridge.

### Priority C --- Authority, feedback and reopening {#priority-c-authority-feedback-and-reopening .anchored-heading}

-   **Q22:** What relational question travelled with each destination?
-   **Q23:** Who had adjudicative authority over the arrows?
-   **Q24:** What feedback loop returned GP/Podiatry evidence to Neurology?
-   **Q25:** What would trigger global specialist reconstruction?

This is the largest substantive revision caused by the GP trial.

### Priority D --- Recurrent GP re-integration as a longitudinal test {#priority-d-recurrent-gp-re-integration-as-a-longitudinal-test .anchored-heading}

-   **Q32:** What happened to the 2019 GP re-linking of feet, tone, gait and Parkinson\'s?
-   **Q34:** What happened to the 2023 GP re-linking of severe pain with Parkinsonian control?
-   **Q47:** Across 2019, 2023 and 2025, how did specialist services respond to repeated relationship-bearing returns?
-   **Q48:** Did 2025 external Movement Disorders/dystonia referral finally transfer the top-level question to a new adjudicative authority?

These questions did not have the same evidential force before the GP corpus was opened.

### Priority E --- Global reconstruction and category genealogy {#priority-e-global-reconstruction-and-category-genealogy .anchored-heading}

-   **Q20 / Q35 / Q46 / Q49:** Is there a true specialist global reconstruction anywhere before the external pathway?
-   **Q29--Q30:** What happened to PRKN and what did "idiopathic" mean?
-   **Q36--Q40:** How did neuropathic language become stable across different speakers and services?

These remain the strongest potential falsifiers or refiners of the mature theory.

## 113. Anti-drift rule for answering the 50 questions {#113-anti-drift-rule-for-answering-the-50-questions .anchored-heading}

Each answer should be entered under four headings:

**Record fact** --- what the source directly establishes.\
**Documentary inference** --- what can cautiously be inferred about information structure or language.\
**Clinical expert question** --- what requires specialist judgment.\
**Effect on current formulation** --- strengthen / qualify / weaken / falsify / no material change.

No answer should be allowed to create a new major case theory unless it first explains why the current information-pathway formulation no longer accounts for the evidence.

The working case should only move when the record forces it to move.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

-   **Answered materially** --- the canonical record now supplies a reasonably clear documentary answer.
-   **Partly answered** --- an important part is established, but the decisive bridge remains open.
-   **Open** --- the present corpus does not yet answer the question.
-   **Expert-dependent** --- the documentary state is reasonably clear but its clinical significance requires specialist opinion.

The answers below are provisional documentary findings, not medical or legal conclusions. "Not identified" always means **not identified in the present canonical pass**, not proof that the event never occurred.

## 114.1 Questions now most worth attacking next {#114-1-questions-now-most-worth-attacking-next .anchored-heading}

The status pass changes what should be searched next. The highest-yield unresolved questions are now:

1.  **Q1--Q2:** prove what referral material Neurology actually possessed and used.
2.  **Q7--Q10:** exhaust the 2017 examination record for distal power, sensory mapping, dynamic toe posture and relational pain examination.
3.  **Q18--Q19:** find any explicit rationale for excluding/not using dystonia in the PRKN phenotype and any structural--dystonic join.
4.  **Q24--Q25:** identify any cross-service feedback rule or global-reconstruction trigger.
5.  **Q28--Q29:** exhaustively trace fresh PRKN reasoning after 2018 and any closure source.
6.  **Q32 / Q47:** follow the 2019 GP re-integration forward and determine whether Neurology ever received or acted on it.
7.  **Q35 / Q46:** perform a dedicated global-reconstruction search across 2023--2025.
8.  **Q36--Q37:** build the source-attributed neuropathic-language genealogy as a table rather than relying on selected examples.
9.  **Q41:** confirm formal authorship/provenance of the shoulder comparator to eliminate an avoidable challenge.
10. **Q48:** check whether any earlier Movement Disorders specialist referral predating 2025 performed the top-level integrative role.

These are now more valuable than repeatedly re-proving that the GP saw the feet--Parkinsonian relationship. That point is already materially established.

## Appendix H --- Canonical analytical checklist for every future source {#appendix-h-canonical-analytical-checklist-for-every-future-source .anchored-heading}

For every new source added to this project, ask the same questions. What is the exact proposition? Who is the source? Is it observation, patient report, interpretation, retrospective summary, copied history or independent specialist opinion? What prior information state does it inherit? What competing explanations are active? What evidence would discriminate them? Does the source change a local branch or the parent model? Does it preserve uncertainty explicitly? Does it convert uncertainty into a working category? Does it create a new owner, destination, review trigger or test? Does it rely on treatment response, and if so, does it treat that response as specific or non-specific? Does it refer back to older letters? Does it quote or paraphrase an earlier source? Does it add independent evidential support, or merely increase documentary persistence? Does it contain a statement that would falsify one of the project\'s working propositions? Finally, does it belong at the level of record fact, documentary inference, clinical expert question or legal conclusion?

This checklist is designed to keep the case falsifiable. The objective is not to accumulate material that agrees with the current theory. The objective is to determine whether the current theory survives contact with every later source, especially sources that show more integration, more specialist review, more explicit ownership or a clearer evidential bridge than has so far been identified.
:::

::: {#section-gp-protocol .section .reader-section .kind-audit section="Pre-registered GP audit protocol"}
::: section-kicker
Pre-registered GP audit protocol
:::

## Pre-registered sequential GP information-state audit --- protocol frozen before full GP/Podiatry source review {#pre-registered-sequential-gp-information-state-audit-protocol-frozen-before-full-gp-podiatry-source-review .source-h1 .anchored-heading}

## Purpose of this blind/staged trial {#purpose-of-this-blind-staged-trial .anchored-heading}

Before reviewing the full GP record, Podiatry SAR, 2019 Podiatry re-referral, 2023 crisis referral, and 2025 St Vincent\'s referral as a connected corpus, the object of this trial is being frozen.

This is **not** a trial of whether the GP was \"right\" or \"wrong.\" It is not a retrospective attempt to recruit the GP as support for the current Neurology critique. It is a prospective source-audit of a different information pathway:

> **What clinical object did the GP believe he was carrying at each point in time, what information did he receive back from specialist services, what uncertainty remained live for him, and how did his subsequent referrals show that he operationalised that information?**

The GP is therefore treated as a longitudinal **information node** with his own changing documentary state, not merely as a source of symptoms before Neurology.

The key variable is not the GP\'s private mental state, which the record cannot directly prove. The object is his **documented and operational information state** as evidenced by:

-   what he records;
-   what he asks;
-   what he carries forward;
-   what he drops;
-   what he sends to another service;
-   which relationships he reconnects;
-   what he appears to treat as resolved, unresolved, structural, neurological, painful, urgent, or requiring another opinion;
-   and how those states change after receiving specialist correspondence.

The trial should therefore avoid phrases such as \"the GP thought\" unless directly supported. Prefer:

> **the GP record represents\...**

> **the referral treats X as\...**

> **the subsequent referral suggests that Y remained operationally live\...**

> **the record is consistent with the GP understanding the specialist response as\...**

where inference rather than direct statement is involved.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## Blindness limitation and protection against confirmation bias {#blindness-limitation-and-protection-against-confirmation-bias .anchored-heading}

This audit is not absolutely blind in the experimental sense. Before seeing the full source set, the analysis already knows selected quoted material from the 2017 GP referral and later themes from prior controlled exercises.

Accordingly, the protection against drift will be **pre-registration and sequential unblinding**, not a false claim of total ignorance.

The rules are:

1.  The hypotheses below are frozen before review of the full GP/Podiatry corpus.
2.  Each chronological stage is assessed using only information that would have been available to the GP by that stage.
3.  Later records must not be used to rewrite an earlier GP information state.
4.  The 2026 PRKN endpoint must not be introduced while assessing GP states before 2026.
5.  Neurology\'s later explanations must not be treated as facts known to the GP unless the record shows they were communicated.
6.  Podiatry findings must not be assumed to have reached the GP or Neurology unless transmission is documented.
7.  Silence must not be converted automatically into belief or agreement.
8.  A later re-referral may show persistence or reactivation of a question; it does not automatically prove that the same question remained continuously active in the GP\'s mind.
9.  Every major inference must identify the contemporaneous words or actions supporting it.
10. Contrary evidence must be recorded even when it weakens the current information-pathway theory.

The working case may move only if the source forces it to move.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## The five chronological information states to be reconstructed {#the-five-chronological-information-states-to-be-reconstructed .anchored-heading}

### GP-State 0 --- Pre-hospital / referral-generation state {#gp-state-0-pre-hospital-referral-generation-state .anchored-heading}

**Source window:** GP record up to and including the 2017 referral.

Questions:

-   What problem had accumulated before referral?
-   Did the GP represent the case as feet plus gait plus tone plus toe posture plus function, or as multiple parallel complaints?
-   What changed over time?
-   Which relationships did the GP himself make, and which were merely temporal coexistence?
-   What remained explicitly open in \"?parkinsonian or other underlying diagnosis\"?
-   Did the GP expect Neurology to explain the entire evolving presentation or only the Parkinsonian signs?

This state establishes the **outgoing information object** before hospital compression.

### GP-State 1 --- Post-Neurology discharge information state {#gp-state-1-post-neurology-discharge-information-state .anchored-heading}

**Source window:** first GP material after receipt of the 2017 Neurology/discharge correspondence, using only information then available.

Core question:

> **What did the specialist return to the GP, and what would a reasonable documentary reading of that return leave live or apparently settled?**

Specific points:

-   Did the discharge correspondence communicate that the feet had been clinically examined, or only that structural abnormalities were noted?
-   Did it communicate \"dystonic posture --- NOT FOOT DROP\" to the GP?
-   Did it communicate \"may have been completely separate,\" \"perhaps peripheral neuropathy,\" and \"Not clear\"?
-   Was Podiatry represented as a definitive destination or as one branch of an unresolved problem?
-   Was PRKN/PARK2 communicated, and at what level of specificity?
-   Was the GP given any explicit reopening rule for the feet--gait--tone relationship?
-   Was any responsibility assigned for integrating Podiatry findings back into Neurology?

### Critical sub-question: apparent specialist clearance {#critical-sub-question-apparent-specialist-clearance .anchored-heading}

One major issue is whether specialist correspondence could reasonably function, from the GP\'s information position, as an **implicit clearance signal** even though Neurology itself retained uncertainty.

The audit must ask:

> **If a GP sends an integrated feet--gait--tone presentation to Neurology and receives back a sophisticated young-onset Parkinsonian discharge synthesis stating that the residual foot material may be separate/peripheral and is \"Not clear,\" what does the subsequent GP record show about whether he treated the neurological relationship as adequately considered, still unresolved, or not his question to reopen?**

This must remain a documentary question. Whether a GP *should* reasonably infer that a movement-disorders-level distal examination had occurred is a clinical/general-practice expert question unless explicitly stated in the correspondence.

We must therefore distinguish:

1.  **what Neurology actually documented examining;**
2.  **what the discharge communication represented to the GP;**
3.  **what the GP\'s later conduct suggests he understood from it;**
4.  **what an expert GP could reasonably infer from such correspondence.**

These are different propositions.

### GP-State 2 --- 2019 Podiatry re-referral state {#gp-state-2-2019-podiatry-re-referral-state .anchored-heading}

**Source window:** GP record and Podiatry material available by the 2019 re-referral.

This is a major test of what survived the discharge.

Ask:

-   How does the GP describe the feet in 2019?
-   Does he describe them purely structurally?
-   Does he reconnect increased tone, abnormal gait, clawing, Parkinson\'s, or another neurological feature?
-   Does the wording suggest that the original integrated relationship remains alive despite the Neurology/Podiatry partition?
-   Is Podiatry being asked to manage a known structural destination, or is the referral itself carrying cross-boundary neurological information?
-   Does any Podiatry SAR material answer a question the GP had posed?
-   Does Podiatry feed anything back that could reasonably trigger Neurology reassessment?
-   If a \"No neuropathy\" field or equivalent appears, what exactly does it mean in that document, who authored it, and what is its evidential scope?

This state may provide an independent check on whether the original integrated object was actually extinguished or merely distributed.

### GP-State 3 --- 2023 crisis-referral state {#gp-state-3-2023-crisis-referral-state .anchored-heading}

**Source window:** GP record up to and including the 2023 urgent/crisis referral.

This is a stress test of the GP\'s longitudinal information state.

Ask:

-   How does the GP characterise the pain now?
-   Does he use inherited neuropathic terminology, his own phenomenological language, or both?
-   Does he reconnect pain with Parkinson\'s control or medication state?
-   Does he remember a prior episode improving when Parkinson\'s control improved?
-   Does he treat the problem as already explained but inadequately controlled, or as causally unresolved?
-   What does \"before we get into trouble here\" or equivalent language indicate about urgency and ownership?
-   Is the GP asking Neurology to manage pain, reassess mechanism, or both?
-   Does the referral transmit safeguarding/endurance information that later specialist notes do or do not carry forward?

This state tests whether severe recurrence caused the GP to regenerate an integrated question even if the specialist pathway remained compartmentalised.

### GP-State 4 --- 2025 St Vincent\'s referral state {#gp-state-4-2025-st-vincent-s-referral-state .anchored-heading}

**Source window:** GP material available at the point of the external referral in 2025, without using the 2026 result.

This state asks what the GP believed still required specialist resolution after years of care.

Questions:

-   Why is another specialist being sought?
-   What problem does the GP say remains unresolved?
-   Does the referral explicitly seek expertise in movement disorders and/or dystonia?
-   Does it reconnect the feet, gait, dystonia, pain, Parkinsonism, genetics, treatment state, or functional deterioration?
-   Does it carry forward the historical PRKN question?
-   Does it challenge, preserve, or bypass the inherited iPD/peripheral-neuropathic framework?
-   Is the referral framed as treatment optimisation, diagnostic second opinion, etiological clarification, movement-phenomenology review, or a mixture?

This state is especially important because it occurs **before** the eventual PRKN result and therefore cannot be dismissed as retrospective reconstruction from genetics.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## The Podiatry SAR as a parallel information stream {#the-podiatry-sar-as-a-parallel-information-stream .anchored-heading}

The Podiatry SAR should not be read merely for whether Podiatry \"got the diagnosis right.\"

It should be analysed as an information stream with its own object and limits:

-   what Podiatry was told;
-   what it observed;
-   what it treated;
-   what it regarded as structural/mechanical;
-   whether neurological descriptors entered its record;
-   whether it recorded clawing, gait, pressure distribution, deformity, neuropathy status, pain sites, or loading patterns;
-   whether findings were communicated back to the GP;
-   whether any findings were communicated back to Neurology;
-   whether the GP later reused Podiatry information in a neurologically integrated way.

The key information-flow question is:

> **Did Podiatry merely receive one branch of the problem, or did its local mechanical findings become evidence available for reintegration into the larger feet--gait--tone relationship?**

This directly tests the existing distinction between **destination** and **ownership of the arrows**.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## Pre-registered hypotheses for the GP pathway {#pre-registered-hypotheses-for-the-gp-pathway .anchored-heading}

The following are hypotheses to be tested, not conclusions to be confirmed.

### H-GP1 --- Integrated-origin hypothesis {#h-gp1-integrated-origin-hypothesis .anchored-heading}

The GP\'s initial information state represented the presentation as an integrated deterioration rather than as independent foot disease plus Parkinsonism.

**Would weaken if:** the full GP record shows longstanding explicit separation of foot disease from neurological change before referral.

### H-GP2 --- Specialist-return / apparent-clearance hypothesis {#h-gp2-specialist-return-apparent-clearance-hypothesis .anchored-heading}

After Neurology, the GP may have had documentary reason to treat the central Parkinsonian diagnosis as specialist-resolved while assuming that the feet had also undergone appropriate neurological consideration, even though the discharge retained \"Not clear.\"

**Would strengthen if:** later GP records defer to Neurology\'s partition without re-asking the neurological relationship despite persistent concern.

**Would weaken if:** the GP immediately records that Neurology had not resolved the feet, explicitly questions the discharge conclusion, or seeks movement-disorders reassessment.

**Clinical expert boundary:** whether such reliance would be reasonable is not for documentary analysis alone.

### H-GP3 --- Residual-question hypothesis {#h-gp3-residual-question-hypothesis .anchored-heading}

Despite specialist partition, some residue of the original integrated question may remain visible in the GP record and later referrals.

**Would strengthen if:** the GP repeatedly reconnects feet with tone, gait, clawing, Parkinson\'s control, or movement state.

**Would weaken if:** later GP records consistently treat the feet as purely structural or neuropathic without any cross-linkage.

### H-GP4 --- Re-integration under stress hypothesis {#h-gp4-re-integration-under-stress-hypothesis .anchored-heading}

When symptoms become severe, the GP may regenerate cross-boundary relationships that routine specialist documentation had allowed to remain compartmentalised.

**Would strengthen if:** the 2023 crisis referral reconnects pain with prior Parkinson\'s control, gait, medication state, or neurological review.

**Would weaken if:** the crisis referral treats the issue solely as established neuropathic pain requiring analgesic escalation.

### H-GP5 --- External-referral reconstruction hypothesis {#h-gp5-external-referral-reconstruction-hypothesis .anchored-heading}

By 2025, the GP may independently reconstruct enough of the unresolved movement/feet problem to seek a different specialist perspective before knowing the PRKN result.

**Would strengthen if:** the St Vincent\'s referral seeks movement-disorders/dystonia expertise and reassembles historically separated components.

**Would weaken if:** the referral is purely administrative, medication-focused, or unrelated to the unresolved lower-limb relationship.

### H-GP6 --- Information asymmetry hypothesis {#h-gp6-information-asymmetry-hypothesis .anchored-heading}

The GP may repeatedly possess cross-domain information that no single downstream specialty holds in the same integrated form.

**Would strengthen if:** GP notes combine Neurology, Podiatry, treatment response, functional deterioration, and patient experience in ways not mirrored in specialist letters.

**Would weaken if:** GP entries merely copy specialist classifications without adding or reconnecting information.

### H-GP7 --- Specialist-authority effect hypothesis {#h-gp7-specialist-authority-effect-hypothesis .anchored-heading}

The GP\'s later pathway may show deference to specialist classifications even where the specialist correspondence itself preserved uncertainty.

This is **not** a claim about inappropriate deference or psychological bias.

It asks whether the information architecture behaves as follows:

> **specialist uncertainty + management destination → GP operational reliance on the specialist arrangement**

**Would strengthen if:** specialist uncertainty is not restated in later GP records while the resulting destinations/categories are carried forward.

**Would weaken if:** the GP repeatedly preserves and actively interrogates the original uncertainty.

### H-GP8 --- GP as re-integration node hypothesis {#h-gp8-gp-as-re-integration-node-hypothesis .anchored-heading}

The GP may function as the service most capable of seeing the distributed whole because information from Neurology, Podiatry, patient reports, medication changes and functional decline returns to primary care.

**Would strengthen if:** later referrals recombine material originating in multiple services.

**Would weaken if:** the GP record becomes as compartmentalised as the specialty pathways.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## Observable markers: what to look for without mind-reading {#observable-markers-what-to-look-for-without-mind-reading .anchored-heading}

The audit should code specific documentary markers rather than intuiting the GP\'s psychology.

### Markers of an active unresolved relationship {#markers-of-an-active-unresolved-relationship .anchored-heading}

-   question marks;
-   \"? cause\" / \"unclear\" / \"not sure\";
-   requests for review rather than simple treatment;
-   explicit linkage of feet with gait, tone, clawing, Parkinson\'s control, or medication state;
-   re-referral after a specialist destination has already been established;
-   reference to prior improvement with neurological treatment;
-   requests for another specialist opinion;
-   language that asks what explains the symptom rather than only how to manage it.

### Markers of apparent closure or operational reliance {#markers-of-apparent-closure-or-operational-reliance .anchored-heading}

-   specialist diagnosis repeated without qualification;
-   foot problem described only under Podiatry/neuropathy headings;
-   absence of the earlier relational language while management destinations persist;
-   referrals framed purely as continuation of an established branch;
-   statements suggesting Neurology has already assessed/cleared the issue;
-   treatment escalation without renewed causal question.

### Markers of re-integration {#markers-of-re-integration .anchored-heading}

-   feet + gait in same sentence/problem statement;
-   feet + Parkinson\'s control;
-   feet + tone/stiffness;
-   toe clawing/curling + movement state;
-   structural findings + abnormal gait;
-   pain + dopaminergic timing;
-   referral explicitly asking a specialist to consider both movement disorder and distal symptoms.

### Markers of service-boundary information loss {#markers-of-service-boundary-information-loss .anchored-heading}

-   GP asks a relationship question, receiving service answers only one branch;
-   Podiatry observes a mechanically relevant fact that never appears in Neurology;
-   Neurology observes a dynamic phenomenon that never appears in Podiatry;
-   severe patient-reported functional/safeguarding information is compressed in onward communication;
-   a qualification such as \"Not clear\" disappears while the resulting category/destination remains.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## The GP-specific counterfactual to test {#the-gp-specific-counterfactual-to-test .anchored-heading}

One pre-registered counterfactual deserves special protection:

> **If the GP sent an integrated feet--gait--tone--toe--deterioration problem to hospital, and the hospital returned a sophisticated neurological synthesis that had itself seen the feet and called their relationship \"Not clear,\" the GP may reasonably have operated on the assumption that the relevant neurological relationship had received specialist consideration even if it had not been fully resolved.**

The record must decide whether that hypothesis has support.

The audit must **not** jump from that hypothesis to:

> the GP believed a Movement Disorders specialist had performed a comprehensive dystonia examination.

That would require evidence or expert opinion.

Instead ask:

-   What did the discharge look like from the GP\'s documentary position?
-   What level of specialist resolution did its sophistication imply?
-   Did the GP\'s later behaviour show reliance on that apparent resolution?
-   Did later GP referrals show that the unresolved relationship resurfaced despite that reliance?

This is potentially important because the case may contain **different information states at different nodes**:

> GP sends an integrated question → hospital compresses the task → Neurology develops a sophisticated central/genetic model and partitions residual feet uncertainty → GP receives the specialist product and may reasonably treat the specialist partition as having evidential weight → later primary-care observations repeatedly regenerate the integrated problem.

That chain is only a hypothesis until the GP record is examined.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## Sequential-unblinding method {#sequential-unblinding-method .anchored-heading}

When the full record is supplied, the analysis should proceed in this order and should write a short conclusion **before opening the next stage**.

### Pass 1 --- GP pre-referral record {#pass-1-gp-pre-referral-record .anchored-heading}

Output:

-   the GP\'s problem representation;
-   timeline of change;
-   relationships explicitly drawn;
-   open questions;
-   what the referral asks Neurology to solve.

Freeze conclusion.

### Pass 2 --- Neurology return as seen by GP {#pass-2-neurology-return-as-seen-by-gp .anchored-heading}

Output:

-   what new information the GP receives;
-   what appears resolved;
-   what remains explicitly unresolved;
-   what destinations are assigned;
-   what review/reopening rules are communicated.

Freeze conclusion.

### Pass 3 --- Podiatry SAR and 2019 re-referral {#pass-3-podiatry-sar-and-2019-re-referral .anchored-heading}

Output:

-   what structural/mechanical information enters primary care;
-   whether neurological relationships remain or reappear;
-   whether information crosses back toward Neurology.

Freeze conclusion.

### Pass 4 --- 2023 crisis referral {#pass-4-2023-crisis-referral .anchored-heading}

Output:

-   how the GP reconstructs the problem under severe pain/endurance pressure;
-   what historical relationships are remembered;
-   whether mechanism is treated as settled or reopened;
-   what exactly Neurology is being asked to do.

Freeze conclusion.

### Pass 5 --- 2025 St Vincent\'s referral {#pass-5-2025-st-vincent-s-referral .anchored-heading}

Output:

-   what remains unresolved before genetic confirmation;
-   what expertise is sought;
-   which historical components are recombined;
-   whether the GP independently moves toward a movement-disorders/dystonia reconstruction.

Freeze conclusion.

### Pass 6 --- Cross-time comparison {#pass-6-cross-time-comparison .anchored-heading}

Only after the five states are separately frozen should the analysis ask:

> **What changed in the GP\'s information state across the pathway?**

The final comparison should map:

> outgoing integrated object → specialist return → operational primary-care state → re-referral state → crisis state → external-specialist referral state.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## Relationship to the existing 50-question audit {#relationship-to-the-existing-50-question-audit .anchored-heading}

The existing 50 questions remain intact. This GP trial provides a new source stream capable of answering or challenging many of them, especially:

-   Q1--Q5: what reached Neurology and whether the integrated trajectory remained live;
-   Q11--Q14: what evidential bridge supported separation/peripheral interpretation;
-   Q23--Q25: ownership, Podiatry feedback and reopening triggers;
-   Q33--Q35: whether later lower-limb information fed back into the parent model;
-   Q46--Q49: whether any later service or complaint process globally reconstructed the case.

The GP corpus should also be allowed to generate **new questions** if it contains information that the current Neurology-centred formulation did not anticipate. New questions may be added, but the pre-registered hypotheses above must remain visible so that later insight is not confused with what was predicted beforehand.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## Anti-drift rule for the GP trial {#anti-drift-rule-for-the-gp-trial .anchored-heading}

This trial is not being conducted to make the GP \"the good clinician\" and Neurology \"the bad clinician.\"

It is not a contest between specialties.

It is an information-flow study.

Possible outcomes include:

1.  **Strong support for the current theory:** the GP repeatedly preserves/reconstructs the integrated relationship while specialist pathways remain more compartmentalised.
2.  **Partial support:** the GP intermittently reconnects the problem, but also substantially adopts the specialist partition.
3.  **Neutral result:** the GP record does not contain enough relational reasoning to tell us much about the information pathway.
4.  **Contrary result:** the GP himself clearly separates the feet from the neurological syndrome before or after admission and treats later cross-boundary events as unrelated.
5.  **Major falsification:** the GP record demonstrates that the full integrated question was in fact communicated, explicitly answered, and later managed under a defined cross-service reopening architecture.

Any of those outcomes must be accepted if supported by the source.

The purpose of freezing the trial now is precisely to make later agreement or disagreement with the working theory evidentially meaningful.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

## Frozen one-paragraph hypothesis before source review {#frozen-one-paragraph-hypothesis-before-source-review .anchored-heading}

> **Before reviewing the full GP/Podiatry pathway, the working hypothesis is that primary care may provide an independent longitudinal view of how the original integrated feet--gait--tone--toe--function problem survived, disappeared, or re-emerged after specialist partition. The most important question is not what the GP privately believed, but what his subsequent documentation and referrals show he treated as resolved, unresolved, or worthy of renewed neurological integration. Particular attention will be paid to whether the sophisticated Neurology discharge could function as an apparent specialist-resolution signal for the GP despite retaining \"Not clear\" around the feet; whether the 2019 Podiatry re-referral preserves cross-boundary neurological information; whether the 2023 crisis referral re-integrates pain with Parkinsonian control; and whether the 2025 St Vincent\'s referral reconstructs a movement-disorders/dystonia question before the later PRKN result. The trial may strengthen, qualify, or falsify the current information-pathway theory, but its object is frozen before the full source set is opened.**

------------------------------------------------------------------------
:::

.kind-front{background:linear-gradient(180deg,rgba(154,106,69,.035),transparent 200px)}
.kind-primary{background:linear-gradient(180deg,rgba(122,49,57,.035),transparent 260px)}
.kind-current{background:linear-gradient(180deg,rgba(92,102,82,.04),transparent 240px)}
.kind-audit{background:rgba(0,0,0,.012)}
#section-frontmatter{padding-top:42px;padding-bottom:44px}
#section-frontmatter h1{font-size:30px;max-width:700px}
#section-frontmatter p{font-family:ui-sans-serif,system-ui,sans-serif;font-size:12px;line-height:1.7;color:var(--soft-ink)}
#section-must-read{padding-top:76px;padding-bottom:86px}
#section-must-read>.section-kicker{font-size:11px;color:var(--accent)}
#section-must-read>h2:first-of-type{font:normal 44px/1.08 Georgia,"Times New Roman",serif;letter-spacing:-.035em;margin:0 0 22px}
#section-must-read>h2:first-of-type + p{font-family:ui-sans-serif,system-ui,sans-serif;font-size:12px;line-height:1.65;color:var(--soft-ink);border-left:2px solid var(--accent);padding-left:16px;margin-bottom:50px}
#section-must-read>h2:not(:first-of-type){margin-top:2.5em}
#section-must-read>h2:not(:first-of-type)::before{content:"";display:block;width:28px;height:2px;background:var(--accent);margin:0 0 18px}
#section-must-read p{max-width:690px}
#section-must-read p.heartbeat{font:normal 28px/1.35 Georgia,"Times New Roman",serif;letter-spacing:-.02em;color:var(--accent);margin:34px 0;max-width:690px}
#section-must-read p.strong-line{font-size:20px;line-height:1.5;margin:28px 0}
#section-must-read p:first-letter{}
#section-current-architecture,#section-current-object{position:relative}
#section-current-architecture::before,#section-current-object::before{content:"CURRENT FORMULATION";position:absolute;right:40px;top:28px;font:800 9px ui-sans-serif,system-ui,sans-serif;letter-spacing:.14em;color:var(--olive)}
h1,h2,h3,h4{scroll-margin-top:28px;color:var(--ink)}
h1{font:normal 46px/1.06 Georgia,"Times New Roman",serif;letter-spacing:-.035em;margin:.1em 0 .65em}
h2{font:normal 31px/1.16 Georgia,"Times New Roman",serif;letter-spacing:-.025em;margin:2.1em 0 .75em}
h3{font:700 15px/1.35 ui-sans-serif,system-ui,sans-serif;letter-spacing:.005em;margin:2.2em 0 .65em;color:var(--ink)}
h4{font:700 12px/1.4 ui-sans-serif,system-ui,sans-serif;text-transform:uppercase;letter-spacing:.08em;margin:2em 0 .55em;color:var(--soft-ink)}
.heading-anchor{opacity:0;margin-left:.35em;color:var(--faint);text-decoration:none;font-size:.65em}
h1:hover .heading-anchor,h2:hover .heading-anchor,h3:hover .heading-anchor,h4:hover .heading-anchor{opacity:1}
p{margin:.78em 0}strong{font-weight:700}
blockquote{margin:1.8em 0;padding:1.1em 1.45em 1.15em;border-left:2px solid var(--accent);background:var(--quote);font-size:18px;line-height:1.65}
blockquote p{margin:.2em 0}
hr{border:0;border-top:1px solid var(--rule);margin:2.8em 0}
ul,ol{padding-left:1.4em}li{margin:.34em 0}
table{width:100%;border-collapse:collapse;margin:1.8em 0 2.2em;font:12px/1.5 ui-sans-serif,system-ui,sans-serif}
th{font-weight:700;text-align:left;color:var(--soft-ink);border-bottom:1px solid var(--ink);padding:9px 8px 9px 0;vertical-align:bottom}
td{border-bottom:1px solid var(--rule);padding:10px 10px 10px 0;vertical-align:top}
code{font-family:ui-monospace,SFMono-Regular,Menlo,monospace;font-size:.88em;background:var(--quote);padding:.1em .3em;border-radius:3px}
mark.search-hit{background:#ecd57b;color:#201e1b;padding:0 .05em}.hidden-by-search{display:none!important}
.appendix-intro{margin:0;padding:32px 76px;border-bottom:1px solid var(--rule);background:rgba(0,0,0,.016);font-family:ui-sans-serif,system-ui,sans-serif;color:var(--soft-ink)}
.appendix-intro strong{display:block;font-family:Georgia,"Times New Roman",serif;font-size:24px;font-weight:normal;color:var(--ink);margin-bottom:5px}
.backtop{position:fixed;right:24px;bottom:20px;border:0;border-radius:50%;width:42px;height:42px;background:var(--ink);color:var(--paper);font-size:18px;cursor:pointer;opacity:.82}
.backtop:hover{opacity:1}
@media(max-width:980px){
  .landing-sequence{grid-template-columns:1fr 1fr}.landing-sequence>div:nth-child(2){border-right:0}.landing-sequence>div{border-bottom:1px solid var(--rule)}.landing-sequence>div:nth-child(3),.landing-sequence>div:nth-child(4){border-bottom:0}
  .boundary-grid,.three-routes{grid-template-columns:1fr}

  .app{display:block;max-width:900px;padding:0 0 60px}.sidebar{position:relative;height:auto;padding:22px 24px 16px;display:grid;grid-template-columns:1fr auto;gap:12px;background:var(--paper);border-bottom:1px solid var(--rule)}
  .brand{margin:0}.version{margin:4px 0 0}.primary-nav{grid-column:1/-1;display:flex;flex-direction:row;flex-wrap:wrap;gap:10px 20px;margin:8px 0}.utility{grid-column:1/-1;margin:4px 0 0}.full-toc{display:none}.paper{box-shadow:none;border-radius:0}
}
@media(max-width:700px){
  body{font-size:16.5px}.sidebar{padding:18px 18px 14px}.version{display:none}.primary-nav{gap:6px 14px}.primary-nav a{font-size:11.5px}.utility{gap:8px}
  .cover{min-height:auto;padding:54px 26px 38px}.cover::before{left:26px;top:42px}.cover h1{font-size:38px}.cover-deck{font-size:18px}.reader-map{padding:28px 26px}.map-grid{grid-template-columns:1fr}.reader-section{padding:42px 27px 52px}#section-must-read{padding-top:52px}#section-must-read>h2:first-of-type{font-size:36px}
  h1{font-size:38px}h2{font-size:28px}table{display:block;overflow-x:auto}.appendix-intro{padding:28px 27px}.backtop{right:14px;bottom:14px}
}
@media print{
  body{background:white;color:black;font-size:11pt}.sidebar,.backtop,.heading-anchor,.cover-actions,.reader-map{display:none!important}.app{display:block;max-width:none;padding:0}.paper{box-shadow:none}.cover{min-height:0;padding:40pt 0 28pt;border-bottom:1px solid #aaa}.cover::before{left:0;top:22pt}.reader-section{padding:20pt 0;border-bottom:0}.appendix-intro{padding:20pt 0;background:none}a{text-decoration:none;color:inherit}
}

/* v0.12 integration layer */
.thesis-band{padding:58px 66px 62px;border-bottom:1px solid var(--rule);background:linear-gradient(180deg,var(--paper),rgba(122,49,57,.025))}
.thesis-intro{font-size:20px;line-height:1.62;max-width:760px;color:var(--soft-ink);margin:0 0 34px}
.thesis-grid{display:grid;grid-template-columns:repeat(3,1fr);gap:16px;margin:30px 0 36px}
.thesis-card{border-top:3px solid var(--accent);padding:20px 18px 18px;background:var(--quote);min-height:190px}
.thesis-card .num{font:800 10px ui-sans-serif,system-ui,sans-serif;letter-spacing:.14em;text-transform:uppercase;color:var(--accent);display:block;margin-bottom:12px}
.thesis-card strong{font:normal 22px/1.32 Georgia,"Times New Roman",serif;letter-spacing:-.02em}
.thesis-card p{font:12px/1.55 ui-sans-serif,system-ui,sans-serif;color:var(--soft-ink);margin:12px 0 0}
.spine-flow{margin:34px 0;padding:24px 26px;border-left:3px solid var(--accent-2);background:var(--quote);font-size:18px;line-height:1.65}
.evidence-levels{display:grid;grid-template-columns:repeat(2,1fr);gap:12px;margin:26px 0}
.evidence-level{padding:16px 18px;border:1px solid var(--rule);background:var(--paper)}
.evidence-level b{display:block;font:800 10px ui-sans-serif,system-ui,sans-serif;letter-spacing:.11em;text-transform:uppercase;color:var(--accent);margin-bottom:7px}
.source-note{font:12px/1.65 ui-sans-serif,system-ui,sans-serif;color:var(--soft-ink);padding:14px 16px;border-left:2px solid var(--rule);background:var(--quote);margin:20px 0}
.reference-cite{font:12px/1.55 ui-sans-serif,system-ui,sans-serif;color:var(--soft-ink);margin-top:12px}
.reference-cite a{color:var(--accent);text-decoration-thickness:1px;text-underline-offset:2px}
.independence-test{font-size:21px;line-height:1.55;padding:28px 30px;margin:28px 0;background:var(--accent-wash);border-left:4px solid var(--accent)}
.inference-ladder{display:flex;flex-wrap:wrap;gap:7px;align-items:center;margin:24px 0;font:12px/1.4 ui-sans-serif,system-ui,sans-serif;color:var(--soft-ink)}
.inference-ladder span{padding:7px 9px;border:1px solid var(--rule);background:var(--paper)}
.inference-ladder i{font-style:normal;color:var(--faint)}
.section-status{font:800 10px ui-sans-serif,system-ui,sans-serif;letter-spacing:.11em;text-transform:uppercase;color:var(--olive);margin-bottom:16px}
.kind-reference{background:linear-gradient(180deg,rgba(92,102,82,.025),transparent 18rem)}
.kind-reference>.section-kicker{color:var(--olive)}
.archive-note{padding:20px 22px;margin:0 0 32px;background:var(--quote);border:1px solid var(--rule);font:13px/1.65 ui-sans-serif,system-ui,sans-serif;color:var(--soft-ink)}
@media(max-width:780px){.thesis-band{padding:42px 24px}.thesis-grid,.evidence-levels{grid-template-columns:1fr}.thesis-card{min-height:0}}


/* v0.17 — strongest case */
.case-thesis{font-size:clamp(28px,4vw,48px);line-height:1.08;letter-spacing:-.035em;max-width:760px;margin:18px 0 18px;font-weight:normal}
.case-subthesis{font-size:20px;line-height:1.55;max-width:760px;color:var(--soft-ink);margin:0 0 30px}
.case-proof{display:grid;grid-template-columns:repeat(5,minmax(0,1fr));border-top:1px solid var(--rule);border-bottom:1px solid var(--rule);margin:34px 0 26px}
.case-proof>div{padding:19px 16px 18px;border-right:1px solid var(--rule)}
.case-proof>div:first-child{padding-left:0}.case-proof>div:last-child{border-right:0;padding-right:0}
.case-proof span{display:block;font-family:ui-sans-serif,system-ui,sans-serif;font-size:10px;letter-spacing:.12em;text-transform:uppercase;color:var(--accent);margin-bottom:7px}
.case-proof strong{display:block;font-size:16px;line-height:1.25;margin-bottom:7px}.case-proof p{font-size:13px;line-height:1.5;color:var(--soft-ink);margin:0}
.case-one-line{font-size:22px;line-height:1.5;padding:24px 0;margin:20px 0 0;border-top:1px solid var(--rule);border-bottom:1px solid var(--rule)}
.case-one-line strong{color:var(--ink)}
.case-chapter-lead{font-size:20px;line-height:1.6;color:var(--soft-ink);max-width:760px}
.evidence-ledger{display:grid;grid-template-columns:1fr 1fr;gap:14px;margin:24px 0 30px}
.evidence-ledger>div{border:1px solid var(--rule);border-radius:10px;padding:18px 20px;background:rgba(255,255,255,.18)}
.evidence-ledger strong{display:block;margin-bottom:6px}.evidence-ledger p{margin:0;color:var(--soft-ink);font-size:15px;line-height:1.55}
.case-verdict{margin:36px 0;padding:28px 30px;border-left:4px solid var(--accent);background:var(--accent-wash);font-size:21px;line-height:1.55}
.case-verdict p{margin:0}
@media(max-width:900px){.case-proof{grid-template-columns:1fr 1fr}.case-proof>div{border-bottom:1px solid var(--rule)}.case-proof>div:nth-child(2n){border-right:0}.case-proof>div:last-child{grid-column:1/-1;border-bottom:0}.evidence-ledger{grid-template-columns:1fr}}
@media(max-width:560px){.case-proof{grid-template-columns:1fr}.case-proof>div{border-right:0;padding-left:0;padding-right:0}.case-proof>div:last-child{grid-column:auto}.case-thesis{font-size:34px}}
</style>
</head>

<article class="paper">
<header class="cover landing-cover" id="top"><div class="cover-kicker">Integrated forensic reading edition · v0.17</div><h1 class="case-thesis">The disease was recognised. The syndrome was divided.</h1><p class="case-subthesis">The GP referred one progressive, multidimensional clinical trajectory. Neurology correctly recognised severe young-onset Parkinsonism and documented a dystonic-looking lower limb. Yet the painful and structural foot territory was allowed to become a separate clinical object before the record shows an equivalent evidential bridge for that separation. The separation then became the architecture of care.</p><div aria-label="The case in five propositions" class="case-proof"><div><span>01 · Referral</span><strong>One trajectory</strong><p>Feet, gait, tone, toe posture, movement, dexterity and functional decline arrived together under an open neurological question.</p></div><div><span>02 · Recognition</span><strong>Neurological disease seen</strong><p>Parkinsonism was identified at high resolution; the lower-limb gait was itself described as dystonic-looking.</p></div><div><span>03 · Division</span><strong>Feet became separate</strong><p>The synthesis allowed corns, callouses, burning and pain to become “completely separate” / peripheral while invoking the patient’s “markedly divorced” distinction.</p></div><div><span>04 · Inheritance</span><strong>The split generated care</strong><p>Referrals, labels, treatment choices, service destinations and later records inherited the specialist representation.</p></div><div><span>05 · Return</span><strong>Evidence crossed the split</strong><p>Treatment-state effects, toe curling, OFF phenomena, dyskinesia, pain response and finally PRKN dystonia repeatedly reconnected the lower-limb territory to neurological disease.</p></div></div><p class="case-one-line">The case is not that Parkinsonism was missed. <strong>It is that the dominant neurological syndrome was correctly recognised, while part of the same presenting lower-limb problem was separated from it without an equally visible evidential bridge — and that separation became durable enough to organise the years that followed.</strong></p><div class="cover-actions landing-actions"><a class="primary-action" href="#section-what-happened">Read the case</a><a href="#section-governing-spine">See the proof</a><a href="#section-main-analysis">Open the full record</a></div></header>
<div class="reader-map landing-boundary" id="reader-map"><div class="map-title">The evidential boundary</div><div class="boundary-grid"><div class="boundary-card"><strong>What the record can carry</strong><p>A high-dimensional neurological trajectory entered hospital; a dystonic-looking lower-limb sign was documented; the painful/structural foot territory became a separate explanatory track; that track generated downstream care; later treatment-state and dystonic evidence repeatedly crossed it; 2026 established that Parkinsonism and dystonia were manifestations of PRKN disease.</p></div><div class="boundary-card"><strong>What remains genuinely open</strong><p>The mechanism of every historical foot-pain episode; the proportion attributable to dystonia, structural pathology, neuropathic mechanisms or interaction; the clinical standard of the 2017 work-up and treatment; causal injury; and any claim about private motive or conscious knowledge of error.</p></div></div><div class="three-routes"><a href="#section-what-happened"><strong>The case</strong><span>The shortest complete narrative.</span></a><a href="#section-must-read"><strong>Patient voice</strong><span>The lived account in the patient’s own words.</span></a><a href="#section-main-analysis"><strong>The record</strong><span>The full evidence, counter-case, falsifiers and audit trail.</span></a></div></div>
<section class="reader-section kind-front" data-section="Document status" id="section-frontmatter">
<div class="section-kicker">Document status</div>
<h1 class="anchored-heading" id="working-full-case-formulation-information-pathway-documentary-architecture-and-longitudinal-reconstruction">Working Full Case Formulation — Information Pathway, Documentary Architecture, and Longitudinal Reconstruction<a class="heading-anchor" href="#working-full-case-formulation-information-pathway-documentary-architecture-and-longitudinal-reconstruction">¶</a></h1>
<p><strong>Status:</strong> Working analytical draft for source-audit development<br/>
<strong>Target use:</strong> Internal formulation, expert-instruction preparation, chronology testing, documentary analysis<br/>
<strong>Not:</strong> A final medical opinion, legal opinion, negligence finding, causation finding, or allegation of motive<br/>
<strong>Working source base:</strong> Longitudinal clinical record, controlled GPT source-audit exercises, and identified CLIN anchors<br/>
<strong>Version:</strong> Reader v0.17 — simplified landing page; analytical architecture integrated through v0.15</p>

</section>
<section class="reader-section kind-primary" data-section="MUST READ — Patient-authored statement" id="section-must-read">
<div class="section-kicker">MUST READ — Patient-authored statement</div>
<h2 class="source-h1 anchored-heading" id="must-read-patient-authored-statement-of-the-case">MUST READ — Patient-authored statement of the case<a class="heading-anchor" href="#must-read-patient-authored-statement-of-the-case">¶</a></h2>
<p><strong>Status:</strong> Primary patient-authored formulation. Read this in full before the analytical sections below. It states what the patient says went wrong and must not be silently rewritten into expert, legal, or institutional findings. Where its propositions exceed the documentary record, the analytical sections below retain the controlling distinction between record fact, documentary inference, clinical expert question, governance question, and legal conclusion.</p>
<h2 class="source-h1 anchored-heading" id="statement-of-what-i-say-went-wrong">Statement of What I Say Went Wrong<a class="heading-anchor" href="#statement-of-what-i-say-went-wrong">¶</a></h2>
<p>My case is not that doctors in 2017 should somehow have possessed knowledge that only became available years later. I do not need hindsight to make this case.</p>
<p>My case is that they had enough information in front of them, at the time, to know that the relationship between my longstanding painful feet and my neurological disease had not been resolved. What I find extraordinarily difficult to understand is how an unresolved question appears to have been converted into an explanation, how that explanation then entered the medical record, and how the existence of that record seems subsequently to have given the explanation increasing authority.</p>
<p class="heartbeat">What began as uncertainty appears to have become history.</p>
<p class="heartbeat">What became history then appears to have influenced treatment.</p>
<p class="heartbeat">And when later evidence repeatedly crossed the boundary that had originally been drawn, the boundary survived.</p>
<p class="heartbeat">That is the central problem.</p>
<p>The safeguarding issue is ultimately even more serious. Whatever argument remains about diagnosis, mechanism or causation, the later medical record itself documents the severity of the physical and psychological state I had reached. By that stage, nobody needed to agree with my theory of the feet in order to recognise that something had gone profoundly wrong for the person living through it.</p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="the-original-presentation-was-one-problem">The original presentation was one problem<a class="heading-anchor" href="#the-original-presentation-was-one-problem">¶</a></h2>
<p>The medicine is important, but the starting point is not complicated.</p>
<p>Idiopathic Parkinson’s disease and PRKN-related Parkinson disease are both Parkinsonian disorders. PRKN disease is particularly associated with very young onset and lower-limb dystonia, and recognised descriptions include dystonia of the legs preceding the fuller Parkinsonian syndrome, sometimes for years.</p>
<p>That matters because the history recorded before Neurology constructed its explanation has a strikingly similar shape.</p>
<p>My painful or “sore” feet dated back to approximately 2004–2005.</p>
<p>When my GP first encountered me in April 2017, I was not presenting with two established diseases called Parkinson’s disease and peripheral neuropathy. I was presenting with bilateral foot pain and an abnormal gait.</p>
<p>Over the following weeks, the neurological nature of the presentation became increasingly obvious. Difficulty initiating movement became apparent. Stiffness and increased tone developed. Reflexes were brisk. Dexterity deteriorated. Work became difficult. I described being unable to relax my muscles and clawing my toes in bed. Eventually my GP raised the possibility of Parkinsonism or another underlying neurological disorder and arranged urgent hospital assessment.</p>
<p>That is the actual starting point.</p>
<p>One patient.</p>
<p>One evolving presentation.</p>
<p>Feet, gait, tone, movement, toe posture, dexterity and function occurring in the same clinical history.</p>
<p>The later division between “Parkinson’s” and “the feet” had not yet been made.</p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="neurology-itself-saw-dystonia-in-the-lower-limb">Neurology itself saw dystonia in the lower limb<a class="heading-anchor" href="#neurology-itself-saw-dystonia-in-the-lower-limb">¶</a></h2>
<p>Neurology correctly recognised severe young-onset Parkinsonism. I was 39.</p>
<p>The syndrome was relatively symmetrical. There was marked rigidity and bradykinesia, brisk reflexes, little tremor and a profoundly abnormal gait.</p>
<p>But one observation is particularly difficult to reconcile with what subsequently happened.</p>
<p>The neurologist himself described the lower-limb appearance as:</p>
<p><strong>“somewhat suggestive of a dystonic posture — NOT FOOT DROP.”</strong></p>
<p>That observation did not come from me.</p>
<p>It was not generated retrospectively.</p>
<p>It was not produced by the later genetic result.</p>
<p>It was the treating neurologist’s own examination finding in 2017.</p>
<p>The hospital was therefore not looking at an ordinary middle-aged case of Parkinson’s disease accompanied coincidentally by sore feet.</p>
<p>It was looking at very-young-onset, atypical Parkinsonism, a dystonic-looking lower limb and a history in which painful feet had been present from the beginning.</p>
<p>The DaTscan referral itself described the unusual dystonic-looking walk and contemplated an autosomal-recessive Parkinsonian disorder.</p>
<p>PRKN/PARK2 was already within the clinical landscape.</p>
<p>That is why what happened next is so difficult for me to understand.</p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="the-dystonic-lower-limb-appears-to-disappear-from-the-problem-that-mattered">The dystonic lower limb appears to disappear from the problem that mattered<a class="heading-anchor" href="#the-dystonic-lower-limb-appears-to-disappear-from-the-problem-that-mattered">¶</a></h2>
<p>I cannot identify in the record a developed neurological investigation of the relationship between:</p>
<p>the longstanding painful feet,</p>
<p>the abnormal gait,</p>
<p>the toe posture,</p>
<p>the dystonic-looking lower limb,</p>
<p>the rigidity,</p>
<p>the abnormal loading of the feet,</p>
<p>and the visible structural lesions.</p>
<p>Instead, the dystonic observation seems simply to reach a dead end.</p>
<p>A different explanation then appears in the synthesis.</p>
<p>The foot or leg symptoms might be “completely separate.”</p>
<p>Or perhaps there was a secondary peripheral neuropathy.</p>
<p>Past alcohol consumption was suggested as a possible contributor.</p>
<p>I have never objected to peripheral neuropathy being considered.</p>
<p>What I object to is something much more fundamental.</p>
<p class="strong-line"><strong>A possibility appears to have been allowed to occupy the place where an unresolved relationship required investigation.</strong></p>
<p>That is not the same thing.</p>
<p>A differential diagnosis is legitimate.</p>
<p>A hypothesis is legitimate.</p>
<p>What is difficult to defend is allowing the hypothesis to become the working destination of symptoms whose relationship to a dystonic neurological syndrome had not been worked through.</p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="i-noticed-the-gap-while-the-process-was-still-open">I noticed the gap while the process was still open<a class="heading-anchor" href="#i-noticed-the-gap-while-the-process-was-still-open">¶</a></h2>
<p>I noticed the underlying problem myself before the admission process had completely finished.</p>
<p>I did not tell the doctors that I had dystonia.</p>
<p>I did not tell them that peripheral neuropathy was wrong.</p>
<p>I did not possess the specialist knowledge to make either claim.</p>
<p>I was a patient who had lived with painful feet from the beginning of the illness, and I noticed something much simpler:</p>
<p><strong>the feet had barely been investigated.</strong></p>
<p>I was drawing attention to an absence.</p>
<p>That should matter.</p>
<p>There was still time to respond.</p>
<p>The documentary process remained open after I physically left hospital. The DaTscan was subsequently performed and incorporated before final consultant sign-off. The record was therefore capable of receiving new information and modifying the final account.</p>
<p>Yet I cannot identify an equivalent attempt to reopen the unresolved relationship between the feet and the neurological syndrome.</p>
<p>Instead, the plan proceeded:</p>
<p><strong>“As per above.”</strong></p>
<p>Those three words are not misconduct.</p>
<p>But their functional importance is enormous.</p>
<p>The material “above” became the information state from which care proceeded.</p>
<p>An unresolved explanation had become operational.</p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="and-almost-immediately-the-feet-began-behaving-like-part-of-the-neurological-treatment-problem">And almost immediately, the feet began behaving like part of the neurological treatment problem<a class="heading-anchor" href="#and-almost-immediately-the-feet-began-behaving-like-part-of-the-neurological-treatment-problem">¶</a></h2>
<p>The information state created in 2017 did not remain unchallenged for years.</p>
<p>It was challenged almost immediately.</p>
<p>After neurological treatment with procyclidine and rasagiline, my physical improvement was dramatic.</p>
<p>My gait improved.</p>
<p>My writing improved.</p>
<p>I could stand straight.</p>
<p>My legs became loose and limber.</p>
<p>Normal physical function returned to an extent I described contemporaneously as almost miraculous.</p>
<p>Then procyclidine was withdrawn.</p>
<p>During that withdrawal, the outside-left-foot problem returned rapidly. I became more “shifty” and “unrelaxed.” My feet became restless. My walk began falling apart.</p>
<p>When treatment was restored, the physical state improved again.</p>
<p>I do not claim that procyclidine removed a corn.</p>
<p>I do not claim that one medication response proves the mechanism of every painful sensation I had ever experienced.</p>
<p>I do not need to make either claim.</p>
<p>The important fact is much more difficult to dismiss:</p>
<p class="strong-line"><strong>the disputed foot territory was behaving according to neurological medication state.</strong></p>
<p>Once that had happened, how could the neurological significance of the feet simply remain where it had been placed before the treatment experiment occurred?</p>
<p>That is the question I cannot answer from the record.</p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="the-structural-lesions-did-not-resolve-the-neurological-question">The structural lesions did not resolve the neurological question<a class="heading-anchor" href="#the-structural-lesions-did-not-resolve-the-neurological-question">¶</a></h2>
<p>There was unquestionably structural disease in my feet.</p>
<p>There were corns and calluses.</p>
<p>There was a particularly persistent painful lesion around the lateral left forefoot, including the fifth metatarsophalangeal region.</p>
<p>But the existence of a structural lesion does not answer what produced the mechanical loading that repeatedly generated or aggravated it.</p>
<p>A callus is produced by pressure.</p>
<p>If dystonia, rigidity, abnormal toe posture or another neurological motor abnormality repeatedly changed how the foot contacted the ground, then Podiatry and Neurology were never rival explanations.</p>
<p>Podiatry could treat the visible lesion.</p>
<p>Neurology could still be responsible for the abnormal biomechanics producing or aggravating it.</p>
<p>That possibility was sitting directly between two services.</p>
<p>I cannot identify where it was investigated.</p>
<p>That absence is astonishing to me because the neurological examination had already described the lower limb as dystonic-looking.</p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="then-an-independent-neurologist-said-i-probably-had-park2">Then an independent neurologist said I probably had PARK2<a class="heading-anchor" href="#then-an-independent-neurologist-said-i-probably-had-park2">¶</a></h2>
<p>The next development should, in my view, have made the original separation harder to maintain.</p>
<p>Professor Daniel Healy did not simply see me and endorse ordinary idiopathic Parkinson’s disease.</p>
<p>He wrote that I <strong>“probably has Park II.”</strong></p>
<p>He recommended testing PARK2 first and moving to a broader panel if it was negative.</p>
<p>By April 2018 my own treating neurologist was recording that PARK2/PRKN was what <strong>“we/Prof Healy suspect is the cause.”</strong></p>
<p>That date matters profoundly.</p>
<p>By then the treating service knew:</p>
<p>I had very-young-onset Parkinsonism;</p>
<p>the presentation was atypical and relatively symmetrical;</p>
<p>the lower limb had looked dystonic;</p>
<p>an autosomal-recessive Parkinsonian disorder had already been considered;</p>
<p>an independent neurologist thought I probably had PARK2;</p>
<p>PARK2 testing remained available;</p>
<p>my feet continued to cramp;</p>
<p>and the disputed foot territory had already changed according to neurological medication state.</p>
<p>How much more was required before the original separation of the feet from the neurological disease at least had to be reopened?</p>
<p>That question does not depend on my eventual genetic result.</p>
<p>They did not need a result from 2026.</p>
<p>They had the problem in front of them in 2017 and 2018.</p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="the-hypothesis-then-became-a-mechanism">The hypothesis then became a mechanism<a class="heading-anchor" href="#the-hypothesis-then-became-a-mechanism">¶</a></h2>
<p>This is why I use the term <strong>path dependence</strong>.</p>
<p>Once peripheral neuropathy entered the record, it no longer had to win the diagnostic argument every year.</p>
<p>It was already there.</p>
<p>The feet had already been allocated.</p>
<p>Later evidence therefore arrived in a system that already had somewhere to put it.</p>
<p>That matters because over the following years the supposed boundary was crossed again and again.</p>
<p>Foot symptoms changed with neurological medication state.</p>
<p>Foot movement was later described as Parkinsonian.</p>
<p>OFF dystonia became an explicit possibility.</p>
<p>Toe curling persisted.</p>
<p>The record itself acknowledged that the earliest symptoms had involved the feet.</p>
<p>Dopaminergic medication repeatedly changed my experience of those feet.</p>
<p>And yet the peripheral-neuropathy formulation survived.</p>
<p>At some point, the question becomes unavoidable:</p>
<p class="strong-line"><strong>What exactly would have been sufficient to dislodge it?</strong></p>
<p>If dystonic phenomenology did not do it;</p>
<p>if anticholinergic treatment response did not do it;</p>
<p>if medication withdrawal affecting the feet did not do it;</p>
<p>if probable PARK2 did not do it;</p>
<p>if continuing cramping did not do it;</p>
<p>if later OFF dystonia did not do it;</p>
<p>if toe curling did not do it;</p>
<p>and if dopaminergic responsiveness of the painful territory did not do it;</p>
<p>then what evidence was the original hypothesis actually vulnerable to?</p>
<p>A clinical hypothesis that apparently survives every contradiction without requiring reconstruction begins to look less like a hypothesis and more like an organising assumption.</p>
<p>That is what I say happened here.</p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="the-language-itself-acquired-authority">The language itself acquired authority<a class="heading-anchor" href="#the-language-itself-acquired-authority">¶</a></h2>
<p>There is another problem.</p>
<p>Once “peripheral neuropathy” and “neuropathic pain” became the medical vocabulary of the case, those terms naturally travelled.</p>
<p>Doctors used them.</p>
<p>Nurses used them.</p>
<p>I used them.</p>
<p>I was trying to communicate with the people responsible for treating me in language they recognised.</p>
<p>The later appearance of the same terminology from multiple mouths cannot automatically be treated as multiple independent confirmations of the original proposition.</p>
<p>If the service first supplied the language, the service cannot later point to the circulation of that language as though the diagnosis had independently reproduced itself throughout the record.</p>
<p>That would confuse repetition with validation.</p>
<p>The origin of a proposition matters.</p>
<p>The evidential bridge matters.</p>
<p>And I cannot identify the point at which the tentative 2017 possibility became independently established strongly enough to justify the increasingly settled language later used.</p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="the-2021-treatment-transition-makes-the-later-history-even-harder-to-comprehend">The 2021 treatment transition makes the later history even harder to comprehend<a class="heading-anchor" href="#the-2021-treatment-transition-makes-the-later-history-even-harder-to-comprehend">¶</a></h2>
<p>The pharmacological history adds another layer.</p>
<p>Procyclidine had produced dramatic symptomatic benefit.</p>
<p>It was later removed because of ocular concerns.</p>
<p>It was replaced with Sinemet.</p>
<p>But Sinemet was not a neutral substitute.</p>
<p>The dopaminergic treatment rapidly became constrained by dyskinesia and chorea.</p>
<p>That creates an obvious therapeutic problem.</p>
<p>If one treatment modality that had provided major control was withdrawn, and its replacement could not simply be increased because excessive dopaminergic exposure produced involuntary movement, then the patient may occupy a narrow therapeutic window.</p>
<p>Too much treatment causes one problem.</p>
<p>Too little may permit another.</p>
<p>That is precisely the sort of situation in which unexplained lower-limb symptoms should become more, not less, interesting.</p>
<p>Yet the feet remained within the inherited neuropathic framework.</p>
