<p>I find that extraordinarily difficult to reconcile with the clinical history.</p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="the-subsequent-record-repeatedly-supplied-reasons-to-reconstruct-the-model">The subsequent record repeatedly supplied reasons to reconstruct the model<a class="heading-anchor" href="#the-subsequent-record-repeatedly-supplied-reasons-to-reconstruct-the-model">¶</a></h2>
<p>The years that followed did not preserve a clean separation between Parkinsonism and the feet.</p>
<p>The opposite happened.</p>
<p>The boundary was repeatedly crossed.</p>
<p>Foot symptoms varied according to medication state.</p>
<p>Dystonia became an explicit consideration.</p>
<p>Toe curling remained.</p>
<p>The feet continued to interfere with walking.</p>
<p>Neurological medication continued to affect them.</p>
<p>The record itself documented that the early history involved the feet.</p>
<p>None of this proves that every painful sensation had one neurological mechanism.</p>
<p>It does something more basic.</p>
<p>It repeatedly undermines confidence in any model that treats “the feet” as a stable, separate object whose neurological relationship was already settled in 2017.</p>
<p>And yet that is approximately how the category seems to have functioned.</p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="by-2023-the-human-consequences-were-becoming-unmistakable">By 2023 the human consequences were becoming unmistakable<a class="heading-anchor" href="#by-2023-the-human-consequences-were-becoming-unmistakable">¶</a></h2>
<p>Meanwhile, the problem was no longer merely diagnostic.</p>
<p>The suffering was becoming severe.</p>
<p>By 2023 the record described the foot problem as a <strong>“heavy psychological burden.”</strong></p>
<p>I described walking as something that <strong>“psychologically consumes”</strong> me.</p>
<p>Another walk could appear as a <strong>“psychological mountain.”</strong></p>
<p>The same clinical record still contained bilateral foot problems, cramping and consideration of Parkinsonian OFF dystonia.</p>
<p>Whatever anybody believed about the precise cause, something had become deeply wrong.</p>
<p>This was not an abstract debate over nomenclature.</p>
<p>A patient was becoming psychologically consumed by a physical problem that remained unresolved after years of specialist care.</p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="by-2024-my-world-was-contracting">By 2024 my world was contracting<a class="heading-anchor" href="#by-2024-my-world-was-contracting">¶</a></h2>
<p>By 2024 the record described my life as <strong>“a lot smaller.”</strong></p>
<p>Activities had contracted.</p>
<p>Foot pain and toe curling were interfering with ordinary function.</p>
<p>Again, nobody had to accept my causal interpretation to understand what this meant.</p>
<p>This was prolonged suffering affecting daily life.</p>
<p>The unresolved clinical problem had become a lived restriction on existence.</p>
<p>And still the historical explanatory structure remained.</p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="by-2025-the-language-was-extreme">By 2025 the language was extreme<a class="heading-anchor" href="#by-2025-the-language-was-extreme">¶</a></h2>
<p>By 2025 my feet could be too painful to tie my runners.</p>
<p>Mornings were described as <strong>“murder in the feet.”</strong></p>
<p>The first 150 footsteps were <strong>“torture.”</strong></p>
<p>Those are not retrospective descriptions invented for a complaint.</p>
<p>They are the language contained within the clinical history itself.</p>
<p>The same record also says that when Parkinson’s medication reached my system, the feet felt better.</p>
<p>And yet the old formulation was still present:</p>
<p><strong>“if there is a degree of peripheral neuropathy here.”</strong></p>
<p>I find the coexistence of those facts very difficult to comprehend.</p>
<p>The patient has an early dystonic-looking lower limb.</p>
<p>The feet respond to neurological medication state.</p>
<p>PARK2 is suspected.</p>
<p>Later OFF dystonia is considered.</p>
<p>Toe curling persists.</p>
<p>Dopaminergic treatment affects the feet.</p>
<p>By 2025 pain is described as torture.</p>
<p>And the old peripheral formulation is still there.</p>
<p>At some point it becomes reasonable to ask whether the persistence of the explanation reflects the strength of its evidence, or simply the strength of its position in the record.</p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="then-sinemet-cr-supplied-another-major-reason-to-reopen-everything">Then Sinemet CR supplied another major reason to reopen everything<a class="heading-anchor" href="#then-sinemet-cr-supplied-another-major-reason-to-reopen-everything">¶</a></h2>
<p>I subsequently experienced profound relief from a longstanding component of the foot pain after using Sinemet CR at night.</p>
<p>Again, I do not need that to prove that every historical pain sensation was dystonia.</p>
<p>I do not claim that treatment response alone proves mechanism.</p>
<p>That is not the point.</p>
<p>The point is that after everything that had already happened, this was yet another substantial challenge to the original partition.</p>
<p>At minimum, it demanded explanation.</p>
<p>At minimum, it made the relationship between dopaminergic state and the painful lower limb impossible to regard as trivial.</p>
<p>At minimum, it should have reopened the question.</p>
<p>What happened instead is what I find most disturbing.</p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="when-i-complained-the-historical-structure-was-defended">When I complained, the historical structure was defended<a class="heading-anchor" href="#when-i-complained-the-historical-structure-was-defended">¶</a></h2>
<p>The response to my complaint continued to refer to <strong>“peripheral neuropathic pain symptomatologies.”</strong></p>
<p>The striking Sinemet response was accommodated by saying that Parkinson’s medication could alter the experience of pain <strong>“of whatever cause.”</strong></p>
<p>That formulation preserves the original structure perfectly.</p>
<p>The drug response can be accepted.</p>
<p>The pain relief can be accepted.</p>
<p>The historical classification need not change.</p>
<p>Everything new can be absorbed without forcing reconstruction of the original model.</p>
<p>That is precisely the problem.</p>
<p>The response also relied on the fact that I had been given an early second opinion from Professor Healy.</p>
<p>But Healy’s actual documented opinion was that I probably had PARK2.</p>
<p>His opinion therefore cannot simply function as retrospective confirmation of an uncomplicated idiopathic-Parkinson’s framework.</p>
<p>That distinction matters.</p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="the-documentary-history-appears-to-have-become-its-own-defence">The documentary history appears to have become its own defence<a class="heading-anchor" href="#the-documentary-history-appears-to-have-become-its-own-defence">¶</a></h2>
<p>This is perhaps what I find hardest to believe.</p>
<p>An uncertain explanation entered the record in 2017.</p>
<p>Subsequent care proceeded from that information state.</p>
<p>The terminology propagated through the record.</p>
<p>Later symptoms were interpreted within it.</p>
<p>The patient himself eventually used its vocabulary when asking for help.</p>
<p>Years of letters therefore accumulated in which the same broad framework appeared again and again.</p>
<p>Then, when the framework was challenged, the existence of those letters could itself be cited as evidence that the issue had always been adequately considered.</p>
<p>That risks becoming circular.</p>
<p>The record proves the continuity of the record.</p>
<p>It does not necessarily prove repeated independent validation of the proposition inside it.</p>
<p>The question is not how many times the words appeared.</p>
<p>The question is what evidence established them.</p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="i-do-not-need-to-allege-malice">I do not need to allege malice<a class="heading-anchor" href="#i-do-not-need-to-allege-malice">¶</a></h2>
<p>I cannot know another person's motive.</p>
<p>I do not need to allege that anybody deliberately harmed me.</p>
<p>I do not need to allege falsification.</p>
<p>The sequence is serious enough without doing so.</p>
<p>What I say is that an early explanatory decision became increasingly difficult to dislodge; that contradictory or qualifying information was repeatedly accommodated without reconstructing the whole problem; and that by the time I challenged the history formally, the accumulated documentation generated by that pathway was capable of being used to defend the pathway itself.</p>
<p>Whether that arose from anchoring, compartmentalisation, professional defensiveness, institutional self-protection, ordinary cognitive bias or some combination of these is a separate question.</p>
<p>The documentary problem exists regardless of motive.</p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="and-then-there-is-safeguarding">And then there is safeguarding<a class="heading-anchor" href="#and-then-there-is-safeguarding">¶</a></h2>
<p>Everything above could still be debated.</p>
<p>A clinician can be wrong without wrongdoing.</p>
<p>A peripheral neuropathy hypothesis can be reasonable.</p>
<p>A mixed structural and neurological foot problem can be genuinely difficult.</p>
<p>A genetic diagnosis can remain uncertain.</p>
<p>A medication response can have more than one interpretation.</p>
<p>I accept all of that.</p>
<p>But safeguarding is different.</p>
<p>By 2023 the medical record itself was describing my condition as a heavy psychological burden.</p>
<p>Walking psychologically consumed me.</p>
<p>Another walk could feel like a psychological mountain.</p>
<p>By 2024 my life was a lot smaller.</p>
<p>By 2025 my mornings were “murder in the feet.”</p>
<p>The first 150 footsteps were “torture.”</p>
<p>Those words describe a human being reaching the limits of what he could tolerate.</p>
<p>At that point, the correctness of the original 2017 differential diagnosis was no longer the only question.</p>
<p>There was another one:</p>
<p><strong>What was happening to the person living inside this unresolved problem?</strong></p>
<p>And:</p>
<p><strong>What was being done to keep him safe?</strong></p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="that-question-did-not-require-diagnostic-agreement">That question did not require diagnostic agreement<a class="heading-anchor" href="#that-question-did-not-require-diagnostic-agreement">¶</a></h2>
<p>Safeguarding did not require anybody to admit that the neuropathy hypothesis was wrong.</p>
<p>It did not require acceptance of my theory.</p>
<p>It did not require PRKN confirmation.</p>
<p>It did not require somebody to concede that the 2017 discharge synthesis had failed.</p>
<p>It did not even require immediate resolution of the pain.</p>
<p>It required recognition of what prolonged unresolved suffering was doing to the patient.</p>
<p>That is why I find this part particularly difficult to understand.</p>
<p>The record available to me shows increasingly serious descriptions of psychological deterioration.</p>
<p>I cannot identify a corresponding documented safeguarding response proportionate to what was being recorded.</p>
<p>I am careful about that formulation.</p>
<p>Absence from the surviving record does not prove absolutely nothing happened.</p>
<p>But where the record itself contains escalating descriptions of suffering, the absence of a visible corresponding protective response is not a trivial documentary gap.</p>
<p>It is a serious question in its own right.</p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="two-connected-failures">Two connected failures<a class="heading-anchor" href="#two-connected-failures">¶</a></h2>
<p>I therefore allege two connected failures.</p>
<p>The first is <strong>clinical and informational</strong>.</p>
<p>An integrated medication-naïve feet–gait–tone–movement presentation was decomposed. The neurological component was investigated and treated at high resolution. The unresolved painful-foot component was given a possible peripheral explanation before its relationship to the dystonic lower limb and neurological disease had been equivalently established. That explanation entered the record and acquired longitudinal authority. Repeated later reasons to reconstruct the relationship did not visibly produce a full reconstruction.</p>
<p>The second is <strong>human and protective</strong>.</p>
<p>While that unresolved pathway continued, the patient's physical suffering became severe and psychologically consuming. His activities contracted. Walking became a psychological burden. The language used to describe mornings became “murder” and “torture.”</p>
<p>Yet the record available to me does not demonstrate a safeguarding response proportionate to that deterioration.</p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="the-question-i-am-left-with">The question I am left with<a class="heading-anchor" href="#the-question-i-am-left-with">¶</a></h2>
<p>I survived this history.</p>
<p>That sentence is more important to me than the diagnostic terminology.</p>
<p>My central question is no longer merely why a tentative 2017 explanation survived so much evidence that should at least have forced it back into question.</p>
<p>It is why the system appears to have been able to document, year after year, the consequences of the unresolved problem without allowing those consequences to break the explanatory structure that had been created at the beginning.</p>
<p>And ultimately it is this:</p>
<p class="strong-line"><strong>How could a record document the dystonic-looking lower limb, dramatic neurological treatment response, probable PARK2, continuing cramping, medication-dependent foot symptoms, OFF dystonia, toe curling, severe pain, psychological consumption, a shrinking life, “murder in the feet” and “torture” — and still arrive at a position in which the historical pathway required defence more urgently than the pathway required reconstruction?</strong></p>
<p>That is what I cannot understand.</p>
<p>That is what I say requires independent explanation.</p>
<p>And that is what I say went wrong.</p>
<hr/>
</section>
<section class="reader-section kind-current narrative-spine" data-section="What happened — plain-language narrative spine" id="section-what-happened"><div class="section-kicker">The strongest supportable narrative · read this first</div><h2 class="source-h1 anchored-heading" id="what-happened">The case</h2><p class="case-chapter-lead">The record is most powerful when read as a sequence of transformations rather than as a dispute over labels. <strong>One evolving clinical trajectory went in. A divided clinical architecture came out.</strong></p><h2 class="anchored-heading" id="what-happened-trajectory">1. The referral described a syndrome becoming visible over time</h2><p>The GP did not refer a settled diagnosis. The referral described an <strong>evolving trajectory</strong>: unusual festinant and stiff gait, bilateral foot problems, difficulty initiating movement, marked stiffness and increased tone in all four limbs, brisk reflexes, inability to relax, toe clawing in bed, worsening dexterity, deteriorating work function and progressive loss of capacity. The question remained open: <strong>“?parkinsonian or other underlying diagnosis.”</strong></p><p>That matters because the clinical object was relational. Feet, gait, tone, movement initiation, toe posture, dexterity and function were not presented as independent diagnoses. They were dimensions of the same worsening history requiring explanation.</p><h2 class="anchored-heading" id="what-happened-recognition">2. Neurology recognised the neurological disease — and also saw the lower-limb dystonia signal</h2><p>The central neurological syndrome was not missed. Neurology documented marked symmetrical tone, brisk reflexes, lead-pipe and axial rigidity, gross bradykinesia and a strikingly abnormal gait. The lower-limb pattern was described as <strong>“somewhat suggestive of a dystonic posture — NOT FOOT DROP.”</strong> The case then entered sophisticated young-onset Parkinsonian and recessive-genetic reasoning.</p><p>This is the point that makes the later history difficult. The record did not begin with an ordinary Parkinsonian syndrome on one side and an unrelated foot complaint on the other. <strong>The neurological examination itself placed dystonia in the lower-limb territory that was already clinically entangled with the feet.</strong></p><h2 class="anchored-heading" id="what-happened-division">3. The syndrome was then divided</h2><p>In the specialist synthesis, the Parkinsonian dimensions were developed at high resolution while the painful and structural foot territory became separable. The leg symptoms involving corns, callouses and burning were proposed as possibly <strong>“completely separate”</strong> or perhaps secondary peripheral neuropathy. The same passage recorded that the patient had <strong>“markedly divorced”</strong> the feelings of leg tension from the corns, callouses and foot pain.</p><p>The patient’s discernment was phenomenological: different experiences felt different and had behaved differently over time. That was clinically useful information. But a distinction in experience is not, by itself, a demonstration of biological independence. <strong>The consequential move was that the distinction acquired enough classificatory force to support a separate explanatory and service track.</strong></p><blockquote><p><strong>The disease was recognised. The syndrome was divided.</strong></p></blockquote><h2 class="anchored-heading" id="what-happened-generativity">4. The division became the pathway</h2><p>Once the specialist representation acquired authority, it stopped being merely descriptive. Parkinsonism received neurological investigation, imaging, treatment strategy, genetic reasoning and specialist correspondence. The feet received structural and peripheral explanations, Podiatry involvement and later neuropathic terminology. Structural foot pathology was real, and peripheral mechanisms remained possible. <strong>But coexistence is not independence.</strong></p><p>The split then generated the record that followed: referrals, service destinations, vocabulary, treatment choices and inherited assumptions. Later clinicians did not repeatedly encounter the untreated 2017 phenotype; they encountered a patient whose case had already been organised for them.</p><blockquote><p><strong>The model did not merely describe the pathway. It helped produce the pathway that later appeared to corroborate the model.</strong></p></blockquote><h2 class="anchored-heading" id="what-happened-boundary">5. The later record repeatedly crossed the division</h2><p>Almost immediately, neurological treatment altered the wider physical state. During the 2017 procyclidine period the patient described his walk returning, his legs becoming loose and limber, posture and dexterity improving, and ordinary movement becoming possible. During planned withdrawal, deterioration again included the feet and gait.</p><p>Across the later record came further lower-limb information that did not respect the original boundary: cramping, toe curling, OFF-type phenomena, medication-state changes, levodopa sensitivity and dyskinesia. In 2023 the GP explicitly recalled severe bilateral burning foot and lower-leg pain against an earlier episode that had settled with better Parkinson’s control. By March 2025 the treating neurologist acknowledged that Sinemet helped morning pain and that Sinemet CR had produced <strong>“profound relief.”</strong></p><p>None of this proves that every historical corn, callus, structural deformity or burning pain was dystonia. It establishes the narrower and more important point that <strong>the lower-limb/foot territory repeatedly behaved as neurologically relevant and repeatedly supplied reasons to reconstruct the original syndrome.</strong></p><h2 class="anchored-heading" id="what-happened-endpoint">6. The endpoint sharpened the original question; it did not create it</h2><p>PARK2 was already a live specialist hypothesis in 2017. Prof Daniel Healy later recorded that the patient probably had PARK2, noted the dramatic response to low-dose procyclidine and rasagiline, and cautioned about dyskinesia with dopaminergic treatment. In 2026, genetic testing established that the Parkinsonism and dystonia were manifestations of compound-heterozygous PRKN disease.</p><p>The 2026 result must not be projected backwards as if it proved the mechanism of every earlier symptom. Its legitimate force is different: <strong>it confirms that the dystonic and Parkinsonian components belonged to the same neurological disease, while the historical record already shows that the lower-limb/foot territory had been part of the unresolved clinical problem from the beginning.</strong></p><h2 class="anchored-heading" id="what-happened-independence">7. What is missing is not documentation. It is independent reconstruction</h2><p>The later record contains abundant documentation, genuine treatment adjustment and sustained clinical attention. What is much harder to identify is a later event in which an appropriately qualified reviewer returned to the source phenotype, temporarily suspended the inherited categories, reconstructed feet–gait–tone–dystonia–pain from first principles, incorporated the treatment-state evidence, and independently re-derived the original separation.</p><p>That distinction matters because <strong>continuity is not convergence</strong>. Repetition of a classification across records can prove that the classification travelled. It does not automatically prove that each later source independently earned it.</p><div class="case-verdict"><p><strong>The strongest supportable case is therefore this:</strong> a progressive multidimensional neurological syndrome entered specialist care; the dominant Parkinsonian disease was correctly recognised; the painful lower-limb/foot territory was nevertheless separated into another clinical object before the surviving record shows an equivalent evidential bridge for that separation; the separation then acquired enough authority to organise subsequent care; later evidence repeatedly crossed it; and the accumulated record shows far more clearly how the inherited model was managed than where it was independently reconstructed from first principles.</p></div><h2 class="anchored-heading" id="what-happened-bottom-line">The question the record has to answer</h2><blockquote><p><strong>What evidence justified making the painful lower-limb/foot territory durably separate from the neurological syndrome — and where is the later independent reconstruction showing that this separation remained justified after the subsequent evidence accumulated?</strong></p></blockquote></section>
<section class="reader-section kind-current thesis-band" data-section="Governing spine — integrated v0.16" id="section-governing-spine">
<div class="section-kicker">Governing spine · integrated v0.17</div>
<h2 class="source-h1 anchored-heading" id="governing-spine">The case in its cleanest current form<a class="heading-anchor" href="#governing-spine">¶</a></h2><p>The case is best understood as <strong>recognition followed by decomposition, then inheritance</strong>. The hospital was not wrong to recognise severe young-onset Parkinsonism. The disputed event is narrower: part of the lower-limb syndrome became a separate explanatory object before the surviving record shows that the separation had earned the longitudinal weight later placed on it.</p><p class="strong-line"><strong>Core proposition:</strong> the founding separation became more durable than the evidence demonstrating its independence.</p><div class="evidence-ledger"><div><strong>Input</strong><p>A progressive referral joined feet, gait, tone, movement, toe posture, dexterity and function under one open neurological question.</p></div><div><strong>Transformation</strong><p>Neurology documented a dystonic-looking lower-limb sign, yet the painful/structural foot territory became separable as “completely separate” / possibly peripheral.</p></div><div><strong>Authority</strong><p>The output carried specialist and institutional authority and generated referrals, terminology, treatment strategy and inherited information states.</p></div><div><strong>Challenge</strong><p>Later treatment-state and dystonic evidence repeatedly crossed the split; the record shows local adaptation more clearly than first-principles reconstruction.</p></div></div><p><strong>Endpoint discipline:</strong> the 2026 PRKN diagnosis confirms that Parkinsonism and dystonia belonged to one genetic disease. It does not prove the mechanism of every historical foot-pain episode, and it is not needed to establish the earlier documentary transformation.</p><blockquote><p><strong>Documentary density is not evidential independence. Authority increases portability; it does not itself increase independent validation.</strong></p></blockquote>



<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="governing-evidential-levels">Keep the evidential levels separate<a class="heading-anchor" href="#governing-evidential-levels">¶</a></h2>
<div class="evidence-levels">
<div class="evidence-level"><b>Record fact</b>What the contemporaneous source actually records: observations, language, treatment, correspondence, meetings and later responses.</div>
<div class="evidence-level"><b>Documentary inference</b>How information was compressed, partitioned, inherited, reclassified or given increasing grammatical authority.</div>
<div class="evidence-level"><b>Clinical expert question</b>Whether the examination, classification, treatment strategy, DBS information, therapeutic-window handling or safeguarding response met appropriate clinical expectations.</div>
<div class="evidence-level"><b>Governance hypothesis</b>Whether complaint and institutional processes independently reconstructed the upstream model or mainly assessed the defensibility of decisions taken within it.</div>
</div>
<div class="callout"><strong>Protected restraint</strong><br/>This architecture supports path dependence, asymmetric evidential resolution, prospective defensibility and a testable hypothesis of retrospective constructive defence. It does <strong>not</strong> presently establish concealment, falsification, improper motive, conscious recognition of error, or that all foot pain was dystonic/PRKN-mediated.</div>
</section>
<section class="reader-section kind-current" data-section="24-Hour Compression Point" id="section-24-hour-compression">
<div class="section-kicker">Critical opening interval · 3–4 July 2017</div>
<h2 class="source-h1 anchored-heading" id="24-hour-compression-point">The 24-Hour Compression Point<a class="heading-anchor" href="#24-hour-compression-point">¶</a></h2>
<p class="strong-line"><strong>The longest-lasting feature of the eight-year pathway may have been created between Monday 3 July and the early Complex Case Meeting 2017.</strong></p>
<p>The significance of this interval is not that Parkinsonism was missed. It was recognised rapidly and investigated at high resolution. Nor is the point that specialist expertise was unavailable. The case was brought into collective Neurology reasoning almost immediately. The question is narrower: <strong>what representation of the patient reached that expertise?</strong></p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="24h-established-chronology">Established chronology<a class="heading-anchor" href="#24h-established-chronology">¶</a></h2>
<p><strong>Monday, 3 July 2017:</strong> the patient entered hospital for neurological assessment. The pre-admission GP material described an integrated feet–gait–tone–movement problem, including abnormal gait, increased tone, difficulty initiating movement, inability to relax, toe clawing, deteriorating dexterity and functional decline under an open neurological question.</p>
<p>The early neurological examination then recovered much of the untreated phenotype and described a strikingly abnormal gait with an apparent foot-drop-like lower-limb posture that was <strong>“somewhat suggestive of a dystonic posture — NOT FOOT DROP.”</strong></p>
<p><strong>Tuesday, 4 July 2017:</strong> the case was considered at the Neurology <strong>Complex Case Meeting</strong>. The surviving specialist synthesis records: <em>“Seen at the Neurology Complex case meeting with my colleagues — likely an IPD albeit with atypical features.”</em> The date follows from the Monday admission chronology together with the department's Tuesday Complex Case Meeting schedule.</p>
<div class="callout"><strong>Temporal hinge</strong><br/>Monday: integrated medication-naïve phenotype and dystonic-looking lower-limb sign.<br/>Tuesday: collective specialist formulation.</div>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="24h-evidential-levels">Evidential levels<a class="heading-anchor" href="#24h-evidential-levels">¶</a></h2>
<p><strong>Documented fact:</strong> the broad GP problem preceded admission; Neurology documented the dystonic-looking gait; the specialist synthesis records Complex Case Meeting review; subsequent contemporaneous correspondence shows collective consultation around treatment and genetics.</p>
<p><strong>Date reconstruction:</strong> Monday 3 July is independently anchored by the admission chronology; the Complex Case Meeting was the Tuesday meeting, fixing the relevant meeting to the early Complex Case Meeting 2017. The record should preserve the specific source establishing the Tuesday schedule alongside this reconstruction.</p>
<p><strong>Not established by the surviving material:</strong> the exact oral presentation made to the meeting; which source documents each participant personally reviewed; whether the GP's relational framing was explicitly reproduced; whether the painful feet and dystonic-looking gait were discussed as one problem; or whether the later partition was generated by the meeting or inherited by it.</p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="24h-two-pathways">Two possible pathways<a class="heading-anchor" href="#24h-two-pathways">¶</a></h2>
<h3>Pathway A — the integrated phenotype reached collective review</h3>
<p>If the Tuesday meeting received the problem substantially as it existed on Monday — longstanding painful feet, abnormal gait, toe posture, inability to relax, severe tone/rigidity, deteriorating dexterity, structural foot abnormalities and a medication-naïve dystonic-looking lower limb — then the later separation of part of the foot syndrome into potentially structural or peripheral-neuropathic territory requires explanation at the level of <strong>collective specialist reasoning</strong>.</p>
<blockquote><p><strong>What evidence led the group to distinguish the painful-foot territory from the neurological lower-limb phenotype, and what findings were considered sufficient to support that separation?</strong></p></blockquote>
<p>Multiple mechanisms could legitimately coexist. The missing evidential object would be the proposition-by-proposition bridge showing how the branches were distinguished, how interaction between them was considered, and what would have triggered reconsideration.</p>
<h3>Pathway B — the problem had already been compressed before collective review</h3>
<p>If the case reached the meeting already reformatted as <strong>young-onset Parkinsonism requiring sophisticated neurological investigation</strong> plus <strong>a potentially separate foot/pain/peripheral problem</strong>, the systems issue moves upstream.</p>
<p>The specialist group could have given entirely reasonable advice to the case as presented while never being asked to adjudicate the relationship that had been central to the original GP presentation. In that scenario, the decisive transformation occurred between Monday's integrated assessment and Tuesday's collective formulation.</p>
<p class="strong-line"><strong>Fact recovery is not relationship preservation.</strong></p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="24h-decisive-question">The decisive question<a class="heading-anchor" href="#24h-decisive-question">¶</a></h2>
<p>The crucial unknown is no longer primarily <em>when</em> the collective formulation occurred. It is:</p>
<blockquote><p><strong>What exactly did the the early Complex Case Meeting 2017 Complex Case Meeting understand the clinical problem to be?</strong></p></blockquote>
<p>If the whole feet–gait–tone–posture–pain relationship was presented and expressly adjudicated, the record should be capable of showing how. If it was not presented as a relationship, then the case becomes one of <strong>problem-representation failure occurring before collective expertise was applied</strong>.</p>
<p>The strongest present formulation is:</p>
<blockquote><p><strong>The right expertise may have been available within twenty-four hours, but the clinically decisive relationship may no longer have been the question placed before it.</strong></p></blockquote>
<p>This is a testable reconstruction, not a finding of clinical breach. Its strongest falsifier would be contemporaneous Complex Case Meeting material showing that the integrated lower-limb relationship was expressly presented, debated and given a reasoned disposition or reopening plan.</p>
<hr/>
</section>
<section class="reader-section kind-current" data-section="Input, transformation and generative authority" id="section-generativity">
<div class="section-kicker">Observable systems event · no mind-reading required</div>
<h2 class="source-h1 anchored-heading" id="input-transformation-generative-authority">Input, transformation and generative authority<a class="heading-anchor" href="#input-transformation-generative-authority">¶</a></h2>
<p class="strong-line"><strong>The 2017 representation can be audited as an observable input → transformation → authority-bearing output → downstream generativity sequence.</strong></p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="generativity-input">1. What was demonstrably available<a class="heading-anchor" href="#generativity-input">¶</a></h2>
<p>The GP referral did not present a bare Parkinsonism query. It linked bilateral foot problems, an unusual festinant/stiff gait, increasing movement-initiation difficulty, marked upper- and lower-limb tone, brisk reflexes, inability to relax, toe clawing, deteriorating dexterity, occupational impairment and an expressly open <em>“?parkinsonian or other underlying diagnosis”</em> question.</p>
<p>Neurology's own medication-naïve examination then added information: markedly increased tone, brisk reflexes, lead-pipe/axial rigidity, gross bradykinesia, structural foot abnormalities and a striking pseudo-foot-drop gait described as somewhat suggestive of <strong>dystonic posture</strong> and explicitly <strong>not true foot drop</strong>.</p>
<div class="callout"><strong>Input adequacy</strong><br/>By the time the specialist model was being constructed, the available clinical object was richer—not poorer—than the original referral. The central question is therefore what happened to the <em>relationships</em> among those facts.</div>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="generativity-transformation">2. The compression is documentarily visible<a class="heading-anchor" href="#generativity-transformation">¶</a></h2>
<p>The hospital's portable referral/problem representation foregrounded difficulty initiating movement, four-limb increased tone/stiffness and wrist cogwheeling. Compared with the GP input, that representation is narrower: the feet, gait, toe clawing, inability to relax, functional trajectory and explicit “parkinsonian or other” relationship lose equivalent prominence.</p>
<blockquote><p><strong>The compression does not need to be inferred from anyone's private mental state. It is visible by comparing what went into the hospital with the clinical object reproduced in the hospital's own portable representation.</strong></p></blockquote>
<p>This does not prove the compression was clinically unreasonable. It identifies the transformation that a later expert or reviewer must evaluate.</p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="generativity-response">3. What response did the relational input require?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#generativity-response">¶</a></h2>
<p>At documentary level, the referral placed the feet–gait–tone relationship inside the problem sent for neurological assessment. Once Neurology additionally documented a dystonic-looking lower-limb sign, any later separation of that territory became an explanatory act requiring an evidential basis.</p>
<p>That does <strong>not</strong> establish what specific 2017 examination, investigation or treatment the standard of care required. Those remain clinical expert questions. The narrower documentary proposition is that the relationship had entered the hospital's information state and therefore could not be treated as though it had never been part of the problem.</p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="generativity-authority">4. The output was authority-bearing<a class="heading-anchor" href="#generativity-authority">¶</a></h2>
<p>The resulting representation came from Consultant Neurology, was recorded as having undergone Complex Case consideration, and generated specialist-to-specialist correspondence, treatment planning and service destinations. That gave it legitimate institutional authority and made later reliance upon it rational.</p>
<blockquote><p><strong>Authority increases portability; it does not itself increase evidential independence.</strong></p></blockquote>
<p>The more authoritative an early representation becomes, the more important it is to distinguish later <em>inheritance</em> of that representation from later <em>independent re-derivation</em> of its premises.</p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="generativity-output">5. The representation was generative<a class="heading-anchor" href="#generativity-output">¶</a></h2>
<p>The 2017 model did not merely describe the pathway. It generated the pathway's future information state: treatment strategy, Podiatry and rehabilitation destinations, follow-up structure, genetic questions, symptom categories, correspondence, and terminology available to GPs, nurses, other services and eventually the patient himself.</p>
<blockquote><p><strong>The model did not merely describe the pathway. It helped produce the pathway that later appeared to corroborate the model.</strong></p></blockquote>
<p>This is why documentary multiplicity cannot be counted naively. A later note, referral or patient communication may be a new document without constituting a new independent evidential event if its organising category descends from the original specialist representation.</p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="generativity-three-part-test">The three-part opening test<a class="heading-anchor" href="#generativity-three-part-test">¶</a></h2>
<div class="independence-test"><strong>Input adequacy:</strong> What clinically material information was demonstrably available?<br/><br/><strong>Representational adequacy:</strong> What relationships did the specialist response preserve, adjudicate, compress or reassign?<br/><br/><strong>Generative authority:</strong> What later classifications, service destinations, management decisions and apparent corroboration were downstream products of that response rather than genuinely independent of it?</div>
<p>This framework remains compatible with conscientious care and legitimate diagnostic uncertainty. It tests the information architecture without converting documentary transformation into a claim about motive.</p>
</section><section class="reader-section kind-current" data-section="Expertise at the point of compression" id="section-expertise">
<div class="section-kicker">Expertise proximity · why the question matters</div>
<h2 class="source-h1 anchored-heading" id="expertise-at-compression">The relevant disease territory was not remote from the network surrounding the case<a class="heading-anchor" href="#expertise-at-compression">¶</a></h2>
<p>The strongest use of the specialist context is not to say that PRKN should have been diagnosed immediately or that every painful foot symptom should have been classified as dystonia. It is to ask whether the <strong>right expertise was applied to the right clinical object</strong>.</p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="expertise-record-facts">What the record itself establishes<a class="heading-anchor" href="#expertise-record-facts">¶</a></h2>
<p>Contemporaneous clinician-to-clinician correspondence described a 39-year-old first presenting to Neurology after an apparent thirteen-year symptom history and sought Daniel Healy's advice on genetics and treatment. The same page states: <strong>“Myself and Tim are keen to hold back on Levodopa insofar as maybe possible unless you'd advise otherwise?”</strong> The page itself does not identify “Tim”; later clinician material identifies him as Professor Timothy Counihan. The exact original date/header of that reproduced page remains a source gap. <span class="record-ref">CLIN-001720–001733; CLIN-003423–003437</span></p>
<p>After personally reviewing the patient in September 2017, Professor Healy wrote that he thought the patient <strong>“probably has Park II”</strong>, recommended testing PARK2 first, recorded a dramatic motor improvement with low-dose procyclidine and rasagiline, and stated that PARK II patients can manage for long periods on relatively low dopamine replacement while becoming markedly dyskinetic if over-used. <span class="record-ref">CLIN-001742–001760</span></p>
<p>The 2026 laboratory report later identified two pathogenic PRKN variants, including <strong>c.1289G&gt;A; p.Gly430Asp (G430D)</strong>. That result establishes the endpoint; it must not be used as proof of what clinicians were required to know in July 2017. <span class="record-ref">CLIN-006195–006203</span></p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="expertise-external-context">Contemporaneous scholarly context — externally verified<a class="heading-anchor" href="#expertise-external-context">¶</a></h2>
<p>Healy and Counihan were co-authors of the 2013 <em>JAMA Neurology</em> paper <em>Parkin Disease: A Clinicopathologic Entity?</em>. The paper described genetically confirmed parkin disease with early onset and dystonia among presenting features, and its genetic series included the <strong>c.1289G&gt;A, p.G430D</strong> variant.</p>
<p>In August 2017, Counihan and colleagues in the University Hospital Galway Neurology Department published <em>Clinical Reasoning: A demure teenager and her dystonic foot</em>. That case involved abnormal gait/dystonic foot phenomenology, compound-heterozygous parkin mutations including <strong>c.1289G&gt;A</strong>, procyclidine treatment, and discussion of dyskinesia risk in recessive Parkinsonism.</p>
<div class="source-note"><strong>Temporal restraint:</strong> the dystonic-foot paper was published online on 14 August 2017, after the July admission. It therefore cannot be treated as a paper Monaghan necessarily had in hand during the admission. Its legitimate use is narrower: it demonstrates that highly adjacent parkin/dystonia expertise was present in the Galway professional environment during the same period. It does not establish who discussed that case with whom or what Counihan personally reviewed in this patient's July presentation.</div>
<p class="reference-cite">External references: <a href="https://jamanetwork.com/journals/jamaneurology/fullarticle/1659731" rel="noopener" target="_blank">Doherty et al., JAMA Neurology 2013 — Parkin Disease: A Clinicopathologic Entity?</a> · <a href="https://www.neurology.org/doi/10.1212/WNL.0000000000004231" rel="noopener" target="_blank">Cullinane et al., Neurology 2017 — A demure teenager and her dystonic foot</a>.</p>
<div class="independence-test"><strong>The case may not show failure to recognise the disease territory. It may show failure to preserve the clinical object to which the available expertise needed to be applied.</strong></div>
<p>The central conditional question is therefore whether the original feet–gait–tone–posture–pain relationship survived the Monday-to-Tuesday transformation with enough force to become the object of collective specialist reasoning. If it did, the evidential basis for the partition requires explanation. If it did not, the possible systems failure is upstream problem representation.</p>
</section><section class="reader-section kind-current" data-section="Continuity is not convergence" id="section-independence">
<div class="section-kicker">Evidential independence · the closed-loop question</div>
<h2 class="source-h1 anchored-heading" id="continuity-not-convergence">Continuity is not convergence<a class="heading-anchor" href="#continuity-not-convergence">¶</a></h2>
<p>The longitudinal record appears to contain very substantial continuity of neurological authorship and interpretation. That continuity is evidentially important: it can demonstrate what the treating specialist consistently thought, how carefully the model was documented, and how management evolved within it. It cannot, merely by accumulating, establish that the disputed originating partition was repeatedly and independently re-derived.</p>
<blockquote><p><strong>Documentary density is not evidential independence. Continuity of authorship is not independent confirmation.</strong></p></blockquote>
<p>This matters because apparently different documentary sources may be downstream of the same originating representation. GP referrals, nursing communications, other service notes and even the patient's own later use of terms such as “neuropathy” may reproduce specialist vocabulary without independently establishing the proposition that vocabulary carries.</p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="independence-counting-rule">Count independent evidential events, not mentions<a class="heading-anchor" href="#independence-counting-rule">¶</a></h2>
<p>The correct forensic question is not “How many documents mention neuropathy?” It is:</p>
<div class="independence-test"><strong>How many genuinely independent evidential events support the disputed classification, and precisely which proposition does each event establish?</strong></div>
<p>Objective support for Parkinsonism, an external specialist opinion supporting PARK2, a new examination, a laboratory result, a fresh treatment-state observation, or a genuinely independent re-derivation of the feet–pain relationship are different evidential events. Repetition, copying, summarising or downstream adoption of an existing category is not the same thing.</p>
<div class="inference-ladder"><span>documentation existed</span><i>→</i><span>subject was mentioned</span><i>→</i><span>possibility was recognised</span><i>→</i><span>proposition was investigated</span><i>→</i><span>relationship was adjudicated</span><i>→</i><span>management was appropriate</span></div>
<p>Those steps are not interchangeable. A later letter can truthfully show that something was discussed without proving that the underlying proposition was independently revalidated.</p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="independence-2025">The 2025 response: explanation versus independent revalidation<a class="heading-anchor" href="#independence-2025">¶</a></h2>
<p>The difficult feature of the complaint stage is not that the originating clinician was forbidden from explaining his own care. His explanation is directly relevant to historical rationale. The evidential distinction is that <strong>self-explanation and independent revalidation perform different functions</strong>.</p>
<p>A response can show that treatment was cautious, second opinions were sought, genetics was discussed, dyskinesia risk was considered and medication was adjusted. All of that may be true while leaving the upstream question untouched: <strong>was the original separation of painful feet, dystonic lower-limb phenomena and Parkinsonism adequately founded and later independently reconstructed?</strong></p>
<p>The parallel institutional question is therefore:</p>
<blockquote><p><strong>Did the hospital independently test the clinical model, or principally test the reasonableness of decisions made within that model?</strong></p></blockquote>
<p>The latter can establish internal coherence and prospective defensibility. It does not automatically establish the validity of the originating representation.</p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="independence-self-sealing">Self-sealing coherence — structural hypothesis, not motive<a class="heading-anchor" href="#independence-self-sealing">¶</a></h2>
<p>A clinical model can become functionally self-sealing without anyone acting dishonestly. New observations may repeatedly be accommodated by auxiliary explanations—pain modulation, local OFF dystonia, structural lesions, pregabalin response, medication timing—without making the parent partition itself the proposition under test.</p>
<p>The strongest systems hypothesis is therefore not conspiracy or concealment. It is <strong>recursive coherence</strong>: the model generates categories; the categories organise care; care produces documentation; documentation demonstrates continuity; later observations are interpreted through the categories; and a complaint is then assessed against the accumulated record. If nobody deliberately interrupts that chain with a first-principles reconstruction, persistence can be mistaken for validation.</p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="independence-weight-bearing">Weight-bearing language<a class="heading-anchor" href="#independence-weight-bearing">¶</a></h2>
<p><strong>A statement's literal truth does not determine the inferential weight it can safely bear.</strong> “Sinemet since 2021,” “second opinion,” “neuropathic pain,” “idiopathic Parkinson's,” “Lyrica removed the pain,” and “as the letters show” may each be accurate in some limited sense while carrying broader implications that require separate proof.</p>
<blockquote><p><strong>Direction is not magnitude; magnitude is not mechanism; documentary continuity is not independent validation.</strong></p></blockquote>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="independence-decisive-test">The decisive independence test<a class="heading-anchor" href="#independence-decisive-test">¶</a></h2>
<div class="independence-test"><strong>Did an appropriately qualified, genuinely independent reviewer ever return to the untreated/source material, temporarily suspend the inherited labels, reconstruct the feet–gait–tone–dystonia–pain syndrome from first principles, incorporate the procyclidine experiment, PARK2 evidence, later dyskinesia, OFF dystonia, toe curling and medication-state pain, and independently conclude that the original causal separation remained the best explanation?</strong></div>
<p>If yes, the closed-loop hypothesis weakens substantially. If no such reconstruction can be identified, extraordinary longitudinal consistency should not automatically be treated as repeated independent validation. It may principally demonstrate continuity of one model.</p>
<div class="callout"><strong>Safeguarding remains separate.</strong><br/>Even if every diagnostic and therapeutic interpretation remained reasonable, the later record's severe language and contraction of life create an independent question about recognition and response to escalating human risk. That issue does not depend on proving the pain mechanism.</div>
</section>
<section class="reader-section kind-current kind-reference" data-section="Current case architecture" id="section-current-architecture">
<div class="section-kicker">Full reference synthesis · preserved in full</div>
<div class="archive-note"><strong>Reference layer.</strong> The integrated governing spine above controls where older formulations differ in emphasis. This section is preserved because its detailed reasoning, counter-case, falsifiers and expert questions remain useful; it should not be read as a separate competing theory.</div><h2 class="source-h1 anchored-heading" id="130-tidied-case-architecture-v0-9">130. Integrated case architecture — v0.17<a class="heading-anchor" href="#130-tidied-case-architecture-v0-9">¶</a></h2>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="130-1-what-is-now-treated-as-established-documentary-fact">130.1 What is now treated as established documentary fact<a class="heading-anchor" href="#130-1-what-is-now-treated-as-established-documentary-fact">¶</a></h2>
<ol>
<li>The GP's 2017 referral carried a broad deteriorating relational problem rather than a bare Parkinsonism query.</li>
<li>The hospital's concise neurological task narrowed that object before detailed Neurology history-taking.</li>
<li>Neurology later recovered many omitted facts, so this is not a simple information-loss case.</li>
<li>The 2017 examination directly documented a striking pseudo-foot-drop gait somewhat suggestive of dystonic posture and explicitly “NOT FOOT DROP,” alongside structural foot abnormalities.</li>
<li>The specialist synthesis simultaneously created a tentative separate/peripheral-neuropathic branch for painful/burning/structural lower-limb material and marked it “Not clear.”</li>
<li>The Parkinsonian/genetic branch received much higher visible evidential resolution than the peripheral-neuropathic branch.</li>
<li>“As per above” made the mixed information state operational without demonstrating that all relationships had been epistemically closed.</li>
<li>Subsequent records show both documentary inheritance and genuine local reassessment.</li>
<li>Primary care repeatedly preserved or regenerated cross-domain relationships while deferring specialist-level adjudication.</li>
<li>By March 2025 the complaint response reconstructed management history more completely than it reconstructed the provenance and revalidation history of the disputed categories.</li>
<li>The admission chronology places the untreated specialist assessment on Monday 3 July 2017 and the department's Tuesday Complex Case Meeting on 4 July, creating a narrow opening interval in which the governing representation may have consolidated.</li><li>Relevant PARK2/dystonia expertise was present in the professional network around the case; this strengthens the question of problem representation but does not prove that every relevant relationship was considered or that PRKN should have been diagnosed in July 2017.</li><li>Longitudinal documentary multiplicity must be distinguished from evidential independence: later repetition of inherited terminology does not by itself constitute fresh revalidation.</li><li>The 2025 complaint response is substantively informative about the originating clinician's rationale, but self-explanation is not equivalent to independent first-principles reconstruction of the framework under challenge.</li><li>The opening 2017 event is now best analysed as observable input → transformation → authority-bearing output → downstream generativity: the hospital had a rich relational input, produced a narrower portable task representation, and that authoritative representation then generated treatment, referrals, destinations and terminology that later records could inherit.</li><li>Because the representation itself generated downstream documentation, documentary multiplicity must be genealogically audited before it is treated as independent convergence.</li></ol>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="130-2-strongest-documentary-inference">130.2 Strongest documentary inference<a class="heading-anchor" href="#130-2-strongest-documentary-inference">¶</a></h2>
<blockquote><p><strong>A broad relational presentation appears to have been transformed very early into a narrower, authority-bearing specialist architecture. The Parkinsonian/PRKN problem then received high-resolution collective reasoning while the painful-foot relationship remained less fully adjudicated. Because that specialist output generated treatment, referrals, service destinations and later terminology, the model did not merely describe the pathway: it helped produce the documentary pathway that later appeared to corroborate it. Later boundary-crossing evidence was repeatedly assimilated locally, while the record more clearly demonstrates continuity and generativity of the originating model than genuinely independent reconstruction of the disputed partition.</strong></p></blockquote>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="130-3-strongest-therapeutic-expert-question">130.3 Strongest therapeutic expert question<a class="heading-anchor" href="#130-3-strongest-therapeutic-expert-question">¶</a></h2>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>Did the inherited separation of the painful-foot territory from the dystonic/Parkinsonian territory later constrain whether PRKN suspicion, anticholinergic response/withdrawal, levodopa sensitivity, dyskinesia, OFF dystonia, medication timing and pain were assembled and tested as a single dose-state lower-limb problem?</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>This is an expert question, not a documentary conclusion.</p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="130-4-strongest-information-quality-question">130.4 Strongest information-quality question<a class="heading-anchor" href="#130-4-strongest-information-quality-question">¶</a></h2>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>Was the 2021 statement that DBS was “only ever for the tremor element” an adequately accurate explanation of the therapeutic scope of DBS in the context then documented?</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>This is deliberately separated from any assertion that DBS should have been offered or performed.</p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="130-5-strongest-governance-question">130.5 Strongest governance question<a class="heading-anchor" href="#130-5-strongest-governance-question">¶</a></h2>
<blockquote><p><strong>When the upstream representation itself was challenged, did governance processes independently reconstruct that representation from source evidence, or principally assess whether the historical decisions were reasonable within the representation already embedded in the record?</strong></p></blockquote>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="130-6-strongest-retrospective-defence-question">130.6 Strongest retrospective-defence question<a class="heading-anchor" href="#130-6-strongest-retrospective-defence-question">¶</a></h2>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>Does the March 2025 response establish independent revalidation of the disputed categories, or does it more strongly establish longstanding documentation and management within those categories?</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="130-7-claims-retired-or-quarantined">130.7 Claims retired or quarantined<a class="heading-anchor" href="#130-7-claims-retired-or-quarantined">¶</a></h2>
<p>The following should not appear as established propositions in future drafts:</p>
<ul>
<li>“the feet were ignored”;</li>
<li>“Neurology never reconsidered”;</li>
<li>“the pain was dystonia”;</li>
<li>“the neuropathy diagnosis was fabricated”;</li>
<li>“PRKN should obviously have been diagnosed in 2017”;</li>
<li>“levodopa response proves neurological causation”;</li>
<li>“there was a therapeutic-window failure”;</li>
<li>“DBS should have been performed”;</li>
<li>“the hospital recognised its error and protected itself”;</li>
<li>“the record was constructed to conceal a mistake.”</li>
</ul>
<p>Permitted substitutes are respectively:</p>
<ul>
<li>relationships were unevenly resolved;</li>
<li>local reassessment occurred without clearly identified global reconstruction;</li>
<li>dystonia/pain relationship requires expert adjudication;</li>
<li>neuropathic classification provenance and revalidation require reconstruction;</li>
<li>PRKN was a specific contemporaneous unresolved hypothesis;</li>
<li>treatment response generated a testable hypothesis but was not diagnostic proof;</li>
<li>the record documents a balancing problem and raises a therapeutic-window question;</li>
<li>the DBS explanation raises an information-quality question;</li>
<li>protective consolidation is a source-dependent hypothesis only;</li>
<li>concealment/falsification are unsupported absent direct evidence.</li>
</ul>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="130-8-the-current-apex-statement">130.8 The current apex statement<a class="heading-anchor" href="#130-8-the-current-apex-statement">¶</a></h2>
<blockquote><p><strong>The strongest current case is that an integrated medication-naïve lower-limb and motor presentation entered hospital with sufficient relational detail to require preservation or explicit adjudication of the feet–gait–tone relationship; that the hospital then produced a narrower, authority-bearing specialist representation before the relationship among painful feet, dystonic-looking posture, structural loading and Parkinsonism had been fully adjudicated; that this representation was generative, producing treatment strategy, service destinations and terminology later inherited across the record; that relevant expertise was available but may have been applied to the narrowed clinical object; that later treatment-state and PRKN-related evidence repeatedly crossed the resulting boundary without a clearly identifiable independent reconstruction of the parent model; and that by 2025 documentary continuity could function as apparent validation even though authority and continuity do not equal independent evidential convergence.</strong></p></blockquote>
</section>
<section class="reader-section kind-current kind-reference" data-section="Current strongest case object" id="section-current-object">
<div class="section-kicker">Full reference synthesis · preserved in full</div>
<div class="archive-note"><strong>Reference layer.</strong> The integrated governing spine above controls where older formulations differ in emphasis. This section is preserved because its detailed reasoning, counter-case, falsifiers and expert questions remain useful; it should not be read as a separate competing theory.</div><h2 class="source-h1 anchored-heading" id="129-strongest-case-object-after-six-pass-resynthesis">129. Strongest case object after six-pass resynthesis<a class="heading-anchor" href="#129-strongest-case-object-after-six-pass-resynthesis">¶</a></h2>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="129-1-the-object">129.1 The object<a class="heading-anchor" href="#129-1-the-object">¶</a></h2>
<p>The strongest case object is no longer “fragmentation” and should not be reduced to “missed dystonia,” “wrong neuropathy,” or “missed PRKN.” It is a longitudinal information-state problem:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>A broad relational presentation was contracted into a narrower specialist task; the omitted material later re-entered as facts, but different parts of the phenotype were resolved at unequal evidential depth. That mixed state became actionable before all relationships were resolved. Service destinations and classifications then travelled more effectively than the uncertainty, provenance and re-entry conditions that originally qualified them. Later boundary-crossing evidence was repeatedly handled through local assimilation/reclassification rather than a clearly identifiable reconstruction of the parent model. By 2025 the accumulated documentary pathway itself was being used retrospectively to explain and defend management, while the disputed category genealogy remained less fully reconstructed.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="129-2-the-2017-fork">129.2 The 2017 fork<a class="heading-anchor" href="#129-2-the-2017-fork">¶</a></h2>
<p>The strongest 2017 internal contrast is now:</p>
<p><strong>High-resolution neurological branch:</strong></p>
<ul>
<li>marked symmetrical tone/rigidity;</li>
<li>gross bradykinesia;</li>
<li>striking gait;</li>
<li>pseudo-foot-drop appearance described as somewhat dystonic and explicitly not true foot drop;</li>
<li>imaging/DaT investigation;</li>
<li>broad young-onset differential;</li>
<li>autosomal-recessive/monogenic reasoning;</li>
<li>specific PRKN/PARK2 suspicion;</li>
<li>outside specialist review.</li>
</ul>
<p><strong>Lower-resolution residual branch:</strong></p>
<ul>
<li>genuine corns/callouses/pes planus/hammertoes;</li>
<li>foot pain;</li>
<li>burning introduced in the specialist synthesis, with later patient-origin support that burning was real;</li>
<li>patient-reported distinction between foot pain and leg/bodily tension;</li>
<li>clinician composite “leg symptoms including corns and callouses and burning”;</li>
<li>possible separate process / possible secondary peripheral neuropathy / “Not clear”;</li>
<li>Podiatry destination.</li>
</ul>
<p>The concern is not that these could not coexist. They plainly could. The documentary question is whether the surviving record shows the discriminating bridge by which the same lower-limb territory was partitioned among dystonic/Parkinsonian, structural/mechanical and peripheral-neuropathic explanations.</p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="129-3-provenance-and-grammatical-status">129.3 Provenance and grammatical status<a class="heading-anchor" href="#129-3-provenance-and-grammatical-status">¶</a></h2>
<p>The strongest provenance finding is that the patient's documented distinction is narrower than the later specialist category. The patient is recorded as distinguishing <strong>foot pain/corns/callouses</strong> from <strong>leg/bodily tension</strong>. The specialist synthesis then creates a broader object — <strong>“leg symptoms including corns and callouses and burning”</strong> — and attaches a possible separate/peripheral-neuropathic hypothesis.</p>
<p>This supports the following documentary chain, subject to the stated qualifications:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>patient phenomena → clinician recomposition → tentative diagnostic hypothesis → named working question → later symptom classification → retrospective affirmative grammar</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>The later 2023 severe bilateral burning episode is genuinely new evidence and prevents the neuropathic pathway being characterised as repetition alone. The narrower point is that fresh evidence and inherited category can coexist, and later repetition is not automatically independent revalidation of the original classification.</p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="129-4-as-per-above-as-the-operational-hinge">129.4 “As per above” as the operational hinge<a class="heading-anchor" href="#129-4-as-per-above-as-the-operational-hinge">¶</a></h2>
<p>“As per above” now has a precise role. It does not erase uncertainty and should not be treated as a declaration that every proposition was solved. Its function is:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>to convert an information state containing both high-confidence and unresolved propositions into a workable management state.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>That allowed medication, referral, rehabilitation, Podiatry, investigations, follow-up and deferred genetics to proceed. The resulting longitudinal vulnerability was not tolerance of uncertainty itself; it was that once uncertainty no longer blocked action, unresolved relationships required a separate ownership/reopening mechanism if they were to remain decision-live.</p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="129-5-distributed-residual-ownership">129.5 Distributed residual ownership<a class="heading-anchor" href="#129-5-distributed-residual-ownership">¶</a></h2>
<p>By discharge, components had owners:</p>
<ul>
<li>Neurology: Parkinsonism, treatment, imaging/genetics, follow-up;</li>
<li>Podiatry: structural foot care;</li>
<li>Physio/OT: rehabilitation and function;</li>
<li>Occupational Health: work capability;</li>
<li>GP: coordination, referrals, prescriptions and longitudinal observation.</li>
</ul>
<p>What is less explicit is ownership of the relationships among those components. The strongest formulation remains:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The components were distributed, while responsibility for reconstructing their relationship is not comparably explicit.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>The GP trial strengthens this further:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The GP kept seeing the arrows; specialist care owned the authority to decide what they meant.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="129-6-local-updating-versus-parent-model-reconstruction">129.6 Local updating versus parent-model reconstruction<a class="heading-anchor" href="#129-6-local-updating-versus-parent-model-reconstruction">¶</a></h2>
<p>Later evidence shows the inherited architecture was permeable:</p>
<ul>
<li>treatment withdrawal linked feet, gait and tension temporally;</li>
<li>later distal power testing supported a Parkinsonian “foot throw”;</li>
<li>OFF dystonia was explicitly considered;</li>
<li>abrupt overnight pain onset was recognised as atypical for peripheral neuropathy;</li>
<li>Fabry was tested as an overlap possibility;</li>
<li>pain varied with dopaminergic state;</li>
<li>pregabalin could reduce pain while toe curling persisted.</li>
</ul>
<p>These are genuine reassessments. The narrow continuing finding is that they are easier to identify as <strong>proposition-specific local updates</strong> than as a single pre-2025 reconstruction of the founding feet–gait–posture–pain relationship.</p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="129-7-march-2025-as-retrospective-test">129.7 March 2025 as retrospective test<a class="heading-anchor" href="#129-7-march-2025-as-retrospective-test">¶</a></h2>
<p>The March 2025 response is now best understood as a test of whether documentary continuity equals evidential genealogy. It contains substantial retrospective clinical reasoning, particularly around treatment choices, dyskinesia risk, Sinemet, pregabalin, Fabry and pain modulation. But it does not equivalently reconstruct how the contested categories obtained their present status.</p>
<p>For pain:</p>
<ul>
<li>2017: possible separate process / perhaps secondary peripheral neuropathy / “Not clear”;</li>
<li>later: “the neuropathy question” / “? neuropathic pain”;</li>
<li>2025: “peripheral neuropathic pain symptomatologies that Liam has” and “neuropathic pain (of whatever cause).”</li>
</ul>
<p>The strongest distinction is:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>the 2025 response reopens the cause of the neuropathic category more clearly than it reopens whether the historical phenotype should itself be classified as neuropathic.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>For Parkinsonism:</p>
<ul>
<li>syndrome-level continuity is strong;</li>
<li>etiology remained specifically open around PRKN/PARK2;</li>
<li>testing was deliberately deferred because it did not alter current management;</li>
<li>no clear pre-2025 documentary bridge has been identified showing PRKN rejection before affirmative iPD language became routine.</li>
</ul>
<p>Thus:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The record demonstrates continuity of the Parkinsonian syndrome far more clearly than it demonstrates closure of its etiology.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="129-8-as-the-letters-show-and-documentary-accretion">129.8 “As the letters show” and documentary accretion<a class="heading-anchor" href="#129-8-as-the-letters-show-and-documentary-accretion">¶</a></h2>
<p>The sequence now supported is:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>mixed 2017 synthesis → “As per above” → actionable plan → inherited summaries (“as per older letters”) → local updates → accumulated longitudinal documentation → retrospective invocation (“As the letters show”).</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>This is not inherently improper. Longitudinal medicine requires documentary inheritance. The information-governance question is narrower:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>Can repeated documentary continuity increase the practical stability of a category even where the original evidential transition and its unresolved qualifiers are not repeatedly reconstructed?</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>The working answer is yes as a documentary mechanism, with proposition-specific exceptions where later evidence genuinely revalidates a claim.</p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="129-9-strongest-governance-formulation">129.9 Strongest governance formulation<a class="heading-anchor" href="#129-9-strongest-governance-formulation">¶</a></h2>
<p>The strongest governance formulation is not “the diagnosis was wrong” and not “nobody cared for the feet.” It is:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The system successfully resolved one high-priority diagnostic object but did not make equally clear who retained responsibility for the unresolved relationships left outside that object.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>A related professional information question is:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>When a tentative specialist classification reorganises downstream care, how much of its provenance, evidential basis, uncertainty, competing evidence and reopening conditions must travel with the label so that repetition does not silently upgrade hypothesis into history?</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>Whether any standard was breached remains an expert/legal question.</p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="129-10-strongest-defence">129.10 Strongest defence<a class="heading-anchor" href="#129-10-strongest-defence">¶</a></h2>
<p>The strongest defence remains substantial:</p>
<ul>
<li>the patient genuinely had heterogeneous lower-limb phenomena;</li>
<li>structural foot disease was real;</li>
<li>symptom dissociations existed;</li>
<li>the Parkinsonian syndrome was strongly supported;</li>
<li>the consultant performed extensive investigation and later genuine reconsideration;</li>
<li>2023 is serious counterevidence to any claim of a frozen model;</li>
<li>later burning symptoms provided fresh grounds for neuropathic consideration;</li>
<li>medicine legitimately proceeds under uncertainty and does not require every causal relationship to be solved before treatment.</li>
</ul>
<p>The competing explanation is therefore:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>clinically appropriate decomposition of genuinely different phenomena, followed by reasonable longitudinal management within a well-supported Parkinsonian framework.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>The record alone cannot decide the medical validity of that defence.</p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="129-11-strongest-falsifiers">129.11 Strongest falsifiers<a class="heading-anchor" href="#129-11-strongest-falsifiers">¶</a></h2>
<p>The case object would be materially weakened by any of the following contemporaneous evidence:</p>
<ol>
<li><strong>2017 lower-limb discriminating bridge:</strong> bedside/ward notes showing detailed distal sensory mapping, proposition-specific motor testing, dynamic toe/posture assessment, structural/loading reasoning and an explicit explanation distinguishing dystonic/Parkinsonian, structural and neuropathic components.</li>
<li><strong>2017 ownership/reopening plan:</strong> complex-case/discharge documentation explicitly stating that the feet–gait–tone relationship remained unresolved, assigning an owner, requiring Podiatry feedback and defining a review trigger.</li>
<li><strong>Independent early reconstruction:</strong> a Beaumont/Movement Disorders assessment that received the original GP relational presentation, rebuilt it from first principles and independently arrived at substantially the same partition.</li>
<li><strong>Pre-2025 global reconstruction:</strong> a later specialist note explicitly recombining structural feet, pain/burning, dynamic toe posture, dystonic gait, OFF state, medication responses and neuropathic possibilities into a newly reasoned parent model.</li>
<li><strong>PRKN closure bridge:</strong> a pre-2025 source explicitly preserving PRKN as unresolved operational shorthand or showing why it was downgraded/excluded before affirmative idiopathic terminology became established.</li>
</ol>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="129-12-shortest-protected-formulations">129.12 Shortest protected formulations<a class="heading-anchor" href="#129-12-shortest-protected-formulations">¶</a></h2>
<p>The following should now be protected from drift unless primary evidence forces revision:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The critical transformation occurred not when the facts disappeared, but between their recovery and their synthesis.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The record is less a story of facts being forgotten than of relationships being reformatted once, then inherited.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The facts came back. The relationships did not come back with equal force.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The sophisticated reasoning is not merely uneven in quantity; it differs in evidential resolution.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>“As per above” marks operational closure before epistemic closure was complete.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The components were distributed, while responsibility for reconstructing their relationship is not comparably explicit.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The GP kept seeing the arrows; specialist care owned the authority to decide what they meant.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>When the model was fresh, uncertainty generated questions; once the model was inherited, uncertainty was more often managed within it.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>By 2025 the response reconstructs management more completely than it reconstructs the evidential genealogy of the disputed classifications.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The deepest issue is the conversion of an incompletely discriminated specialist classification into a durable organising structure for later facts.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The record is unusually well equipped to defend the decisions that were made, but materially less well equipped to show that the relationships excluded from those decisions were equivalently tested.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The retrospective defence may convert documentary continuity into evidential continuity.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>Do not infer recognised error, institutional protection, concealment or falsification without internal contemporaneous evidence demonstrating that transition.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="129-13-endpoint-discipline">129.13 Endpoint discipline<a class="heading-anchor" href="#129-13-endpoint-discipline">¶</a></h2>
<p>The 2026 PRKN result remains an endpoint only. It establishes that Parkinsonism and dystonia ultimately received a common molecular explanation. It does not prove that every historical foot symptom was dystonic, that peripheral/structural explanations were necessarily wrong, or that earlier clinicians should have reached the molecular diagnosis. Its documentary significance is that it resolves a question the historical record itself had already generated and deliberately left open.</p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="129-14-therapeutic-phenotype-consequence-branch">129.14 Therapeutic-phenotype consequence branch<a class="heading-anchor" href="#129-14-therapeutic-phenotype-consequence-branch">¶</a></h2>
<p>This branch is subordinate to the information-pathway case object. It does not convert the documentary case into a retrospective claim that the historical pain was PRKN-mediated, dystonic, or necessarily caused by under-treatment.</p>
<p>The expert-level question is narrower:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>Did the inherited separation of the painful-foot territory from the dystonic/Parkinsonian territory later constrain whether a PRKN-relevant therapeutic phenotype was assembled and tested as a whole?</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>The record contains a potentially important sequence:</p>
<ul>
<li>PRKN/PARK2 was a specific contemporaneous hypothesis rather than a later invention;</li>
<li>the 2017 lower limb was directly described as dystonic-looking rather than true foot drop;</li>
