<li>external specialist advice reportedly linked PARK2 to low-dose dopaminergic responsiveness and dyskinesia risk;</li>
<li>later levodopa exposure was constrained by dyskinesia/chorea;</li>
<li>later records contain possible OFF dystonia, insufficient-coverage reasoning, medication-timed lower-limb symptoms and pain modulation;</li>
<li>sustained/overnight dopaminergic coverage later changed the exposure pattern and was followed by reported marked morning improvement.</li>
</ul>
<p>None of these propositions independently proves a common mechanism. Together they justify a movement-disorders expert question about whether the longitudinal record contains both a <strong>dopaminergic ceiling</strong> (dyskinesia at higher exposure) and possible <strong>dopaminergic trough phenomena</strong> (OFF dystonia, morning symptoms, pain or gait deterioration at lower/waning exposure), without a clearly visible unified therapeutic-window model of the feet.</p>
<p>The protected formulation is:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The question is not whether levodopa response proves the cause of pain. It is whether repeated medication-state effects, dystonic lower-limb findings and treatment-limiting dyskinesia should have triggered structured testing of a dose-state lower-limb hypothesis.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>A separate information-quality question concerns the 2021 DBS explanation. If the source wording is accurately preserved as DBS being “only ever for the tremor element” and not helping anything else, the expert question is not whether DBS should have been performed. It is:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>Was the information supplied about the therapeutic scope of DBS sufficiently accurate for a young, levodopa-responsive patient already developing dyskinesia and motor-state complexity?</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>This is deliberately kept separate from the foot-pain causation case. It is an information-quality / informed-discussion question requiring contemporaneous standards and expert review.</p>
<p>The strongest falsifiers for this branch would be contemporaneous notes showing that the treating team explicitly modelled and tested the lower-limb symptoms against ON/OFF state, formulation, timing and dyskinesia constraints, or that the apparent DBS wording is incomplete/misleading when read in full context.</p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="129-15-prospective-defensibility-asymmetric-epistemic-documentation-and-constructive-retrospective-defence">129.15 Prospective defensibility, asymmetric epistemic documentation, and constructive retrospective defence<a class="heading-anchor" href="#129-15-prospective-defensibility-asymmetric-epistemic-documentation-and-constructive-retrospective-defence">¶</a></h2>
<p>A further documentary branch now requires explicit protection against both under-reading and overstatement.</p>
<p>The record contains unusually detailed contemporaneous explanations for decisions that later became central to the 2025 complaint response: why levodopa was delayed, why genetic confirmation might not alter immediate management, why the patient was said to understand the strategy, why external advice was sought, and even the contemporaneous possibility that the team might later regret not starting levodopa earlier.</p>
<p>The existence of such forward-looking reasoning does <strong>not</strong> establish that the record was written dishonestly or in anticipation of litigation. The strongest benign explanation remains <strong>ordinary conscientious documentation of a difficult young-onset case</strong>. A second plausible layer is <strong>prospective defensibility</strong>: deliberately recording why a difficult decision was made so that it could later be understood and defended. That can be entirely proper.</p>
<p>The serious documentary question begins one step later:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>Did the record become progressively better at preserving the rationale for the decisions actually made than at preserving and re-testing the relationships excluded by those decisions?</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>This is best described as <strong>asymmetric epistemic documentation</strong> or, where it affects later framing, <strong>defensive problem-framing</strong>. These terms do not imply dishonesty, concealment or motive.</p>
<p>The strongest supported formulation is:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The record is unusually well equipped to defend the decisions that were made, but materially less well equipped to show that the relationships excluded from those decisions were equivalently tested.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>A related asymmetry concerns dopaminergic management. The rationale for caution at the upper end of exposure is extensively preserved: long-term strategy, concern about early dyskinesia, external advice and later treatment-limiting chorea/dyskinesia. The corresponding lower-end question—whether restricted or waning exposure permitted OFF dystonia, gait failure or painful lower-limb phenomena—is less visibly developed as a unified model. This does not establish under-treatment. It identifies an asymmetry in what the record explains at high resolution.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="129-15-1-march-2025-as-an-explicitly-explanatory-defensive-information-product">129.15.1 March 2025 as an explicitly explanatory/defensive information product<a class="heading-anchor" href="#129-15-1-march-2025-as-an-explicitly-explanatory-defensive-information-product">¶</a></h3>
<p>The complaint response should be analysed as what it openly is: a retrospective explanatory document prepared for review, not an ordinary contemporaneous clinic note. There is nothing inherently improper about that function.</p>
<p>Its historical method nevertheless requires proposition-level discrimination. The following are not equivalent:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>documentation existed → a subject was mentioned → a possibility was recognised → a proposition was investigated → a relationship was adjudicated → the resulting management was appropriate.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>The central risk is therefore not necessarily factual falsity. It is that a retrospective narrative may assemble true pieces into an argument whose logical force exceeds what the underlying historical evidence establishes.</p>
<p>The protected formulation is:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The retrospective defence may convert documentary continuity into evidential continuity.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>For example, repeated documentation can strongly establish that Sinemet was prescribed, that pain effects were recognised, that neuropathy was considered, or that PRKN was once discussed. It does not automatically establish that the relevant causal proposition was independently re-derived, that formulation/timing tested an OFF-state hypothesis, or that a provisional category was revalidated each time it reappeared.</p>
<p>This is best labelled <strong>constructive defensibility</strong> when facts are assembled into a coherent retrospective defence without evidence that the historical reasoning was as complete or linear as the retrospective account may suggest. It remains a documentary inference, not an allegation of deception.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="129-15-2-evidential-tiers-and-stopping-rule">129.15.2 Evidential tiers and stopping rule<a class="heading-anchor" href="#129-15-2-evidential-tiers-and-stopping-rule">¶</a></h3>
<p>The present evidential position should be held at the following levels:</p>
<ol>
<li><strong>Ordinary conscientious documentation — strongly plausible.</strong></li>
<li><strong>Prospective defensibility — plausible and documentarily supported as a style/function.</strong></li>
<li><strong>Defensive problem-framing / asymmetric epistemic documentation — strongest serious hypothesis presently supported.</strong></li>
<li><strong>Constructive retrospective defence — meaningful and testable, but requires source-by-source checking.</strong></li>
<li><strong>Institutional protective consolidation after recognition of error — hypothesis only; not established.</strong></li>
<li><strong>Deliberate concealment, falsification or deceptive record construction — not supported by the present record.</strong></li>
</ol>
<p>The anti-drift stopping rule is explicit:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>Do not infer a moment of recognised error, institutional protection, concealment, or falsification unless internal contemporaneous material demonstrates the transition.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>To move materially from Tier 4 to Tier 5 would require a temporal sequence visible in internal evidence: open uncertainty before a particular point; recognition that the earlier interpretation may have materially affected care; and subsequent communications showing a decision to frame, restrict, consolidate or defend the earlier position rather than independently reopen it.</p>
<p>The relevant evidence is therefore institutional “backstage” material rather than another public-facing clinical letter: QPS communications, complaint-response drafts and tracked changes, SIMT/PAR materials, internal Neurology/Clinical Director correspondence, review-selection records, version histories, and evidence showing whether contrary sources were specifically considered.</p>
<p>The strongest rebuttal to Tier 5 would be internal material showing a transparent attempt to reconstruct the case from first principles, preserve inconvenient evidence, obtain independent movement-disorders expertise, and determine whether the earlier model was wrong.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="129-15-3-relationship-to-the-core-case-object">129.15.3 Relationship to the core case object<a class="heading-anchor" href="#129-15-3-relationship-to-the-core-case-object">¶</a></h3>
<p>This branch does not replace the established mechanism. It sharpens one consequence of it:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>operational closure can generate documentary continuity; documentary continuity can later supply retrospective defensibility; but neither step by itself proves that the unresolved relationships embedded in the original state were independently re-tested.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>The revised complete mechanism is therefore:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>broad relational GP problem → narrower hospital task → factual recovery with altered relationship structure → asymmetric evidential resolution → operational closure (“As per above”) → distributed residual ownership → classification/documentary inheritance → local assimilation/reclassification → documentary accretion → prospective rationale becomes retrospectively reusable → constructive retrospective defence (“As the letters show”) → external reopening → later molecular resolution of one historically open branch.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>The strongest concise statement remains:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The record is strongest at showing why the chosen pathway made sense from inside the pathway; it is weaker at showing that the relationships left outside that pathway were equivalently re-derived as new evidence accumulated.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<hr/>
</section>
<section class="reader-section kind-current" data-section="Core thesis and anti-drift rules" id="section-core-thesis">
<div class="section-kicker">Core thesis and anti-drift rules</div>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="core-thesis-tidied-hierarchy">Core thesis — tidied hierarchy<a class="heading-anchor" href="#core-thesis-tidied-hierarchy">¶</a></h3>
<p>The case is presently strongest when kept in four separate evidential layers. The first two are documentary; the third requires clinical expert adjudication; the fourth is a governance/institutional hypothesis requiring additional sources. None should be allowed to borrow certainty from the layer above it.</p>
<h4 class="anchored-heading" id="layer-1-established-record-architecture">Layer 1 — Established record architecture<a class="heading-anchor" href="#layer-1-established-record-architecture">¶</a></h4>
<p>The GP sent a broad, medication-naive relational problem involving feet, gait, tone, movement initiation, toe phenomena, dexterity, function and deterioration under an open “parkinsonian or other” question. Hospital Neurology then established the Parkinsonian syndrome at high evidential resolution. The same 2017 record also contained structural foot pathology, an observed pseudo-foot-drop gait described as somewhat suggestive of dystonic posture, painful foot phenomena, and a tentative separate/peripheral-neuropathic branch.</p>
<p>The record later shows that these branches did not receive equivalent evidential development. The Parkinsonian/genetic branch received detailed examination, imaging, DaT investigation, complex differential reasoning, genetic consideration and outside specialist input. The painful/structural/peripheral branch was plausible and partly supported, but the surviving record does not show an equally explicit proposition-by-proposition bridge discriminating dystonic/Parkinsonian, structural/mechanical and neuropathic components within the same lower-limb territory.</p>
<h4 class="anchored-heading" id="layer-2-strongest-documentary-inference">Layer 2 — Strongest documentary inference<a class="heading-anchor" href="#layer-2-strongest-documentary-inference">¶</a></h4>
<p>The strongest case object is therefore:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>A broad relational problem was contracted into a narrower specialist task; omitted material later re-entered as facts, but the relationships among those facts were reformatted and resolved at unequal evidential depth. That mixed state became operational before all explanatory relationships were closed. The resulting classifications, service destinations and management rationales became more portable than the provenance, uncertainty and reopening conditions that originally qualified them. Later cross-boundary evidence generated genuine local reassessment, but the surviving record more clearly documents local assimilation/reclassification than a first-principles reconstruction of the parent model.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>The shortest protected statement is:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The decomposition became durable before its clinical basis had been adequately established.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>The controlling internal contrast remains:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The problem is not lack of sophistication. It is where the sophistication was applied.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>And the same-author control remains:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>When the model was fresh, uncertainty generated questions. Once the model was inherited, uncertainty was more often managed within it.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>“As per above” is treated as the operational hinge: not a declaration that uncertainty disappeared, but the point at which a mixed information state became sufficiently organised for treatment, referral and follow-up to proceed. “As the letters show” later illustrates how accumulated documentary continuity could acquire retrospective explanatory force.</p>
<h4 class="anchored-heading" id="layer-3-clinical-expert-questions-generated-by-the-documentary-architecture">Layer 3 — Clinical expert questions generated by the documentary architecture<a class="heading-anchor" href="#layer-3-clinical-expert-questions-generated-by-the-documentary-architecture">¶</a></h4>
<p>The documentary case does not prove that the historical pain was dystonic, PRKN-mediated, neuropathic, mechanical, or caused by under-treatment. It does generate focused expert questions, especially whether the inherited partition later constrained how the following were assembled and tested:</p>
<ul>
<li>the objectively documented dystonic-looking lower-limb sign;</li>
<li>specific contemporaneous PRKN/PARK2 suspicion;</li>
<li>the dramatic early procyclidine/rasagiline response and withdrawal observations;</li>
<li>later removal of Kemadrin/procyclidine because of ocular-pressure concern;</li>
<li>substitution with levodopa;</li>
<li>treatment-limiting chorea/dyskinesia and explicit attempts to keep levodopa exposure minimal;</li>
<li>later OFF-dystonia reasoning, evening under-coverage questions, morning foot symptoms and medication-state pain effects;</li>
<li>and the later reported marked improvement after sustained overnight dopaminergic coverage.</li>
</ul>
<p>The protected therapeutic formulation is:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The question is not whether treatment response proves the cause of pain. It is whether repeated medication-state effects, dystonic lower-limb findings and treatment-limiting dyskinesia should have triggered structured testing of a dose-state lower-limb hypothesis.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>The DBS issue remains separate: the record preserves the 2021 statement that DBS was “only ever for the tremor element” and would “not help with anything else.” The documentary fact is secure; whether that was an adequately accurate account of DBS for this patient is an expert information-quality question, not a conclusion that DBS should have been performed.</p>
<h4 class="anchored-heading" id="layer-4-governance-and-retrospective-defensibility-questions">Layer 4 — Governance and retrospective-defensibility questions<a class="heading-anchor" href="#layer-4-governance-and-retrospective-defensibility-questions">¶</a></h4>
<p>The record contains unusually detailed contemporaneous rationales for decisions that later became central to the March 2025 complaint response. This supports examination of <strong>prospective defensibility</strong> and <strong>asymmetric epistemic documentation</strong>: whether the record became especially effective at preserving the rationale for decisions actually made while remaining less developed in showing that excluded relationships were equivalently re-tested.</p>
<p>The strongest supported formulation is:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The record is unusually well equipped to defend the decisions that were made, but materially less well equipped to show that the relationships excluded from those decisions were equivalently tested.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>The related retrospective risk is:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The retrospective defence may convert documentary continuity into evidential continuity.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>This does not establish institutional bad faith. “Protective consolidation after recognised error” remains a hypothesis only, requiring internal contemporaneous evidence such as complaint-response drafts, QPS/SIMT/PAR material, tracked changes, internal emails, review-selection records or minutes showing when concern was recognised and how contrary evidence was handled. Deliberate concealment, falsification or deceptive record construction are not supported by the present record.</p>
<h4 class="anchored-heading" id="present-complete-mechanism">Present complete mechanism<a class="heading-anchor" href="#present-complete-mechanism">¶</a></h4>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>broad relational GP problem → narrower hospital task → factual recovery with altered relationship structure → asymmetric evidential resolution → operational closure (“As per above”) → distributed residual ownership → classification/documentary inheritance → local assimilation/reclassification → documentary accretion → prospective rationale becomes retrospectively reusable → constructive retrospective defence (“As the letters show”) → external reopening → later molecular resolution of one historically open branch</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<h4 class="anchored-heading" id="motive-firewall">Motive firewall<a class="heading-anchor" href="#motive-firewall">¶</a></h4>
<p>The case should not presently be pleaded or framed as a motive case. Do not infer recognised error, conscious protection, concealment, falsification, dishonesty, deliberate diagnostic persistence or institutional self-protection from the documentary architecture alone. The current evidence can support a question about <strong>protective effects of the information architecture</strong> without supporting a conclusion about protective intent.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="anti-drift-rule">Anti-drift rule<a class="heading-anchor" href="#anti-drift-rule">¶</a></h3>
<p>From this version onward, new material may <strong>strengthen, qualify, weaken, or falsify</strong> the current formulation, but it should not silently change the object of the case. The object remains the <strong>information pathway</strong>: how the presenting problem was framed, which relationships were investigated at high or low resolution, how unresolved material became operational, what became portable through later documentation, what reopened locally, and whether local revisions ever became global reconstruction.</p>
<p>The file should therefore resist drift into simpler but weaker narratives such as:</p>
<ul>
<li>“the feet were ignored”;</li>
<li>“the neurologist was unsophisticated”;</li>
<li>“peripheral neuropathy was simply wrong”;</li>
<li>“PRKN should obviously have been diagnosed in 2017”;</li>
<li>“the 2026 result proves what clinicians should have known earlier”;</li>
<li>or “every later treatment response proves neurological causation.”</li>
</ul>
<p>The controlling formulations remain:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The problem is not lack of sophistication. It is where the sophistication was applied.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The counterfactual is not different diagnosis. It is different investigative object.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The facts came back. The relationships did not come back with equal force.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>“As per above” did not make the uncertainties disappear; it made an arrangement of those uncertainties operationally durable.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The inherited architecture was permeable, not frozen: later evidence generated genuine local reassessment, but the surviving record more clearly documents local updating than global reconstruction.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The critical transformation occurred not when the facts disappeared, but between their recovery and their synthesis.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The record is less a story of facts being forgotten than of relationships being reformatted once, then inherited.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>“As per above” marks operational closure before epistemic closure was complete; “As the letters show” later demonstrates how documentary continuity could acquire retrospective explanatory authority.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The GP kept seeing the arrows; specialist care owned the authority to decide what they meant.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>And the controlling counterfactual is deliberately narrower than a prediction of diagnostic outcome:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The counterfactual is not necessarily a different diagnosis. It is a different investigative object — a different question architecture.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>The purpose of this file is to stage that formulation while preserving the distinction between record fact, documentary inference, clinical expert question, and legal conclusion.</p>
<hr/>
</section>
<section class="reader-section kind-analysis" data-section="Main evidential analysis" id="section-main-analysis">
<div class="section-kicker">Main evidential analysis</div>
<div class="archive-note"><strong>Deep evidence layer.</strong> The sections above state the current integrated case. What follows preserves the detailed reasoning that built and stress-tested it, including contrary evidence and falsifiers. Repetition below is retained where it records how a proposition was tested, not because each repetition is a new evidential event.</div><h2 class="anchored-heading" id="1-evidential-discipline">1. Evidential discipline<a class="heading-anchor" href="#1-evidential-discipline">¶</a></h2>
<p>Every proposition in this file should be tagged mentally as one of four things.</p>
<p><strong>Record fact</strong> means the source directly records an observation, statement, prescription, referral, test, treatment response, or result. A record fact proves that the statement or event appears in the record. It does not automatically prove the clinical truth of every proposition embedded in the statement.</p>
<p><strong>Documentary or linguistic inference</strong> concerns how information behaves in the record: what is foregrounded, compressed, inherited, repeated, rephrased, hardened, backgrounded, locally updated, or globally reconstructed. It is not a medical-standard conclusion.</p>
<p><strong>Clinical expert question</strong> includes what examination was reasonably required, how particular signs should have been interpreted, whether a differential was adequate, whether genetic testing should have occurred, and when later evidence ought to have triggered broader reassessment.</p>
<p><strong>Legal conclusion</strong> includes negligence, breach, causation, procedural impropriety, dishonesty, concealment, bad faith, or competence. None should be asserted from documentary architecture alone.</p>
<p>The case should remain disciplined in this hierarchy throughout.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="2-hindsight-control">2. Hindsight control<a class="heading-anchor" href="#2-hindsight-control">¶</a></h2>
<p>The February 2026 PRKN result is highly important, but it must be used only after the historical record has been analysed on its own terms.</p>
<p>The later result may establish that pathogenic Parkin variants were eventually found and that the receiving neurologist attributed Parkinsonism and dystonia to compound-heterozygous Parkin mutations. It also shows that the eventual molecular answer concerned substantially the same PRKN/PARK2 question already raised in 2017 and still suspected in 2018.</p>
<p>It does <strong>not</strong> by itself establish what clinicians should have known in 2017, that every foot symptom was dystonia, that structural foot disease was irrelevant, that peripheral neuropathy was impossible, or that any earlier decision was negligent.</p>
<p>The correct use of 2026 is:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>endpoint to an already-existing historical question</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>not:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>retrospective proof of what every earlier symptom meant.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="3-treatment-response-control">3. Treatment-response control<a class="heading-anchor" href="#3-treatment-response-control">¶</a></h2>
<p>Treatment response must never be treated as diagnostic proof. The record itself contains a useful same-author control in the 2019 shoulder episode: the treating neurologist expressly explains that gabapentin response does not identify cause because multiple competing processes could improve, and similarly that steroid response would not necessarily provide a definable answer.</p>
<p>That same evidential discipline must be applied to procyclidine, pregabalin/Lyrica, Stalevo, Sinemet, and Sinemet CR. Each response may be clinically important. None is, by itself, a mechanism detector.</p>
<p>This becomes especially important later when pain improves with both pregabalin and dopaminergic medication. The record may therefore contain meaningful evidence about modifiability without automatically resolving etiology.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="4-the-entry-object-an-integrated-trajectory-of-a-constellation">4. The entry object: an integrated trajectory of a constellation<a class="heading-anchor" href="#4-the-entry-object-an-integrated-trajectory-of-a-constellation">¶</a></h2>
<p>The GP referral at <code>CLIN-000851–000884</code> is best characterised as an <strong>integrated trajectory of a constellation</strong>, with trajectory predominating.</p>
<p>The sequence is temporally organised. The GP first notes an unusual “festinant and stiff gait” while the patient is attending with bilateral foot problems, including corns/callouses and a global description that the feet are “not in a good state.” The referral then moves into an explicitly changing state: “over recent weeks” it had become “increasingly evident” that there was difficulty initiating movement, marked stiffness and increased tone in upper and lower limbs, difficulty relaxing, and toe clawing in bed.</p>
<p>The narrative then proceeds to worsening dexterity and function. Work that had been manageable becomes increasingly difficult; “of late” dexterity is worse; simple repetitive tasks are affected. The GP then synthesises the trajectory as increased tone with abnormal gait “now affecting” employment and daily activities, under the open question “?parkinsonian or other underlying diagnosis,” and concludes that the patient “certainly seems to be deteriorating,” with urgency because persistence threatens employment.</p>
<p>The referral does not prove that all manifestations share one cause. It does show that, at referral, these features are still travelling together as the clinical object requiring explanation.</p>
<p><strong>Working formulation:</strong></p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The GP referred not merely a symptom constellation, but a worsening relationship among feet, gait, toe posture, tone, movement initiation, dexterity and function under an open neurological question.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>Neurology later reconstructs substantial chronology independently. The defensible proposition is therefore not that trajectory disappeared, but that the <strong>shape of what was understood to be evolving together changed</strong>.</p>
<p>[PLACEHOLDER: insert exact GP referral transcription and source provenance.]</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="5-hospital-precis-relational-compression-rather-than-permanent-fact-loss">5. Hospital PRECIS: relational compression rather than permanent fact loss<a class="heading-anchor" href="#5-hospital-precis-relational-compression-rather-than-permanent-fact-loss">¶</a></h2>
<p>The hospital PRECIS or immediate task representation foregrounded a narrower motor-neurological subset: difficulty initiating movement, increased tone/stiffness in all four limbs, cogwheeling, and a request for neurological assessment.</p>
<p>That compression is not inherently improper. Referral and admission fields routinely simplify complex histories. The relevant question is what kind of information lost salience.</p>
<p>The material most clearly backgrounded included the painful/problematic feet, toe-clawing/postural phenomena, the feet–gait relationship, functional deterioration and employment impact, and the integrated question of how feet, gait, tone, movement and decline related.</p>
<p>Yet many omitted facts later returned. Neurology recovered foot history, structural abnormalities, abnormal gait, rigidity, bradykinesia, functional impairment and deterioration. The simple model “PRECIS omitted the feet, therefore Neurology never saw them” is not supported.</p>
<p>The stronger concept is <strong>relational compression</strong>:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The facts came back more successfully than the original relationships among them.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>The motor signs retained in the PRECIS subsequently received deep neurological development. The backgrounded material often returned as history, structural findings, podiatric material, or possible peripheral phenomena. The question is whether the original relationships regained equivalent organising prominence.</p>
<p>[PLACEHOLDER: construct exact GP-vs-PRECIS comparison table: retained / omitted / transformed / relationally disconnected.]</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="5a-counterfactual-question-architecture-different-investigative-object-not-necessarily-different-diagnosis">5A. Counterfactual question architecture: different investigative object, not necessarily different diagnosis<a class="heading-anchor" href="#5a-counterfactual-question-architecture-different-investigative-object-not-necessarily-different-diagnosis">¶</a></h2>
<p>The full GP referral and the hospital PRECIS should be treated as two materially different <strong>problem representations</strong>.</p>
<p>The GP referral did not merely contain more facts. It organised those facts as a deteriorating relationship under an open question. The feet, abnormal gait, increasingly evident movement-initiation difficulty, tone/stiffness, inability to relax, toe clawing, declining dexterity, loss of function and threatened employment were presented together and through time. The GP's formulation remained open: <code>?parkinsonian or other underlying diagnosis</code>.</p>
<p>The PRECIS, by contrast, presented a narrower but entirely legitimate neurological task: difficulty initiating movement, four-limb tone/stiffness, cogwheeling and a request for neurological opinion. That representation was already strongly shaped around a central motor syndrome.</p>
<p>The documentary counterfactual should therefore <strong>not</strong> be framed as:</p>
<blockquote>
<p>full GP referral → different diagnosis.</p>
</blockquote>
<p>The safer formulation is:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>full GP referral → different investigative object.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>Or, more precisely:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>Had the integrated feet–gait–tone–toe–deterioration trajectory remained the neurologist's primary organising problem, the relationships within that phenotype would have remained more explicitly decision-live at the outset.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>This is still a counterfactual. The record cannot establish what the clinician definitely would have done. Its evidential basis comes from two internal controls.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="5a-1-same-author-control-the-2019-shoulder-problem">5A.1 Same-author control: the 2019 shoulder problem<a class="heading-anchor" href="#5a-1-same-author-control-the-2019-shoulder-problem">¶</a></h3>
<p>When the same neurologist later encountered a comparatively fresh shoulder/arm problem, his documented reasoning was explicitly generative. He asked whether the problem was in the shoulder or radicular; used focal weakness and other examination findings to localise possibilities; generated radial-nerve, posterior-cord/brachial-plexus, root, structural, inflammatory and malignant alternatives; tied investigations to those propositions; stated limitations of negative tests; and explicitly refused to treat gabapentin or steroid response as diagnostic proof.</p>
<p>His language included the proposition that the actual cause was important and that he genuinely did not know the cause. Uncertainty therefore functioned as an engine of investigation:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>problem → competing explanations → focused examination → discriminating observations → targeted investigation → limits of evidence → preserved uncertainty.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>This does not prove that a chronic mixed lower-limb problem should have been investigated identically. The shoulder was more acute, focal and anatomically localisable. Its value is narrower: it shows that explicit proposition-by-proposition causal discrimination was available within this clinician's own documented reasoning style.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="5a-2-same-anatomy-control-later-foot-throw-discrimination">5A.2 Same-anatomy control: later foot-throw discrimination<a class="heading-anchor" href="#5a-2-same-anatomy-control-later-foot-throw-discrimination">¶</a></h3>
<p>The later lower-limb record provides a second internal comparator. At <code>CLIN-002286</code>, an apparent footdrop-like foot throw was not merely labelled Parkinsonian. The neurologist recorded the discriminator: dorsiflexion, plantarflexion, inversion and eversion power were normal, and on that basis described the phenomenon as actually Parkinsonian.</p>
<p>That is important because it demonstrates that the clinician could apply:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>question → specific distal examination → discriminating finding → classification</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>within the same anatomical territory.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="5a-3-what-a-fuller-initial-problem-representation-might-have-kept-live">5A.3 What a fuller initial problem representation might have kept live<a class="heading-anchor" href="#5a-3-what-a-fuller-initial-problem-representation-might-have-kept-live">¶</a></h3>
<p>The documentary record therefore supports a limited inference that preserving the GP's full relational framing might have made questions such as the following more explicit at the outset:</p>
<ul>
<li>Is the apparent footdrop true weakness, dystonic posture, painful adaptation, structural gait change, or a mixture?</li>
<li>Is toe clawing fixed deformity, intermittent/dynamic posture, pain behaviour, or something else?</li>
<li>Are distal power and sensation normal or abnormal?</li>
<li>Does foot pain track loading, gait, tone or movement state?</li>
<li>Does abnormal posture plausibly alter loading through structurally abnormal feet?</li>
<li>Which observations favour peripheral neuropathy, and which observations weaken it?</li>
<li>Are the structural and neurological findings independent, causally related, or mixed?</li>
</ul>
<p>These are <strong>illustrative discriminators</strong>, not a claim that each test was clinically mandatory in 2017. Whether a particular examination or investigation was required is an expert question.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="5a-4-why-this-matters-to-the-genetic-construction">5A.4 Why this matters to the genetic construction<a class="heading-anchor" href="#5a-4-why-this-matters-to-the-genetic-construction">¶</a></h3>
<p>The eventual 2017 genetic reasoning was highly sophisticated. It selected very young onset, akinetic rigidity, marked symmetry, lack of dominant family history and preserved cognition as explicit PRKN/PARK2 indicators. The dystonic-looking lower limb remained visible in the broader atypical/autosomal-recessive picture but was not one of the enumerated PRKN indicators.</p>
<p>The counterfactual should again be modest. It is not:</p>
<blockquote>
<p>full GP referral → dystonia incorporated into PRKN → earlier molecular diagnosis.</p>
</blockquote>
<p>It is:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>if feet, toe posture and gait had remained part of the originating problem representation, the lower-limb phenomenology might have retained greater salience as the phenotype was partitioned and the genetic model was constructed.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>Whether that would have changed the differential, testing or treatment cannot be determined from the record alone.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="5a-5-most-likely-difference-partition-quality-rather-than-service-destination">5A.5 Most likely difference: partition quality rather than service destination<a class="heading-anchor" href="#5a-5-most-likely-difference-partition-quality-rather-than-service-destination">¶</a></h3>
<p>The likely management pathway might still have been very similar. Severe Parkinsonism would still have justified neurological investigation and treatment. Genuine structural foot disease would still have justified Podiatry. The counterfactual is principally about the <strong>bridge between those destinations</strong>.</p>
<p>Instead of the relationship becoming implicit within:</p>
<blockquote>
<p>Parkinsonism → Neurology<br/>
feet → Podiatry<br/>
burning/pain → possible peripheral process</p>
</blockquote>
<p>a fuller preserved question architecture might have yielded an explicit statement closer to:</p>
<blockquote>
<p>Parkinsonism is strongly supported; structural foot pathology is genuine; however abnormal gait, toe posture, loading and pain may contain both neurological and structural contributions, and that relationship remains an active proposition requiring defined follow-up or discriminating evidence.</p>
</blockquote>
<p>Again, this is not offered as text the clinician was required to write. It illustrates the kind of <strong>relational uncertainty</strong> that the GP referral made visible.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="5a-6-strongest-counterfactual-formulation">5A.6 Strongest counterfactual formulation<a class="heading-anchor" href="#5a-6-strongest-counterfactual-formulation">¶</a></h3>
<p>The formulation to carry forward is:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The evidence does not justify saying that the full GP referral would have produced a different diagnosis. It does support a documentary counterfactual that preserving the integrated feet–gait–tone–toe–deterioration trajectory as the neurologist's initial problem representation would have made the relationships within that phenotype more explicitly decision-live. The same clinician's later record demonstrates that, when relationships were presented as unresolved propositions, he was capable of highly granular discrimination among them.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>The cleanest shorthand is:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The counterfactual is not different diagnosis. It is different investigative object.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="5a-7-counterfactual-limits-and-falsifiers">5A.7 Counterfactual limits and falsifiers<a class="heading-anchor" href="#5a-7-counterfactual-limits-and-falsifiers">¶</a></h3>
<p>This inference weakens materially if the canonical record shows that, before or during the initial investigative design, the neurologist actually had the full GP referral in front of him, expressly reconstructed its integrated feet–gait–tone–toe trajectory, and used that relationship as the organising problem despite the PRECIS.</p>
<p>It would also weaken if a contemporaneous note shows the lower-limb relationships being examined proposition-by-proposition before the final partition, even if the later discharge summary compressed that work.</p>
<p>The shoulder comparator cannot establish causation by the PRECIS. The difference may also reflect problem structure: an acute focal shoulder syndrome has clearer anatomical discriminators than a longstanding heterogeneous lower-limb syndrome. The correct conclusion is therefore one of <strong>plausible problem-representation effect</strong>, not deterministic causation.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="6-admission-examination-what-was-actually-observed">6. Admission examination: what was actually observed<a class="heading-anchor" href="#6-admission-examination-what-was-actually-observed">¶</a></h2>
<p>The 2017 admission examination is clinically substantial for the Parkinsonian syndrome. It documents markedly increased and fairly symmetrical tone, brisk reflexes, lower-limb tremor, lead-pipe rigidity, axial rigidity, gross bradykinesia, absent or reduced arm swing, a strikingly abnormal gait, an apparent footdrop-like phenomenon described as “somewhat suggestive of a dystonic posture — NOT FOOT DROP,” a left-lower-limb kick/throw, pes planus, hammertoes, and mild oedema.</p>
<p>Those are important findings. They should not be minimised.</p>
<p>The analytical issue is that the surviving record is much richer for the general Parkinsonian motor syndrome than for the <strong>distal relational map</strong> among dynamic posture, toe configuration, fixed deformity, loading, pressure areas, pain distribution, pain provocation, sensation, distal power and gait mechanics.</p>
<p>The 2017 examination block does not visibly contain, at comparable granularity, directional ankle power, detailed distal sensory mapping, dynamic toe posture during gait/loading, pressure-site mapping, pain reproduction under load, fixed-versus-dynamic deformity analysis, or an explicit bridge linking the dystonic-looking gait to painful structural foot findings.</p>
<p>This must be stated narrowly:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>not documented at comparable resolution</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>rather than:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>never examined.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>[PLACEHOLDER: verify all examination CLIN ranges and identify any overlooked distal examination details.]</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="7-the-synthesisexamination-differential">7. The synthesis–examination differential<a class="heading-anchor" href="#7-the-synthesisexamination-differential">¶</a></h2>
<p>The later 2017 synthesis becomes far more analytically elaborate than the distal bedside map. The record moves from observed signs into young-onset Parkinsonism, possible young-onset Parkinson’s disease, atypical/synucleinopathy possibilities, autosomal-recessive disease, PRKN/PARK2, PARK6, PARK7, PARK9, inheritance reasoning, genetic counselling, prognosis and treatment strategy.</p>
<p>For PRKN/PARK2, the record explicitly selects phenotype features such as very young onset, akinetic rigidity, lack of dominant family history, marked symmetry and excellent cognition. This is high-resolution phenotype-to-etiology reasoning.</p>
<p>The comparison is therefore not “many words versus few words.” It is analytical:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>high-resolution central/genetic phenotype construction versus lower-resolution documented reconstruction of the disputed distal structural–dynamic–pain relationship.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>The 2017 record is genuinely sophisticated. The strongest wording is therefore:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The problem is not lack of sophistication. It is where the sophistication was applied.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>This should remain a central formulation because it avoids the inaccurate suggestion that the final note was naive or unsophisticated.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="8-the-dystonic-looking-lower-limb-partial-incorporation-not-disappearance">8. The dystonic-looking lower limb: partial incorporation, not disappearance<a class="heading-anchor" href="#8-the-dystonic-looking-lower-limb-partial-incorporation-not-disappearance">¶</a></h2>
<p>The dystonic-looking lower-limb observation was not simply forgotten. It appears in the examination and also in broader atypical/autosomal-recessive reasoning. A contemporaneous request describes the walk as “almost dystonically unusual” beside suspicion of an autosomal-recessive Parkinsonian condition.</p>
<p>However, when the record later gives an explicit PRKN/PARK2 indicator list, the stated features are age, akinetic rigidity, family pattern, symmetry and cognition. The dystonic-looking lower limb is not one of the enumerated PRKN indicators.</p>
<p>The safest formulation is:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The dystonic-looking gait remained visible and contributed to the broad atypical/recessive impression, but it was not visibly integrated into the explicit high-resolution PRKN rationale or into an equally high-resolution feet–pain phenotype.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>This is a documentary distinction. Whether the sign was too nonspecific to deserve greater etiological weight is a clinical expert question.</p>
<p>[PLACEHOLDER: verify exact wording and original CLIN source of the “almost dystonically unusual” request.]</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="9-the-lower-limb-branch-before-operational-closure">9. The lower-limb branch before operational closure<a class="heading-anchor" href="#9-the-lower-limb-branch-before-operational-closure">¶</a></h2>
<p>The final 2017 reasoning does not make a confident disease-level peripheral neuropathy diagnosis. The language remains conditional: the leg/foot material “may have been completely separate,” “perhaps a secondary peripheral neuropathy,” possibly related to prior alcohol exposure, ending with “Not clear.”</p>
<p>The patient’s own distinction between leg tension and corns/callouses/foot pain is also documented. That is important counterevidence against a claim that separation was invented without basis. The patient experienced the phenomena as distinct.</p>
<p>But phenomenological distinction and biological independence are not identical. The record therefore contains, at once:</p>
<ul>
<li>genuine structural pathology;</li>
<li>genuine patient-perceived difference;</li>
<li>a possible peripheral explanation;</li>
<li>a neurological gait/posture abnormality;</li>
<li>uncertainty about their relationship.</li>
</ul>
<p>The core question is not whether uncertainty was legitimate. It is what happened to that unresolved relationship once workable management destinations became available.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="10-podiatry-destination-without-necessarily-resolving-the-relationship">10. Podiatry: destination without necessarily resolving the relationship<a class="heading-anchor" href="#10-podiatry-destination-without-necessarily-resolving-the-relationship">¶</a></h2>
<p>Podiatry was a legitimate destination for structural disease. The record contains pes planus, hammertoes, corns/callouses, pressure lesions, recurrent focal pathology, debridement and offloading. This case should never be formulated as “all foot disease was neurological.”</p>
<p>The narrower question is whether Podiatry answered <strong>where</strong> the structural component should be managed while leaving unresolved <strong>how</strong> structural pathology interacted with dynamic neurological posture, gait and pain.</p>
<p>This is the destination-versus-explanation distinction.</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>A destination can produce practical completion without explanatory completion.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>The related ownership question is:</p>
<blockquote>
<p>Who owned the relationship between services once the patient had a structural foot pathway and a neurological movement pathway?</p>
</blockquote>
<p>[PLACEHOLDER: map all Podiatry referrals, feedback loops, and whether any neurological re-integration trigger was specified.]</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="11-as-per-above-as-operational-hinge">11. ‘As per above’ as operational hinge<a class="heading-anchor" href="#11-as-per-above-as-operational-hinge">¶</a></h2>
<p>At <code>CLIN-001613–001614</code> the record states:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>Plan/Recommendations: As per above.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>The material “above” included severe Parkinsonism, dystonic-looking gait, structural feet, possible separate/peripheral explanation, “Not clear,” genetic uncertainty, specific PRKN reasoning, Podiatry, medication strategy, rehabilitation and follow-up.</p>
<p>Therefore “As per above” should not be read as proof that all those questions were solved.</p>
<p>Its more plausible immediate function is:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>the mixed information state above is sufficient to operate as the plan.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>This is the distinction between <strong>epistemic closure</strong> and <strong>operational closure</strong>.</p>
<p>The key working phrase is:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>unresolved synthesis → actionable management state</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>The phrase did not erase uncertainty; it made an arrangement of uncertainty operationally durable.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="12-what-travelled-best-and-what-travelled-least-well">12. What travelled best, and what travelled least well<a class="heading-anchor" href="#12-what-travelled-best-and-what-travelled-least-well">¶</a></h2>
<p>Once the synthesis operated as the plan, several elements became highly portable:</p>
<ul>
<li>Parkinsonism/Parkinson’s/iPD as the main neurological frame;</li>
<li>medication strategy and later levodopa/dyskinesia logic;</li>
<li>Podiatry as structural-foot destination;</li>
<li>Parkinson nurse/Neurology follow-up;</li>
<li>later neuropathic pain terminology;</li>
<li>the previous letters and summaries themselves.</li>
</ul>
<p>What appears less portable as an active question includes:</p>
<ul>
<li>the full force of “perhaps” and “Not clear”;</li>
<li>the original feet–gait–tone relationship;</li>
<li>the specific PRKN/PARK2 question after deferral;</li>
<li>the significance of the dystonic-looking lower limb within the whole lower-limb phenotype;</li>
<li>the question of who owned the cross-service relationship.</li>
</ul>
<p>The uncertainty may remain findable in the archive. The issue is whether it remains operationally active.</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>Decisions, destinations and usable working categories travelled more efficiently than the relationships that originally qualified them.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="13-prkn-as-the-clearest-internal-example-of-management-without-resolution">13. PRKN as the clearest internal example of management without resolution<a class="heading-anchor" href="#13-prkn-as-the-clearest-internal-example-of-management-without-resolution">¶</a></h2>
<p>Prof Daniel Healy’s 2017 specialist correspondence independently strengthens the PRKN/PARK2 hypothesis and recommends testing it first, with a panel if negative. This is important because the later molecular diagnosis was not an unforeseen concept retrospectively imposed on the case.</p>
<p>In 2018 the treating neurologist still records PRKN/PARK2 as what he and Prof Healy suspect is the cause, but testing is deliberately deferred because it does not particularly change current management and there is “no great loss in ‘not knowing’ currently right now.”</p>
<p>This is one of the clearest conceptual sources in the whole record because it explicitly separates:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>epistemic resolution</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>from</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>management sufficiency.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>The clinical reasonableness of that deferral is an expert question. The documentary significance is that unresolved causation could be consciously tolerated when treatment did not depend on resolving it.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="14-the-reopening-problem">14. The reopening problem<a class="heading-anchor" href="#14-the-reopening-problem">¶</a></h2>
<p>Deferral is not, by itself, the objection. The longitudinal question is:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>How does “we do not need to know right now” later become “now we should reopen the question”?</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>A robust architecture for managed uncertainty might contain a named owner, review date, trigger, test threshold, change-in-phenotype trigger, treatment-state signal, or specialist referral criterion.</p>
<p>For PRKN, the question remained conceptually preserved, but the route back to testing appears only weakly scheduled in the surviving record. For the feet–gait–tone relationship, the record is even less clear about a named reopening mechanism.</p>
<p>This is the difference between uncertainty that is merely tolerated and uncertainty that is actively managed over time.</p>
<p>[PLACEHOLDER: audit all explicit review triggers in 2017–2019, including DAT, pending investigations, genetics, Podiatry, and lower-limb reassessment.]</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="15-procyclidine-withdrawal-as-boundary-crossing-evidence">15. Procyclidine withdrawal as boundary-crossing evidence<a class="heading-anchor" href="#15-procyclidine-withdrawal-as-boundary-crossing-evidence">¶</a></h2>
<p>Later treatment-state changes generate empirical information capable of testing the original partition. During procyclidine withdrawal, the patient reports tension building, feet becoming restless and gait falling apart. A later clinician-authored account records rapid return of the outside-left-foot phenomenon as one of the first changes after withdrawal.</p>
<p>This does not prove that dystonia caused the structural lesion or that all foot pain was neurological. It does show that multiple features across the documentary boundary co-varied with neurological treatment state.</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>Treatment perturbation generated information capable of testing the partition.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>The important question is what level of the model that information was allowed to revise.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="16-structural-truth-and-neurological-truth-can-coexist">16. Structural truth and neurological truth can coexist<a class="heading-anchor" href="#16-structural-truth-and-neurological-truth-can-coexist">¶</a></h2>
<p>Podiatry generated genuine local mechanical information: recurrent pressure lesions, focal pathology, debridement, offloading, structural foot type and toe deformity. Neurology generated genuine dynamic information: movement state, treatment state, dystonic-looking gait, later Parkinsonian foot throw, OFF phenomena and dopaminergic effects.</p>
<p>Neither stream invalidates the other.</p>
<p>The possible missing operation is the <strong>join</strong>:</p>
<blockquote>
<p>focal mechanical pathology + dynamic neurological state → what is the relationship?</p>
</blockquote>
<p>The case should therefore protect the formulation:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>Structural truth and neurological contribution can both be genuine. The unresolved question is who owned the arrow between them.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="17-documentary-inheritance-becomes-explicit">17. Documentary inheritance becomes explicit<a class="heading-anchor" href="#17-documentary-inheritance-becomes-explicit">¶</a></h2>
<p>By 2019 the treating neurologist expressly relies on prior documentation: “all previous details are as per older letters as previously supplied,” with related statements that everything is as detailed in earlier summaries and will not be reiterated for brevity. The 2017 synthesis is itself reproduced in later longitudinal material.</p>
<p>This establishes documentary inheritance as a record fact.</p>
<p>It does not prove uncritical copying. It does show that a mature case representation existed and later encounters could begin from it rather than rebuilding the entire case.</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The chronic case acquired an information state that later encounters could inherit.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>Repeated same-author terminology therefore strongly proves persistence. It is weaker evidence of independent revalidation unless the underlying proposition is actually retested.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="18-the-2019-shoulder-episode-as-same-author-internal-control">18. The 2019 shoulder episode as same-author internal control<a class="heading-anchor" href="#18-the-2019-shoulder-episode-as-same-author-internal-control">¶</a></h2>
<p>The 2019 shoulder episode is methodologically valuable because it offers a same-author control. In the same general documentary environment, a comparatively fresh focal problem is reasoned through in a very different form.</p>
<p>The shoulder sequence is highly explicit:</p>
<ol>
<li>a new worsening symptom is described;</li>
<li>the clinician asks whether the source is shoulder or radicular/disc-related;</li>
<li>examination findings are used to localise the problem;</li>
<li>multiple anatomical alternatives are generated;</li>
<li>observations are used to favour or weaken particular hypotheses;</li>
<li>MRI and other tests are linked to specific propositions;</li>
<li>limitations of negative tests are stated;</li>
<li>gabapentin response is expressly declared non-diagnostic;</li>
<li>possible steroid response is also treated as non-specific;</li>
<li>uncertainty is preserved in explicit language such as “I genuinely do not know what the cause is here.”</li>
</ol>
<p>This weakens explanations based simply on inability to reason analytically, inability to write detailed differentials, or habitual brevity.</p>
<p>The shoulder problem was different—acute, focal, anatomically localisable and amenable to structural tests—so the comparison is not perfect. But it is a powerful control for documentary capacity.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="19-generative-uncertainty-versus-contained-uncertainty">19. Generative uncertainty versus contained uncertainty<a class="heading-anchor" href="#19-generative-uncertainty-versus-contained-uncertainty">¶</a></h2>
<p>The shoulder comparator reveals two different uses of uncertainty.</p>
<p><strong>Fresh shoulder:</strong></p>
<blockquote>
<p>uncertainty → question generation → discrimination → test → stated limitations → continued uncertainty if evidence remains non-discriminating</p>
</blockquote>
<p><strong>Inherited PRKN/feet pathway:</strong></p>
<blockquote>
<p>uncertainty → workable management → local updates → uncertainty may remain technically open while categories remain operational</p>
</blockquote>
<p>This should not be simplified to “shoulder = thinking, feet = no thinking.” The chronic pathway contains substantial fresh reasoning.</p>
<p>The stronger distinction is:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>fresh-model construction</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>versus</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>inherited-model updating.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>The core formulation is:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>When the model was fresh, uncertainty generated questions. Once the model was inherited, uncertainty was more often managed within it.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>This is a documentary description, not a claim about psychology or motive.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="20-2020-foot-throw-local-discrimination-inside-the-inherited-pathway">20. 2020 foot throw: local discrimination inside the inherited pathway<a class="heading-anchor" href="#20-2020-foot-throw-local-discrimination-inside-the-inherited-pathway">¶</a></h2>
<p>The later foot-throw episode is important contrary evidence to any rigid-inheritance thesis. The clinician tests dorsiflexion, plantarflexion, inversion and eversion power and concludes that the apparent footdrop-like phenomenon is “actually parkinsonian” because power is normal.</p>
<p>This is a clean:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>observation → discriminator → classification</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>sequence.</p>
<p>It proves that the inherited lower-limb model was permeable and that targeted distal discrimination did occur later.</p>
<p>It also supplies an internal comparator for the 2017 record: this is what a more explicitly discriminating distal examination can look like in the same clinician’s documentation.</p>
<p>The remaining question is whether this local reclassification ever propagated backwards into a reconstruction of the original feet–gait–pain relationship.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="21-2023-the-strongest-counterexample-to-a-static-framework-theory">21. 2023: the strongest counterexample to a static-framework theory<a class="heading-anchor" href="#21-2023-the-strongest-counterexample-to-a-static-framework-theory">¶</a></h2>
<p>The 2023 review contains substantial fresh reasoning and must be treated fairly. The neurologist considers ON versus OFF dystonia, uses medication timing and evening behaviour to reason toward OFF-state impairment, states that literal overnight onset is not typical of peripheral neuropathy, revisits the historical neuropathy question, considers Fabry disease as an overlap possibility, seeks further testing, and incorporates the patient’s own belief that some of the foot problem may be separate.</p>
<p>This means the defensible contrast is not:</p>
<blockquote>
<p>shoulder = analytical reasoning / feet = no analytical reasoning.</p>
</blockquote>
<p>It is:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>shoulder = fresh causal model-building from the ground up</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>versus</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>feet/Parkinsonism = substantial local re-analysis within a pre-existing longitudinal model.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>The inherited architecture was therefore <strong>permeable, not frozen</strong>.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="22-local-updating-versus-global-reconstruction">22. Local updating versus global reconstruction<a class="heading-anchor" href="#22-local-updating-versus-global-reconstruction">¶</a></h2>
<p>The record contains local answers to multiple questions:</p>
<ul>
<li>apparent footdrop can be Parkinsonian where directional power is normal;</li>
<li>cramping may be OFF dystonia;</li>
<li>abrupt overnight onset argues against ordinary peripheral neuropathy;</li>
<li>evening difficulty may be OFF-related;</li>
<li>Fabry can be tested as an overlap possibility;</li>
<li>dopaminergic treatment can alter pain.</li>
</ul>
<p>What is harder to identify is a later source saying, in substance:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>Given these accumulated revisions, let us reconstruct from first principles the original relationship among structural feet, dystonic posture, pain, loading, gait, OFF state, neuropathic possibilities and the original presentation.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>That is the distinction between <strong>local updating</strong> and <strong>global reconstruction</strong>.</p>
<p>[PLACEHOLDER: conduct dedicated canonical search for any post-2020 source performing such a global reconstruction.]</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="23-semantic-migration-in-the-neuropathic-branch">23. Semantic migration in the neuropathic branch<a class="heading-anchor" href="#23-semantic-migration-in-the-neuropathic-branch">¶</a></h2>
<p>The 2017 peripheral/neuropathy wording is expressly tentative. Over time the record uses formulations such as “peripheral neuropathy question,” possible peripheral neuropathic disorder, “? neuropathic pain,” neuropathic pain, and eventually “peripheral neuropathic pain symptomatologies.”</p>
<p>At the same time, disease-level neuropathy can remain conditional: “If there is a degree of peripheral neuropathy here...”</p>
<p>These propositions must be separated:</p>
<ol>
<li>peripheral neuropathy as disease;</li>
<li>neuropathic pain as symptom phenotype;</li>
<li>cause of that pain;</li>
<li>response to medication commonly used for neuropathic pain.</li>
</ol>
<p>The 2025 phrase “Whatever the underlying cause for the peripheral neuropathic pain symptomatologies...” places uncertainty grammatically on the <strong>cause</strong>, while the symptom category itself is expressed comparatively affirmatively.</p>
<p>The strongest formulation is:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>classification hardening without equivalent disease-level diagnostic hardening</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>or:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>uncertainty migrated from classification toward etiology.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>[PLACEHOLDER: build complete dated corpus of every “neuropathy/neuropathic/neuralgic/burning/sensitivity” use by source and speaker.]</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="24-2023-onward-uncertainty-becomes-operationally-expensive">24. 2023 onward: uncertainty becomes operationally expensive<a class="heading-anchor" href="#24-2023-onward-uncertainty-becomes-operationally-expensive">¶</a></h2>
<p>By March 2023 severe bilateral burning foot/lower-leg pain is described with language including “death wish,” GP concern, a request for review before “we get into trouble,” debilitating pain and no quality of life despite high-dose pregabalin.</p>
<p>This should not be overread as proving suicidal intent or plan. Its documentary importance is that the unresolved pain problem becomes contemporaneously safety- and endurance-relevant.</p>
<p>The later record contains genuine response and reconsideration. The appropriate sequence is:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>severe symptom failure → pressure for reconsideration → genuine local neurological/pain reasoning → local rescue</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>not:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>crisis → no thought → same plan.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="25-recursive-manageability">25. Recursive manageability<a class="heading-anchor" href="#25-recursive-manageability">¶</a></h2>
<p>A possible longitudinal mechanism is:</p>
<blockquote>
<p>unresolved mechanism → severe symptom failure → pressure for reconsideration → local intervention → manageability restored → pressure for global explanation falls → unresolved parent relationship survives → later recurrence</p>
</blockquote>
<p>This is a documentary model, not established causation.</p>
<p>Its value is that it explains how active care and unresolved explanation can coexist for years.</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>Successful local rescue may restore operational sufficiency without producing explanatory closure.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="26-pregabalin-treatment-benefit-versus-mechanistic-validation">26. Pregabalin: treatment benefit versus mechanistic validation<a class="heading-anchor" href="#26-pregabalin-treatment-benefit-versus-mechanistic-validation">¶</a></h2>
<p>The record supports genuine pregabalin benefit at points. It also supports severe pain despite high-dose pregabalin, later recurrence, residual pain, persistent toe curling and substantial activity restriction.</p>
<p>Therefore “Lyrica removed the foot pain” is best read as a later favourable snapshot or compressed summary, not as proof of durable bilateral eradication or proof of neuropathic mechanism.</p>
<p>The appropriate distinction is between:</p>
<ul>
<li>absolute symptom level;</li>
<li>counterfactual medication effect;</li>
<li>functional restoration;</li>
<li>adaptation/activity restriction.</li>
</ul>
<p>A patient can be meaningfully better on a drug while still having major residual pain and a smaller life.</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>A favourable treatment snapshot can be accurate while still being an incomplete summary of longitudinal efficacy.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>[PLACEHOLDER: build dated pain / dose / function / activity / dopaminergic-state table.]</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="27-2025-neurological-reopening-and-sinemet-cr">27. 2025 neurological reopening and Sinemet CR<a class="heading-anchor" href="#27-2025-neurological-reopening-and-sinemet-cr">¶</a></h2>
<p>By March 2025 the foot pain is again dominant, with severe morning pain, first steps described as torture, “I won’t live with it,” and relief after Parkinson medication begins working. Peripheral neuropathy remains conditional, painful dystonia is acknowledged, and controlled-release Sinemet is introduced.</p>
<p>This is meaningful neurological reopening. It is not merely passive symptom treatment.</p>
<p>At the same time, the note does not visibly reconstruct the entire historical genealogy of the feet classification.</p>
<p>The strongest phrase is:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>therapeutic reopening without full classificatory reconstruction.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>The later report of “profound relief currently” after Sinemet CR is important treatment-state evidence but still not a mechanism detector. It raises the expert question: what should this observation do to the inherited differential?</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="28-the-complaint-changes-the-object-of-review">28. The complaint changes the object of review<a class="heading-anchor" href="#28-the-complaint-changes-the-object-of-review">¶</a></h2>
<p>The March 2025 complaint is significant because it challenges the longitudinal reasoning itself, including classification persistence and adequacy of reassessment, and seeks independent review of the reasoning from 2017 to 2025.</p>
<p>The complaint is not proof that its allegations are correct.</p>
<p>Its documentary significance is that the <strong>genealogy of the reasoning</strong> becomes the object requiring explanation.</p>
<p>A routine clinic note may reasonably focus on current management. A complaint response to a historical reasoning challenge is being asked a different question: how did the framework arise, and how was it tested over time?</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="29-as-the-letters-show-retrospective-authority">29. ‘As the letters show’: retrospective authority<a class="heading-anchor" href="#29-as-the-letters-show-retrospective-authority">¶</a></h2>
<p>The 2025 response explicitly relies on the accumulated letters. This is legitimate use of contemporaneous records. The letters are strong evidence that decisions occurred, treatment strategies existed, and certain understandings persisted.</p>
<p>The source-audit distinction is:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>Does documentary continuity prove persistence of a reasoning pathway, or independent revalidation of the propositions that originally created it?</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>A response can accurately show that levodopa was delayed for stated reasons, Lyrica was used, Sinemet later helped pain, and dyskinesia risks were considered. Those facts do not automatically establish that the original feet/pain classification was independently re-derived or that PRKN had been closed.</p>
<p>The strongest formulation is:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>A long coherent pathway can retrospectively resemble validation of the pathway.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>That is a normal documentary phenomenon and should not be framed as dishonesty or retrospective falsification.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="30-parkinsons-diagnosis-versus-parkinsons-etiology">30. Parkinson’s diagnosis versus Parkinson’s etiology<a class="heading-anchor" href="#30-parkinsons-diagnosis-versus-parkinsons-etiology">¶</a></h2>
<p>The record demonstrates continuity of the Parkinsonian diagnosis very strongly. It less clearly demonstrates resolution of Parkinson’s etiology before 2026.</p>
<p>Early use of “idiopathic” appears elastic enough to coexist with autosomal-recessive suspicion, so the word alone cannot safely be treated as proof that monogenic disease was excluded.</p>
<p>By March 2025, however, the complaint response states the service’s “belief and understanding” that the patient has idiopathic Parkinson’s disease. The documentary question is therefore:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>What happened to the previously specific PRKN/PARK2 hypothesis?</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>The question is not: why did clinicians fail to know a 2026 result in 2017?</p>
<p>The strongest formulation is:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The record demonstrates continuity of the Parkinson’s diagnosis far more clearly than it demonstrates resolution of Parkinson’s etiology.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>[PLACEHOLDER: complete idiopathic/iPD/PRKN/recessive terminology audit.]</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="31-2026-endpoint">31. 2026 endpoint<a class="heading-anchor" href="#31-2026-endpoint">¶</a></h2>
<p>The 2026 result resolves the historical PRKN branch. It establishes a molecular endpoint and provides later specialist attribution of Parkinsonism and dystonia to Parkin mutations.</p>
<p>Its documentary significance is that the historical PRKN question was not empty speculation; it was specific, prospective, independently reinforced, deliberately left unresolved, and eventually answered.</p>
<p>The correct line is:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The endpoint tells us what eventually happened to a question the record itself had already asked.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>It must not be used to prove every historical foot symptom was dystonic or to determine historical standard of care without expert evidence.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="32-full-information-pathway-mechanism">32. Full information-pathway mechanism<a class="heading-anchor" href="#32-full-information-pathway-mechanism">¶</a></h2>
<p>The present working mechanism can be staged as follows.</p>
<p><strong>Stage 1 — Integrated referral:</strong> feet, gait, tone, toe phenomena, movement difficulty, function and deterioration travel together under an open neurological question.</p>
<p><strong>Stage 2 — Relational compression:</strong> the hospital task representation foregrounds cardinal motor-neurological signs and reduces the prominence of the integrated lower-limb relationship.</p>
<p><strong>Stage 3 — Selective high-resolution construction:</strong> Parkinsonism and genetic etiology receive extensive examination and synthesis.</p>
<p><strong>Stage 4 — Factual recovery without equivalent relational recovery:</strong> omitted facts return, but their relationships remain disproportionately uncertain.</p>
<p><strong>Stage 5 — Operational partition:</strong> Neurology, Podiatry, rehabilitation, symptomatic treatment and deferred genetics provide workable destinations.</p>
<p><strong>Stage 6 — “As per above”:</strong> the mixed state becomes the plan.</p>
<p><strong>Stage 7 — Documentary inheritance:</strong> older letters and summaries become reusable information objects.</p>
<p><strong>Stage 8 — Boundary crossings:</strong> treatment state, OFF phenomena, structural findings, pain recurrence and new examinations repeatedly cross the earlier compartments.</p>
<p><strong>Stage 9 — Local updating:</strong> new evidence produces genuine branch-level reassessment.</p>
<p><strong>Stage 10 — Restored manageability:</strong> local treatment can reduce immediate pressure for global explanation.</p>
<p><strong>Stage 11 — Accumulated continuity:</strong> years of care produce a coherent documentary pathway.</p>
<p><strong>Stage 12 — Retrospective authority:</strong> “As the letters show” uses that pathway itself as explanatory evidence.</p>
<p><strong>Stage 13 — External endpoint:</strong> 2026 molecularly resolves one of the oldest unresolved etiological questions.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="33-strongest-full-formulation">33. Strongest full formulation<a class="heading-anchor" href="#33-strongest-full-formulation">¶</a></h2>
<p>The strongest current formulation is:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>A broad, deteriorating relational problem entered hospital from primary care: painful/problematic feet, abnormal gait, tone/stiffness, inability to relax, toe clawing/posture, movement-initiation difficulty, dexterity loss, functional decline and an open “?parkinsonian or other underlying diagnosis.” The hospital's immediate neurological task contracted that object around initiation difficulty, four-limb tone/stiffness and cogwheeling. Neurology later recovered much of the omitted history and identified a severe very-young-onset Parkinsonian syndrome, including a lower-limb appearance described as somewhat dystonic and genuine structural foot disease. The critical documentary transformation therefore occurred not because the facts vanished, but between recovery and synthesis: the Parkinsonian/dystonic branch received high-resolution examination, differential reasoning, imaging, DaT investigation, complex-case discussion, genetic reasoning and external specialist input, while overlapping painful/structural/peripheral lower-limb phenomena were grouped more tentatively as potentially separate or neuropathic without an equally visible proposition-by-proposition discriminator connecting or separating those branches. “Plan/Recommendations: As per above” then made that mixed information state operational: treatment, referrals, rehabilitation, Podiatry, follow-up and deferred genetics could proceed while important explanatory relationships remained “perhaps” / “Not clear.” The components acquired service destinations, but responsibility for the arrows between them was less explicit. Later documentation increasingly inherited previous summaries; tentative categories became reusable; new observations repeatedly crossed the original partition and produced genuine local reclassification—Parkinsonian foot throw, OFF dystonia, medication-state pain modulation, renewed neuropathic questions—without a clearly identifiable pre-2025 reconstruction of the original whole. Primary care did not simply lose the relationships: the GP repeatedly regenerated feet–gait–tone–pain links but deferred neurological adjudication to specialist services.
