By March 2025, the treating neurologist's retrospective response reconstructed management history and treatment rationale much more fully than it reconstructed the evidential genealogy by which the disputed pain/neuropathy and etiological categories acquired their later grammatical certainty. “As the letters show” therefore functions most securely as evidence of documentary and management continuity, and only proposition-specifically as evidence of independent revalidation. The external 2025 Movement Disorders/dystonia referral is the clearest later reopening of the parent model. The 2026 PRKN result then resolves one etiological question the historical record itself had generated and deliberately left open; it does not prove what clinicians should have known earlier or establish one mechanism for every historical foot symptom.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="strongest-documentary-object">Strongest documentary object<a class="heading-anchor" href="#strongest-documentary-object">¶</a></h3>
<p>The case should now be described, at its narrowest and strongest, as:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>problem-scope contraction followed by asymmetric epistemic resolution, operational closure, distributed residual ownership, and documentary/classification inheritance.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>The deepest issue is not loss of facts. It is:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>the conversion of an incompletely discriminated specialist classification into a durable organising structure for later facts.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>The most important internal asymmetry is:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>the Parkinsonian/dystonic branch is positively demonstrated at much higher evidential resolution than the peripheral-neuropathic branch, while the surviving documentation does not visibly supply an equivalent bridge explaining how those branches were discriminated within the same lower-limb territory.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>That is a documentary finding. Whether the partition was clinically correct is a clinical-expert question. Whether the resulting ownership/provenance structure met professional or governance standards is a separate expert/governance question.</p>
<hr/>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="counterfactual-refinement">Counterfactual refinement<a class="heading-anchor" href="#counterfactual-refinement">¶</a></h3>
<p>The full formulation should also preserve a narrower counterfactual distinction. The GP referral and the PRECIS were not merely longer and shorter versions of the same question. They were differently organised clinical objects. The GP asked, in effect, what explained a deteriorating relationship among feet, gait, tone, toe posture, movement and function; the PRECIS presented a concentrated motor-neurological task. Because the same neurologist later demonstrated explicit fresh hypothesis-generation in the shoulder problem and specific distal discrimination in a later foot-throw episode, there is a documentary basis for considering <strong>question architecture</strong> as one plausible contributor to the difference in investigative granularity.</p>
<p>The claim should remain:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>different investigative object may plausibly have produced different investigative emphasis</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>not:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>different referral would have produced a different diagnosis.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="34-strongest-defence-and-counter-case">34. Strongest defence and counter-case<a class="heading-anchor" href="#34-strongest-defence-and-counter-case">¶</a></h2>
<p>A fair final document must preserve the strongest contrary evidence.</p>
<p>First, Parkinsonism was real and strongly supported. This is not a case in which a weak Parkinsonian hypothesis displaced everything else.</p>
<p>Second, structural foot disease was real and legitimately treated.</p>
<p>Third, the patient himself distinguished some foot pain from leg tension, giving a contemporaneous reason for separation.</p>
<p>Fourth, the dystonic-looking gait was recognised rather than missed.</p>
<p>Fifth, PRKN uncertainty was explicit rather than concealed.</p>
<p>Sixth, later care contains genuine fresh reasoning: foot throw, OFF dystonia, abrupt onset against neuropathy, Fabry testing, medication-state reasoning and pain analysis.</p>
<p>Seventh, chronic disease care legitimately relies on longitudinal summaries; re-deriving a mature case from zero at every appointment is neither realistic nor necessarily desirable.</p>
<p>Eighth, the shoulder problem was acute, focal, anatomically localisable and amenable to structural testing. Greater proposition-by-proposition detail may therefore be clinically appropriate without implying path-dependent constraint.</p>
<p>Ninth, absence of detailed documentation is not proof that an examination was not performed.</p>
<p>These considerations materially weaken any formulation based on “no thought,” “no reassessment,” “feet ignored,” “structure unreal,” or “framework frozen.”</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="35-what-would-falsify-or-materially-weaken-the-formulation">35. What would falsify or materially weaken the formulation<a class="heading-anchor" href="#35-what-would-falsify-or-materially-weaken-the-formulation">¶</a></h2>
<p>The analysis should specify its own falsifiers.</p>
<p><strong>Falsifier 1: contemporaneous 2017 distal reconstruction.</strong> A note mapping dynamic toe/foot posture, fixed versus dynamic deformity, distal power, sensation, pain distribution, loading/pressure sites and gait relationship, followed by explicit reasoning on neurological versus structural versus peripheral mechanisms.</p>
<p><strong>Falsifier 2: explicit ownership/reopening plan.</strong> A source stating that the feet–gait–tone relationship remained unresolved, Neurology retained ownership, Podiatry findings were to return, and specified triggers would reopen the model.</p>
<p><strong>Falsifier 3: formal PRKN closure before 2025.</strong> A negative PRKN test, specialist downgrading, reasoned rejection, or explicit statement that iPD was only operational shorthand pending molecular clarification.</p>
<p><strong>Falsifier 4: later global reconstruction.</strong> A later Neurology or Movement Disorders source gathering structural feet, pain, dystonia, gait, OFF state, neuropathy, treatment responses and genetics into a new parent model.</p>
<p><strong>Falsifier 5: shoulder provenance failure.</strong> If the key shoulder reasoning was not authored by the treating neurologist or was copied from another source, its force as same-author control would diminish.</p>
<p><strong>Falsifier 6: full-referral framing already active.</strong> If the canonical 2017 record shows that the treating neurologist reviewed the complete GP referral before designing the investigation, explicitly reconstructed the integrated feet–gait–tone–toe–deterioration trajectory, and treated that relationship as a primary decision-live problem, the claim that the PRECIS plausibly altered the initial question architecture would weaken substantially.</p>
<p>The final case should actively search for these sources rather than assume they do not exist.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="36-expert-questions-movement-disorders">36. Expert questions — Movement Disorders<a class="heading-anchor" href="#36-expert-questions-movement-disorders">¶</a></h2>
<p>A Movement Disorders expert should be asked focused questions rather than a global negligence question.</p>
<ol>
<li>What phenotype is documented in the contemporaneous 2017 record?</li>
<li>What lower-limb relationships would ordinarily be clinically important in a very-young-onset Parkinsonian presentation with painful feet, toe phenomena, structural deformity and dystonic-looking gait?</li>
<li>What does the documented examination establish and not establish?</li>
<li>What examination would distinguish fixed deformity from dynamic dystonia or mixed loading?</li>
<li>What significance, if any, should be attached to the dystonic-looking gait in 2017?</li>
<li>What significance should be attached to foot/gait changes with procyclidine withdrawal?</li>
<li>What significance should be attached to later normal directional foot power and Parkinsonian reclassification?</li>
<li>Was PRKN a clinically meaningful hypothesis at the time?</li>
<li>What would PRKN confirmation ordinarily change in diagnosis, prognosis, counselling, referral or treatment, if anything?</li>
<li>What later observations would ordinarily reduce confidence in the original structural/peripheral partition?</li>
<li>At what point, if any, would repeated OFF/dystonia/treatment-state observations justify global reassessment?</li>
<li>How should the 2026 endpoint be used without hindsight?</li>
</ol>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="37-expert-questions-pain-neuropathy">37. Expert questions — pain/neuropathy<a class="heading-anchor" href="#37-expert-questions-pain-neuropathy">¶</a></h2>
<ol>
<li>What findings are ordinarily required to classify pain as neuropathic?</li>
<li>How discriminating are burning and exquisite sensitivity?</li>
<li>What weight can be given to pregabalin response?</li>
<li>What significance does severe pain despite high-dose pregabalin have?</li>
<li>What significance does literal overnight onset have?</li>
<li>Can Parkinsonian OFF states magnify or generate pain without proving that the underlying pain is directly Parkinsonian?</li>
<li>How should substantial Sinemet/Sinemet-CR response alter the differential?</li>
<li>How should persistent toe curling despite analgesic benefit be interpreted?</li>
<li>What objective findings would weaken or falsify a peripheral/neuropathic classification?</li>
<li>What evidence would justify describing a symptom phenotype as neuropathic while disease-level neuropathy remains uncertain?</li>
</ol>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="38-expert-questions-podiatry-biomechanics">38. Expert questions — podiatry/biomechanics<a class="heading-anchor" href="#38-expert-questions-podiatry-biomechanics">¶</a></h2>
<ol>
<li>Could dystonic gait or abnormal lower-limb posture contribute to focal loading and recurrent callus formation?</li>
<li>How can primary structural disease be distinguished from secondary loading effects?</li>
<li>Can pes planus/hammertoes coexist with neurologically driven toe posture or dystonia?</li>
<li>What does recurrent fifth-MTPJ pathology signify mechanically?</li>
<li>What examination would help decide whether a structural and neurological mechanism are independent or mixed?</li>
<li>What information would ideally travel back to Neurology where dynamic posture is also present?</li>
</ol>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="39-documentary-linguistic-questions">39. Documentary/linguistic questions<a class="heading-anchor" href="#39-documentary-linguistic-questions">¶</a></h2>
<ol>
<li>How does incorporation by reference affect later semantic stability?</li>
<li>Does repeated same-author wording constitute independent corroboration?</li>
<li>What is the significance of grammatical movement from “perhaps” to affirmative noun phrases?</li>
<li>How does the record distinguish category uncertainty from cause uncertainty?</li>
<li>How do “As per above” and “As the letters show” function prospectively and retrospectively?</li>
<li>Does the shoulder episode demonstrate a materially different discourse form?</li>
<li>Can differences be described as information-state/path dependence without inferring motive?</li>
<li>When does continuity become documentary recursion rather than new evidential support?</li>
</ol>
<hr/>

<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="41-placeholder-ledger-unresolved-source-issues">41. Placeholder ledger — unresolved source issues<a class="heading-anchor" href="#41-placeholder-ledger-unresolved-source-issues">¶</a></h2>

<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="42-proposition-ledger-strongly-supported-documentary-propositions">42. Proposition ledger — strongly supported documentary propositions<a class="heading-anchor" href="#42-proposition-ledger-strongly-supported-documentary-propositions">¶</a></h2>
<p><strong>P1.</strong> The GP referral described an integrated and worsening feet–gait–tone–movement–function presentation.</p>
<p><strong>P2.</strong> The hospital PRECIS foregrounded the cardinal motor-neurological signs and did not preserve the full relational framing.</p>
<p><strong>P3.</strong> Neurology later recovered many omitted component facts.</p>
<p><strong>P4.</strong> The admission examination documented a dystonic-looking footdrop-like gait and structural foot abnormalities.</p>
<p><strong>P5.</strong> The surviving documented distal structural–dynamic–pain examination is less granular than the final central/genetic synthesis.</p>
<p><strong>P6.</strong> The 2017 synthesis generated a specific PRKN/PARK2 hypothesis.</p>
<p><strong>P7.</strong> Prof Healy independently supported PARK2 and recommended testing.</p>
<p><strong>P8.</strong> PRKN remained suspected in 2018 while testing was deliberately deferred because management could continue without knowing.</p>
<p><strong>P9.</strong> “As per above” made an unresolved synthesis operational.</p>
<p><strong>P10.</strong> Later notes explicitly relied on older letters/summaries.</p>
<p><strong>P11.</strong> Later lower-limb evidence crossed the original documentary compartments and produced genuine local reassessment.</p>
<p><strong>P12.</strong> The same neurologist demonstrated explicit fresh hypothesis-testing in the shoulder problem.</p>
<p><strong>P13.</strong> Neuropathic pain language became more affirmative at symptom-classification level while disease-level neuropathy could remain conditional.</p>
<p><strong>P14.</strong> The 2025 response relied explicitly on accumulated letters to explain historical management.</p>
<p><strong>P15.</strong> The 2026 PRKN result resolved a question already present in 2017–2018.</p>
<p><strong>P16.</strong> The GP referral and hospital PRECIS presented materially different problem representations: the former an integrated deteriorating relationship under an open diagnosis, the latter a narrower motor-neurological task.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="43-proposition-ledger-documentary-inferences-requiring-careful-language">43. Proposition ledger — documentary inferences requiring careful language<a class="heading-anchor" href="#43-proposition-ledger-documentary-inferences-requiring-careful-language">¶</a></h2>
<p><strong>I1.</strong> The PRECIS may have contributed to relational rather than factual compression.</p>
<p><strong>I2.</strong> The 2017 synthesis shows asymmetric allocation of analytical resolution.</p>
<p><strong>I3.</strong> Operational destinations may have reduced immediate pressure for global explanatory resolution.</p>
<p><strong>I4.</strong> The chronic record shows path dependence through inheritance of a mature information state.</p>
<p><strong>I5.</strong> Later reasoning is more often local/accommodative than globally reconstructive.</p>
<p><strong>I6.</strong> The shoulder episode supports a difference in information-state reasoning mode rather than lack of analytical capacity.</p>
<p><strong>I7.</strong> Neuropathic terminology underwent semantic/classificatory hardening.</p>
<p><strong>I8.</strong> By 2025 documentary continuity had acquired retrospective explanatory authority.</p>
<p><strong>I9.</strong> Preserving the GP referral as the primary organising document would plausibly have kept the feet–gait–tone–toe relationship more explicitly decision-live; this is a documentary counterfactual about investigative emphasis, not a prediction of diagnostic outcome.</p>
<p>Each of these remains an inference about the organisation of the record, not a medical-standard conclusion.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="44-proposition-ledger-expert-questions">44. Proposition ledger — expert questions<a class="heading-anchor" href="#44-proposition-ledger-expert-questions">¶</a></h2>
<p><strong>E1.</strong> Whether the 2017 distal examination was clinically adequate.</p>
<p><strong>E2.</strong> Whether Podiatry allocation was appropriate without more neurological integration.</p>
<p><strong>E3.</strong> Whether PRKN testing should have occurred earlier.</p>
<p><strong>E4.</strong> Whether earlier PRKN confirmation would have altered treatment, prognosis, counselling or referral.</p>
<p><strong>E5.</strong> Whether particular foot pain episodes were dystonic, neuropathic, structural, mixed, or Parkinsonian-modulated.</p>
<p><strong>E6.</strong> Whether 2023 or 2025 evidence required global reconstruction.</p>
<p><strong>E7.</strong> Whether the difference between shoulder and feet reasoning was clinically appropriate.</p>
<p><strong>E8.</strong> Whether the 2025 complaint response adequately addressed the provenance challenge rather than only management within the existing framework.</p>
<p><strong>E9.</strong> Whether the fuller GP problem representation would reasonably have required different or more granular distal examination, differential testing, or cross-service integration.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="45-propositions-not-to-assert-on-the-present-documentary-record">45. Propositions not to assert on the present documentary record<a class="heading-anchor" href="#45-propositions-not-to-assert-on-the-present-documentary-record">¶</a></h2>
<p>Do not assert deliberate concealment, dishonesty, bad faith, incompetence, negligence, malpractice, intentional anchoring, conscious refusal to reconsider, that every foot symptom was dystonia, that no peripheral neuropathy existed, that structural disease was irrelevant, or that 2026 proves what clinicians should have known in 2017.</p>
<p>Do not convert four GPT outputs into four independent expert opinions. Their value is convergence across separately produced documentary analyses, especially where they identify the same source anchors, chronology and counterevidence. Prompt-seeded evaluative convergence should be given less weight than source-level convergence.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="46-suggested-final-chapter-architecture">46. Suggested final chapter architecture<a class="heading-anchor" href="#46-suggested-final-chapter-architecture">¶</a></h2>
<p><strong>Chapter 1 — What entered Neurology.</strong> Build the GP trajectory without projecting later categories backwards.</p>
<p><strong>Chapter 2 — What the hospital task representation selected.</strong> Show PRECIS compression and distinguish fact loss from relationship compression.</p>
<p><strong>Chapter 2A — The counterfactual investigative object.</strong> Compare the GP and PRECIS as competing question architectures; use the shoulder and later foot-throw as same-author controls; keep the conclusion at investigative emphasis rather than predicted diagnosis.</p>
<p><strong>Chapter 3 — What was actually examined.</strong> Separate history, observation, immediate interpretation and later synthesis.</p>
<p><strong>Chapter 4 — How the sophisticated 2017 model was built.</strong> Explain Parkinsonism, atypicality, genetic thinking, PRKN, dystonic-looking gait, structural feet and peripheral possibility.</p>
<p><strong>Chapter 5 — How uncertainty became a plan.</strong> Focus on “Not clear,” Podiatry, genetics and “As per above.”</p>
<p><strong>Chapter 6 — How the plan became an inherited information state.</strong> Use older-letter and summary language.</p>
<p><strong>Chapter 7 — How biology repeatedly crossed the partitions.</strong> Use treatment-state changes, Podiatry truth, foot throw, OFF dystonia, neuropathy timing and pain modulation.</p>
<p><strong>Chapter 8 — The author’s own internal control.</strong> Use the shoulder episode to show fresh-model reasoning.</p>
<p><strong>Chapter 9 — Semantic migration and pain classification.</strong> Trace neuropathic terminology.</p>
<p><strong>Chapter 10 — When management becomes endurance/safety relevant.</strong> Use 2023 onward.</p>
<p><strong>Chapter 11 — The complaint forces a genealogy test.</strong> Distinguish management explanation from classification provenance.</p>
<p><strong>Chapter 12 — PRKN endpoint.</strong> Introduce 2026 last.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="47-working-expert-instruction-strategy">47. Working expert-instruction strategy<a class="heading-anchor" href="#47-working-expert-instruction-strategy">¶</a></h2>
<p>The expert should initially be asked to evaluate propositions rather than conclusions. The most useful instructions will likely begin with the contemporaneous phenotype, ask what examination and differential would ordinarily be expected, ask what significance should be assigned to dynamic lower-limb signs and treatment-state changes, and then ask what later information would reasonably require broader reassessment.</p>
<p>The expert should be given the strongest contrary evidence at the same time: real structural disease, patient-perceived distinction, detailed Parkinsonian examination, genuine later reassessments, chronic-care efficiency and the anatomical differences between the shoulder and feet problems.</p>
<p>A neutral expert package should therefore be adversarial to the working theory rather than merely confirmatory.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="48-why-the-case-is-strongest-as-an-information-pathway-case">48. Why the case is strongest as an information-pathway case<a class="heading-anchor" href="#48-why-the-case-is-strongest-as-an-information-pathway-case">¶</a></h2>
<p>The case is not presently strongest as a simple missed-diagnosis case, a simple failure-to-examine case, a simple “neuropathy was wrong” case, or a simple “PRKN should have been diagnosed in 2017” case. Those formulations are too crude and invite easy rebuttal.</p>
<p>The stronger account is that a broad, worsening relational clinical object entered; its immediate representation became narrower; the specialist process recovered facts but unevenly reconstructed relationships; the final synthesis achieved exceptional sophistication in one domain; unresolved relationships nonetheless became actionable; that action state became portable; new evidence repeatedly crossed the categories; local reasoning remained active and sometimes sophisticated; the original parent relationship is less visibly reconstructed than its branches are updated; a fresh same-author problem demonstrates what full proposition-by-proposition causal reconstruction looks like in this clinician’s own writing; by 2025 accumulated documentary continuity was itself being used retrospectively as explanatory evidence; and by 2026 one of the oldest unresolved etiological questions was finally molecularly resolved.</p>
<p>That architecture is more precise, more falsifiable and less accusatory than a simple anchoring narrative.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="49-formulations-to-protect">49. Formulations to protect<a class="heading-anchor" href="#49-formulations-to-protect">¶</a></h2>
<p><strong>Core:</strong></p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The problem is not lack of sophistication. It is where the sophistication was applied.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p><strong>Information state:</strong></p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>When the model was fresh, uncertainty generated questions. Once the model was inherited, uncertainty was more often managed within it.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p><strong>PRECIS:</strong></p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The facts came back. The relationships did not come back with equal force.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p><strong>Counterfactual / question architecture:</strong></p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The counterfactual is not different diagnosis. It is different investigative object.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The full GP referral made the relationships themselves part of the presenting question; the PRECIS made the motor-neurological subset the immediate task.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p><strong>As per above:</strong></p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>“As per above” did not make the uncertainties disappear; it made an arrangement of those uncertainties operationally durable.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p><strong>Longitudinal:</strong></p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The inherited architecture was permeable, not frozen: later evidence repeatedly crossed its boundaries and generated genuine local reassessment. The surviving record nevertheless shows local updating more clearly than a later global reconstruction of the founding feet–gait–tone–pain relationship.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p><strong>Retrospective:</strong></p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>Continuity of a pathway can retrospectively resemble validation of the pathway.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p><strong>PRKN:</strong></p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The record demonstrates continuity of the Parkinson’s diagnosis far more clearly than it demonstrates resolution of Parkinson’s etiology.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="50-next-build-tasks">50. Next-build tasks<a class="heading-anchor" href="#50-next-build-tasks">¶</a></h2>
<ul>
<li>[PLACEHOLDER: build verified master chronology with exact dates and CLIN ranges.]</li>
<li>[PLACEHOLDER: create fact / inference / expert question / legal question matrix.]</li>
<li>[PLACEHOLDER: create proposition genealogy for peripheral neuropathy / neuropathic pain.]</li>
<li>[PLACEHOLDER: create proposition genealogy for idiopathic Parkinson’s / PRKN.]</li>
<li>[PLACEHOLDER: create ownership-of-arrows map: Neurology / Podiatry / GP / Movement Disorders nursing / external specialist.]</li>
<li>[PLACEHOLDER: create treatment-state matrix: procyclidine / pregabalin / Sinemet / Stalevo / Sinemet CR.]</li>
<li>[PLACEHOLDER: verify whether any independent Movement Disorders specialist reconstructed the founding phenotype before the external 2025 pathway.]</li>
<li>[PLACEHOLDER: verify original and repeated provenance of “I am not a movement disorders neurologist”.]</li>
<li>[PLACEHOLDER: verify shoulder imaging results and preserve only those present in the canonical record.]</li>
<li>[PLACEHOLDER: verify whether the treating neurologist had, read, quoted, or explicitly worked from the complete GP referral before or during the initial investigative design; distinguish access from use as organising frame.]</li>
<li>[PLACEHOLDER: identify every “not clear”, “we simply don’t know”, “if”, “perhaps”, “whatever cause”, “as per above”, “as detailed above”, and “as the letters show”.]</li>
<li>[PLACEHOLDER: prepare neutral expert instruction letter.]</li>
<li>[PLACEHOLDER: prepare strongest-benign-explanation rebuttal section.]</li>
<li>[PLACEHOLDER: prepare falsification appendix for each major proposition.]</li>
</ul>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="appendix-a-why-relationship-preservation-matters">Appendix A — Why relationship preservation matters<a class="heading-anchor" href="#appendix-a-why-relationship-preservation-matters">¶</a></h2>
<p>A longitudinal record can be rich in observations and still lose the question that originally made those observations clinically meaningful together. That distinction is central here. The GP referral placed feet, gait, tone, toe posture, movement initiation, dexterity, deterioration and work impact within a single evolving communication. Later specialist care recovered much of this material, often in greater detail. But factual recovery does not necessarily equal relational recovery.</p>
<p>Consider the difference between recording pes planus and asking whether abnormal dynamic posture changes loading through a pes planus foot. Consider the difference between documenting hammertoes and asking whether reported toe clawing is fixed deformity, dynamic dystonia, or both. Consider the difference between recording burning pain and asking whether its timing, distribution, loading relationship and medication-state behaviour support a peripheral neuropathic mechanism, a central modulation mechanism, a dystonic mechanism, structural pain, or a mixture.</p>
<p>The documentary question is therefore not whether enough facts exist somewhere in the chart. It is whether the relationships among those facts remain visible, testable and owned over time.</p>
<p>This is what the phrase “who owned the arrows?” is intended to capture. Each service can own a node. Podiatry can own structural lesions. Neurology can own Parkinsonism. Primary care can own continuity. Nursing can own symptom escalation and medication communication. Yet the arrow between structural loading and neurological posture may remain difficult to assign. A fragmented system can still work well when the relationship itself has a clear owner or reopening mechanism. The source-audit question is whether that architecture is visible here.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="appendix-b-why-operational-closure-is-not-a-criticism-by-itself">Appendix B — Why operational closure is not a criticism by itself<a class="heading-anchor" href="#appendix-b-why-operational-closure-is-not-a-criticism-by-itself">¶</a></h2>
<p>Operational closure is normal medicine. A patient cannot remain indefinitely in diagnostic suspension while every causal question is solved. Treatment, rehabilitation, referral and follow-up often proceed under uncertainty. The 2017 record itself demonstrates this explicitly.</p>
<p>The analytical value of operational closure is that it explains how a record can be simultaneously sophisticated, uncertain and actionable. “As per above” can therefore be read as a management device rather than a declaration of epistemic certainty.</p>
<p>The vulnerability arises only if unresolved questions later become difficult to reactivate because the working arrangement functions adequately. The 2018 PRKN passage is especially useful because the clinician expressly states that current management does not depend on knowing the molecular cause. That may be entirely defensible. The longitudinal issue is what happens to the question after it stops obstructing immediate action.</p>
<p>This distinction should protect the case from the false premise that all uncertainty requires immediate closure. The more precise question is whether uncertainty was <strong>managed</strong>, including how it was carried, who owned it, and what would trigger its return to the foreground.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="appendix-c-why-the-shoulder-comparator-matters-without-proving-causation">Appendix C — Why the shoulder comparator matters without proving causation<a class="heading-anchor" href="#appendix-c-why-the-shoulder-comparator-matters-without-proving-causation">¶</a></h2>
<p>The shoulder comparator is strongest when used narrowly. It does not prove that the feet should have been investigated with identical tests or that the same level of anatomical localisation was clinically required. Different problems warrant different methods.</p>
<p>Its value is to establish a documentary capability. The same neurologist could explicitly separate observation from inference, name multiple competing hypotheses, state what each finding would mean, articulate the purpose and limits of tests, and reject treatment response as a simple proof of mechanism.</p>
<p>That matters because it makes one class of explanation less persuasive: the idea that sparse relational reasoning elsewhere merely reflects this clinician’s normal inability or unwillingness to document analytical uncertainty. The record shows that, under at least some conditions, he documented it in detail.</p>
<p>The inference that remains is not personal but structural: mature inherited problems may be processed differently from fresh ones. That is consistent with ordinary clinical efficiency, and it is also consistent with documentary path dependence. The reason for the difference cannot be settled by language alone. The comparison simply identifies the difference more securely.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="appendix-d-why-anchoring-is-not-the-preferred-primary-term">Appendix D — Why ‘anchoring’ is not the preferred primary term<a class="heading-anchor" href="#appendix-d-why-anchoring-is-not-the-preferred-primary-term">¶</a></h2>
<p>“Anchoring” is a cognitive label. It suggests that a clinician fixed on an initial explanation and insufficiently adjusted thereafter. The documentary record cannot directly reveal cognitive state. It can show stable categories, repeated wording, inherited summaries, local reinterpretations, missing bridges and delayed reconstruction.</p>
<p>For that reason, the primary terminology should remain observational:</p>
<ul>
<li>relational compression;</li>
<li>inherited information state;</li>
<li>operational closure;</li>
<li>documentary inheritance;</li>
<li>semantic hardening;</li>
<li>local updating;</li>
<li>global reconstruction;</li>
<li>generative versus accommodative reasoning;</li>
<li>path dependence.</li>
</ul>
<p>An expert may later conclude that anchoring is an appropriate clinical description. Until then, the record-based language is both stronger and fairer because it describes what can actually be demonstrated.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="appendix-e-why-the-2025-response-is-analytically-special">Appendix E — Why the 2025 response is analytically special<a class="heading-anchor" href="#appendix-e-why-the-2025-response-is-analytically-special">¶</a></h2>
<p>The 2025 response is not merely another entry in the treatment history. It is a response to a challenge about the history of reasoning itself. That changes the level of analysis.</p>
<p>When the response says “As the letters show,” it invokes documentary continuity as evidence. That is entirely legitimate for proving that treatments were offered, risks considered, and management choices made. But if the complaint concerns how a tentative 2017 classification became a durable later category, the response must be analysed for whether it reconstructs that classificatory genealogy.</p>
<p>This is where the phrase “Whatever the underlying cause for the peripheral neuropathic pain symptomatologies...” matters. It preserves uncertainty about cause while grammatically treating the symptom category as comparatively settled. That may simply be ordinary shorthand. But if the complaint challenges the parent category itself, beginning inside the category answers a different level of question.</p>
<p>The response should therefore be read proposition by proposition: what does it explain well, what does it presuppose, what does it reopen, and what remains inherited.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="appendix-f-why-the-prkn-endpoint-should-be-introduced-last">Appendix F — Why the PRKN endpoint should be introduced last<a class="heading-anchor" href="#appendix-f-why-the-prkn-endpoint-should-be-introduced-last">¶</a></h2>
<p>The eventual PRKN result is unusually powerful because it resonates with early age, symmetry, rigidity, cognition and dystonic features. That very power creates risk. If introduced too early, it can cause the analyst to reinterpret every prior ambiguity in its light.</p>
<p>The safer method is chronological sequestration. First determine what the 2017 record actually observed and thought. Then determine what happened to the PRKN question in 2018 and thereafter. Only then introduce 2026.</p>
<p>Used this way, the endpoint does something precise: it demonstrates that a historical etiological branch, already generated from the contemporaneous phenotype and independently reinforced, was ultimately real and resolvable. It does not decide the standard of care or every historical symptom mechanism.</p>
<p>This disciplined sequencing strengthens rather than weakens the significance of the endpoint.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="appendix-g-draft-one-page-theory">Appendix G — Draft one-page theory<a class="heading-anchor" href="#appendix-g-draft-one-page-theory">¶</a></h2>
<p>The patient entered Neurology in 2017 with a worsening, integrated lower-limb and movement presentation in which painful/problematic feet, abnormal gait, toe posture, increased tone, movement-initiation difficulty, dexterity loss and functional decline were still being presented together under an open diagnostic question. The hospital’s short task representation foregrounded the most recognisable neurological signs, particularly initiation difficulty, tone/stiffness and cogwheeling. During the full admission Neurology recovered much of the omitted history and documented a severe, symmetrical Parkinsonian syndrome, a strikingly abnormal footdrop-like gait considered somewhat dystonic rather than true footdrop, and genuine structural foot abnormalities.</p>
<p>The final 2017 synthesis was sophisticated. It developed young-onset Parkinsonism, atypicality, autosomal-recessive reasoning and a specific PRKN/PARK2 hypothesis. At the same time, the painful/structural foot material was left at lower relational resolution: possibly separate, perhaps peripheral neuropathy, “Not clear.” The practical plan could nevertheless proceed through Neurology, Podiatry, medication, rehabilitation and follow-up. “Plan/Recommendations: As per above” therefore appears to have made an unresolved but elaborate synthesis operational without demonstrating complete explanatory closure.</p>
<p>The later record makes inheritance of that information state explicit through reliance on older letters and summaries. PRKN itself provides a clear example of uncertainty becoming operationally tolerable: in 2018 it remained the suspected cause, yet testing was deferred because current management would not change and there was “no great loss in not knowing” at that time.</p>
<p>Subsequent biology repeatedly generated information crossing the operational compartments. Feet and gait changed with neurological treatment state. A later foot-throw was specifically examined and classified as Parkinsonian. OFF dystonia was considered. Abrupt onset was used against straightforward peripheral neuropathy. Fabry was investigated. Pregabalin and dopaminergic therapies both altered symptom states. Structural Podiatry findings remained real. These are not signs of a closed or thoughtless system. They show meaningful local updating.</p>
<p>The documentary question is whether those local revisions were ever assembled into a new account of the original whole. The surviving record more clearly shows individual branches being adjusted than the original feet–gait–tone–pain relationship being re-derived globally.</p>
<p>The 2019 shoulder episode provides a powerful internal comparator. The same neurologist, when faced with a fresh focal problem, explicitly constructs competing anatomical hypotheses, links examination findings to those hypotheses, orders tests for particular questions, specifies limitations of negative tests, and expressly refuses to treat medication response as proof of cause. This demonstrates analytical capacity and supports a narrower information-state interpretation: fresh problems are visibly modelled from the ground up, while chronic inherited problems are more often updated inside an existing architecture.</p>
<p>By 2025 the accumulated record had acquired retrospective explanatory force. The complaint asked for review of reasoning across the whole period, and the response relied on what “the letters show.” That reliance may accurately prove continuity of treatment and decision-making while leaving a separate source-audit question about whether the founding classifications were independently revalidated.</p>
<p>The February 2026 PRKN result is important only after that historical analysis is complete. It resolved the same specific genetic question generated in 2017, independently reinforced, and deliberately left unresolved in 2018. It does not prove that every historical foot symptom was dystonia or that all earlier decisions were wrong. It confirms that at least one major unresolved etiological branch was real, specific and ultimately answerable.</p>
<p>The current strongest formulation is therefore not a claim of diagnostic incompetence. It is a claim about the architecture of information: <strong>the problem is not lack of sophistication; it is where the sophistication was applied.</strong> And the same-author comparator supports a second formulation: <strong>when the model was fresh, uncertainty generated questions; once the model was inherited, uncertainty was more often managed within it.</strong></p>
</section>
<div class="appendix-intro" id="supporting-material"><strong>Supporting record &amp; audit material</strong>The material below preserves the natural-control work, the 50-question anti-drift audit, answer-status pass and pre-registered source-testing protocols. It is intentionally complete, but no longer interrupts the main reading path.</div><section class="reader-section kind-support kind-audit" data-section="Canonical GP information-state trial" id="section-gp-trial">
<div class="section-kicker">Canonical GP information-state trial</div>
<h2 class="source-h1 anchored-heading" id="part-xxxii-canonical-gp-information-state-trial-sequential-findings-after-unblinding">Part XXXII — Canonical GP information-state trial: sequential findings after unblinding<a class="heading-anchor" href="#part-xxxii-canonical-gp-information-state-trial-sequential-findings-after-unblinding">¶</a></h2>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="114-trial-status-and-source-discipline">114. Trial status and source discipline<a class="heading-anchor" href="#114-trial-status-and-source-discipline">¶</a></h2>
<p>This section records the results of the pre-registered GP information-state audit after review of the canonical record supplied after the hypotheses had been frozen.</p>
<p>The exercise is not literally blind in the experimental sense. Earlier controlled work had already exposed selected quotations and broad themes. The protection against drift is therefore procedural rather than absolute: the object, hypotheses, timepoints and falsifiers were frozen before the canonical GP/Podiatry sequence was examined as a whole.</p>
<p>The trial asks only what the GP's <strong>documented and operational information state</strong> shows at each timepoint. It does not infer private mental state. It also does not treat later PRKN results as information available to the GP before 2026.</p>
<p>The most important finding is that the GP pathway is not a simple story of specialist framing being passively copied into primary care. The GP repeatedly preserves or regenerates the relationships among feet, gait, tone, Parkinsonian state and pain, while relying on specialist Neurology to adjudicate questions beyond primary-care scope.</p>
<p>That distinction now becomes an important control for the wider case.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="115-state-1-before-hospital-integrated-relational-object">115. State 1 — Before hospital: integrated relational object<a class="heading-anchor" href="#115-state-1-before-hospital-integrated-relational-object">¶</a></h2>
<p>The pre-hospital GP state is strongly integrated.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="115-1-april-2017-begins-with-feet-and-gait-together">115.1 April 2017 begins with feet and gait together<a class="heading-anchor" href="#115-1-april-2017-begins-with-feet-and-gait-together">¶</a></h3>
<p>The first GP presentation is coded as a FOOT/TOE complaint. The record contains bilateral foot pain and abnormal gait together, with marked mallet toes, onychogryphosis, callouses and pressure areas. Podiatry is planned, but the GP does not stop at a structural explanation.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="115-2-june-2017-explicitly-joins-neurology-to-gait-feet-and-muscle-tension">115.2 June 2017 explicitly joins Neurology to gait, feet and muscle tension<a class="heading-anchor" href="#115-2-june-2017-explicitly-joins-neurology-to-gait-feet-and-muscle-tension">¶</a></h3>
<p>Before the urgent referral, the GP records movement-initiation difficulty and says he may seek a neurological opinion of the patient's:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>gait, feet and "muscle tension" issues.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>This is direct evidence that the GP's neurological question included the feet rather than beginning from a pre-separated Parkinsonism object.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="115-3-occupational-health-independently-increases-concern">115.3 Occupational Health independently increases concern<a class="heading-anchor" href="#115-3-occupational-health-independently-increases-concern">¶</a></h3>
<p>A second doctor contacts the GP regarding the gait. The GP records that this colleague echoes his concerns and decides to refer to Neurology even before older records are obtained.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="115-4-the-urgent-referral-preserves-the-full-trajectory">115.4 The urgent referral preserves the full trajectory<a class="heading-anchor" href="#115-4-the-urgent-referral-preserves-the-full-trajectory">¶</a></h3>
<p>The 29 June referral presents:</p>
<ul>
<li>bilateral foot issues and poor foot condition;</li>
<li>unusual festinant and stiff gait;</li>
<li>increasingly evident movement-initiation difficulty;</li>
<li>marked upper- and lower-limb stiffness/tone;</li>
<li>inability to relax;</li>
<li>toe clawing in bed;</li>
<li>worsening dexterity;</li>
<li>work and daily-activity decline;</li>
<li>an open question of "?parkinsonian or other underlying diagnosis";</li>
<li>explicit deterioration and employment risk.</li>
</ul>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="frozen-state-1-conclusion">Frozen State-1 conclusion<a class="heading-anchor" href="#frozen-state-1-conclusion">¶</a></h3>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>Before hospitalisation, the GP's clinical object is relational and prospective: feet, gait, tone, toe posture, movement initiation and function are travelling together under an open neurological question.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>This strongly confirms the pre-registered hypothesis that the GP began with an integrated problem representation.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="116-state-2-during-admission-and-specialist-return-diagnosis-gains-authority-but-the-feet-are-not-simply-detached">116. State 2 — During admission and specialist return: diagnosis gains authority, but the feet are not simply detached<a class="heading-anchor" href="#116-state-2-during-admission-and-specialist-return-diagnosis-gains-authority-but-the-feet-are-not-simply-detached">¶</a></h2>
<p>The second state produces a more complex result than the initial hypothesis predicted.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="116-1-during-the-admission-the-gp-re-contacts-podiatry-as-parkinson-s">116.1 During the admission the GP re-contacts Podiatry "as Parkinson's"<a class="heading-anchor" href="#116-1-during-the-admission-the-gp-re-contacts-podiatry-as-parkinson-s">¶</a></h3>
<p>On 6 July 2017, while the hospital admission is still ongoing, the GP phones Podiatry and records that the case will be marked as a priority:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>"as Parkinson's."</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>This is important.</p>
<p>The GP does not merely continue a pre-existing structural-foot referral in a separate silo. He actively updates the Podiatry pathway with the newly emerging neurological diagnosis.</p>
<p>Documentarily, this is an early cross-service integration signal:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>feet destination + Parkinsonian information travel together.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>It does not establish what detailed information Podiatry received or what causal relationship the GP believed existed.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="116-2-the-short-discharge-summary-presented-to-the-gp-is-parkinsonism-dominant">116.2 The short discharge summary presented to the GP is Parkinsonism-dominant<a class="heading-anchor" href="#116-2-the-short-discharge-summary-presented-to-the-gp-is-parkinsonism-dominant">¶</a></h3>
<p>The electronic discharge summary identifies Parkinson's disease as the primary diagnosis and summarises the chief complaint as movement-initiation difficulty, bradykinesia, reduced dexterity, impaired balance and unsteady gait. It records clonazepam for leg tension and a Podiatry referral for "issues with feet reported by the patient."</p>
<p>This short summary does not reproduce the integrated GP trajectory or the full later lower-limb uncertainty.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="116-3-the-gp-subsequently-records-receipt-of-extensive-neurology-correspondence">116.3 The GP subsequently records receipt of extensive Neurology correspondence<a class="heading-anchor" href="#116-3-the-gp-subsequently-records-receipt-of-extensive-neurology-correspondence">¶</a></h3>
<p>On 18 July the GP records:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>"Extensive correspondence Dr Thomas Monaghan - Diagnosis early Parkinsonism. Further testing awaited but positive DaTscan and clinical features - see scanning."</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>The long Neurology correspondence is addressed to the GP and includes the full sophisticated synthesis, including the foot-history discussion, "completely separate / perhaps secondary peripheral neuropathy / Not clear," the dystonic-looking gait and the genetic reasoning.</p>
<p>The GP therefore appears to have received far more than the short electronic discharge summary.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="116-4-what-changes-in-the-gp-record-immediately-afterwards">116.4 What changes in the GP record immediately afterwards<a class="heading-anchor" href="#116-4-what-changes-in-the-gp-record-immediately-afterwards">¶</a></h3>
<p>The GP codes Parkinson's disease/Parkinsonism and manages medication titration, certification and return-to-work issues within that diagnosis.</p>
<p>There is no contemporaneous GP note visible here explicitly reopening the neurological-foot relationship immediately after receipt of the long Neurology letter.</p>
<p>That does not establish that the GP concluded the question was fully resolved.</p>
<p>It does establish that the specialist diagnosis becomes the dominant operative category in primary care.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="frozen-state-2-conclusion">Frozen State-2 conclusion<a class="heading-anchor" href="#frozen-state-2-conclusion">¶</a></h3>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The Neurology diagnosis acquires strong operational authority in the GP record, but the GP does not simply route the feet away from Parkinsonism: even during the admission he updates Podiatry "as Parkinson's." The evidence therefore supports specialist-authority effects without supporting a simple "GP adopted feet as separate" theory.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>This qualifies the pre-registered "apparent specialist clearance" hypothesis. Specialist authority is real; complete relational extinguishing is not demonstrated.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="117-state-3-podiatry-20172019-structural-truth-accumulates-while-the-gp-later-explicitly-reconnects-the-feet-to-parkinsonian-tone-and-gait">117. State 3 — Podiatry 2017–2019: structural truth accumulates, while the GP later explicitly reconnects the feet to Parkinsonian tone and gait<a class="heading-anchor" href="#117-state-3-podiatry-20172019-structural-truth-accumulates-while-the-gp-later-explicitly-reconnects-the-feet-to-parkinsonian-tone-and-gait">¶</a></h2>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="117-1-podiatry-establishes-genuine-local-pathology">117.1 Podiatry establishes genuine local pathology<a class="heading-anchor" href="#117-1-podiatry-establishes-genuine-local-pathology">¶</a></h3>
<p>The Podiatry record documents real and recurrent mechanical/structural findings and treatment:</p>
<ul>
<li>callus at the left fifth MTPJ;</li>
<li>nail pathology;</li>
<li>bilateral toe props;</li>
<li>tight gastroc/soleus;</li>
<li>recurrent callus debridement;</li>
<li>insoles/offloading;</li>
<li>clawed toes;</li>
<li>slight leg-length difference;</li>
<li>pronating foot type;</li>
<li>lateral-foot pain;</li>
<li>pressure/offloading modifications.</li>
</ul>
<p>This strongly confirms that the structural branch was clinically real and locally productive.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="117-2-podiatry-also-records-dynamic-improvement-in-gait-after-parkinson-s-diagnosis-treatment">117.2 Podiatry also records dynamic improvement in gait after Parkinson's diagnosis/treatment<a class="heading-anchor" href="#117-2-podiatry-also-records-dynamic-improvement-in-gait-after-parkinson-s-diagnosis-treatment">¶</a></h3>
<p>At the first Podiatry treatment in September 2017, the patient reports:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>"my gait was very bad but it's not any more"</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>while attending after the Parkinson's diagnosis.</p>
<p>This is patient-reported treatment-state/disease-state information within the structural service. It is not mechanistic proof.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="117-3-podiatry-repeatedly-documents-clawed-toes">117.3 Podiatry repeatedly documents clawed toes<a class="heading-anchor" href="#117-3-podiatry-repeatedly-documents-clawed-toes">¶</a></h3>
<p>By March and June 2018, clawed toes are objective findings in the Podiatry record.</p>
<p>The structural record therefore contains a feature that also belongs to the original GP relational presentation.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="117-4-the-key-2019-gp-re-referral-explicitly-reconnects-the-branches">117.4 The key 2019 GP re-referral explicitly reconnects the branches<a class="heading-anchor" href="#117-4-the-key-2019-gp-re-referral-explicitly-reconnects-the-branches">¶</a></h3>
<p>The 2019 Podiatry re-referral is one of the strongest sources in the GP information-state trial.</p>
<p>The GP writes:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>"Seen by podiatry in the past due to feet issues in relating to increased tone and abnormal gait relating to Parkinson's disease."</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>He then states:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>"Parkinsons at very young age has issues with 'clawing feet' and pain."</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>The referral also marks callus, rearfoot/arch pain and gait abnormalities.</p>
<p>This is not the language of a GP who has simply inherited a conclusion that the feet are clinically independent of the neurological syndrome.</p>
<p>It is a relational formulation:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>feet issues ↔ increased tone ↔ abnormal gait ↔ Parkinson's disease ↔ clawing feet ↔ pain.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="117-5-the-no-neuropathy-tick-requires-restraint">117.5 The "No neuropathy" tick requires restraint<a class="heading-anchor" href="#117-5-the-no-neuropathy-tick-requires-restraint">¶</a></h3>
<p>The same 2019 form visibly ticks a Low-Risk item labelled "No neuropathy."</p>
<p>This is documentary evidence that established neuropathy was not being carried as a risk-state proposition on that referral.</p>
<p>However the same form also ticks "No deformity" despite separate evidence of clawing/toe deformity. The risk checklist should therefore not be elevated into a specialist neurological exclusion test. It is evidence about the referral form's recorded risk state, not definitive proof against neuropathic mechanisms.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="frozen-state-3-conclusion">Frozen State-3 conclusion<a class="heading-anchor" href="#frozen-state-3-conclusion">¶</a></h3>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>By 2019 the GP demonstrably retains or regenerates the original cross-domain relationship. Structural disease is real and continues to receive Podiatry care, but the GP's own referral explicitly links recurrent foot problems to increased tone, abnormal gait, very-young Parkinson's and clawing feet.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>This substantially weakens any theory that the GP's post-specialist information state became simply "Parkinsonism over here / feet over there."</p>
<p>It instead supports:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>relational persistence within primary care despite specialist partition.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="118-state-4-2023-crisis-the-gp-actively-reopens-the-painparkinsonian-relationship">118. State 4 — 2023 crisis: the GP actively reopens the pain–Parkinsonian relationship<a class="heading-anchor" href="#118-state-4-2023-crisis-the-gp-actively-reopens-the-painparkinsonian-relationship">¶</a></h2>
<p>The 26 March 2023 urgent Neurology referral is a particularly strong information-state source because it shows what the GP does when the pain branch becomes severe.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="118-1-the-gp-does-not-merely-transmit-neuropathic-pain">118.1 The GP does not merely transmit "neuropathic pain"<a class="heading-anchor" href="#118-1-the-gp-does-not-merely-transmit-neuropathic-pain">¶</a></h3>
<p>He reports severe bilateral burning of the feet and lower legs, escalating pregabalin and death-wish language.</p>
<p>But his reasoning does not stop at symptom classification or analgesia.</p>
<p>He recalls a prior episode in which pain had:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>"suddenly ... settled with better control of his parkinsons."</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>He then states:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>"I am worried about him"</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>and asks Neurology to review:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>"before we get into trouble here."</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="118-2-why-this-matters-to-information-flow">118.2 Why this matters to information flow<a class="heading-anchor" href="#118-2-why-this-matters-to-information-flow">¶</a></h3>
<p>The GP is carrying a cross-boundary historical observation into the specialist referral:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>severe pain now + prior pain improvement with Parkinsonian control → Neurology should reassess.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>This is not equivalent to concluding that the pain is Parkinsonian.</p>
<p>It is exactly the preservation of an arrow:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>pain state ↔ Parkinsonian control state.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="frozen-state-4-conclusion">Frozen State-4 conclusion<a class="heading-anchor" href="#frozen-state-4-conclusion">¶</a></h3>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>When the lower-limb pain becomes severe in 2023, the GP explicitly reintroduces Parkinsonian treatment-state history as relevant contrary information and sends the problem back to Neurology. The original relational question has therefore not disappeared from primary care.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>This is strong evidence that the GP functions as a longitudinal re-integration node rather than merely a passive carrier of the inherited neuropathic/structural compartments.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="119-state-5-2025-specialist-authority-constraint-becomes-explicit">119. State 5 — 2025: specialist authority constraint becomes explicit<a class="heading-anchor" href="#119-state-5-2025-specialist-authority-constraint-becomes-explicit">¶</a></h2>
<p>The 2025 GP pathway is especially informative because it shows what happens when the patient challenges the inherited diagnosis itself.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="119-1-the-gp-does-not-independently-endorse-the-dystonia-theory">119.1 The GP does not independently endorse the dystonia theory<a class="heading-anchor" href="#119-1-the-gp-does-not-independently-endorse-the-dystonia-theory">¶</a></h3>
<p>When the patient asks for Botox and frames the problem as dystonia versus dyskinesia, the GP does not adopt that mechanistic conclusion.</p>
<p>Instead, he states that Botox suitability is outside his scope and that he has already referred for a second neurological opinion regarding dystonia and the patient's concerns.</p>
<p>This is professional boundary language, not dismissal of the question.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="119-2-the-st-vincent-s-referral-records-an-inherited-diagnostic-state-plus-an-unresolved-challenge">119.2 The St Vincent's referral records an inherited diagnostic state plus an unresolved challenge<a class="heading-anchor" href="#119-2-the-st-vincent-s-referral-records-an-inherited-diagnostic-state-plus-an-unresolved-challenge">¶</a></h3>
<p>On 11 July 2025 the GP writes that the patient has carried a young-onset Parkinson's diagnosis for eight years but is now questioning the whole diagnosis and believes he has a complex dystonia instead.</p>
<p>The GP explicitly says that the patient's question about the positive predictive value of the DAT scan is:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>"way above my knowledge level!"</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>He therefore seeks an urgent second opinion from a neurologist specifically because of expertise in:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>movement disorders but also dystonia.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="119-3-the-gp-attempts-to-transfer-the-inherited-specialist-record">119.3 The GP attempts to transfer the inherited specialist record<a class="heading-anchor" href="#119-3-the-gp-attempts-to-transfer-the-inherited-specialist-record">¶</a></h3>
<p>The referral says there is extensive correspondence that cannot be attached to the form and that the GP intends to Healthmail it to the receiving Parkinson's nurse specialist or consultant.</p>
<p>This shows an important dual information state:</p>
<ol>
<li>the inherited eight-year specialist model remains highly authoritative and portable;</li>
<li>the GP accepts that a specialist-level challenge to that model is now live and requires external adjudication.</li>
</ol>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="119-4-later-2025-correspondence-reinforces-authority-constraint">119.4 Later 2025 correspondence reinforces authority constraint<a class="heading-anchor" href="#119-4-later-2025-correspondence-reinforces-authority-constraint">¶</a></h3>
<p>When medication adjustment is requested, the GP states that he cannot reasonably adjust levodopa without Neurology specialist guidance because doing so would be beyond ordinary GP expertise.</p>
<p>This makes the specialist hierarchy explicit.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="frozen-state-5-conclusion">Frozen State-5 conclusion<a class="heading-anchor" href="#frozen-state-5-conclusion">¶</a></h3>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>By 2025 the GP's record does not show independent confidence that the inherited Neurology model is correct or incorrect. It shows a primary-care clinician recognising the limits of his own authority, preserving the patient's challenge as a specialist question, and deliberately seeking a movement-disorders/dystonia opinion.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>This strongly supports <strong>authority constraint</strong> as part of the information-flow architecture.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="120-cross-time-result-what-happened-to-the-gp-s-clinical-object">120. Cross-time result — what happened to the GP's clinical object?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#120-cross-time-result-what-happened-to-the-gp-s-clinical-object">¶</a></h2>
<p>The sequential GP pathway can now be summarised without hindsight:</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="2017-before-specialist-assessment">2017 before specialist assessment<a class="heading-anchor" href="#2017-before-specialist-assessment">¶</a></h3>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>feet + gait + muscle tension + toe clawing + movement-initiation difficulty + functional decline → open neurological question</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="during-after-admission">During/after admission<a class="heading-anchor" href="#during-after-admission">¶</a></h3>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>young-onset Parkinsonism becomes the authoritative specialist diagnosis; Podiatry remains active, but the GP updates it "as Parkinson's"</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="2019">2019<a class="heading-anchor" href="#2019">¶</a></h3>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>recurrent foot problem → GP explicitly reconnects feet to increased tone + abnormal gait + very-young Parkinson's + clawing feet + pain</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="2023">2023<a class="heading-anchor" href="#2023">¶</a></h3>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>severe burning pain → GP recalls prior pain settling with better Parkinson's control → urgent neurological reopening</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="2025">2025<a class="heading-anchor" href="#2025">¶</a></h3>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>diagnostic challenge/dystonia theory → GP states knowledge/scope limits → seeks specialist Movement Disorders/dystonia second opinion</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="overall-finding">Overall finding<a class="heading-anchor" href="#overall-finding">¶</a></h3>
<p>The best current description is not:</p>
<blockquote>
<p>the GP originally integrated the case, then specialist correspondence caused him to forget the relationship.</p>
</blockquote>
<p>It is:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>the GP repeatedly preserved or regenerated the relationship, but specialist Neurology retained authority to adjudicate it.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>That gives the case a more precise architecture:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>primary care repeatedly sees/carries the arrows → specialist services own the high-authority explanatory model → local services manage components → GP re-escalates when the arrows become clinically salient → specialist hierarchy determines whether the parent model is reconstructed.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>This is stronger and fairer than a simple fragmentation account.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="121-the-apparent-specialist-clearance-hypothesis-qualified-not-rejected">121. The apparent specialist-clearance hypothesis: qualified, not rejected<a class="heading-anchor" href="#121-the-apparent-specialist-clearance-hypothesis-qualified-not-rejected">¶</a></h2>
<p>The trial does not support a strong proposition that the GP received the Neurology letter and therefore considered the feet neurologically "cleared."</p>
<p>The 2019 and 2023 sources are inconsistent with such a complete inference.</p>
<p>However a narrower authority proposition survives:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>Once a specialist Neurology service had constructed a sophisticated Parkinsonian diagnosis and had itself discussed the feet as possibly separate/peripheral, the GP could reasonably continue to operate within that specialist architecture while still noticing and re-referring relationship-crossing information.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>The important distinction is:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>reliance on specialist adjudicative authority ≠ loss of relational awareness.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>The GP record supports the former and contradicts a strong version of the latter.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="122-does-the-gp-expect-that-an-ordinary-specialist-neurological-movement-examination-has-already-occurred">122. Does the GP expect that an ordinary specialist neurological/movement examination has already occurred?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#122-does-the-gp-expect-that-an-ordinary-specialist-neurological-movement-examination-has-already-occurred">¶</a></h2>
<p>The record does not directly state:</p>
<blockquote>
<p>"I assume Neurology examined the feet for dystonia."</p>
</blockquote>
<p>That private expectation should not be attributed to the GP.</p>
<p>But the record does show professional reliance patterns:</p>
<ul>
<li>he sends an integrated problem to Neurology;</li>
<li>he receives extensive specialist correspondence and adopts the Parkinsonism diagnosis operationally;</li>
<li>he continues to send cross-domain observations back to Neurology when they become salient;</li>
<li>he says specialist questions such as DAT interpretation, Botox suitability and levodopa adjustment exceed his knowledge/scope;</li>
<li>he seeks a Movement Disorders/dystonia specialist when the inherited model itself becomes contested.</li>
</ul>
<p>The strongest documentary inference is therefore:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The GP treated specialist neurological adjudication as authoritative for questions beyond primary-care scope, while continuing to supply relational observations that could test that adjudication.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>Whether a reasonable GP would specifically assume that a particular movement-disorders examination of the feet had occurred is a professional-standard/expert question.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="123-natural-control-the-gp-versus-the-specialist-pathway">123. Natural control: the GP versus the specialist pathway<a class="heading-anchor" href="#123-natural-control-the-gp-versus-the-specialist-pathway">¶</a></h2>
<p>The GP pathway now functions as an important internal control.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="specialist-pathway">Specialist pathway<a class="heading-anchor" href="#specialist-pathway">¶</a></h3>
<p>The specialist record achieves exceptionally high resolution in:</p>
<ul>
<li>Parkinsonian syndrome construction;</li>
<li>genetics;</li>
<li>medication strategy;</li>
<li>later local questions.</li>
</ul>
<p>The unresolved structural–dynamic lower-limb relationship becomes distributed across Podiatry, pain/neuropathy, Parkinsonian state and later dystonia/OFF reasoning.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="gp-pathway">GP pathway<a class="heading-anchor" href="#gp-pathway">¶</a></h3>
<p>The GP repeatedly writes relationship-bearing formulations:</p>
<ul>
<li>"gait, feet and muscle tension";</li>
<li>Podiatry prioritised "as Parkinson's";</li>
<li>foot issues related to increased tone and abnormal gait relating to Parkinson's;</li>
<li>very-young Parkinson's with clawing feet and pain;</li>
<li>prior pain settling with better Parkinson's control;</li>
<li>later need for a Movement Disorders/dystonia opinion.</li>
</ul>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="new-working-formulation">New working formulation<a class="heading-anchor" href="#new-working-formulation">¶</a></h3>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The GP pathway preserves the arrows more consistently than it resolves them. The specialist pathway resolves individual nodes more authoritatively than it reconstructs all of the arrows.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>This may be one of the strongest information-flow formulations in the case.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="124-implications-for-who-owned-the-arrows">124. Implications for "Who Owned the Arrows?"<a class="heading-anchor" href="#124-implications-for-who-owned-the-arrows">¶</a></h2>
<p>The GP trial significantly refines the ownership question.</p>
<p>It is no longer enough to ask:</p>
<blockquote>
<p>Who noticed the relationships?</p>
</blockquote>
<p>The GP plainly did, repeatedly.</p>
<p>The stronger question is:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>Who had the authority and responsibility to adjudicate those relationships once primary care repeatedly returned them to specialist services?</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>The longitudinal division begins to look like:</p>
<ul>
<li><strong>GP:</strong> identifies/re-identifies relationships, carries cross-time information, escalates;</li>
<li><strong>Podiatry:</strong> establishes and treats local mechanical truth;</li>
<li><strong>Neurology:</strong> holds authoritative Parkinsonian diagnosis and movement-state interpretation;</li>
<li><strong>Movement Disorders nursing:</strong> manages specialist treatment state and escalation;</li>
<li><strong>later external Movement Disorders specialist:</strong> receives the reopened top-level diagnostic question.</li>
</ul>
<p>The possible vulnerability is therefore not that nobody saw the arrows.</p>
<p>It is that:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>the service with the broadest authority to adjudicate them may have processed later crossings mainly as local questions without an equivalently visible global reconstruction.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>That remains an expert/documentary question rather than a concluded failure.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="125-revised-implications-for-the-50-question-audit">125. Revised implications for the 50-question audit<a class="heading-anchor" href="#125-revised-implications-for-the-50-question-audit">¶</a></h2>
<p>The first canonical GP pass does <strong>not</strong> require replacing the 50-question protocol. It requires changing the level at which several questions operate.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="125-1-questions-materially-answered-or-retired">125.1 Questions materially answered or retired<a class="heading-anchor" href="#125-1-questions-materially-answered-or-retired">¶</a></h3>
<p>The record materially answers the underlying question <strong>whether the GP lost the integrated feet–gait–tone relationship after specialist involvement</strong>: the strong version should be retired. The 2019 re-referral explicitly reconnects feet with increased tone, abnormal gait and Parkinson's, and the 2023 urgent referral reconnects severe pain with prior improvement under better Parkinsonian control.</p>
<p>Accordingly, any question framed as “did anyone downstream still see the relationship?” is now too weak.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="125-2-questions-reframed-upward">125.2 Questions reframed upward<a class="heading-anchor" href="#125-2-questions-reframed-upward">¶</a></h3>
<p>The ownership questions must now operate at the level of <strong>adjudicative authority</strong>:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The GP kept seeing and transmitting arrows. Who had authority to decide what they meant, and what did specialist care do when those arrows were returned?</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>This affects Q22–Q25, Q32–Q35 and Q46–Q50 most strongly.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="125-3-questions-strengthened">125.3 Questions strengthened<a class="heading-anchor" href="#125-3-questions-strengthened">¶</a></h3>
<p>The GP trial strengthens the need to answer the specialist evidential-bridge questions. Because primary care later continued to link feet with Parkinsonian tone/gait and pain with Parkinsonian control, the record must more clearly explain what evidence allowed specialist separation/peripheral categories to remain operationally durable.</p>
<p>This strengthens Q11–Q15 and Q36–Q40.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="125-4-questions-unchanged">125.4 Questions unchanged<a class="heading-anchor" href="#125-4-questions-unchanged">¶</a></h3>
<p>The following remain essentially unchanged:</p>
<ul>
<li>the 2017 distal examination questions (Q6–Q10);</li>
<li>dystonic-sign provenance and PRKN incorporation (Q16–Q20);</li>
<li>PRKN deferral/closure genealogy (Q26–Q30);</li>
<li>same-author shoulder control (Q41–Q45).</li>
</ul>
<p>The GP corpus does not answer those specialist-level questions.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="125-5-new-controlling-distinction">125.5 New controlling distinction<a class="heading-anchor" href="#125-5-new-controlling-distinction">¶</a></h3>
<p>The trial establishes a distinction the original 50 questions did not state sharply enough:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>recognition of a relationship is not the same as authority to adjudicate it.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>Primary care could recognise, preserve, and repeatedly refer a relationship while still depending on specialist Neurology or Movement Disorders to determine its clinical meaning.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="125-6-revised-q50-direction">125.6 Revised Q50 direction<a class="heading-anchor" href="#125-6-revised-q50-direction">¶</a></h3>
<p>The strongest pre-2026 theory is no longer “the integrated relationship was lost downstream.” It is:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The integrated relationship remained visible in primary care and repeatedly re-entered specialist pathways. The unresolved question is how the specialist system with adjudicative authority processed those returns, and why repeated local revisions are easier to identify than a global specialist reconstruction of the original whole.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="126-strongest-counterarguments-exposed-by-the-gp-trial">126. Strongest counterarguments exposed by the GP trial<a class="heading-anchor" href="#126-strongest-counterarguments-exposed-by-the-gp-trial">¶</a></h2>
<p>The trial also strengthens the defence in several ways.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="126-1-the-pathway-was-not-completely-fragmented">126.1 The pathway was not completely fragmented<a class="heading-anchor" href="#126-1-the-pathway-was-not-completely-fragmented">¶</a></h3>
<p>The GP repeatedly transmitted Parkinsonian context into Podiatry and later returned pain information to Neurology.</p>
<p>Any claim of absolute siloing should be retired.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="126-2-podiatry-had-genuine-pathology-to-treat">126.2 Podiatry had genuine pathology to treat<a class="heading-anchor" href="#126-2-podiatry-had-genuine-pathology-to-treat">¶</a></h3>
<p>The GP's relational framing does not invalidate the structural branch.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="126-3-the-gp-himself-worked-within-the-parkinson-s-diagnosis-for-years">126.3 The GP himself worked within the Parkinson's diagnosis for years<a class="heading-anchor" href="#126-3-the-gp-himself-worked-within-the-parkinson-s-diagnosis-for-years">¶</a></h3>
<p>This shows the specialist diagnosis was clinically persuasive and operationally useful, not merely imposed and resisted from the outset.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="126-4-the-2025-dystonia-theory-initially-came-from-the-patient-not-the-gp">126.4 The 2025 dystonia theory initially came from the patient, not the GP<a class="heading-anchor" href="#126-4-the-2025-dystonia-theory-initially-came-from-the-patient-not-the-gp">¶</a></h3>
<p>The GP appropriately did not present himself as able to adjudicate a specialist movement-disorders dispute and sought expert review.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="126-5-the-gp-record-does-not-prove-what-neurology-was-required-to-do">126.5 The GP record does not prove what Neurology was required to do<a class="heading-anchor" href="#126-5-the-gp-record-does-not-prove-what-neurology-was-required-to-do">¶</a></h3>
<p>It shows what information was generated and returned. Clinical significance, standard of care and required response remain expert questions.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="127-falsifiers-and-source-gaps-after-the-gp-trial">127. Falsifiers and source gaps after the GP trial<a class="heading-anchor" href="#127-falsifiers-and-source-gaps-after-the-gp-trial">¶</a></h2>
<p>The following could materially change the current formulation:</p>
<ol>
<li><strong>Full-referral proof:</strong> evidence that the treating neurologist demonstrably read the entire GP referral before structuring the first assessment and explicitly treated the integrated relationship as the primary investigative object.</li>
<li><strong>Specialist reconstruction:</strong> a later source showing that recurrent GP relationship signals were globally synthesised and the parent model re-derived.</li>
<li><strong>Podiatry feedback:</strong> evidence of detailed Podiatry-to-GP or Podiatry-to-Neurology communications not presently visible in the SAR that supplied an explicit structural/dynamic synthesis.</li>
<li><strong>GP contrary state:</strong> contemporaneous GP documentation stating that the foot problem was neurologically settled and wholly independent after specialist review.</li>
<li><strong>2019 referral authorship/provenance issue:</strong> evidence that the relational wording on the 2019 referral was not authored or adopted by the GP.</li>
</ol>
<p>Until such evidence appears, the GP pathway should be treated as evidence of <strong>relational persistence plus specialist authority constraint</strong>.</p>
<hr/>
