<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="128-protected-formulation-added-by-the-gp-trial">128. Protected formulation added by the GP trial<a class="heading-anchor" href="#128-protected-formulation-added-by-the-gp-trial">¶</a></h2>
<p>The following formulation should now be protected from drift unless contrary primary evidence requires revision:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The GP did not simply lose the original feet–gait–tone relationship after Neurology became involved. He repeatedly carried or regenerated it: first by seeking Neurology for gait, feet and muscle tension; then by prioritising Podiatry "as Parkinson's"; later by re-referring foot problems as related to increased tone and abnormal gait in very-young Parkinson's with clawing feet and pain; and in 2023 by explicitly reminding Neurology that prior pain had settled with better Parkinsonian control. By 2025, when the inherited diagnosis itself was contested, he did not claim authority to resolve the dispute but sought a Movement Disorders/dystonia specialist. The resulting information-flow problem is therefore not simply loss of relationship in primary care. It is a question of how relationship-bearing information repeatedly generated in primary care was adjudicated within a specialist architecture whose existing model remained authoritative.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>The shortest form is:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The GP kept seeing the arrows; specialist care owned the authority to decide what they meant.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>This sentence is a documentary synthesis, not a legal or clinical conclusion.</p>
<hr/>
</section>
<section class="reader-section kind-audit" data-section="50-question anti-drift inquiry" id="section-questions">
<div class="section-kicker">50-question anti-drift inquiry</div>
<div class="archive-note"><strong>Audit archive.</strong> This material is intentionally exhaustive. It is retained for reproducibility, falsification and source-checking; it is not the recommended linear reading path.</div><h2 class="source-h1 anchored-heading" id="part-xxxi-anti-drift-inquiry-protocol-the-50-questions-the-record-must-answer">Part XXXI — Anti-drift inquiry protocol: the 50 questions the record must answer<a class="heading-anchor" href="#part-xxxi-anti-drift-inquiry-protocol-the-50-questions-the-record-must-answer">¶</a></h2>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="101-the-impulse-behind-these-questions">101. The impulse behind these questions<a class="heading-anchor" href="#101-the-impulse-behind-these-questions">¶</a></h2>
<p>These questions are not a new case theory. They are a <strong>stress-testing instrument for the existing theory</strong>.</p>
<p>The strongest current formulation already has a defined centre of gravity, now refined by the GP information-state trial:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>integrated GP trajectory → specialist problem compression / selective reconstruction → high-resolution Parkinsonian-genetic construction → operational partition of unresolved lower-limb material → documentary inheritance → repeated GP re-integration of feet/gait/tone/pain relationships → specialist local updating → no presently identified equivalent global specialist reconstruction → accumulated continuity → retrospective authority</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>The GP trial changes one important assumption: <strong>primary care did not simply lose the arrows after specialist involvement.</strong> The record now shows the GP repeatedly preserving or regenerating relationships among feet, gait, tone, pain and Parkinsonian control and returning those relationships to specialist services. The revised questions therefore ask increasingly about <strong>adjudicative authority, specialist response, feedback and reconstruction</strong>, rather than merely whether the relationship survived downstream.</p>
<p>The danger at this stage is not lack of material. It is <strong>drift</strong>: allowing each new document, striking phrase, later diagnosis, or treatment response to pull the case toward a different and weaker explanation.</p>
<p>The questions below therefore arise from ten specific reasoning impulses.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="impulse-1-protect-the-original-clinical-object">Impulse 1 — Protect the original clinical object<a class="heading-anchor" href="#impulse-1-protect-the-original-clinical-object">¶</a></h3>
<p>Before asking whether later decisions were right or wrong, establish exactly what clinical problem was presented at the threshold. The current theory depends on a difference between the <strong>GP's integrated trajectory</strong> and the <strong>PRECIS-shaped neurological task</strong>. The first questions therefore test what information actually reached the neurologist and what representation governed the encounter.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="impulse-2-find-the-bridges-not-merely-the-endpoints">Impulse 2 — Find the bridges, not merely the endpoints<a class="heading-anchor" href="#impulse-2-find-the-bridges-not-merely-the-endpoints">¶</a></h3>
<p>The record contains many endpoints: Parkinsonism, Podiatry, possible neuropathy, PRKN suspicion, iPD language, OFF dystonia, neuropathic pain terminology. The weakness or strength of the case lies in the <strong>evidential bridges</strong> between them. Every major transition must therefore be asked: what evidence earned this move?</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="impulse-3-separate-examination-from-synthesis">Impulse 3 — Separate examination from synthesis<a class="heading-anchor" href="#impulse-3-separate-examination-from-synthesis">¶</a></h3>
<p>A sophisticated discharge synthesis cannot be allowed to backfill the bedside record. The questions must preserve the distinction between what was <strong>observed</strong>, what was <strong>reported</strong>, what was <strong>interpreted</strong>, and what was later <strong>constructed</strong>.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="impulse-4-track-ownership-of-unresolved-relationships">Impulse 4 — Track ownership of unresolved relationships<a class="heading-anchor" href="#impulse-4-track-ownership-of-unresolved-relationships">¶</a></h3>
<p>A problem can be divided among appropriate services without the relationship among those services being owned. The questions therefore ask not only “who treated what?” but “who remained responsible for the arrow between them?”</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="impulse-5-identify-reopening-architecture">Impulse 5 — Identify reopening architecture<a class="heading-anchor" href="#impulse-5-identify-reopening-architecture">¶</a></h3>
<p>Uncertainty is not itself a defect. The key question is whether unresolved material had a route back into active inquiry. The record must be searched for <strong>review dates, triggers, thresholds, feedback loops, and explicit return conditions</strong>.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="impulse-6-distinguish-local-updating-from-global-reconstruction">Impulse 6 — Distinguish local updating from global reconstruction<a class="heading-anchor" href="#impulse-6-distinguish-local-updating-from-global-reconstruction">¶</a></h3>
<p>The chronic pathway contains real reasoning. That must be preserved. The question is whether each local correction remained local or whether accumulated corrections ever caused the parent model to be rebuilt.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="impulse-7-use-the-clinician-as-his-own-control">Impulse 7 — Use the clinician as his own control<a class="heading-anchor" href="#impulse-7-use-the-clinician-as-his-own-control">¶</a></h3>
<p>The 2019 shoulder episode and the 2020 foot-throw show that the same clinician could reason explicitly through competing propositions and discriminators. The questions use those episodes as controls for <strong>capacity and reasoning form</strong>, not as claims that every clinical problem should look identical.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="impulse-8-track-semantic-migration">Impulse 8 — Track semantic migration<a class="heading-anchor" href="#impulse-8-track-semantic-migration">¶</a></h3>
<p>Words such as “perhaps,” “neuropathy,” “neuropathic,” “idiopathic,” “belief and understanding,” “removed,” and “profound relief” carry different epistemic force. The record must be asked where uncertainty moved, where categories hardened, and whether stronger language was earned by stronger evidence.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="impulse-9-preserve-contrary-evidence-and-falsifiers">Impulse 9 — Preserve contrary evidence and falsifiers<a class="heading-anchor" href="#impulse-9-preserve-contrary-evidence-and-falsifiers">¶</a></h3>
<p>The theory should become stronger by surviving hostile testing, not by excluding inconvenient sources. Several questions below are designed specifically to <strong>weaken or defeat</strong> the current formulation if the record contains a stronger integrative source than has yet been identified.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="impulse-10-keep-2026-last">Impulse 10 — Keep 2026 last<a class="heading-anchor" href="#impulse-10-keep-2026-last">¶</a></h3>
<p>The PRKN result is an endpoint to a historical question, not a master key. Questions about 2017–2025 should first be answered using the information available at those times. Only then should 2026 be used to show what ultimately happened to the question the record had already generated.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="102-questions-15-which-problem-representation-actually-governed-the-specialist-encounter">102. Questions 1–5 — Which problem representation actually governed the specialist encounter?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#102-questions-15-which-problem-representation-actually-governed-the-specialist-encounter">¶</a></h2>
<p><strong>GP-trial effect:</strong> The GP side is no longer uncertain: before admission he explicitly linked gait, feet and “muscle tension,” and the urgent referral preserved feet → gait → tone → toe-clawing → dexterity/function → deterioration under an open diagnostic question. What remains unresolved is what <strong>specialist problem representation</strong> governed the first neurological investigation.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q1-what-exact-referral-material-was-physically-or-electronically-available-to-the-treating-neurologist-before-his-first-substantive-assessment">Q1. What exact referral material was physically or electronically available to the treating neurologist before his first substantive assessment?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q1-what-exact-referral-material-was-physically-or-electronically-available-to-the-treating-neurologist-before-his-first-substantive-assessment">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> The counterfactual still depends on whether the full GP referral was absent, inaccessible, merely not foregrounded, or actually available to the neurologist.</p>
<p><strong>Current status:</strong> <strong>Open / partially bounded.</strong> The canonical record proves GP direct referral and reproduces the hospital PRECIS in the Neurology letter, but does not yet prove whether the full GP referral was read by the treating neurologist before assessment.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> Strengthen, qualify, or falsify the “different investigative object” counterfactual.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q2-of-the-materials-available-which-representation-demonstrably-governed-the-neurologist-s-initial-task-the-full-gp-trajectory-the-hospital-precis-or-an-independently-reconstructed-problem">Q2. Of the materials available, which representation demonstrably governed the neurologist's initial task: the full GP trajectory, the hospital PRECIS, or an independently reconstructed problem?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q2-of-the-materials-available-which-representation-demonstrably-governed-the-neurologist-s-initial-task-the-full-gp-trajectory-the-hospital-precis-or-an-independently-reconstructed-problem">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> Availability is not use. The GP trial now makes the distinction sharper because the GP's own problem representation is established and materially broader than the PRECIS.</p>
<p><strong>Current status:</strong> <strong>Open.</strong> The specialist letter explicitly reproduces the PRECIS-shaped referral task, while the subsequent history independently recovers much of the fuller story.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> Determine whether the key mechanism is <strong>entry compression</strong>, <strong>later specialist repartition</strong>, or both.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q3-which-elements-of-the-gp-s-integrated-object-did-neurology-independently-recover-during-history-and-examination-and-which-relationships-among-those-elements-were-not-reconstructed-with-equivalent-force">Q3. Which elements of the GP's integrated object did Neurology independently recover during history and examination, and which relationships among those elements were not reconstructed with equivalent force?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q3-which-elements-of-the-gp-s-integrated-object-did-neurology-independently-recover-during-history-and-examination-and-which-relationships-among-those-elements-were-not-reconstructed-with-equivalent-force">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> The record already disproves a simple “facts omitted and never seen” theory. The question is now explicitly <strong>facts versus relationships</strong>.</p>
<p><strong>Current status:</strong> <strong>Partly answered.</strong> Feet, structural abnormalities, gait, deterioration and dystonic-looking lower-limb posture substantially re-entered; the unresolved issue is the degree of relational reconstruction.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> Refine the mechanism from “loss of information” to “uneven reconstruction of relationships.”</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q4-at-what-exact-point-did-the-specialist-record-change-the-object-from-what-explains-this-integrated-deteriorating-presentation-to-more-compartmental-questions-such-as-parkinsonism-structural-feet-and-possible-peripheral-symptoms">Q4. At what exact point did the specialist record change the object from “what explains this integrated deteriorating presentation?” to more compartmental questions such as Parkinsonism, structural feet, and possible peripheral symptoms?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q4-at-what-exact-point-did-the-specialist-record-change-the-object-from-what-explains-this-integrated-deteriorating-presentation-to-more-compartmental-questions-such-as-parkinsonism-structural-feet-and-possible-peripheral-symptoms">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> This locates the <strong>partition event</strong> rather than assuming it occurred at referral.</p>
<p><strong>Current status:</strong> <strong>Open.</strong> The evidence suggests a progressive transformation rather than a single sentence doing all the work.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> Identify whether the decisive transition occurred at PRECIS, examination, impression, discharge synthesis, or only when the plan became operational.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q5-what-information-did-the-gp-receive-back-from-neurology-and-what-did-that-return-communication-leave-him-entitled-or-expected-to-regard-as-neurologically-adjudicated-versus-still-unresolved">Q5. What information did the GP receive back from Neurology, and what did that return communication leave him entitled or expected to regard as neurologically adjudicated versus still unresolved?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q5-what-information-did-the-gp-receive-back-from-neurology-and-what-did-that-return-communication-leave-him-entitled-or-expected-to-regard-as-neurologically-adjudicated-versus-still-unresolved">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> The GP trial has shown that he continued to preserve the relationship later. The question is no longer simply whether he “lost” the integrated object, but what the specialist return reasonably communicated about the status of that object.</p>
<p><strong>Current status:</strong> <strong>Partly answered.</strong> The GP recorded extensive Neurology correspondence and adopted Parkinsonism as the operative diagnosis, yet his later referrals continued to reconnect feet with tone/gait/Parkinsonian state.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> Test the narrower <strong>specialist-authority / apparent-resolution</strong> hypothesis without asserting that the GP believed the feet had been cleared.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="103-questions-610-what-did-the-2017-examination-actually-discriminate">103. Questions 6–10 — What did the 2017 examination actually discriminate?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#103-questions-610-what-did-the-2017-examination-actually-discriminate">¶</a></h2>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q6-what-lower-limb-findings-in-2017-were-direct-observations-what-were-patient-reported-history-and-what-were-immediate-interpretations">Q6. What lower-limb findings in 2017 were direct observations, what were patient-reported history, and what were immediate interpretations?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q6-what-lower-limb-findings-in-2017-were-direct-observations-what-were-patient-reported-history-and-what-were-immediate-interpretations">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> “Dystonic posture,” “footdrop appearance,” structural deformity, burning pain and toe clawing do not all have the same evidential status.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> Prevent retrospective blending of history, examination and synthesis.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q7-was-distal-power-specifically-tested-in-2017-including-dorsiflexion-plantarflexion-inversion-and-eversion-and-if-so-where-is-it-documented">Q7. Was distal power specifically tested in 2017, including dorsiflexion, plantarflexion, inversion and eversion, and if so where is it documented?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q7-was-distal-power-specifically-tested-in-2017-including-dorsiflexion-plantarflexion-inversion-and-eversion-and-if-so-where-is-it-documented">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> The 2020 foot-throw episode shows the same clinician later used exactly these discriminators. Their presence or absence in 2017 is therefore an unusually strong internal comparator.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> Directly affect the synthesis–examination differential.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q8-was-there-a-mapped-sensory-examination-of-the-painful-feet-in-2017-including-modality-and-distribution">Q8. Was there a mapped sensory examination of the painful feet in 2017, including modality and distribution?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q8-was-there-a-mapped-sensory-examination-of-the-painful-feet-in-2017-including-modality-and-distribution">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> A peripheral/neuropathic explanation has a different evidential bridge if supported by documented sensory findings than if based mainly on burning quality, history and treatment response.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> Strengthen or weaken the proposition that the peripheral classification outran the documented distal examination.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q9-was-dynamic-toe-posture-examined-at-rest-in-bed-on-standing-during-gait-under-loading-or-after-repeated-movement">Q9. Was dynamic toe posture examined at rest, in bed, on standing, during gait, under loading, or after repeated movement?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q9-was-dynamic-toe-posture-examined-at-rest-in-bed-on-standing-during-gait-under-loading-or-after-repeated-movement">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> The GP's “claws his toes” observation and Neurology's dystonic-looking gait raise a dynamic-versus-fixed question. Hammertoes alone do not answer it.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> Clarify whether the structural and dystonic propositions were actually placed against one another.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q10-was-pain-examined-relationallylocation-provocation-pressure-sites-footwear-loading-gait-phase-or-reproduction-during-examination">Q10. Was pain examined relationally—location, provocation, pressure sites, footwear, loading, gait phase, or reproduction during examination?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q10-was-pain-examined-relationallylocation-provocation-pressure-sites-footwear-loading-gait-phase-or-reproduction-during-examination">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> The case concerns the relationship between structural disease, dynamic posture and pain, not merely the existence of pain.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> A detailed relational pain examination would materially weaken the current claim of lower distal relational resolution.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="104-questions-1115-what-earned-the-first-partition">104. Questions 11–15 — What earned the first partition?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#104-questions-1115-what-earned-the-first-partition">¶</a></h2>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q11-what-exact-positive-evidence-supported-the-statement-that-the-foot-leg-material-may-have-been-completely-separate">Q11. What exact positive evidence supported the statement that the foot/leg material “may have been completely separate”?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q11-what-exact-positive-evidence-supported-the-statement-that-the-foot-leg-material-may-have-been-completely-separate">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> “Separate” is not merely uncertainty; it is one candidate relationship. The record should be asked what observations specifically favoured it.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> Reveal whether separation was grounded in patient phenomenology, examination, chronology, structural findings, or another discriminator.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q12-what-exact-evidence-supported-perhaps-a-secondary-peripheral-neuropathy">Q12. What exact evidence supported “perhaps a secondary peripheral neuropathy”?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q12-what-exact-evidence-supported-perhaps-a-secondary-peripheral-neuropathy">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> Burning symptoms and historical alcohol exposure may be compatible with neuropathy, but compatibility is not the same as discrimination.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> Establish whether the neuropathy branch had a clear contemporaneous evidential bridge.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q13-what-evidence-at-the-same-time-pointed-against-separation-or-against-peripheral-neuropathy">Q13. What evidence at the same time pointed against separation or against peripheral neuropathy?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q13-what-evidence-at-the-same-time-pointed-against-separation-or-against-peripheral-neuropathy">¶</a></h3>
<p>Examples might include the dystonic-looking gait, toe phenomena, treatment-state behaviour, symmetry, abruptness, or absence of sensory signs.</p>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> A fair analysis needs the full evidential balance, not only evidence supporting the branch that survived.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> Show whether the 2017 formulation was genuinely balanced or whether one branch acquired operational priority without comparable discrimination.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q14-did-the-clinician-state-what-would-have-made-him-abandon-or-downgrade-the-separate-peripheral-hypothesis">Q14. Did the clinician state what would have made him abandon or downgrade the “separate/peripheral” hypothesis?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q14-did-the-clinician-state-what-would-have-made-him-abandon-or-downgrade-the-separate-peripheral-hypothesis">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> This is the earliest falsifiability question. A hypothesis can be appropriately provisional yet later become durable if no revision threshold is specified.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> Identify whether the original uncertainty had an internal stopping rule.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q15-how-much-causal-weight-did-the-specialist-place-on-the-patient-s-statement-that-leg-tension-felt-distinct-from-corns-callouses-foot-pain-especially-against-the-gp-s-continuing-integrated-framing-and-neurology-s-own-dystonic-looking-gait-observation">Q15. How much causal weight did the specialist place on the patient's statement that leg tension felt distinct from corns/callouses/foot pain, especially against the GP's continuing integrated framing and Neurology's own dystonic-looking gait observation?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q15-how-much-causal-weight-did-the-specialist-place-on-the-patient-s-statement-that-leg-tension-felt-distinct-from-corns-callouses-foot-pain-especially-against-the-gp-s-continuing-integrated-framing-and-neurology-s-own-dystonic-looking-gait-observation">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> The patient's distinction remains important contrary evidence, but the GP trial now supplies an independent longitudinal comparator: primary care later continued to link feet with Parkinsonian tone/gait. The question is therefore not whether the patient's phenomenology existed, but how far it legitimately carried the specialist inference toward separation.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> Prevent overstatement in either direction: neither dismissing patient phenomenology nor allowing it to function silently as biological proof of independence.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="105-questions-1620-what-happened-to-the-dystonic-looking-lower-limb">105. Questions 16–20 — What happened to the dystonic-looking lower limb?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#105-questions-1620-what-happened-to-the-dystonic-looking-lower-limb">¶</a></h2>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q16-where-is-the-phrase-somewhat-suggestive-of-a-dystonic-posture-not-foot-drop-next-referred-to-if-anywhere">Q16. Where is the phrase “somewhat suggestive of a dystonic posture — NOT FOOT DROP” next referred to, if anywhere?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q16-where-is-the-phrase-somewhat-suggestive-of-a-dystonic-posture-not-foot-drop-next-referred-to-if-anywhere">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> This traces whether a potentially important observation remained active or became archival.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> Establish the persistence or disappearance of the sign as a live proposition.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q17-how-does-the-almost-dystonically-unusual-walk-in-the-dat-request-relate-textually-and-temporally-to-the-autosomal-recessive-suspicion">Q17. How does the “almost dystonically unusual” walk in the DAT request relate textually and temporally to the autosomal-recessive suspicion?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q17-how-does-the-almost-dystonically-unusual-walk-in-the-dat-request-relate-textually-and-temporally-to-the-autosomal-recessive-suspicion">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> The sign did not simply vanish. It appears to contribute at least to the broader atypical/recessive impression. Exact chronology matters.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> Prevent the case from overstating exclusion of dystonia from genetic reasoning.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q18-is-there-any-note-explaining-why-dystonia-was-not-included-in-the-explicit-enumerated-prkn-park2-feature-list">Q18. Is there any note explaining why dystonia was not included in the explicit enumerated PRKN/PARK2 feature list?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q18-is-there-any-note-explaining-why-dystonia-was-not-included-in-the-explicit-enumerated-prkn-park2-feature-list">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> Omission may be entirely reasonable if the sign was judged nonspecific. The record should be asked whether that judgment was made explicitly.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> A reasoned explanation would weaken the documentary-asymmetry inference.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q19-was-the-lower-limb-dystonic-appearance-ever-linked-to-the-patient-s-toe-posture-painful-feet-loading-or-structural-deformity">Q19. Was the lower-limb dystonic appearance ever linked to the patient's toe posture, painful feet, loading or structural deformity?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q19-was-the-lower-limb-dystonic-appearance-ever-linked-to-the-patient-s-toe-posture-painful-feet-loading-or-structural-deformity">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> Recognition of dystonia is not the same as relational integration.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> Directly test whether the missing “join” actually exists elsewhere in the record.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q20-did-any-later-clinicianespecially-a-movement-disorders-specialistreconstruct-the-2017-dystonic-looking-lower-limb-in-light-of-subsequent-phenotype-evolution">Q20. Did any later clinician—especially a Movement Disorders specialist—reconstruct the 2017 dystonic-looking lower limb in light of subsequent phenotype evolution?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q20-did-any-later-clinicianespecially-a-movement-disorders-specialistreconstruct-the-2017-dystonic-looking-lower-limb-in-light-of-subsequent-phenotype-evolution">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> A later specialist reconstruction could perform the global operation currently thought absent.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> Strong potential falsifier of the “no clearly identifiable global reconstruction” proposition.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="106-questions-2125-what-exactly-did-as-per-above-make-operational">106. Questions 21–25 — What exactly did “As per above” make operational?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#106-questions-2125-what-exactly-did-as-per-above-make-operational">¶</a></h2>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q21-what-propositions-sat-immediately-above-clin-001613001614-and-which-were-settled-probable-possible-or-expressly-unresolved">Q21. What propositions sat immediately above <code>CLIN-001613–001614</code>, and which were settled, probable, possible, or expressly unresolved?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q21-what-propositions-sat-immediately-above-clin-001613001614-and-which-were-settled-probable-possible-or-expressly-unresolved">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> “As per above” can only be interpreted by identifying the epistemic status of what it incorporates.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> Clarify whether the plan inherited uncertainty explicitly or merely inherited decisions built around it.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q22-which-service-destinations-were-assigned-and-what-unresolved-relational-question-was-communicated-to-each-destination-and-back-to-the-gp">Q22. Which service destinations were assigned, and what unresolved relational question was communicated to each destination and back to the GP?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q22-which-service-destinations-were-assigned-and-what-unresolved-relational-question-was-communicated-to-each-destination-and-back-to-the-gp">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> Destination and handover content are different. The GP trial shows that primary care later continued to carry the neurological relationship into Podiatry referrals, so the issue is now whether specialist handoff explicitly preserved that relationship or whether the GP regenerated it independently.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> Distinguish simple service allocation from an integrated handover architecture.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q23-after-discharge-which-clinician-or-service-possessed-the-adjudicative-authority-to-decide-what-the-feetgaittonepain-relationship-meant-when-the-gp-continued-to-notice-and-transmit-those-relationships">Q23. After discharge, which clinician or service possessed the <strong>adjudicative authority</strong> to decide what the feet–gait–tone–pain relationship meant when the GP continued to notice and transmit those relationships?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q23-after-discharge-which-clinician-or-service-possessed-the-adjudicative-authority-to-decide-what-the-feetgaittonepain-relationship-meant-when-the-gp-continued-to-notice-and-transmit-those-relationships">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> The GP trial materially changes the old “who owned the arrows?” question. The GP demonstrably kept seeing arrows. The sharper question is who had the specialist authority and responsibility to adjudicate them.</p>
<p><strong>Current status:</strong> <strong>Reframed, not answered.</strong> Primary care repeatedly transmitted relationship-bearing information; the specialist owner of global integration is still not clearly identified in the surviving record.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> A clearly named integrative specialist owner would weaken the current authority/ownership formulation.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q24-what-formal-or-informal-feedback-loop-existed-for-gp-and-podiatry-observations-to-return-to-neurology-as-evidence-capable-of-changing-the-specialist-model">Q24. What formal or informal feedback loop existed for GP and Podiatry observations to return to Neurology as evidence capable of changing the specialist model?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q24-what-formal-or-informal-feedback-loop-existed-for-gp-and-podiatry-observations-to-return-to-neurology-as-evidence-capable-of-changing-the-specialist-model">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> The 2019 GP Podiatry re-referral explicitly reconnects feet with increased tone, abnormal gait and Parkinson's. That proves relationship information survived in primary care. The unanswered question is how such information was supposed to reach and alter specialist reasoning.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> Strongly test whether the apparent partition had a genuine cross-service re-entry mechanism.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q25-what-recurrent-observation-from-the-gp-podiatry-patient-or-treatment-state-would-have-been-sufficient-to-trigger-global-specialist-reconstruction-rather-than-another-local-branch-response">Q25. What recurrent observation from the GP, Podiatry, patient, or treatment state would have been sufficient to trigger <strong>global specialist reconstruction</strong>, rather than another local branch response?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q25-what-recurrent-observation-from-the-gp-podiatry-patient-or-treatment-state-would-have-been-sufficient-to-trigger-global-specialist-reconstruction-rather-than-another-local-branch-response">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> The record now contains several relationship-bearing returns from primary care. The key issue is no longer whether uncertainty could re-enter the system at all, but what threshold would make the inherited specialist model itself decision-live again.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> A defined specialist trigger would materially strengthen the defence that uncertainty was actively governed rather than merely accommodated.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="107-questions-2630-how-was-prkn-uncertainty-managed-after-2017">107. Questions 26–30 — How was PRKN uncertainty managed after 2017?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#107-questions-2630-how-was-prkn-uncertainty-managed-after-2017">¶</a></h2>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q26-what-exactly-did-the-2018-phrase-no-great-loss-in-not-knowing-currently-right-now-apply-to">Q26. What exactly did the 2018 phrase “no great loss in ‘not knowing’ currently right now” apply to?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q26-what-exactly-did-the-2018-phrase-no-great-loss-in-not-knowing-currently-right-now-apply-to">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> It may refer narrowly to immediate medication management or more broadly to the value of molecular diagnosis. The scope matters.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> Define the true reasoning behind deferral without retrospectively expanding it.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q27-was-any-future-date-clinical-trigger-family-event-treatment-development-or-technology-milestone-specified-for-reconsidering-prkn-testing">Q27. Was any future date, clinical trigger, family event, treatment development, or technology milestone specified for reconsidering PRKN testing?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q27-was-any-future-date-clinical-trigger-family-event-treatment-development-or-technology-milestone-specified-for-reconsidering-prkn-testing">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> Deferral with a reopening plan is different from open-ended deferral.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> Directly test the “uncertainty without reopening architecture” proposition.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q28-after-2018-where-does-prkn-park2-remain-visible-in-the-treating-neurologist-s-active-reasoning-rather-than-merely-in-copied-history">Q28. After 2018, where does PRKN/PARK2 remain visible in the treating neurologist's active reasoning rather than merely in copied history?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q28-after-2018-where-does-prkn-park2-remain-visible-in-the-treating-neurologist-s-active-reasoning-rather-than-merely-in-copied-history">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> Persistence in the archive is not the same as persistence as an operative hypothesis.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> Establish when, if ever, the specific PRKN question lost operational salience.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q29-is-there-any-pre-2025-source-explicitly-downgrading-rejecting-or-closing-prkn-park2">Q29. Is there any pre-2025 source explicitly downgrading, rejecting, or closing PRKN/PARK2?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q29-is-there-any-pre-2025-source-explicitly-downgrading-rejecting-or-closing-prkn-park2">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> This is the single strongest potential falsifier of the “missing etiological bridge” formulation.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> A reasoned closure source would substantially alter the genetics narrative.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q30-when-ipd-or-idiopathic-parkinson-s-disease-is-used-is-it-clinical-shorthand-a-contrastive-etiological-category-or-an-explicit-statement-that-monogenic-disease-has-become-unlikely">Q30. When “iPD” or “idiopathic Parkinson's disease” is used, is it clinical shorthand, a contrastive etiological category, or an explicit statement that monogenic disease has become unlikely?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q30-when-ipd-or-idiopathic-parkinson-s-disease-is-used-is-it-clinical-shorthand-a-contrastive-etiological-category-or-an-explicit-statement-that-monogenic-disease-has-become-unlikely">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> The author's use of “idiopathic” appears elastic. Each occurrence needs provenance and function, not dictionary assumptions.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> Prevent overstatement that later iPD wording necessarily equals formal rejection of PRKN.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="108-questions-3135-what-did-later-boundary-crossing-evidence-do-to-the-model">108. Questions 31–35 — What did later boundary-crossing evidence do to the model?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#108-questions-3135-what-did-later-boundary-crossing-evidence-do-to-the-model">¶</a></h2>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q31-when-procyclidine-withdrawal-produced-renewed-foot-tension-gait-phenomena-did-the-clinician-treat-that-as-evidence-about-the-original-partition-or-only-as-a-medication-management-observation">Q31. When procyclidine withdrawal produced renewed foot/tension/gait phenomena, did the clinician treat that as evidence about the original partition or only as a medication-management observation?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q31-when-procyclidine-withdrawal-produced-renewed-foot-tension-gait-phenomena-did-the-clinician-treat-that-as-evidence-about-the-original-partition-or-only-as-a-medication-management-observation">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> This is an early natural perturbation capable of testing whether separated phenomena move together.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> Clarify whether treatment-state information propagated to the parent model.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q32-when-the-gp-re-referred-to-podiatry-in-2019-stating-that-the-feet-issues-related-to-increased-tone-and-abnormal-gait-from-parkinson-s-and-describing-very-young-onset-parkinson-s-with-clawing-feet-and-pain-what-did-specialist-services-do-with-that-re-integrated-formulation">Q32. When the GP re-referred to Podiatry in 2019 stating that the feet issues related to increased tone and abnormal gait from Parkinson's, and describing very-young-onset Parkinson's with “clawing feet” and pain, what did specialist services do with that re-integrated formulation?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q32-when-the-gp-re-referred-to-podiatry-in-2019-stating-that-the-feet-issues-related-to-increased-tone-and-abnormal-gait-from-parkinson-s-and-describing-very-young-onset-parkinson-s-with-clawing-feet-and-pain-what-did-specialist-services-do-with-that-re-integrated-formulation">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> This is a major result of the GP trial. The GP did not merely maintain a structural-foot destination; he explicitly reconnected the feet to the neurological syndrome.</p>
<p><strong>Current status:</strong> <strong>GP side answered; specialist-response side open.</strong></p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> Determine whether the relationship was merely visible downstream or actually fed back into neurological adjudication.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q33-when-the-2020-foot-throw-was-declared-actually-parkinsonian-because-distal-power-was-normal-did-that-reclassification-prompt-reconsideration-of-the-earlier-dystonic-looking-footdrop-like-gait-or-the-gp-s-recurring-feetgait-relationship">Q33. When the 2020 foot-throw was declared “actually parkinsonian” because distal power was normal, did that reclassification prompt reconsideration of the earlier dystonic-looking footdrop-like gait or the GP's recurring feet–gait relationship?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q33-when-the-2020-foot-throw-was-declared-actually-parkinsonian-because-distal-power-was-normal-did-that-reclassification-prompt-reconsideration-of-the-earlier-dystonic-looking-footdrop-like-gait-or-the-gp-s-recurring-feetgait-relationship">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> This is a near-anatomical repeat and a strong example of specialist proposition-testing. It could have propagated backwards into the historical model.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> Strong test of local update versus backward reconstruction.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q34-when-the-gp-s-urgent-2023-referral-reported-severe-burning-feet-lower-leg-pain-recalled-that-earlier-pain-had-settled-with-better-parkinson-s-control-stated-i-am-worried-about-him-and-sought-urgent-specialist-review-how-did-neurology-treat-that-relationship-bearing-referral">Q34. When the GP's urgent 2023 referral reported severe burning feet/lower-leg pain, recalled that earlier pain had settled with better Parkinson's control, stated “I am worried about him,” and sought urgent specialist review, how did Neurology treat that relationship-bearing referral?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q34-when-the-gp-s-urgent-2023-referral-reported-severe-burning-feet-lower-leg-pain-recalled-that-earlier-pain-had-settled-with-better-parkinson-s-control-stated-i-am-worried-about-him-and-sought-urgent-specialist-review-how-did-neurology-treat-that-relationship-bearing-referral">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> The GP trial shows that the 2023 referral itself is a top-level re-integration signal, not merely a request to treat established neuropathic pain.</p>
<p><strong>Current status:</strong> <strong>Partly answered.</strong> Neurology later performed substantial local reconsideration—OFF phenomena, dystonia, neuropathy timing, Fabry, levodopa state—but the scope of reconstruction remains the question.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> Clarify whether specialist care answered the relationship the GP returned or mainly decomposed it again into local mechanisms.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q35-when-off-dystonia-off-related-walking-abrupt-onset-against-neuropathy-fabry-levodopa-response-and-the-gp-s-parkinsonian-pain-linkage-were-all-simultaneously-available-in-2023-did-any-specialist-note-explicitly-rebuild-the-original-parent-model">Q35. When OFF dystonia, OFF-related walking, abrupt onset against neuropathy, Fabry, levodopa response and the GP's Parkinsonian-pain linkage were all simultaneously available in 2023, did any specialist note explicitly rebuild the original parent model?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q35-when-off-dystonia-off-related-walking-abrupt-onset-against-neuropathy-fabry-levodopa-response-and-the-gp-s-parkinsonian-pain-linkage-were-all-simultaneously-available-in-2023-did-any-specialist-note-explicitly-rebuild-the-original-parent-model">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> 2023 is now not merely a counterexample to rigid inheritance; it is a convergence point where <strong>primary-care re-integration and specialist local re-analysis coincide</strong>.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> If a genuine global reconstruction exists here, the current central formulation must be narrowed materially.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="109-questions-3640-how-did-neuropathic-language-acquire-stability">109. Questions 36–40 — How did neuropathic language acquire stability?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#109-questions-3640-how-did-neuropathic-language-acquire-stability">¶</a></h2>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q36-what-is-the-complete-chronological-and-source-specific-sequence-of-the-words-peripheral-neuropathy-neuropathic-disorder-neuropathic-symptoms-neuropathic-pain-neuropathic-pain-and-peripheral-neuropathic-pain-symptomatologies">Q36. What is the complete chronological and <strong>source-specific</strong> sequence of the words “peripheral neuropathy,” “neuropathic disorder,” “neuropathic symptoms,” “? neuropathic pain,” “neuropathic pain,” and “peripheral neuropathic pain symptomatologies”?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q36-what-is-the-complete-chronological-and-source-specific-sequence-of-the-words-peripheral-neuropathy-neuropathic-disorder-neuropathic-symptoms-neuropathic-pain-neuropathic-pain-and-peripheral-neuropathic-pain-symptomatologies">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> These are not synonyms, and the GP trial now shows that different services did not necessarily carry the same classification. The genealogy must therefore identify <strong>who is speaking</strong>, not merely when the term appears.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> Establish whether classificatory hardening is real, gradual, discontinuous, or only apparent.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q37-at-each-strengthening-of-neuropathic-language-what-new-discriminating-evidence-had-appeared-and-did-that-evidence-come-from-neurology-gp-podiatry-patient-report-treatment-response-or-simple-inheritance-of-terminology">Q37. At each strengthening of neuropathic language, what new discriminating evidence had appeared, and did that evidence come from Neurology, GP, Podiatry, patient report, treatment response, or simple inheritance of terminology?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q37-at-each-strengthening-of-neuropathic-language-what-new-discriminating-evidence-had-appeared-and-did-that-evidence-come-from-neurology-gp-podiatry-patient-report-treatment-response-or-simple-inheritance-of-terminology">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> The issue is not whether the category was plausible but whether stronger grammatical certainty was earned by stronger evidence <strong>within the same evidential chain</strong>, rather than by cross-service repetition.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> Directly test the “evidential bridge” behind semantic migration.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q38-did-pregabalin-response-ever-function-in-the-record-as-proof-of-neuropathic-mechanism-or-only-as-evidence-of-therapeutic-usefulness">Q38. Did pregabalin response ever function in the record as proof of neuropathic mechanism, or only as evidence of therapeutic usefulness?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q38-did-pregabalin-response-ever-function-in-the-record-as-proof-of-neuropathic-mechanism-or-only-as-evidence-of-therapeutic-usefulness">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> The same neurologist explicitly treated gabapentin response as non-diagnostic in the shoulder case.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> A careful non-specific interpretation would strengthen the defence against claims of circularity.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q39-what-did-the-principal-contrary-evidence-do-to-confidence-in-the-neuropathic-classification-severe-recurrent-pain-despite-600-mg-day-pregabalin-the-2019-gp-podiatry-form-s-no-neuropathy-tick-with-its-documentary-limitations-abrupt-onset-argued-against-neuropathy-in-2023-and-repeated-parkinsonian-treatment-state-effects">Q39. What did the principal contrary evidence do to confidence in the neuropathic classification: severe recurrent pain despite 600 mg/day pregabalin, the 2019 GP/Podiatry form's “No neuropathy” tick (with its documentary limitations), abrupt onset argued against neuropathy in 2023, and repeated Parkinsonian treatment-state effects?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q39-what-did-the-principal-contrary-evidence-do-to-confidence-in-the-neuropathic-classification-severe-recurrent-pain-despite-600-mg-day-pregabalin-the-2019-gp-podiatry-form-s-no-neuropathy-tick-with-its-documentary-limitations-abrupt-onset-argued-against-neuropathy-in-2023-and-repeated-parkinsonian-treatment-state-effects">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> No single item disproves neuropathic pain. The question is whether the category demonstrates a visible response to cumulative contrary evidence across services.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> Test whether the category had a meaningful falsification threshold.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q40-by-2025-when-disease-level-neuropathy-remains-conditional-but-peripheral-neuropathic-pain-symptomatologies-is-affirmative-is-the-clinician-distinguishing-phenotype-from-disease-or-has-uncertainty-simply-migrated-downstream">Q40. By 2025, when disease-level neuropathy remains conditional but “peripheral neuropathic pain symptomatologies” is affirmative, is the clinician distinguishing phenotype from disease, or has uncertainty simply migrated downstream?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q40-by-2025-when-disease-level-neuropathy-remains-conditional-but-peripheral-neuropathic-pain-symptomatologies-is-affirmative-is-the-clinician-distinguishing-phenotype-from-disease-or-has-uncertainty-simply-migrated-downstream">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> This is the core linguistic question in <code>CLIN-003240</code>.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> Refine whether the best formulation is benign phenotype/etiology separation, classificatory hardening, or both.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="110-questions-4145-what-does-the-same-author-comparator-really-establish">110. Questions 41–45 — What does the same-author comparator really establish?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#110-questions-4145-what-does-the-same-author-comparator-really-establish">¶</a></h2>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q41-are-all-key-shoulder-passages-unquestionably-authored-by-the-treating-neurologist-contemporaneous-and-not-copied-from-another-clinician">Q41. Are all key shoulder passages unquestionably authored by the treating neurologist, contemporaneous, and not copied from another clinician?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q41-are-all-key-shoulder-passages-unquestionably-authored-by-the-treating-neurologist-contemporaneous-and-not-copied-from-another-clinician">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> The comparator only works if authorship and provenance are secure.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> This is a direct falsifier of the same-author control if provenance fails.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q42-does-the-shoulder-record-contain-a-complete-test-result-revision-loop-or-only-question-discrimination-planned-testing">Q42. Does the shoulder record contain a complete test → result → revision loop, or only question → discrimination → planned testing?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q42-does-the-shoulder-record-contain-a-complete-test-result-revision-loop-or-only-question-discrimination-planned-testing">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> Controlled GPT outputs have differed on whether final imaging-mediated closure is present.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> Determine how strongly the comparator can be stated without overclaiming.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q43-which-exact-reasoning-moves-in-the-shoulder-episode-have-a-true-analogue-in-the-feet-pathway-and-which-do-not">Q43. Which exact reasoning moves in the shoulder episode have a true analogue in the feet pathway, and which do not?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q43-which-exact-reasoning-moves-in-the-shoulder-episode-have-a-true-analogue-in-the-feet-pathway-and-which-do-not">¶</a></h3>
<p>Examples: explicit alternatives, expected findings, non-discriminating treatment response, negative-test limitations, revision thresholds.</p>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> The comparator should identify a difference in reasoning form, not merely note length.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> Make the comparison more precise and less vulnerable to “apples versus oranges” criticism.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q44-is-the-shoulder-difference-better-explained-by-information-state-or-by-the-shoulder-being-acute-focal-and-anatomically-testable">Q44. Is the shoulder difference better explained by information state, or by the shoulder being acute, focal and anatomically testable?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q44-is-the-shoulder-difference-better-explained-by-information-state-or-by-the-shoulder-being-acute-focal-and-anatomically-testable">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> This is the strongest benign alternative explanation.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> The answer may reduce the strength of the path-dependence inference without eliminating it.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q45-does-the-2020-foot-throw-or-2023-review-show-that-the-clinician-could-deploy-shoulder-like-proposition-testing-within-the-inherited-pathway-when-a-sufficiently-specific-question-became-live">Q45. Does the 2020 foot-throw or 2023 review show that the clinician could deploy shoulder-like proposition-testing within the inherited pathway when a sufficiently specific question became live?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q45-does-the-2020-foot-throw-or-2023-review-show-that-the-clinician-could-deploy-shoulder-like-proposition-testing-within-the-inherited-pathway-when-a-sufficiently-specific-question-became-live">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> The answer is probably yes, and that matters. It shifts the theory from “inheritance suppressed reasoning” to “inheritance shaped the scope at which reasoning occurred.”</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> Protect the strongest formulation from drifting into an unsustainable incapacity narrative.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="111-questions-4650-did-local-revisions-ever-become-global-reconstruction">111. Questions 46–50 — Did local revisions ever become global reconstruction?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#111-questions-4650-did-local-revisions-ever-become-global-reconstruction">¶</a></h2>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q46-is-there-anywhere-from-2018-to-2025-a-specialist-note-that-gathers-structural-feet-dystonic-posture-pain-gait-off-state-possible-neuropathy-treatment-response-and-genetics-into-one-renewed-synthesis">Q46. Is there anywhere from 2018 to 2025 a <strong>specialist</strong> note that gathers structural feet, dystonic posture, pain, gait, OFF state, possible neuropathy, treatment response and genetics into one renewed synthesis?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q46-is-there-anywhere-from-2018-to-2025-a-specialist-note-that-gathers-structural-feet-dystonic-posture-pain-gait-off-state-possible-neuropathy-treatment-response-and-genetics-into-one-renewed-synthesis">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> The GP trial now establishes that primary care itself repeatedly re-integrated portions of the problem. The missing operation, if missing, is therefore specifically <strong>specialist adjudicative reconstruction</strong>, not generic recognition.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> A genuine specialist integrative note would materially weaken the current central proposition.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q47-how-did-specialist-services-respond-to-the-gp-s-repeated-relationship-bearing-returns-in-2019-2023-and-2025">Q47. How did specialist services respond to the GP's repeated relationship-bearing returns in 2019, 2023 and 2025?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q47-how-did-specialist-services-respond-to-the-gp-s-repeated-relationship-bearing-returns-in-2019-2023-and-2025">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> These referrals form a longitudinal natural experiment. The GP repeatedly sends back links among feet, gait/tone, pain, Parkinsonian control and later dystonia. The record must show whether each return altered the specialist parent model, generated only a local response, or was not visibly integrated.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> This is now one of the strongest tests of the <strong>authority-to-adjudicate-the-arrows</strong> formulation.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q48-does-the-2025-gp-move-toward-external-movement-disorders-dystonia-review-represent-the-first-clearly-documented-transfer-of-the-top-level-relationship-question-to-a-new-specialist-authority">Q48. Does the 2025 GP move toward external Movement Disorders/dystonia review represent the first clearly documented transfer of the top-level relationship question to a new specialist authority?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q48-does-the-2025-gp-move-toward-external-movement-disorders-dystonia-review-represent-the-first-clearly-documented-transfer-of-the-top-level-relationship-question-to-a-new-specialist-authority">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> By 2025 the GP expressly recognises limits of his own scope and seeks specialist expertise rather than independently adjudicating dystonia/Botox or DAT-scan meaning. This may represent a genuine <strong>global re-routing</strong> of the unresolved problem.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> Clarify whether 2025 is merely another referral or the first explicit change in who is being asked to own the whole explanatory problem.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q49-does-the-2025-complaint-response-reconstruct-the-genealogy-of-the-challenged-categories-and-answer-the-recurrent-gp-generated-relationship-information-or-primarily-explain-why-management-was-reasonable-from-inside-the-inherited-specialist-categories">Q49. Does the 2025 complaint response reconstruct the genealogy of the challenged categories and answer the recurrent GP-generated relationship information, or primarily explain why management was reasonable from inside the inherited specialist categories?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q49-does-the-2025-complaint-response-reconstruct-the-genealogy-of-the-challenged-categories-and-answer-the-recurrent-gp-generated-relationship-information-or-primarily-explain-why-management-was-reasonable-from-inside-the-inherited-specialist-categories">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> The complaint makes provenance the object of review, while the GP history supplies an independent longitudinal source showing that the relationships remained live outside the treating specialist's documentation.</p>
<p><strong>What could change:</strong> Determine whether 2025 contains the global reconstruction otherwise missing from the clinical notes.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="q50-before-introducing-the-2026-result-what-is-the-strongest-formulation-that-survives-the-gp-information-state-trial-the-specialist-record-the-podiatry-record-the-shoulder-control-every-contrary-source-and-every-identified-falsifier">Q50. Before introducing the 2026 result, what is the strongest formulation that survives the GP information-state trial, the specialist record, the Podiatry record, the shoulder control, every contrary source, and every identified falsifier?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#q50-before-introducing-the-2026-result-what-is-the-strongest-formulation-that-survives-the-gp-information-state-trial-the-specialist-record-the-podiatry-record-the-shoulder-control-every-contrary-source-and-every-identified-falsifier">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Why this is asked:</strong> This remains the anti-drift capstone, but its expected answer has changed because the GP trial falsified a strong “downstream loss of the arrows” version of the theory.</p>
<p><strong>Current expected answer, subject to the preceding 49 questions:</strong></p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>The record shows an integrated deteriorating presentation generated in primary care; a narrower specialist task representation followed by substantial factual recovery and high-resolution Parkinsonian/genetic construction; comparatively lower documented adjudication of the distal feet–gait–posture–pain relationship; and an operational partition that became portable. Primary care did not simply adopt complete separation: the GP repeatedly preserved or regenerated relationships among feet, gait, tone, pain and Parkinsonian control and returned those relationships to specialist services. Specialist care repeatedly performed genuine local reassessment, but the surviving record still more clearly documents proposition-specific updates than a single specialist reconstruction of the founding relationship. The key longitudinal question is therefore not whether the arrows were seen, but how the service with authority to adjudicate them processed recurrent relationship-bearing information and what threshold would have caused the inherited specialist model itself to be rebuilt.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="112-priority-order-after-the-gp-information-state-trial">112. Priority order after the GP information-state trial<a class="heading-anchor" href="#112-priority-order-after-the-gp-information-state-trial">¶</a></h2>
<p>The GP trial changes the order of attack. The highest-value questions are now those that test <strong>specialist adjudication of repeatedly returned relationship information</strong>.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="priority-a-what-governed-the-first-specialist-encounter">Priority A — What governed the first specialist encounter?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#priority-a-what-governed-the-first-specialist-encounter">¶</a></h3>
<ul>
<li><strong>Q1:</strong> What did Neurology actually receive?</li>
<li><strong>Q2:</strong> Which representation actually governed the initial task?</li>
<li><strong>Q4:</strong> Where did the integrated object become partitioned?</li>
<li><strong>Q5:</strong> What did the specialist return communicate to the GP as resolved versus unresolved?</li>
</ul>
<p>These determine whether the initial mechanism is best described as entry compression, specialist repartition, or both.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="priority-b-what-earned-the-first-specialist-partition">Priority B — What earned the first specialist partition?<a class="heading-anchor" href="#priority-b-what-earned-the-first-specialist-partition">¶</a></h3>
<ul>
<li><strong>Q7–Q10:</strong> What distal discriminating examination was actually documented?</li>
<li><strong>Q11–Q15:</strong> What positive, negative and patient-derived evidence earned separation/peripheral reasoning?</li>
</ul>
<p>These remain central because the GP trial does not answer the specialist evidential bridge.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="priority-c-authority-feedback-and-reopening">Priority C — Authority, feedback and reopening<a class="heading-anchor" href="#priority-c-authority-feedback-and-reopening">¶</a></h3>
<ul>
<li><strong>Q22:</strong> What relational question travelled with each destination?</li>
<li><strong>Q23:</strong> Who had adjudicative authority over the arrows?</li>
<li><strong>Q24:</strong> What feedback loop returned GP/Podiatry evidence to Neurology?</li>
<li><strong>Q25:</strong> What would trigger global specialist reconstruction?</li>
</ul>
<p>This is the largest substantive revision caused by the GP trial.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="priority-d-recurrent-gp-re-integration-as-a-longitudinal-test">Priority D — Recurrent GP re-integration as a longitudinal test<a class="heading-anchor" href="#priority-d-recurrent-gp-re-integration-as-a-longitudinal-test">¶</a></h3>
<ul>
<li><strong>Q32:</strong> What happened to the 2019 GP re-linking of feet, tone, gait and Parkinson's?</li>
<li><strong>Q34:</strong> What happened to the 2023 GP re-linking of severe pain with Parkinsonian control?</li>
<li><strong>Q47:</strong> Across 2019, 2023 and 2025, how did specialist services respond to repeated relationship-bearing returns?</li>
<li><strong>Q48:</strong> Did 2025 external Movement Disorders/dystonia referral finally transfer the top-level question to a new adjudicative authority?</li>
</ul>
<p>These questions did not have the same evidential force before the GP corpus was opened.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="priority-e-global-reconstruction-and-category-genealogy">Priority E — Global reconstruction and category genealogy<a class="heading-anchor" href="#priority-e-global-reconstruction-and-category-genealogy">¶</a></h3>
<ul>
<li><strong>Q20 / Q35 / Q46 / Q49:</strong> Is there a true specialist global reconstruction anywhere before the external pathway?</li>
<li><strong>Q29–Q30:</strong> What happened to PRKN and what did “idiopathic” mean?</li>
<li><strong>Q36–Q40:</strong> How did neuropathic language become stable across different speakers and services?</li>
</ul>
<p>These remain the strongest potential falsifiers or refiners of the mature theory.</p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="113-anti-drift-rule-for-answering-the-50-questions">113. Anti-drift rule for answering the 50 questions<a class="heading-anchor" href="#113-anti-drift-rule-for-answering-the-50-questions">¶</a></h2>
<p>Each answer should be entered under four headings:</p>
<p><strong>Record fact</strong> — what the source directly establishes.<br/>
<strong>Documentary inference</strong> — what can cautiously be inferred about information structure or language.<br/>
<strong>Clinical expert question</strong> — what requires specialist judgment.<br/>
<strong>Effect on current formulation</strong> — strengthen / qualify / weaken / falsify / no material change.</p>
<p>No answer should be allowed to create a new major case theory unless it first explains why the current information-pathway formulation no longer accounts for the evidence.</p>
<p>The working case should only move when the record forces it to move.</p>
<hr/>
</section>
<section class="reader-section kind-audit" data-section="Canonical answer-status pass" id="section-answer-status">
<div class="section-kicker">Canonical answer-status pass</div>
<h2 class="source-h1 anchored-heading" id="part-xxxii-first-canonical-answer-status-pass-on-the-50-questions">Part XXXII — First canonical answer-status pass on the 50 questions<a class="heading-anchor" href="#part-xxxii-first-canonical-answer-status-pass-on-the-50-questions">¶</a></h2>
<p>This section converts the 50 questions from a static questionnaire into a live audit. The labels mean:</p>
<ul>
<li><strong>Answered materially</strong> — the canonical record now supplies a reasonably clear documentary answer.</li>
<li><strong>Partly answered</strong> — an important part is established, but the decisive bridge remains open.</li>
<li><strong>Open</strong> — the present corpus does not yet answer the question.</li>
<li><strong>Expert-dependent</strong> — the documentary state is reasonably clear but its clinical significance requires specialist opinion.</li>
</ul>
<p>The answers below are provisional documentary findings, not medical or legal conclusions. “Not identified” always means <strong>not identified in the present canonical pass</strong>, not proof that the event never occurred.</p>
<table>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Q</th>
<th>Status</th>
<th>First canonical answer / remaining issue</th>
<th>Effect on formulation</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><strong>1</strong></td>
<td>Partly answered</td>
<td>GP direct referral is established and the Neurology letter reproduces the hospital PRECIS. The corpus does not yet prove whether the treating neurologist actually read the full GP referral before substantive assessment.</td>
<td>Counterfactual remains conditional.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>2</strong></td>
<td>Partly answered</td>
<td>The explicit specialist referral block is PRECIS-shaped; the later Neurology history independently reconstructs much of the fuller story. What governed the <em>initial</em> investigative task remains unresolved.</td>
<td>Supports compression + later recovery, not simple information absence.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>3</strong></td>
<td>Answered materially</td>
<td>Neurology recovers feet history, structural abnormalities, deterioration, strikingly abnormal gait and dystonic-looking lower-limb posture. What returns less clearly is the GP's original integrated relationship among those facts.</td>
<td>Strengthens <strong>facts ≠ relationships</strong>.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>4</strong></td>
<td>Partly answered</td>
<td>No single partition sentence does all the work. The transformation appears progressive: PRECIS narrows the task; examination builds Parkinsonism; <code>CLIN-001142–149</code> formulates separate/peripheral possibilities; “As per above” makes the mixed state operational.</td>
<td>Shifts from a single compression event to a staged transformation.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>5</strong></td>
<td>Partly answered</td>
<td>GP receives “extensive correspondence,” records early Parkinsonism/positive DaTscan and adopts Parkinsonism operationally. Later GP referrals nevertheless continue to reconnect feet with tone/gait/Parkinsonian state.</td>
<td>Retires strong “specialist clearance made GP forget” theory; preserves specialist-authority question.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>6</strong></td>
<td>Answered materially</td>
<td>Direct observations include marked symmetric tone, brisk reflexes, striking gait, footdrop-like/dystonic appearance, rigidity/bradykinesia, pes planus and hammertoes. Corns/callouses/burning are substantially historical/report material; “dystonic” is immediate interpretation of observed gait.</td>
<td>Supports observation/history/interpretation separation.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>7</strong></td>
<td>Open with negative documentary finding</td>
<td>No 2017 four-direction distal power examination has been identified; the later 2020 note does document dorsiflexion/plantarflexion/inversion/eversion as normal.</td>
<td>Maintains synthesis–examination differential, subject to absence-of-documentation caution.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>8</strong></td>
<td>Open with negative documentary finding</td>
<td>No mapped distal sensory examination supporting the 2017 peripheral-neuropathy possibility has been identified in the surviving examination block.</td>
<td>Keeps neuropathy evidential bridge under scrutiny.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>9</strong></td>
<td>Open with negative documentary finding</td>
<td>Toe clawing appears in GP history and structural hammertoes in examination, but no detailed fixed-versus-dynamic toe-posture mapping has yet been identified.</td>
<td>Supports lower relational resolution.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>10</strong></td>
<td>Open with negative documentary finding</td>
<td>No detailed 2017 pain-loading/gait-phase/provocation map has been identified.</td>
<td>Supports lower relational resolution, without proving examination never occurred.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>11</strong></td>
<td>Partly answered</td>
<td>Separation is supported by the patient's own distinction between leg tension and foot pain/corns/callouses, historical improvement of foot pain when off his feet/bar work, and genuine structural pathology.</td>
<td>Shows separation had a contemporaneous basis; it was not invented arbitrarily.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>12</strong></td>
<td>Partly answered</td>
<td>Peripheral-neuropathy possibility appears supported mainly by burning phenomenology and historical alcohol exposure; no equally clear documented sensory/neurophysiological bridge has been identified.</td>
<td>Supports “plausible but weakly discriminated” rather than “unsupported.”</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>13</strong></td>
<td>Answered materially</td>
<td>Contrary/qualifying evidence includes dystonic-looking gait, toe/postural phenomena, severe Parkinsonism, later treatment-state coupling and later clinician use of abrupt onset against neuropathy.</td>
<td>Demonstrates competing evidence accumulated across time.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>14</strong></td>
<td>Open with negative documentary finding</td>
<td>No explicit abandonment threshold for the separate/peripheral branch has been identified.</td>
<td>Supports “documentarily difficult to falsify,” not literal unfalsifiability.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>15</strong></td>
<td>Partly answered</td>
<td>The neurologist expressly records that the patient had “markedly divorced” tension from foot pain/corns/callouses and places this immediately after the separate/peripheral formulation. Exact causal weight cannot be quantified.</td>
<td>Important defence; does not establish biological independence.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>16</strong></td>
<td>Answered materially</td>
<td>The dystonic-looking lower-limb observation reappears in the DAT request as a walk that is “almost dystonically unusual.”</td>
<td>Retires any claim that dystonia simply disappeared.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>17</strong></td>
<td>Answered materially</td>
<td>In the DAT request, the dystonically unusual walk appears directly alongside suspicion of autosomal-recessive Parkinson's.</td>
<td>Shows partial incorporation into broader recessive reasoning.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>18</strong></td>
<td>Open</td>
<td>No source has yet been identified explaining why dystonia was omitted from the explicit PRKN feature enumeration.</td>
<td>Omission remains documentary asymmetry; clinical significance expert-dependent.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>19</strong></td>
<td>Partly answered</td>
<td>The specialist record recognises dystonic-looking gait and structural feet in the same admission, but no explicit 2017 synthesis joining dystonic posture to toe posture, loading, corns/callouses and pain has been identified.</td>
<td>Preserves the “missing join” question.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>20</strong></td>
<td>Partly answered</td>
<td>No pre-external-pathway Movement Disorders specialist reconstruction of the whole 2017 distal phenotype has been identified. The later St Vincent's/2026 endpoint unifies Parkinsonism+dystonia, but the December 2025 specialist reasoning is missing.</td>
<td>Global reconstruction remains unproven before external referral.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>21</strong></td>
<td>Answered materially</td>
<td>“As per above” incorporates strong Parkinsonism, structural feet, dystonic-looking gait, separate/peripheral possibilities marked “Not clear,” genetic uncertainty, treatment/referrals and follow-up.</td>
<td>Strong support for operational rather than complete epistemic closure.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>22</strong></td>
<td>Partly answered</td>
<td>Destinations are visible: Neurology, Podiatry, Physio, OT, Parkinson nursing, deferred genetics. The unresolved cross-domain question is not comparably explicit in the handover language.</td>
<td>Strengthens destination-versus-handover distinction.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>23</strong></td>
<td>Partly answered</td>
<td>The GP repeatedly returns relationship-bearing information to Neurology and later explicitly recognises specialist scope limits. A named owner of the <em>global relationship</em> is not identified, but specialist neurological adjudication functionally remains with Neurology.</td>
<td>Reframes “who owned the arrows?” as <strong>who had authority to adjudicate them?</strong></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>24</strong></td>
<td>Partly answered</td>
<td>Informal re-entry routes exist: GP re-referral, Podiatry, Parkinson nurse, clinic review. No defined closed-loop mechanism requiring Podiatry/GP relational findings to be synthesised back into Neurology has been identified.</td>
<td>Supports weak reopening architecture, not no feedback at all.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>25</strong></td>
<td>Open with negative documentary finding</td>
<td>No explicit threshold has been identified for when recurrent cross-boundary evidence would require rebuilding the parent specialist model.</td>
<td>Central longitudinal vulnerability remains.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>26</strong></td>
<td>Answered materially</td>
<td>“No great loss in not knowing” applies expressly to PRKN/genetic resolution because testing was not expected to change current management at that time.</td>
<td>Strong evidence for management-sufficiency principle.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>27</strong></td>
<td>Answered materially</td>
<td>No fixed review date is specified. Testing could be done on patient request; WGS is contemplated later when the gene library is more evolved.</td>
<td>Supports preserved question but weakly specified reopening mechanism.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>28</strong></td>
<td>Partly answered</td>
<td>PRKN remains explicitly active in 2018. A later PRKN passage at <code>CLIN-002208</code> is part of carried-forward/reproduced earlier material; no comparably clear fresh active PRKN reasoning after 2018 has yet been identified.</td>
<td>Supports loss of operational salience rather than explicit rejection.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>29</strong></td>
<td>Open with negative documentary finding</td>
<td>No pre-2025 source explicitly rejecting, downgrading or closing PRKN/PARK2 has been identified.</td>
<td>Missing etiological bridge remains strong.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>30</strong></td>
<td>Answered in part</td>
<td>Early “idiopathic” language coexists with autosomal-recessive suspicion, showing elastic/shorthand use. By 2023 “cause other than iPD” is contrastive; by 2025 “belief and understanding” is more affirmative.</td>
<td>Supports changing function of “iPD,” not a single stable meaning.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>31</strong></td>
<td>Answered materially</td>
<td>Procyclidine withdrawal and rapid return of foot/tension/gait phenomena are recorded as treatment-state information; no global reconstruction of the founding partition is identified at that point.</td>
<td>Strong example of boundary-crossing evidence locally absorbed.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>32</strong></td>
<td>Partly answered</td>
<td>GP 2019 explicitly reconnects feet to increased tone/abnormal gait/Parkinson's and “clawing feet” with pain. No specialist note has yet been identified showing that this re-integrated referral caused neurological reconstruction.</td>
<td>Strongens GP-as-longitudinal-control and specialist-response question.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>33</strong></td>
<td>Partly answered</td>
