````html
<td>2020 foot-throw is proposition-specifically reclassified as Parkinsonian using normal distal power. No explicit backward reconsideration of the 2017 dystonic-looking gait/feet relationship has been identified.</td>
<td>Strong local-update/global-reconstruction comparator.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>34</strong></td>
<td>Answered materially at first response level</td>
<td>2023 GP urgent referral explicitly reconnects severe burning pain with prior improvement under better Parkinsonian control. Neurology subsequently considers OFF state, dystonia, neuropathy timing, Fabry and levodopa effects.</td>
<td>Shows genuine specialist reopening, but leaves scope question.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>35</strong></td>
<td>Open with strong negative documentary finding</td>
<td>Despite unusually rich 2023 local reasoning, no note has yet been identified that explicitly rebuilds the entire 2017 structural–dystonic–pain–gait parent model.</td>
<td>Current central formulation survives its strongest counterexample.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>36</strong></td>
<td>Partly answered</td>
<td>The semantic chain is visible from “perhaps peripheral neuropathy / Not clear” through later neuropathy-question / possible-disorder / “? neuropathic pain” / neuropathic-pain formulations to 2025 “peripheral neuropathic pain symptomatologies.” A complete source-by-source corpus table is still needed.</td>
<td>Supports semantic migration, but final genealogy remains to be tabulated.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>37</strong></td>
<td>Partly answered</td>
<td>Compatible evidence accumulates—burning/sensitivity and some pregabalin benefit—but no single clearly documented discriminating event has yet been found that explains the stronger later symptom classification against competing structural/dystonic/OFF evidence.</td>
<td>Supports “evidence visible; transition less visible.”</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>38</strong></td>
<td>Answered materially</td>
<td>Pregabalin/Lyrica response is repeatedly used as therapeutic evidence; no clear passage has been identified treating response alone as proof of peripheral neuropathy.</td>
<td>Weakens a crude circularity claim; preserves documentary-recursion concern.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>39</strong></td>
<td>Answered materially</td>
<td>Contrary evidence does not visibly abolish the neuropathic symptom category. Instead disease-level neuropathy remains conditional while other mechanisms are added.</td>
<td>Supports classificatory stability + etiological flexibility.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>40</strong></td>
<td>Answered provisionally</td>
<td>The fairest reading is both: “peripheral neuropathic pain symptomatologies” may be phenotype shorthand, while grammar also shows uncertainty has migrated chiefly to cause.</td>
<td>Preserves strongest defence and strongest criticism simultaneously.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>41</strong></td>
<td>Partly answered</td>
<td>The shoulder reasoning sits in the treating neurologist's 14 Nov 2019 longitudinal letter and is written in the same first-person clinical voice. Final provenance/signature can still be verified if needed.</td>
<td>Same-author comparator remains strong.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>42</strong></td>
<td>Answered materially</td>
<td>The canonical corpus shows question → discrimination → planned targeted testing and test limitations. It does <strong>not</strong> currently show the final shoulder MRI result/revision; by 2020 the syndrome is described as largely settled.</td>
<td>Comparator should not claim a completed imaging-closure loop.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>43</strong></td>
<td>Answered materially</td>
<td>True analogues exist in feet pathway: 2020 normal distal power → Parkinsonian foot throw; 2023 abrupt onset against neuropathy; ON/OFF dystonia; Fabry testing. Less visible are global re-derivation and explicit parent-category revision thresholds.</td>
<td>Refines difference to <strong>scope</strong>, not presence/absence of reasoning.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>44</strong></td>
<td>Expert/documentary mixed</td>
<td>Both explanations remain plausible: information-state inheritance and the shoulder's acute/focal/testable anatomy. The record alone cannot apportion causation between them.</td>
<td>Path-dependence inference should remain moderate, not exclusive.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>45</strong></td>
<td>Answered materially</td>
<td>Yes. 2020 and 2023 prove shoulder-like proposition-testing can occur inside the inherited pathway when a sufficiently specific local question becomes live.</td>
<td>Retires any claim that inheritance eliminated analytical reasoning.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>46</strong></td>
<td>Open with strong negative documentary finding</td>
<td>No specialist note from 2018–2025 has yet been identified that gathers structural feet, dystonic posture, pain, gait, OFF state, neuropathy, treatment response and genetics into one renewed synthesis.</td>
<td>Core global-reconstruction question remains live.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>47</strong></td>
<td>Partly answered</td>
<td>2019 GP re-integration has no identified neurological reconstruction response; 2023 produces substantial local specialist reconsideration; 2025 ultimately opens a new external Movement Disorders/dystonia pathway.</td>
<td>Creates a longitudinal test of specialist response to recurrent GP re-integration.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>48</strong></td>
<td>Answered provisionally</td>
<td>The 2025 referrals are the first clearly identified later transfer of the disputed dystonia/top-level relationship question to a new Movement Disorders/dystonia specialist authority. Earlier Healy input was important but principally genetic/PARK2-focused.</td>
<td>Supports “global re-routing” in 2025, subject to any missing earlier source.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>49</strong></td>
<td>Answered provisionally</td>
<td>The March 2025 response is detailed on treatment rationale, pain modulation and dyskinesia risk, but it does not reconstruct PRKN provenance and begins from an affirmative neuropathic-pain symptom category. It explains the pathway more clearly from inside the inherited framework than it re-derives the framework.</td>
<td>Strengthens retrospective-authority/genealogy distinction.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><strong>50</strong></td>
<td>Answered provisionally pre-2026</td>
<td>The strongest surviving historical formulation is now: integrated GP object; selective specialist construction/partition; repeated primary-care re-integration; real specialist local reassessment; no presently identified specialist global reconstruction before external re-routing.</td>
<td>This is the current anti-drift capstone.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="114-1-questions-now-most-worth-attacking-next">114.1 Questions now most worth attacking next<a class="heading-anchor" href="#114-1-questions-now-most-worth-attacking-next">¶</a></h2>
<p>The status pass changes what should be searched next. The highest-yield unresolved questions are now:</p>
<ol>
<li><strong>Q1–Q2:</strong> prove what referral material Neurology actually possessed and used.</li>
<li><strong>Q7–Q10:</strong> exhaust the 2017 examination record for distal power, sensory mapping, dynamic toe posture and relational pain examination.</li>
<li><strong>Q18–Q19:</strong> find any explicit rationale for excluding/not using dystonia in the PRKN phenotype and any structural–dystonic join.</li>
<li><strong>Q24–Q25:</strong> identify any cross-service feedback rule or global-reconstruction trigger.</li>
<li><strong>Q28–Q29:</strong> exhaustively trace fresh PRKN reasoning after 2018 and any closure source.</li>
<li><strong>Q32 / Q47:</strong> follow the 2019 GP re-integration forward and determine whether Neurology ever received or acted on it.</li>
<li><strong>Q35 / Q46:</strong> perform a dedicated global-reconstruction search across 2023–2025.</li>
<li><strong>Q36–Q37:</strong> build the source-attributed neuropathic-language genealogy as a table rather than relying on selected examples.</li>
<li><strong>Q41:</strong> confirm formal authorship/provenance of the shoulder comparator to eliminate an avoidable challenge.</li>
<li><strong>Q48:</strong> check whether any earlier Movement Disorders specialist referral predating 2025 performed the top-level integrative role.</li>
</ol>
<p>These are now more valuable than repeatedly re-proving that the GP saw the feet–Parkinsonian relationship. That point is already materially established.</p>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="appendix-h-canonical-analytical-checklist-for-every-future-source">Appendix H — Canonical analytical checklist for every future source<a class="heading-anchor" href="#appendix-h-canonical-analytical-checklist-for-every-future-source">¶</a></h2>
<p>For every new source added to this project, ask the same questions. What is the exact proposition? Who is the source? Is it observation, patient report, interpretation, retrospective summary, copied history or independent specialist opinion? What prior information state does it inherit? What competing explanations are active? What evidence would discriminate them? Does the source change a local branch or the parent model? Does it preserve uncertainty explicitly? Does it convert uncertainty into a working category? Does it create a new owner, destination, review trigger or test? Does it rely on treatment response, and if so, does it treat that response as specific or non-specific? Does it refer back to older letters? Does it quote or paraphrase an earlier source? Does it add independent evidential support, or merely increase documentary persistence? Does it contain a statement that would falsify one of the project's working propositions? Finally, does it belong at the level of record fact, documentary inference, clinical expert question or legal conclusion?</p>
<p>This checklist is designed to keep the case falsifiable. The objective is not to accumulate material that agrees with the current theory. The objective is to determine whether the current theory survives contact with every later source, especially sources that show more integration, more specialist review, more explicit ownership or a clearer evidential bridge than has so far been identified.</p>
</section>
<section class="reader-section kind-audit" data-section="Pre-registered GP audit protocol" id="section-gp-protocol">
<div class="section-kicker">Pre-registered GP audit protocol</div>
<h2 class="source-h1 anchored-heading" id="pre-registered-sequential-gp-information-state-audit-protocol-frozen-before-full-gp-podiatry-source-review">Pre-registered sequential GP information-state audit — protocol frozen before full GP/Podiatry source review<a class="heading-anchor" href="#pre-registered-sequential-gp-information-state-audit-protocol-frozen-before-full-gp-podiatry-source-review">¶</a></h2>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="purpose-of-this-blind-staged-trial">Purpose of this blind/staged trial<a class="heading-anchor" href="#purpose-of-this-blind-staged-trial">¶</a></h2>
<p>Before reviewing the full GP record, Podiatry SAR, 2019 Podiatry re-referral, 2023 crisis referral, and 2025 St Vincent's referral as a connected corpus, the object of this trial is being frozen.</p>
<p>This is <strong>not</strong> a trial of whether the GP was "right" or "wrong." It is not a retrospective attempt to recruit the GP as support for the current Neurology critique. It is a prospective source-audit of a different information pathway:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>What clinical object did the GP believe he was carrying at each point in time, what information did he receive back from specialist services, what uncertainty remained live for him, and how did his subsequent referrals show that he operationalised that information?</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>The GP is therefore treated as a longitudinal <strong>information node</strong> with his own changing documentary state, not merely as a source of symptoms before Neurology.</p>
<p>The key variable is not the GP's private mental state, which the record cannot directly prove. The object is his <strong>documented and operational information state</strong> as evidenced by:</p>
<ul>
<li>what he records;</li>
<li>what he asks;</li>
<li>what he carries forward;</li>
<li>what he drops;</li>
<li>what he sends to another service;</li>
<li>which relationships he reconnects;</li>
<li>what he appears to treat as resolved, unresolved, structural, neurological, painful, urgent, or requiring another opinion;</li>
<li>and how those states change after receiving specialist correspondence.</li>
</ul>
<p>The trial should therefore avoid phrases such as "the GP thought" unless directly supported. Prefer:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>the GP record represents...</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>the referral treats X as...</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>the subsequent referral suggests that Y remained operationally live...</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>the record is consistent with the GP understanding the specialist response as...</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>where inference rather than direct statement is involved.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="blindness-limitation-and-protection-against-confirmation-bias">Blindness limitation and protection against confirmation bias<a class="heading-anchor" href="#blindness-limitation-and-protection-against-confirmation-bias">¶</a></h2>
<p>This audit is not absolutely blind in the experimental sense. Before seeing the full source set, the analysis already knows selected quoted material from the 2017 GP referral and later themes from prior controlled exercises.</p>
<p>Accordingly, the protection against drift will be <strong>pre-registration and sequential unblinding</strong>, not a false claim of total ignorance.</p>
<p>The rules are:</p>
<ol>
<li>The hypotheses below are frozen before review of the full GP/Podiatry corpus.</li>
<li>Each chronological stage is assessed using only information that would have been available to the GP by that stage.</li>
<li>Later records must not be used to rewrite an earlier GP information state.</li>
<li>The 2026 PRKN endpoint must not be introduced while assessing GP states before 2026.</li>
<li>Neurology's later explanations must not be treated as facts known to the GP unless the record shows they were communicated.</li>
<li>Podiatry findings must not be assumed to have reached the GP or Neurology unless transmission is documented.</li>
<li>Silence must not be converted automatically into belief or agreement.</li>
<li>A later re-referral may show persistence or reactivation of a question; it does not automatically prove that the same question remained continuously active in the GP's mind.</li>
<li>Every major inference must identify the contemporaneous words or actions supporting it.</li>
<li>Contrary evidence must be recorded even when it weakens the current information-pathway theory.</li>
</ol>
<p>The working case may move only if the source forces it to move.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="the-five-chronological-information-states-to-be-reconstructed">The five chronological information states to be reconstructed<a class="heading-anchor" href="#the-five-chronological-information-states-to-be-reconstructed">¶</a></h2>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="gp-state-0-pre-hospital-referral-generation-state">GP-State 0 — Pre-hospital / referral-generation state<a class="heading-anchor" href="#gp-state-0-pre-hospital-referral-generation-state">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Source window:</strong> GP record up to and including the 2017 referral.</p>
<p>Questions:</p>
<ul>
<li>What problem had accumulated before referral?</li>
<li>Did the GP represent the case as feet plus gait plus tone plus toe posture plus function, or as multiple parallel complaints?</li>
<li>What changed over time?</li>
<li>Which relationships did the GP himself make, and which were merely temporal coexistence?</li>
<li>What remained explicitly open in "?parkinsonian or other underlying diagnosis"?</li>
<li>Did the GP expect Neurology to explain the entire evolving presentation or only the Parkinsonian signs?</li>
</ul>
<p>This state establishes the <strong>outgoing information object</strong> before hospital compression.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="gp-state-1-post-neurology-discharge-information-state">GP-State 1 — Post-Neurology discharge information state<a class="heading-anchor" href="#gp-state-1-post-neurology-discharge-information-state">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Source window:</strong> first GP material after receipt of the 2017 Neurology/discharge correspondence, using only information then available.</p>
<p>Core question:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>What did the specialist return to the GP, and what would a reasonable documentary reading of that return leave live or apparently settled?</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>Specific points:</p>
<ul>
<li>Did the discharge correspondence communicate that the feet had been clinically examined, or only that structural abnormalities were noted?</li>
<li>Did it communicate "dystonic posture — NOT FOOT DROP" to the GP?</li>
<li>Did it communicate "may have been completely separate," "perhaps peripheral neuropathy," and "Not clear"?</li>
<li>Was Podiatry represented as a definitive destination or as one branch of an unresolved problem?</li>
<li>Was PRKN/PARK2 communicated, and at what level of specificity?</li>
<li>Was the GP given any explicit reopening rule for the feet–gait–tone relationship?</li>
<li>Was any responsibility assigned for integrating Podiatry findings back into Neurology?</li>
</ul>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="critical-sub-question-apparent-specialist-clearance">Critical sub-question: apparent specialist clearance<a class="heading-anchor" href="#critical-sub-question-apparent-specialist-clearance">¶</a></h3>
<p>One major issue is whether specialist correspondence could reasonably function, from the GP's information position, as an <strong>implicit clearance signal</strong> even though Neurology itself retained uncertainty.</p>
<p>The audit must ask:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>If a GP sends an integrated feet–gait–tone presentation to Neurology and receives back a sophisticated young-onset Parkinsonian discharge synthesis stating that the residual foot material may be separate/peripheral and is "Not clear," what does the subsequent GP record show about whether he treated the neurological relationship as adequately considered, still unresolved, or not his question to reopen?</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>This must remain a documentary question. Whether a GP <em>should</em> reasonably infer that a movement-disorders-level distal examination had occurred is a clinical/general-practice expert question unless explicitly stated in the correspondence.</p>
<p>We must therefore distinguish:</p>
<ol>
<li><strong>what Neurology actually documented examining;</strong></li>
<li><strong>what the discharge communication represented to the GP;</strong></li>
<li><strong>what the GP's later conduct suggests he understood from it;</strong></li>
<li><strong>what an expert GP could reasonably infer from such correspondence.</strong></li>
</ol>
<p>These are different propositions.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="gp-state-2-2019-podiatry-re-referral-state">GP-State 2 — 2019 Podiatry re-referral state<a class="heading-anchor" href="#gp-state-2-2019-podiatry-re-referral-state">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Source window:</strong> GP record and Podiatry material available by the 2019 re-referral.</p>
<p>This is a major test of what survived the discharge.</p>
<p>Ask:</p>
<ul>
<li>How does the GP describe the feet in 2019?</li>
<li>Does he describe them purely structurally?</li>
<li>Does he reconnect increased tone, abnormal gait, clawing, Parkinson's, or another neurological feature?</li>
<li>Does the wording suggest that the original integrated relationship remains alive despite the Neurology/Podiatry partition?</li>
<li>Is Podiatry being asked to manage a known structural destination, or is the referral itself carrying cross-boundary neurological information?</li>
<li>Does any Podiatry SAR material answer a question the GP had posed?</li>
<li>Does Podiatry feed anything back that could reasonably trigger Neurology reassessment?</li>
<li>If a "No neuropathy" field or equivalent appears, what exactly does it mean in that document, who authored it, and what is its evidential scope?</li>
</ul>
<p>This state may provide an independent check on whether the original integrated object was actually extinguished or merely distributed.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="gp-state-3-2023-crisis-referral-state">GP-State 3 — 2023 crisis-referral state<a class="heading-anchor" href="#gp-state-3-2023-crisis-referral-state">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Source window:</strong> GP record up to and including the 2023 urgent/crisis referral.</p>
<p>This is a stress test of the GP's longitudinal information state.</p>
<p>Ask:</p>
<ul>
<li>How does the GP characterise the pain now?</li>
<li>Does he use inherited neuropathic terminology, his own phenomenological language, or both?</li>
<li>Does he reconnect pain with Parkinson's control or medication state?</li>
<li>Does he remember a prior episode improving when Parkinson's control improved?</li>
<li>Does he treat the problem as already explained but inadequately controlled, or as causally unresolved?</li>
<li>What does "before we get into trouble here" or equivalent language indicate about urgency and ownership?</li>
<li>Is the GP asking Neurology to manage pain, reassess mechanism, or both?</li>
<li>Does the referral transmit safeguarding/endurance information that later specialist notes do or do not carry forward?</li>
</ul>
<p>This state tests whether severe recurrence caused the GP to regenerate an integrated question even if the specialist pathway remained compartmentalised.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="gp-state-4-2025-st-vincent-s-referral-state">GP-State 4 — 2025 St Vincent's referral state<a class="heading-anchor" href="#gp-state-4-2025-st-vincent-s-referral-state">¶</a></h3>
<p><strong>Source window:</strong> GP material available at the point of the external referral in 2025, without using the 2026 result.</p>
<p>This state asks what the GP believed still required specialist resolution after years of care.</p>
<p>Questions:</p>
<ul>
<li>Why is another specialist being sought?</li>
<li>What problem does the GP say remains unresolved?</li>
<li>Does the referral explicitly seek expertise in movement disorders and/or dystonia?</li>
<li>Does it reconnect the feet, gait, dystonia, pain, Parkinsonism, genetics, treatment state, or functional deterioration?</li>
<li>Does it carry forward the historical PRKN question?</li>
<li>Does it challenge, preserve, or bypass the inherited iPD/peripheral-neuropathic framework?</li>
<li>Is the referral framed as treatment optimisation, diagnostic second opinion, etiological clarification, movement-phenomenology review, or a mixture?</li>
</ul>
<p>This state is especially important because it occurs <strong>before</strong> the eventual PRKN result and therefore cannot be dismissed as retrospective reconstruction from genetics.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="the-podiatry-sar-as-a-parallel-information-stream">The Podiatry SAR as a parallel information stream<a class="heading-anchor" href="#the-podiatry-sar-as-a-parallel-information-stream">¶</a></h2>
<p>The Podiatry SAR should not be read merely for whether Podiatry "got the diagnosis right."</p>
<p>It should be analysed as an information stream with its own object and limits:</p>
<ul>
<li>what Podiatry was told;</li>
<li>what it observed;</li>
<li>what it treated;</li>
<li>what it regarded as structural/mechanical;</li>
<li>whether neurological descriptors entered its record;</li>
<li>whether it recorded clawing, gait, pressure distribution, deformity, neuropathy status, pain sites, or loading patterns;</li>
<li>whether findings were communicated back to the GP;</li>
<li>whether any findings were communicated back to Neurology;</li>
<li>whether the GP later reused Podiatry information in a neurologically integrated way.</li>
</ul>
<p>The key information-flow question is:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>Did Podiatry merely receive one branch of the problem, or did its local mechanical findings become evidence available for reintegration into the larger feet–gait–tone relationship?</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>This directly tests the existing distinction between <strong>destination</strong> and <strong>ownership of the arrows</strong>.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="pre-registered-hypotheses-for-the-gp-pathway">Pre-registered hypotheses for the GP pathway<a class="heading-anchor" href="#pre-registered-hypotheses-for-the-gp-pathway">¶</a></h2>
<p>The following are hypotheses to be tested, not conclusions to be confirmed.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="h-gp1-integrated-origin-hypothesis">H-GP1 — Integrated-origin hypothesis<a class="heading-anchor" href="#h-gp1-integrated-origin-hypothesis">¶</a></h3>
<p>The GP's initial information state represented the presentation as an integrated deterioration rather than as independent foot disease plus Parkinsonism.</p>
<p><strong>Would weaken if:</strong> the full GP record shows longstanding explicit separation of foot disease from neurological change before referral.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="h-gp2-specialist-return-apparent-clearance-hypothesis">H-GP2 — Specialist-return / apparent-clearance hypothesis<a class="heading-anchor" href="#h-gp2-specialist-return-apparent-clearance-hypothesis">¶</a></h3>
<p>After Neurology, the GP may have had documentary reason to treat the central Parkinsonian diagnosis as specialist-resolved while assuming that the feet had also undergone appropriate neurological consideration, even though the discharge retained "Not clear."</p>
<p><strong>Would strengthen if:</strong> later GP records defer to Neurology's partition without re-asking the neurological relationship despite persistent concern.</p>
<p><strong>Would weaken if:</strong> the GP immediately records that Neurology had not resolved the feet, explicitly questions the discharge conclusion, or seeks movement-disorders reassessment.</p>
<p><strong>Clinical expert boundary:</strong> whether such reliance would be reasonable is not for documentary analysis alone.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="h-gp3-residual-question-hypothesis">H-GP3 — Residual-question hypothesis<a class="heading-anchor" href="#h-gp3-residual-question-hypothesis">¶</a></h3>
<p>Despite specialist partition, some residue of the original integrated question may remain visible in the GP record and later referrals.</p>
<p><strong>Would strengthen if:</strong> the GP repeatedly reconnects feet with tone, gait, clawing, Parkinson's control, or movement state.</p>
<p><strong>Would weaken if:</strong> later GP records consistently treat the feet as purely structural or neuropathic without any cross-linkage.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="h-gp4-re-integration-under-stress-hypothesis">H-GP4 — Re-integration under stress hypothesis<a class="heading-anchor" href="#h-gp4-re-integration-under-stress-hypothesis">¶</a></h3>
<p>When symptoms become severe, the GP may regenerate cross-boundary relationships that routine specialist documentation had allowed to remain compartmentalised.</p>
<p><strong>Would strengthen if:</strong> the 2023 crisis referral reconnects pain with prior Parkinson's control, gait, medication state, or neurological review.</p>
<p><strong>Would weaken if:</strong> the crisis referral treats the issue solely as established neuropathic pain requiring analgesic escalation.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="h-gp5-external-referral-reconstruction-hypothesis">H-GP5 — External-referral reconstruction hypothesis<a class="heading-anchor" href="#h-gp5-external-referral-reconstruction-hypothesis">¶</a></h3>
<p>By 2025, the GP may independently reconstruct enough of the unresolved movement/feet problem to seek a different specialist perspective before knowing the PRKN result.</p>
<p><strong>Would strengthen if:</strong> the St Vincent's referral seeks movement-disorders/dystonia expertise and reassembles historically separated components.</p>
<p><strong>Would weaken if:</strong> the referral is purely administrative, medication-focused, or unrelated to the unresolved lower-limb relationship.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="h-gp6-information-asymmetry-hypothesis">H-GP6 — Information asymmetry hypothesis<a class="heading-anchor" href="#h-gp6-information-asymmetry-hypothesis">¶</a></h3>
<p>The GP may repeatedly possess cross-domain information that no single downstream specialty holds in the same integrated form.</p>
<p><strong>Would strengthen if:</strong> GP notes combine Neurology, Podiatry, treatment response, functional deterioration, and patient experience in ways not mirrored in specialist letters.</p>
<p><strong>Would weaken if:</strong> GP entries merely copy specialist classifications without adding or reconnecting information.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="h-gp7-specialist-authority-effect-hypothesis">H-GP7 — Specialist-authority effect hypothesis<a class="heading-anchor" href="#h-gp7-specialist-authority-effect-hypothesis">¶</a></h3>
<p>The GP's later pathway may show deference to specialist classifications even where the specialist correspondence itself preserved uncertainty.</p>
<p>This is <strong>not</strong> a claim about inappropriate deference or psychological bias.</p>
<p>It asks whether the information architecture behaves as follows:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>specialist uncertainty + management destination → GP operational reliance on the specialist arrangement</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p><strong>Would strengthen if:</strong> specialist uncertainty is not restated in later GP records while the resulting destinations/categories are carried forward.</p>
<p><strong>Would weaken if:</strong> the GP repeatedly preserves and actively interrogates the original uncertainty.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="h-gp8-gp-as-re-integration-node-hypothesis">H-GP8 — GP as re-integration node hypothesis<a class="heading-anchor" href="#h-gp8-gp-as-re-integration-node-hypothesis">¶</a></h3>
<p>The GP may function as the service most capable of seeing the distributed whole because information from Neurology, Podiatry, patient reports, medication changes and functional decline returns to primary care.</p>
<p><strong>Would strengthen if:</strong> later referrals recombine material originating in multiple services.</p>
<p><strong>Would weaken if:</strong> the GP record becomes as compartmentalised as the specialty pathways.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="observable-markers-what-to-look-for-without-mind-reading">Observable markers: what to look for without mind-reading<a class="heading-anchor" href="#observable-markers-what-to-look-for-without-mind-reading">¶</a></h2>
<p>The audit should code specific documentary markers rather than intuiting the GP's psychology.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="markers-of-an-active-unresolved-relationship">Markers of an active unresolved relationship<a class="heading-anchor" href="#markers-of-an-active-unresolved-relationship">¶</a></h3>
<ul>
<li>question marks;</li>
<li>"? cause" / "unclear" / "not sure";</li>
<li>requests for review rather than simple treatment;</li>
<li>explicit linkage of feet with gait, tone, clawing, Parkinson's control, or medication state;</li>
<li>re-referral after a specialist destination has already been established;</li>
<li>reference to prior improvement with neurological treatment;</li>
<li>requests for another specialist opinion;</li>
<li>language that asks what explains the symptom rather than only how to manage it.</li>
</ul>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="markers-of-apparent-closure-or-operational-reliance">Markers of apparent closure or operational reliance<a class="heading-anchor" href="#markers-of-apparent-closure-or-operational-reliance">¶</a></h3>
<ul>
<li>specialist diagnosis repeated without qualification;</li>
<li>foot problem described only under Podiatry/neuropathy headings;</li>
<li>absence of the earlier relational language while management destinations persist;</li>
<li>referrals framed purely as continuation of an established branch;</li>
<li>statements suggesting Neurology has already assessed/cleared the issue;</li>
<li>treatment escalation without renewed causal question.</li>
</ul>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="markers-of-re-integration">Markers of re-integration<a class="heading-anchor" href="#markers-of-re-integration">¶</a></h3>
<ul>
<li>feet + gait in same sentence/problem statement;</li>
<li>feet + Parkinson's control;</li>
<li>feet + tone/stiffness;</li>
<li>toe clawing/curling + movement state;</li>
<li>structural findings + abnormal gait;</li>
<li>pain + dopaminergic timing;</li>
<li>referral explicitly asking a specialist to consider both movement disorder and distal symptoms.</li>
</ul>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="markers-of-service-boundary-information-loss">Markers of service-boundary information loss<a class="heading-anchor" href="#markers-of-service-boundary-information-loss">¶</a></h3>
<ul>
<li>GP asks a relationship question, receiving service answers only one branch;</li>
<li>Podiatry observes a mechanically relevant fact that never appears in Neurology;</li>
<li>Neurology observes a dynamic phenomenon that never appears in Podiatry;</li>
<li>severe patient-reported functional/safeguarding information is compressed in onward communication;</li>
<li>a qualification such as "Not clear" disappears while the resulting category/destination remains.</li>
</ul>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="the-gp-specific-counterfactual-to-test">The GP-specific counterfactual to test<a class="heading-anchor" href="#the-gp-specific-counterfactual-to-test">¶</a></h2>
<p>One pre-registered counterfactual deserves special protection:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>If the GP sent an integrated feet–gait–tone–toe–deterioration problem to hospital, and the hospital returned a sophisticated neurological synthesis that had itself seen the feet and called their relationship "Not clear," the GP may reasonably have operated on the assumption that the relevant neurological relationship had received specialist consideration even if it had not been fully resolved.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>The record must decide whether that hypothesis has support.</p>
<p>The audit must <strong>not</strong> jump from that hypothesis to:</p>
<blockquote>
<p>the GP believed a Movement Disorders specialist had performed a comprehensive dystonia examination.</p>
</blockquote>
<p>That would require evidence or expert opinion.</p>
<p>Instead ask:</p>
<ul>
<li>What did the discharge look like from the GP's documentary position?</li>
<li>What level of specialist resolution did its sophistication imply?</li>
<li>Did the GP's later behaviour show reliance on that apparent resolution?</li>
<li>Did later GP referrals show that the unresolved relationship resurfaced despite that reliance?</li>
</ul>
<p>This is potentially important because the case may contain <strong>different information states at different nodes</strong>:</p>
<blockquote>
<p>GP sends an integrated question → hospital compresses the task → Neurology develops a sophisticated central/genetic model and partitions residual feet uncertainty → GP receives the specialist product and may reasonably treat the specialist partition as having evidential weight → later primary-care observations repeatedly regenerate the integrated problem.</p>
</blockquote>
<p>That chain is only a hypothesis until the GP record is examined.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="sequential-unblinding-method">Sequential-unblinding method<a class="heading-anchor" href="#sequential-unblinding-method">¶</a></h2>
<p>When the full record is supplied, the analysis should proceed in this order and should write a short conclusion <strong>before opening the next stage</strong>.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="pass-1-gp-pre-referral-record">Pass 1 — GP pre-referral record<a class="heading-anchor" href="#pass-1-gp-pre-referral-record">¶</a></h3>
<p>Output:</p>
<ul>
<li>the GP's problem representation;</li>
<li>timeline of change;</li>
<li>relationships explicitly drawn;</li>
<li>open questions;</li>
<li>what the referral asks Neurology to solve.</li>
</ul>
<p>Freeze conclusion.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="pass-2-neurology-return-as-seen-by-gp">Pass 2 — Neurology return as seen by GP<a class="heading-anchor" href="#pass-2-neurology-return-as-seen-by-gp">¶</a></h3>
<p>Output:</p>
<ul>
<li>what new information the GP receives;</li>
<li>what appears resolved;</li>
<li>what remains explicitly unresolved;</li>
<li>what destinations are assigned;</li>
<li>what review/reopening rules are communicated.</li>
</ul>
<p>Freeze conclusion.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="pass-3-podiatry-sar-and-2019-re-referral">Pass 3 — Podiatry SAR and 2019 re-referral<a class="heading-anchor" href="#pass-3-podiatry-sar-and-2019-re-referral">¶</a></h3>
<p>Output:</p>
<ul>
<li>what structural/mechanical information enters primary care;</li>
<li>whether neurological relationships remain or reappear;</li>
<li>whether information crosses back toward Neurology.</li>
</ul>
<p>Freeze conclusion.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="pass-4-2023-crisis-referral">Pass 4 — 2023 crisis referral<a class="heading-anchor" href="#pass-4-2023-crisis-referral">¶</a></h3>
<p>Output:</p>
<ul>
<li>how the GP reconstructs the problem under severe pain/endurance pressure;</li>
<li>what historical relationships are remembered;</li>
<li>whether mechanism is treated as settled or reopened;</li>
<li>what exactly Neurology is being asked to do.</li>
</ul>
<p>Freeze conclusion.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="pass-5-2025-st-vincent-s-referral">Pass 5 — 2025 St Vincent's referral<a class="heading-anchor" href="#pass-5-2025-st-vincent-s-referral">¶</a></h3>
<p>Output:</p>
<ul>
<li>what remains unresolved before genetic confirmation;</li>
<li>what expertise is sought;</li>
<li>which historical components are recombined;</li>
<li>whether the GP independently moves toward a movement-disorders/dystonia reconstruction.</li>
</ul>
<p>Freeze conclusion.</p>
<h3 class="anchored-heading" id="pass-6-cross-time-comparison">Pass 6 — Cross-time comparison<a class="heading-anchor" href="#pass-6-cross-time-comparison">¶</a></h3>
<p>Only after the five states are separately frozen should the analysis ask:</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>What changed in the GP's information state across the pathway?</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<p>The final comparison should map:</p>
<blockquote>
<p>outgoing integrated object → specialist return → operational primary-care state → re-referral state → crisis state → external-specialist referral state.</p>
</blockquote>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="relationship-to-the-existing-50-question-audit">Relationship to the existing 50-question audit<a class="heading-anchor" href="#relationship-to-the-existing-50-question-audit">¶</a></h2>
<p>The existing 50 questions remain intact. This GP trial provides a new source stream capable of answering or challenging many of them, especially:</p>
<ul>
<li>Q1–Q5: what reached Neurology and whether the integrated trajectory remained live;</li>
<li>Q11–Q14: what evidential bridge supported separation/peripheral interpretation;</li>
<li>Q23–Q25: ownership, Podiatry feedback and reopening triggers;</li>
<li>Q33–Q35: whether later lower-limb information fed back into the parent model;</li>
<li>Q46–Q49: whether any later service or complaint process globally reconstructed the case.</li>
</ul>
<p>The GP corpus should also be allowed to generate <strong>new questions</strong> if it contains information that the current Neurology-centred formulation did not anticipate. New questions may be added, but the pre-registered hypotheses above must remain visible so that later insight is not confused with what was predicted beforehand.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="anti-drift-rule-for-the-gp-trial">Anti-drift rule for the GP trial<a class="heading-anchor" href="#anti-drift-rule-for-the-gp-trial">¶</a></h2>
<p>This trial is not being conducted to make the GP "the good clinician" and Neurology "the bad clinician."</p>
<p>It is not a contest between specialties.</p>
<p>It is an information-flow study.</p>
<p>Possible outcomes include:</p>
<ol>
<li><strong>Strong support for the current theory:</strong> the GP repeatedly preserves/reconstructs the integrated relationship while specialist pathways remain more compartmentalised.</li>
<li><strong>Partial support:</strong> the GP intermittently reconnects the problem, but also substantially adopts the specialist partition.</li>
<li><strong>Neutral result:</strong> the GP record does not contain enough relational reasoning to tell us much about the information pathway.</li>
<li><strong>Contrary result:</strong> the GP himself clearly separates the feet from the neurological syndrome before or after admission and treats later cross-boundary events as unrelated.</li>
<li><strong>Major falsification:</strong> the GP record demonstrates that the full integrated question was in fact communicated, explicitly answered, and later managed under a defined cross-service reopening architecture.</li>
</ol>
<p>Any of those outcomes must be accepted if supported by the source.</p>
<p>The purpose of freezing the trial now is precisely to make later agreement or disagreement with the working theory evidentially meaningful.</p>
<hr/>
<h2 class="anchored-heading" id="frozen-one-paragraph-hypothesis-before-source-review">Frozen one-paragraph hypothesis before source review<a class="heading-anchor" href="#frozen-one-paragraph-hypothesis-before-source-review">¶</a></h2>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>Before reviewing the full GP/Podiatry pathway, the working hypothesis is that primary care may provide an independent longitudinal view of how the original integrated feet–gait–tone–toe–function problem survived, disappeared, or re-emerged after specialist partition. The most important question is not what the GP privately believed, but what his subsequent documentation and referrals show he treated as resolved, unresolved, or worthy of renewed neurological integration. Particular attention will be paid to whether the sophisticated Neurology discharge could function as an apparent specialist-resolution signal for the GP despite retaining "Not clear" around the feet; whether the 2019 Podiatry re-referral preserves cross-boundary neurological information; whether the 2023 crisis referral re-integrates pain with Parkinsonian control; and whether the 2025 St Vincent's referral reconstructs a movement-disorders/dystonia question before the later PRKN result. The trial may strengthen, qualify, or falsify the current information-pathway theory, but its object is frozen before the full source set is opened.</strong></p>
</blockquote>
<hr/>
</section>
</article>
</main>
</div>
<button class="backtop" id="topBtn" title="Back to top">↑</button>
<script>
(function(){
  const root=document.documentElement;
  const themeBtn=document.getElementById('themeBtn');
  const stored=localStorage.getItem('case-theme');
  if(stored) root.dataset.theme=stored;
  themeBtn.addEventListener('click',()=>{
    const next=root.dataset.theme==='dark'?'light':'dark';
    root.dataset.theme=next; localStorage.setItem('case-theme',next);
  });
  document.getElementById('printBtn').addEventListener('click',()=>window.print());
  document.getElementById('topBtn').addEventListener('click',()=>window.scrollTo({top:0,behavior:'smooth'}));

  const links=[...document.querySelectorAll('.toc a')];
  const targets=links.map(a=>document.getElementById(a.hash.slice(1))).filter(Boolean);
  const obs=new IntersectionObserver(entries=>{
    const hit=entries.filter(e=>e.isIntersecting).sort((a,b)=>a.boundingClientRect.top-b.boundingClientRect.top)[0];
    if(!hit) return;
    links.forEach(a=>a.classList.toggle('active',a.hash==='#'+hit.target.id));
  },{rootMargin:'-10% 0px -78% 0px',threshold:[0,1]});
  targets.forEach(t=>obs.observe(t));

  const search=document.getElementById('searchBox');
  let timer;
  search.addEventListener('input',()=>{
    clearTimeout(timer); timer=setTimeout(()=>runSearch(search.value.trim()),140);
  });
  function clearMarks(){
    document.querySelectorAll('mark.search-hit').forEach(m=>m.replaceWith(document.createTextNode(m.textContent)));
  }
  function runSearch(q){
    clearMarks();
    document.querySelectorAll('.reader-section').forEach(s=>s.classList.remove('hidden-by-search'));
    if(q.length<2) return;
    const qq=q.toLowerCase();
    document.querySelectorAll('.reader-section').forEach(section=>{
      if(!section.innerText.toLowerCase().includes(qq)){ section.classList.add('hidden-by-search'); return; }
      const walker=document.createTreeWalker(section,NodeFilter.SHOW_TEXT,{acceptNode(node){
        if(!node.nodeValue.toLowerCase().includes(qq)) return NodeFilter.FILTER_REJECT;
        if(node.parentElement.closest('script,style,mark')) return NodeFilter.FILTER_REJECT;
        return NodeFilter.FILTER_ACCEPT;
      }});
      const nodes=[]; while(walker.nextNode()) nodes.push(walker.currentNode);
      nodes.slice(0,40).forEach(node=>{
        const val=node.nodeValue, lower=val.toLowerCase(); let pos=0; const frag=document.createDocumentFragment(); let ix;
        while((ix=lower.indexOf(qq,pos))!==-1){
          frag.appendChild(document.createTextNode(val.slice(pos,ix)));
          const mk=document.createElement('mark'); mk.className='search-hit'; mk.textContent=val.slice(ix,ix+q.length); frag.appendChild(mk);
          pos=ix+q.length;
        }
        frag.appendChild(document.createTextNode(val.slice(pos))); node.replaceWith(frag);
      });
    });
    const first=document.querySelector('mark.search-hit'); if(first) first.scrollIntoView({behavior:'smooth',block:'center'});
  }
})();
</script>
</body>
</html>
````
